XV. Concerning Chapter 3.

### Chapter 3.

### On the introduction of the fiery Science <term>Scienz: a spiritual "knowing" or magnetic longing that acts as a creative force</term> into the manifestation of nature and essence; how the "Science" introduces itself into fire, what that is, and how multiplication occurs.

### The Gate of the Great Mystery <note>original: *Mysterii*</note> of all Secrets.

->Summaries.<-

In the word "He created" <note>original: *Schuf*</note>, as in the act of speaking, lies the ground. §. 1. 2. The 1st Species of Nature <note>Latin: *Species Naturae*; the fundamental forms of natural existence</note>, as the Harsh Quality, grasps, makes dark, hard, sharp, cold, and comprehensible. 3. The 2nd Species of Nature is the sting of sensitivity, the Bitter; it draws toward itself, and from this, sensitivity originates <note>original: *urständet*</note>. 4. The 3rd quality is Anxiety <note>original: *Angst*; a state of contraction or agonizing tension</note>, a root of the fire and a hunger for freedom. 5. These are the first 3 qualities of the Single Will, and God's revelation in His word of power, 6. 7. where the Harshness belongs to the Father; there the bitter sting is the property of the Son; 8. and Anxiety is the property of the Holy Spirit. 9. In the Creation within natural life, they are called Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>original: *Sal, Sulphur und Mercurius*; the three Paracelsian principles of matter</note>. 10. With these 3 qualities, the spiritual world has introduced itself into a tangible essence. 11. The 4th quality is the igniting of the fire, with which light and darkness separate into two Principles <note>original: *Principia*</note>. 12. The 1st Divine Will introduces itself in power into the Word for revelation and further into a desire within the First 3 qualities toward nature; 13. But in the Anxiety, it grasps itself in another will toward the freedom, in which grasping a shock <note>original: *Schrack*; a sudden jolt, crack, or terror</note> occurs, 14. and this shock creates Hell within the dark impression. 15. Now, when Love enters into the Wrath, the fire is startled and gives off a flash of lightning, 16. for Love is gentle and the ground of Light, 17. in which God’s kingdom is understood; just as God's strength is understood in the fire; and in the darkness, God's wrath and Hell are understood. 18. An exhortation: that one should not understand this high, supernatural sense in an earthly way! 19. In this igniting of fire lies the ground of all secrets. 20. Through this, the spirit separates itself upward into the fiery Science of the powers; and the Essence <note>original: *Ens*</note> of Love remains in the center, out of which comes the Tincture <note>A purifying, transformative spiritual essence</note>; whose name is Sophia <note>Greek: Wisdom</note>. 21. The Fire-Soul, which is the Father's property, is the male: the Light-Soul is the female. 22. 23. But the Water-Spirit goes downward; from which the external water, metals, stones, and earth also have their beginning. 24. The fourth separation goes into the darkness, where everything lies in Phantasy <note>original: *Phantasey*; here meaning the realm of formless mirrors or illusory shadows</note>. 25. The 5th Species of Nature is the true Love-fire, whose powers draw the Tincture of fire and light—as Sophia—into themselves, 26. and this is Father and Son, from which the Holy Spirit goes forth. 27. To whomsoever God reveals Sophia should sink themselves in humility. 28. This quality is the everlasting revelation of the Father in His Son. 29. 30. The 6th Species of Nature

# Chapter 3. Election of Grace.

Nature is the resonance of the powers, wherein the Holy Spirit audibly expresses Himself within the embrace of Love, 31. which spiritual resonance has infused itself into the creation. 32. Within this resides the true understanding, the senses, and the mind: The senses possess both love and wrath within themselves; they are righteous as long as they remain in the <term>Temperature</term> <term>Temperatur: a state of perfect balance and harmony where no single quality dominates others</term>; but in self-will <note>original: *Eigenheit*</note> they are deceitful. 33. Thus Adam, through the devil's interference, raised himself out of this equality, 34. and desired to taste all the qualities in their distinctness. 35. But as soon as one quality exalts itself, it becomes a hostile opposition to the others. 36. The 7 <term>Species of Nature</term> <term>Latin: *Species Naturæ*; the seven fundamental forms or "qualities" that Böhme believes constitute all existence</term>, as the enclosed essence of all powers, is rightly called Paradise, or the whole of Nature, the inner Divine Heaven. 37. The outer world is the creature: the inner is a generating essence. 38. From these 7 forms also originate the 7 days with their names; 39. the Day of Rest is Christ. <note>original Latin: *ibid.* (in the same place)</note> Finally, these 7 properties in the inner world are spiritual, in a clear essence: in the outer world they are evil and good, and therefore in conflict. 40.

As the precious Moses describes the creation of the world, he says: God spoke, "Let there be!" (Genesis 1:3). Thus it came to be; and then he says: "In the beginning God created the Heaven and the Earth" (Genesis 1:1). And in John 1:1, 2 it is written: God made all things through His Word.

2. In this lies the ground and deep understanding: for from eternity nothing has existed but only God in His Trinity in His Wisdom, as previously mentioned, and therein the <term>Science</term> <term>Scienz: from the Latin *scientia*, used here by Böhme to mean a creative, magnetic "knowing" or a longing that draws potential into being</term>, as the speaking, the breathing-out of oneself, the grasping, the forming, and the leading into properties. The "grasping" is the "He created" <note>original: *Schuf*</note>, and the <term>Science</term> (acting as the desire) is the beginning from the <term>Temperature</term> toward distinctness; for the entire foundation lies in this: that it is said God created through the Word. The Word remains in God, and goes out with the <term>Science</term> (as with the desire) from itself into a division, which is to be understood thus: the <term>Science</term> is eternal in the Word because it originates in the Will; in the Word it is God, and in the division (that is, in the grasping or "comprehension"), it is the beginning of Nature.

->The 1st Species of Nature.<-

3. The 1st form of Nature is Harshness <note>original: *Herbe*; also translated as "Astringency," the force of contraction</note>, as the graspability of itself; its manifestations, which arise within its enclosure, are these: 1st, Darkness, for the act of grasping overshadows the free will in the <term>Science</term>; 2nd, it is the cause of Hardness, for that which is drawn together is hard and rough, yet in the Eternal it is to be understood only as spirit; 3rd, it is a cause of Sharpness; 4th, a primal

...cause of the cold, as the cold-firing property; fifthly, a cause of all being, or tangibility. In the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original Latin: *Mysterio Magno*; Böhme’s term for the primal state of all potentiality before creation</note> it is the mother of all salts and a root of nature. In the Mystery, it is called by the single word **Salt** <note>original Latin: *Sal*</note>, acting as a spiritual sharpness, the <term>origin</term> <note>original German: *Urstand*</note> of **God's** wrath, as well as the origin of the kingdom of joy.

### Concerning the 2nd Form of Nature.

4. The second form in the <term>Science</term> <note>original German: *Scienß*; here meaning the divine knowledge or the structural "knowing" of the universe</note> is the sting of sensitivity, which is the "drawing" or attraction itself, from which feeling and sensitivity originate. For the more the harshness impresses itself, the greater this sting becomes, acting as a raging, raving, and breaking force. Its divisions into qualities are these: bitterness, woe, pain, and stirring—the beginning of resistance within the <term>Temperature</term> <term>Temperatur: the state of perfect balance between all properties</term>. It is a cause of spiritual life and also a cause of "pulsing" or torment <note>Böhme frequently puns on the German words *Qual* (torment) and *Quelle* (source/wellspring)</note>. It is a father or root of the <term>mercurial life</term> <note>refers to the principle of motion and communication, often associated with the chemical element Mercury in alchemy</note> in all living and growing things; a cause of the fleeting senses, as well as a cause of exalted joys in the light, and a cause of hostile adversity within the severe impression of hardness, from which conflict and resistance arise.

### Concerning the 3rd Form of Nature.

5. The third form in the Science is Anguish, which arises in the conflict between the harshness and the stinging bitterness. It is an "Ens" <note>Latin: "essence" or "being"</note> of feeling, the beginning of essence and of the mind, a root of fire and of all painfulness. It is a hunger and thirst for freedom, looking toward the <term>Abyss</term> <note>original German: *Ungrunde*; the "Groundless" or the nothingness out of which God manifests</note>. It is a revelation of the eternal, bottomless will within the Science, where the will introduces itself into a spiritual form. It is also a cause of dying—the birth of death—and yet not death itself, but rather where the beginning of natural life arises. It is precisely the root where **God** and Nature are distinguished—not as a separation, but because of the "Temperature" in the Godhead, so that here the audible, sensory life emerges, from which the Creation took its origin.

6. These three aforementioned forms—Harshness, the Bitter-Sting, and Anguish—are the first three in the Science of the Unified Will, which is called the Father of all beings; they take their foundation and origin in the Science from the Trinity of the Godhead.

7. It is not to be understood that these forms are God Himself; rather, they are His revelation in His Word of power. First, there is **Harshness** <note>original German: *Herbe*; also translated as astringency or contraction</note>, which is the beginning of strength and power, acting as a foundation from which everything comes and has its <term>origin</term> <note>original German: *urständet*; a term Böhme uses to describe the primordial "standing up" or emergence of being</note>, arising from the Father’s property in the Word.

8. Secondly, there is the **bitter sting**, as the beginning of life, which has its origin in the Son’s property from the Word; for it is the cause of all powers and distinctions, as well as of speech, understanding, and the five senses.

9. Thirdly, there is **Anguish**, which originates from the Holy Spirit’s property in the Word. It is the cause of both fires: the light-fire of love, and the painful fire of consumption. It is the true origin of established creaturely life, and also of dying—leading to both joy and sorrow—the root of all life, arising from the **Science** <term>Scienz: the divine blueprint or structural "knowing" of the eternal Will</term> of the one eternal Will.

10. In the **Creation** of natural life, following the **Compaction** <term>Compaction: the process of spiritual forces densifying into physical matter</term> in the creative act, these first three properties are called **Salt**, **Sulphur**, and **Mercury**. Here, the spirit-life has introduced itself into a visible, tangible **Matter**, which is present in all things—in the life of the flesh and in the growing things of the earth—both **Spiritually** and **Corporally**, with nothing excepted; for all the beings of this world consist of these, as is evident to the eye and known to the experienced.

11. For in this way the Invisible—the spiritual world—has introduced itself through these first three forms into a visible, tangible essence: spiritual according to the spirits, and tangible according to the bodies. Also, the entire earth with all its materials originates from this, as do the entire stars <note>referring to the "astrum" or celestial influences</note> and the elements. However, one must look further and progress through all seven forms if one wishes to understand the sun, stars, and elements, as follows hereafter.

### Concerning the 4th **Species of Nature** <note>original Latin: *Specie Naturæ*</note>.

12. The 4th form in the **Science** of the Single Will is the ignition of the fire. Here, light and darkness separate, each into its own **Principle** <note>original Latin: *Principium*; Böhme views the universe as divided into "Principles" or realms of existence</note>. For here is the origin of light, as well as of the true life in the sensitivity of the first three forms, and the true distinction between anguish and joy. This occurs as follows:

13. The first Will in the **Trinity** <term>Dreyfaltigkeit: the Triune nature of God as Father, Son, and Holy Spirit</term>, which is God (outside of...

For in the Clash is death, and in the Clash the **Heartiness** <note>original: *Herzigkeit*; refers to the essential quality of the "Heart" or Center of God</note> gathers itself into being, as into a **Mercurial** Spirit-Water, out of which—in the **Compaction** at the beginning of the creation of the Earth—stones, metals, and the Mercurial, Sulphuric water were born, from which metals and stones have their origin.

15. This Clash creates in the first three properties (Harsh, Bitter, and Anguish), following the dark Compaction in itself, the hostile, terrible life of the **Wrath** <note>original: *Grimm*; also translated as fierceness or fury</note> or Anger of GOD, of devouring and consuming; for it is the ignition of the fire, as the essence of the painfulness or consumability of the fire. Following the dark Compaction, it is called **Hell** or a Hidden Cave, as a life of pain gathering itself within itself, which is only sensitive and manifest to itself, and compared to the whole Groundless is rightly called a hidden cave, which is not manifest in the light, and yet is a cause of the fire's ignition: to be understood in the same way that the night dwells in the day, and yet neither is the other.

16. So now understand the ignition of the fire correctly: it happens through a conjunction of the first three properties, in their gathering into Wrath; and on the other hand from the lovely freedom of **Being** <note>original: *Entis*; the Latin genitive for "of being" or "of essence"</note> in the Balance, where love and wrath go into one another. ...happens also a **Shock**: In Love, the **Shock** <note>original: *Schrack*; a sudden flash, crack, or terror that marks the transition from darkness to light</note> is a beginning of the flash or radiance, as the unitary Love makes itself perceptible, as Majestic or shining, as the beginning of the Joyful-realm, in the manner that light becomes shining within the fire. Also in Love is the beginning of the **differentiation of powers**, so that the powers become expressive in the Shock, from which the scent and taste of distinctions arise; and in the first three [properties] the painful nature of the fire is understood.

17. For (1) **Harshness** <note>The first property of nature: contraction and density</note> impresses and consumes, and (2) **Bitterness** is the sting of woe, and (3) **Anguish** is now death, and also the new fire-life, for it is the mother of **Sulphur**; and the <term>Ens</term> <note>Ens: the essential being or "stuff" of a thing</note> of Love gives to the Anguish—as the Sulphur-mother—a refreshment for new life, from which the brilliance of fire originates. For we see that the light is gentle, and the fire painful: thus we understand that the ground of Light originates from the **Temperature** <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect balance or harmony</note>, as from the unification out of the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>Ungrund: the "Groundless" abyss or the absolute nothingness of God before manifestation</note> of unitary Love, which is called God; and the fire originates from the guiding Will in the Word, out of the <term>Scienz</term> <note>Scienz: the divine structural "knowing" or blueprint that directs the will</note> through the Impression and introduction into the first three properties.

18. In the **Light**, the Kingdom of God is now understood, as the kingdom of Love; and in the **Fire**, God's strength and omnipotence is understood, as the spiritual creaturely life; and in the **Darkness**, death, hell, and the wrath of God, and the anxious poison-life are understood, as is to be understood in the earth, stones, metals, and creatures of the outer created world.

19. And I admonish the reader not to understand the high supernatural sense (where I speak of God and the birthing of the <term>Mysterium Magnum</term> <note>original: *Great Mystery*; the chaos or potentiality from which all things are created</note>) in an earthly way: for I only indicate thereby the ground from which the earthly has come. Thus I must often speak so that the reader understands it, and ponders it, and swings himself into the inner ground; for I must often give earthly names to the Heavenly for the sake of that through which the earthly has been expressed.

20. In the ignition of the Fire lies the entire ground of all secrecy, for the **Shock** of ignition is called in nature <term>Sal Nitri</term> <note>Sal Nitri: literally saltpeter, but here used alchemically to mean the "nitrous" spark or the explosive potential of life</note>, as a root of all salts of the powers, a differentiation of nature, where the **Science** <term>Scienz: the divine blueprint</term> divides into infinity, and yet always in the Shock, as a shock of the

### XV. Concerning the 3rd Chapter.

The differentiation within the essence thus remains. In the ignition of the fire (understood according to the inner magical fire), the Spirit of God moves as a weaving presence, in the same way that air originates from fire. For therein originates the **Single Element**, which in the outer world has unfolded itself into four elements; understand it thus:

21. In the flash of fire and light is the differentiation; the spirit separates itself above itself—understand this as into the fiery <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; the divine structural "knowing" or blueprint that directs the will</note> of the powers—for it exits the fire-terror as a new life, and yet it is no new life, but has merely taken on the form of Nature. And the <term>Ens</term> <note>Ens: the essential being or "stuff" of a thing</note> of Love remains in the middle, standing as a center of the spirit, and gives out from itself an **Oil** (understood spiritually), in which the light lives; for it is the <term>Ens</term> of fiery love. From this fiery <term>Ens</term> of love, the **Tincture** <note>A vitalizing spiritual force or "essence" that can transform or "color" a substance</note> goes out with the spirit into the heights, as the spiritual-water, the power from fire and light, whose name is called **Virgin Sophia**. <note>original: *4. lib. Eſdræ.c.14. v. 39. ſeqv.*—referring to the apocryphal book of 2 Esdras (4 Esdras in some traditions), Chapter 14, verse 39 and following, which describes a divine cup of water like fire given to the prophet.</note>

22. You dear wise ones, if you only knew Her, it would be good for you: this same "spiritual-water" is true **Humility**, which immediately **transmutes** <note>changes form or nature</note> within the <term>Temperature</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect balance or harmony where all properties are unified</note> and is drawn back in by the light; for it is the soul of the light according to Love. The fire is the **Man**, as the Father’s property—namely the fire-soul—and herein lie the two **Tinctures**, as Man and Woman, the two Loves, which are Divine within the <term>Temperature</term>. These were separated in **Adam** when his imagination turned away from the <term>Temperature</term> toward the outside, and they were unified again in **Christ**.

23. O you dear wise ones, understand this meaning, for herein lies the **Pearl** <note>A common metaphor in Böhme's work for the hidden divine wisdom or the "Pearl of Great Price."</note> of the whole world; it is understood well enough by our own, and we should not give it to the animals. <note>An allusion to the biblical warning not to "cast pearls before swine," implying these truths are for the spiritually awakened.</note>

24. The third differentiation out of the fire comes from the mortification <note>original: *Ertödtung*; the "killing" or settling of the fiery energy into a fixed state</note> of the fire, arising from the essence of the first three [properties], from the **Spirit of Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt**. It goes downward as a mute, unfeeling life and is the **Water-Spirit**, from which the material water of the outer world has its beginning. Within this, the first three properties produce metals, stones, and earths through their working, born out of the properties of the <term>Salnitri</term> <note>original: *Salnitri*; a vital, explosive spiritual "saltpeter" or energy from which material substances are formed</note>. Therein one can also understand the higher essence through the **Impression** <note>The process by which spiritual forces condense or "press" themselves into material form</note> of the <term>Ens</term> of Love—

## Chapter 3. The Election of Grace.

...ought to be, as in the noble metals and stones. This salnitric ground <note>original: *salnitrische Grund*; Böhme uses this term to describe a hidden, explosive spiritual potency or "divine saltpeter" within matter that acts as a life-force</note> is opened up by the sun, so that it possesses a growing life; this is understood well enough by our people here, for it is covered with the curse. We should rightly let ourselves be satisfied with that which eternally gladdens us, and we do not wish to drive a "joy-ape" <note>original: *Freuden-Affen*; an archaic German idiom referring to a false, mocking, or superficial imitation of joy</note> into the beast, and yet hereafter we will indicate what is of use to us.

25. The fourth separation goes into the darkness, where all beings also lie within and are weaving, just as in the light-world and in the outer elementary world; but everything there goes into the Phantasey <note>original: *Phantasey*; in this context, it refers to a realm of shadowy illusions, delusions, or the subjective "self-made" world of the dark ego</note>, according to the quality of the property. We do not wish to report anything further of this here, because of the false light that is understood therein, and also for the sake of man's audacity. However, it is hereby indicated to the Pharisee <note>A reference to those who are legalistic or "self-righteous" but lack true spiritual insight</note> that he has no true understanding of hell and the Phantasey—what their quality and intention may be, or what they are for. Seeing as nothing is outside of God, and yet it is outside of God, but only in another torment <note>original: *Qual*; can mean quality, source, or agony</note>, and another life, also another natural light, which is known to the <term>Magis</term> <note>original: *Magis*; the "Magi" or spiritual adepts who study the hidden mysteries of God and Nature</note>.

->Of the 5th Species of Nature.<-

26. The fifth form in the <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; the divine structural "knowing" or blueprint that directs the will</note> is now the true Love-fire, which separates itself in the light out of the painful fire, within which the Divine Love is now understood in essence. For the powers separate themselves in the "fire-shock" <note>referring to the *Feuerschrecke* mentioned on the previous page</note> and become desirous within themselves, wherein one understands every kind of the first three forms <note>the first three properties of nature: contraction, friction, and fire</note>, but now no longer in painfulness, but in a kingdom of joy, and in their hunger or desire, however one might put it. Thus, in the <term>Scienz</term>, they draw themselves into being; they draw the Tincture <note>a vitalizing spiritual essence that transforms matter</note> from the fire and light—namely the Virgin Sophia <note>Divine Wisdom, personified as a celestial maiden</note>—into themselves. She is their food, as the greatest gentleness; the "well-doing" and "well-tasting" are grasped in the desire of the first three in essence, which is called the Body of the Tincture, as the Divine Essence, Christ's heavenly corporeality.

27. Dear sons, if only you understood it, where Christ says in John 3:13 that He had come from heaven and was in heaven: this Tincture is the power of speaking in the Word, and the essence is its enclosure, where the Word becomes substantial. This essence is the Spirit-water <note>original: *Geist-Wasser*; the spiritual substance or "living water" that provides eternal life</note> of which Christ said He would give us to drink, which would [become] in us a spring—

<summary>Böhme describes the fourth and fifth qualities of nature, contrasting the "Phantasey" of the dark world with the "Love-fire" and "Spirit-water" of the divine realm, specifically identifying the Virgin Sophia as the essence of Christ's heavenly body.</summary>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 3.

...fountains of eternal life spring forth; the tincture <term>Tincture: in this context, a transformative spiritual essence that "colors" or spiritualizes base matter into a higher state</term> transforms it into spiritual blood, for it is their soul; it is Father and Son, from whom the Holy Spirit proceeds as the power.

28. O you dear sons, if you understand this, do not allow your spirit to lift itself up in joy within; rather, bend it into the greatest humility before God, and show him his continued unworthiness, so that he does not rush into self-love and self-will, as Adam and Lucifer did, who introduced the pearl into the Phantasey <note>original: *Phantasey*; refers to a realm of shadows, self-delusion, or the "imagination" of the ego separated from God</note> and broke themselves off from the Whole. Consider well in what a heavy lodging the soul lies captive; humility, and wanting nothing but God's mercy, is the best and most useful practice for those who have known the Virgin Sophia <note>Sophia is the personification of Divine Wisdom, often depicted by Böhme as the spiritual bride of the soul</note>: It is a high thing that God reveals to you; see well to what you do, do not make a "flying Lucifer" out of it, or you will regret it eternally.

29. This fifth form has all the powers of Divine Wisdom within itself, and is the center in which God the Father reveals Himself in His Son through the speaking Word. It is the stalk of the growth of eternal life; likewise, of spiritual creatures, a food for fiery souls, as well as for the angels, and that which cannot be expressed: for it is the eternal, everlasting revelation of the Triune Deity, where all properties of holy Wisdom qualify <note>original: *qualificiren*; an archaic term meaning to interact, operate, or exert an influence</note> within in a sensory way, as a taste, smell, and a life of the love-fire qualifying within one another. It is called the power of the glory of God, which poured itself into all created things during the Creation, and lies hidden in the center of every thing according to that thing's property, like a tincture in the living body <note>original: *Corpore*</note>, from which science <note>original: *Scienz*; here meaning the inner active principle or "knowledge" inherent in nature's growth</note> all things grow and bloom and give their fruits; this power lies within the Quintessence <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of matter</note> and is a cure for diseases.

30. If the four elements could be placed into a state of harmony <note>original: *Temperatur*; the perfect balance of the four elements where no single quality overwhelms the others</note>, then the glorious pearl would be manifest in its effect; but the curse of God’s wrath keeps it imprisoned because of man’s unworthiness—this is well understood by the physicians <note>original: *Medicis*</note>.

31. The sixth form in knowledge <note>original: *Scienz*</note> is in the Divine

<meta>This page continues Chapter 3 of Böhme’s treatise, specifically detailing the "sixth form" of divine manifestation. It links human speech and perception to the cosmic "resonance" of the Holy Spirit.</meta>

### Chapter 3. The Election of Grace. 33

31. Power is the speaking, as the Divine Mouth, the resonance of the powers, where the Holy Spirit leads itself forth audibly out of the enclosed power within the embrace of love; this is to be understood by us in the image of God in man, through his speech. Thus, there is also a sensory, active speaking within the Divine Power in the Temperature <term>Temperature: Böhme’s term for a state of perfect balance and harmony where all conflicting qualities of nature are unified and "tempered"</term>, which active speaking is rightly understood in the five senses <note>original: *Sensibus*</note>, as a spiritual seeing, hearing, smelling, tasting, and feeling, where the revelation of the powers work within one another. The Spirit expresses this workmanship in a resonant sound, as is understood in man, as well as in the "spoken word" within the created creatures—both the living and the silent growing things, and the like.

32. For there it is understood how the spiritual world, as the spiritual resonance, has introduced itself into the creation, from which the sound of all beings originates. In material things, this is called a Mercurial power <term>Mercurial Power: a creative, moving force that mediates between the fiery spiritual center and physical matter; in alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of life and communication</term>, arising from the fiery hardness, wherein the other powers have and give their cooperation so that it becomes a tone or a song, as can be recognized in living beings; in the silent ones, however, it remains a sound. And as one sees in a stringed instrument, how all melodies lie within one another in a single work, which the understanding can then bring forth.

33. Furthermore, in the sixth form, the true understanding of the senses is to be understood by us; for when the spirit has led itself out of the properties, it is again in the Temperature, and has all properties within itself. Of that which the Body <note>original: *Corpus*</note> is a substantial power, the spirit is a flying <note>In the alchemical sense of "volatile" or active</note> power, as a sensory one, in which the Mind <term>Mind: (German: *Gemüthe*) refers to the deep emotional and spiritual center of a person, the seat of the will and disposition</term> is understood, from which the senses originate. For the senses originate from the multiplicity of the infinite properties out of the fire-terror <term>Fire-terror: (German: *Feuerschrecke*) the sudden, energetic "flash" or "shock" that occurs when the dark, contractive powers of nature are ignited by the light</term>; therefore they have both Centers, namely God’s love and wrath, within them. Because they stand in the Temperature, they are righteous; but as soon as they go out from it and swing themselves into their own trial <note>original: *Proba*</note>, to find themselves in properties and to know themselves, then the Lie is born, so that they speak from their own will, and hold the other properties as false and despise them. Thus, they lead themselves immediately into their own lust, in which the heavy fall of Adam and Lucifer is to be contemplated and recognized by us.

<summary>Böhme describes the "sixth property" of nature as the realm of speech and the five senses, explaining how divine power becomes audible and perceptible. He warns that when the senses move away from divine balance toward self-will, it leads to the same spiritual fall experienced by Adam and Lucifer.</summary>

This power lies within the Fifth Essence <term>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the highest, purest element that sustains life and health</term> and is a cure <term>original: *Cura*</term> for sicknesses.

30. If the four elements can be placed into the Temperature <term>Temperature: the state of perfect balance where conflicting forces are harmonized</term>, then the glorious Pearl is manifest in its effect; but the curse of God’s wrath keeps it imprisoned because of human unworthiness—a fact well understood by the physicians <term>original: *Medicis*</term>.

### Chapter 3. The Election of Grace.

32. ### XV. Of Chapter 3.

34. For Adam was placed into the balanced harmony <term>Balanced harmony (Temperatur): the state where all spiritual qualities exist in perfect equilibrium</term> with the [divine] qualities: but his <term>Science</term> <term>Science (Scienz): here meaning an active, inner "knowing" or the driving force of the will</term> led itself into division, into false desire, through the Devil’s infection and his persuasion or suggestion; in which suggestion the desire rose up within the balanced harmony and led into the multiplicity of the qualities, as each individual quality [sought to be] a selfhood.

35. For the soul wanted to taste how it tasted when the balanced harmony was pulled apart; namely, how heat and cold, as well as dryness and moisture, hardness and softness, bitterness, sweetness, sourness, and tartness, and so forth, all qualities tasted in their distinctness. Yet God forbade him this, not to eat of this growth—that is, of the revelation of the knowledge of Good and Evil—in which taste the fiery hunger first arose. Thus the life-forms lost the Manna, which is God’s bread from the essence of Love, and could no longer taste how it was in the balanced harmony within a single will. Because of this, the life-forms immediately fell into a great hunger, and the multiplicity of qualities imprinted themselves, through which the coarseness of the flesh arose. The beast-like desire became manifest in him in the multiplicity of the <term>Science</term>, the qualities of the powers. At once, the divided qualities in the Spirit of the World <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note> forced their way into him, such as heat and cold; also the bitter-stinging woe touched him, all of which could not have existed in the balanced harmony. From this, diseases of the flesh also immediately arose in him, for the qualities had come into conflict and opposition.

36. As soon as one quality now raises itself above the other, or is ignited by something so that it swings upward in its activity, it becomes a hostile opposition to the others. From this arises woe and sickness: for the conflict immediately leads into the "Three Firsts" <note>The three primary forces of nature in Böhme's system: contraction, friction, and movement</note>, where the <term>Turba</term> <term>Turba: a Latin term used by Böhme to describe a "disturbance" or the destructive wrath of nature that breaks harmony</term> then gives birth to itself and awakens the chamber of death, so that the poisonous torment gains the mastery. And that is precisely the heavy fall of Adam.

For this reason, we see the world only halfway, for Paradise (as the inner world), which blossomed forth through the external earth in Adam’s innocence, we have lost.

39. Therefore, God commanded us to rest on the same, for it is the true image of God, in which God has always formed Himself into an eternal being from eternity. And if we were willing to see it, he is Christ, namely the right human being created in Adam, who fell and led himself into unrest in the 6 days' works with the <term>Science</term>, and awakened the dark world, and brought it up, which God again <term>Tinged (tingirte)<span>To "tinge" is to transform or permeate a substance with a spiritual quality, like a catalyst changing a base metal into gold.</span></term> with His highest <term>Love-Tincture</term>, in the name of JESUS, in the human being, and led him into the eternal Sabbath of rest.

# Chapter 4.
## On the Origin of Creation.

### Summaries.

An exhortation to the reader. §. 1. 2. <term>Creation<gloss>The divine act of bringing the universe into being.</gloss></term> stands within the Word of God. 3. Eternal creatures have their origin in the Single Will; temporal creatures in the spoken Word. 4. 5. The three <term>Principles<gloss>The primary levels of reality: the dark fire-world, the light world, and the external material world.</gloss></term> also produce three kinds of life. 6. While the Single Will, introducing itself into the Trinity, is not itself a <term>Principle</term>; 7. yet in the comprehension of the Word, as in the "Fire-Root," the 1st <term>Principle</term> originates. 8. The 2nd <term>Principle</term> is understood in the separation of Light from Fire, representing Love and Wrath. 9. The 3rd <term>Principle</term> is understood in the seven days of the Work of Creation, 10. which essence God captured into the Word out of both inner worlds; and this is the external world, 11-13. spoken forth from the <term>Word "Let there be"<gloss>original: "Verbo Fiat"</gloss></term> into a spiritual essence, in a living and growing quality. 14. Out of this <term>Mystery<gloss>original: "Mysterio," the hidden source of all things.</gloss></term>, the essence of the Word has been made manifest, 15. as Heaven, Fire, and Air. (Genesis 1: 1.) 16. Heaven is the Upper-World with the four elements; and this spiritual essence has cast out from itself the Earth. *Ibid.* <note>Ibidem: in the same place/verse.</note> God's speaking Word is the spiritual world, according to Love and Wrath; and yet it is not evil in God, but only in the selfhood of the creature. 17. Thus, the whole of God has spoken Himself out into Time according to all worlds, 18. and the external world stands with its hosts like a clockwork: 19. the angels, however, are from both fires, 20. 21. and are called flames of Fire and Light. 22. Their substance is an <term>Enclosure<gloss>original: "Infassung," a framework or containing structure.</gloss></term> of eternal nature; 23. they have 7 Princely Regiments and 3 <term>Hierarchies<gloss>Ordered ranks of spiritual beings.</gloss></term>; 24. and they dwell in the Balanced Harmony. Their foundation is God’s most singular will; and they have free will to turn themselves toward the 1st or 2nd <term>Principle</term>, just as God Himself. 25. 26. The 1st <term>Hierarchy</term> stands in the Father’s quality, as in the Fire-Tincture; 27. the 2nd in the Light-Fire-Tincture, according to the Son’s quality; *Ibid.* and the 3rd stands in the selfhood of nature according to the center of darkness, into which Lucifer turned himself, and he stands with it in the government of the "Place of This World"; *Ibid.* he also shapes himself into certain plants and base metals. 28. Lucifer manifested the Realm of Fantasy with his own willing in his creaturely being. 29. From the Earth it can be seen how, in the creation of the angels, the <term>Wrath<gloss>In Böhme’s thought, *Grimm* (Wrath or Fierceness) is the dark, foundational fire of nature that becomes "evil" only when separated from the Light.</gloss></term> of nature was also powerfully moved; 32. yet it was Lucifer’s own will that brought about his fall. *Ibid.* The objection of Reason. 33. Answer: Lucifer was an angel; his will was free in the <term>Balanced Harmony<gloss>The *Temperatur*, a state where all spiritual qualities are in perfect equilibrium.</gloss></term>. 34. However, he led it willfully into <term>Fantasy<gloss>*Phantasey* here refers to a false, self-centered reflection or a distorted image of reality, separated from God.</gloss></term>: 35. and is now a prince in the Wrath of God and a throne-prince of anger in the kingdom of Fantasy; Love is his poison. 36. Answering a rational objection using the example of a thistle. 37. The will of the <term>Abyss<gloss>The *Ungrund*, the "Groundless" or the nothingness before God manifests into existence.</gloss></term> is neither evil nor good; 38. and so also is the will in the <term>Spirit of the World<gloss>Original Latin: *Spiritus Mundi*; the governing soul or atmosphere of the material universe.</gloss></term>, but the first three <note>referring to the first three properties of eternal nature: contraction, expansion, and rotation/anguish</note> press themselves in. 39. Thus, the first will is not to blame, but rather the first three, acting as nature. 40. The groundless will is One. 41. From this will, the ground of nature is born from eternity: 42. nature, however, encloses itself in self-will and makes itself hostile; this is the fall, wherein God does not beget his Trinity. 43. When the creature does something, it is not God who does it, but the creature’s own will. 44. <term>Science<gloss>The *Scientz*, or the inner driving force and "knowing" of the soul's will.</gloss></term> is indeed ashamed, but cannot break the Fantasy. *Ibid.* In God, all is good; the distinction exists only in the creature, which makes the darkness manifest within itself. 45. Now, the dark kingdom of Fantasy and Lucifer are one will and being; because he wished to live in Fantasy alone, he was cast out and locked in darkness, 46. and possesses a <term>Magical Fire-Light<gloss>A false, cold, or scorching light that mimics the true light of God but lacks its life-giving love.</gloss></term> of cold and heat. *Ibid.* How it is to be understood that Michael fought with him. 47. 48. Highest Love came to the aid of mankind. 49.

->**Address to the Reader**<-

Kind reader, I admonish you: be a human being and not an irrational animal. Do not let yourself be misled by the chatter of the Sophists with their calf-like understanding, who do not know what they are prattling about—who only bicker and bite, yet neither know nor understand what they are lusting after, and have no ground in <term>Sense<gloss>Original Latin: *Senſu*; refers to actual perception or experiential understanding.</gloss></term>.

2. Also, do not let this pen, or the hand that holds the pen, lead you astray: the Highest has carved it thus and breathed His breath into it. Therefore, we truly know, see, and recognize such things—not out of delusion from another’s hand, nor through <term>Astral impulses<gloss>The *Astralische Einfälle*; Böhme was often accused of merely channeling planetary or astrological influences rather than divine revelation.</gloss></term>, as we are accused. A gate has been opened to us in the <term>Holy Ternary<gloss>Original Latin: *Ternario Sancto*; the holy manifestation of the Trinity in the spiritual world.</gloss></term> to see and to know what the Lord wishes humanity to know at this time, so that the strife may come to an end and people no longer bicker over God. For this reason, He reveals Himself, and this should be no wonder to us; rather, we ourselves should be that very wonder which He has brought forth in the fullness of time—if we recognize what we are, depart from strife into the balanced harmony of the single will, and love one another.

# XV. Concerning Chapter 4.

3. The entire *Creation*, of both the eternal and also the temporal *creatures* and beings, stands in the Word of Divine Power.

4. The eternal beings originate from the <term>Science<gloss>From the Latin *Scientia*, meaning a divine, active knowledge or "knowingness" through which the spirit manifests.</gloss></term> of speaking, as if from the singular will of the <term>Abyss<gloss>The *Ungrund*, the groundless "nothing" before God manifests into existence.</gloss></term>, which has introduced itself into <term>Particularity<gloss>Individual, specific existence as opposed to the undifferentiated whole.</gloss></term> through the word of speaking together with that Science.

5. And the temporal beings originate in the spoken word, as in a mirror-image of the eternal; for the spoken word, in its *substance*, has led itself back into an external mirror for its own contemplation.

6. The distribution of the *Science* from the Abyss into the Ground—including the introduction of the speaking word into a re-speaking of the "Essence of all Essences" towards both the evil and the good—works like this: Three <term>Principles<gloss>Original Latin: *Principia*; the fundamental levels or dimensions of reality in Böhme's system.</gloss></term> are born within the Essence of all Essences, where each one is the cause of the other. Within these, one also understands a threefold life, acting as three distinct forms of Divine revelation.

7. First: the true Godhead in itself as a <term>Trinity<gloss>Original: *Dreyfaltigkeit*</gloss></term>, within the *Science* of the Abyss in the Singular Will, where God begets God. Specifically, the Singular Will that introduces itself into the Trinity is not a *Principle* <note>original: *Principium*</note>, for there is nothing before Him; thus, He can have no beginning from anything. Rather, He is Himself His own beginning—the Nothing, and also His "Something."

8. But in the word of the singular Divine power, where the singular *Science* of the begetting of the Trinity breathes itself out: there originates the beginning of the first *Principle*. Yet this is not in the "Ground" of the speaking (as the Trinity), but rather in the grasping of distinction. Here, distinction takes root in nature for the sake of sensitivity and movement. This sensitivity divides itself into two essences: into <term>Wrath<gloss>The *Grimm*, the fierce, dark quality of foundational nature.</gloss></term>—following the <term>Impression<gloss>The "contracting" or "drawing-in" force of the spirit that creates density.</gloss></term> in the darkness—into a cold, painful fire where heat originates. Here one understands the 1st *Principle* in the **Fire-Root**, which is the <term>Center<gloss>Original: *Centrum*</gloss></term> of nature.

9. And the second *Principle* is understood in the separation of the fire, where the Divine *Science* within the fire separates itself into the Light. There, it has introduced itself into nature and essence for the revelation of the Divine <term>Kingdom of Joy<gloss>The *Freudenreich*, the realm of light, love, and spiritual harmony.</gloss></term>, where the 

<note>catchword: Word</note>

### Chapter 4. The Election of Grace.

The Word of power stands within an actual birthing, where the <term>Mind<gloss>Latin: *Mens*; the spiritual intelligence or intent.</gloss></term> works within the <term>Essence<gloss>Latin: *Ens*; the being or fundamental substance.</gloss></term>; there, the division occurs between two <term>Principles<gloss>Original: *Principia*; the foundational dimensions of Böhme's reality.</gloss></term>. According to the first, God calls Himself an angry, jealous God and a consuming fire; and according to the other, a loving, merciful God, who does not—and cannot—will evil. <note>Böhme is explaining how God can be both a God of love and a "consuming fire" (Hebrews 12:29) by attributing these qualities to different "Principles" or layers of divine manifestation.</note>

10. The third <term>Principle</term> is understood through the "seven days of creation" <note>Original: *sieben Tagewercken*, referring to the Genesis creation account.</note>, where the seven properties of nature introduced themselves into a single being for the sake of comprehensibility in the Seventh [property]; which being in itself is holy, pure, and good, and is called the eternal, uncreated heaven, as the dwelling-place of God, or the Kingdom of God; *also*, *Paradise*, the pure element, the Divine <term>Essence<gloss>Latin: *Ens*.</gloss></term>, or whatever else one might call it according to its specific quality.

11. God has grasped and moved that same singular being of the Divine handiwork, which has existed from eternity, with the <term>Science<gloss>Latin: *Scienz*; an active, creative "knowingness" through which the spirit manifests.</gloss></term> of His unsearchable will. He enclosed it in the Word of His speaking, and spoke it forth out of the first <term>Principle</term> of the painful, dark world of fire, and out of the holy light-flaming world of love, as a pre-modeling of the inner spiritual world.

12. And that is now the external visible world with its stars and elements; yet it is not to be understood that it existed previously as a distinct spiritual being. Rather, it was the <term>Great Mystery<gloss>Latin: *Mysterium Magnum*; the chaotic "mother" or potentiality from which all things are brought forth by the Word.</gloss></term>, where all things stood within Wisdom, in a spiritual form within the <term>Science</term> of fire and light, like a wrestling play of love. It did not exist in created spirits, but in the <term>Science</term> of such <term>internal modeling<gloss>German: *Inmodelung*; the formative process by which inner spiritual forces take shape.</gloss></term>, where Wisdom thus played with itself in power. The singular Will grasped this same internal modeling into the Word, and let the <term>Science</term> go free from the Singular Will, so that every power might introduce itself into a form according to its own property, within its own will, in the liberated <term>Science</term>.

13. The Divine "Shaping" <note>Original: *Schuf*, implying the creative/forming act.</note>, as the desire of eternal nature—which is called the <term>Fiat<gloss>Latin for "let it be done"; the creative command of God's power.</gloss></term> of the powers—has grasped this as if in a <term>solidification<gloss>Latin: *Compaction*; a thickening of spiritual properties into physical substance.</gloss></term> of properties. Thus Moses speaks: "In the beginning," as if in that same enclosure of the <term>Great Mystery</term>, "God created heaven and earth," and

### XV. Concerning Chapter 4.

...said, "Let all kinds of creatures go forth, each according to its property." <note>A paraphrase of the creation of animals and plants in Genesis 1.</note>

14. This is now for us to understand: that in the <term>Word "Let there be"<gloss>Latin: *Verbo Fiat*; the creative command of God.</gloss></term>, the <term>Great Mystery<gloss>Latin: *Mysterium Magnum*; the chaotic or potential state of all things before they are manifested.</gloss></term> was grasped into a being—moving from the inner spiritual being into a graspable one. Within this graspability lies the <term>Science<gloss>Latin: *Scienz*; Böhme uses this to mean an active, innate "knowingness" or a formative impulse within nature.</gloss></term> of life. This exists in two properties: a mental one and an essential one. That is, one truly living, from the ground of eternity, which stands in the wisdom of the Word; and one budding forth, born within the essence's own internal <term>Science</term>, which is the growth wherein the growing life stands as the "mute life." <note>Böhme distinguishes between the conscious life of spirits/humans and the "mute" or silent life of plants and minerals.</note>

15. From this Mystery, the <term>Quintessence<gloss>Latin: *Quinta Essentia*; the purest, fifth element that underlies the other four.</gloss></term>—as the <term>Essence<gloss>Latin: *Ens*; the fundamental substance.</gloss></term> of the Word—initially became manifest and substantial. Upon this, all three <term>Principles<gloss>The three realms of Böhme's cosmology: the dark-fire world, the light-love world, and the external material world.</gloss></term> were suspended. Then the essence divided itself: namely, the spiritual into spiritual being, and the mute into mute being, such as earth, stones, metals, and material water.

16. The first three <note>Refers to the first three "Qualities" or properties of Böhme’s seven-fold nature: Attraction, Friction, and Bitterness/Motion.</note> first grasped themselves into a spiritual being, as in heaven, fire, and air; for Moses says: "In the beginning God created heaven and earth." The word "heaven" encompasses the spiritual element—the spiritual Upper-World—working through the four elements. There, the single element unfolded itself with the properties of the first three, within which nature lies with its seven forms. That same spiritual being cast off from itself the coarse, grasped, mute being—the <term>Matter<gloss>Latin: *Materian*.</gloss></term> of the earth and what is contained within it—according to and out of the properties of the seven forms of nature and their distribution. Then, each form, with its distribution or multiplication, introduced itself into being. One sees this in the "growing spirit," which, out of the <term>salnitric seething<gloss>Original: *salnitrischen Sude*; Böhme's term for the volatile, energetic tension or "boiling" of spiritual forces that generates life.</gloss></term> of the two fires, carries the <term>Science</term> of every property upward into the desire of the upper spirit-life, from which the earth also receives power. In this upper and lower power, the earth's <term>Science</term> introduces itself into a plant. The sun kindles this plant with its light-fire so that fruit grows from it, in the same way that the internal magical Sun of God's light kindles the Inner Nature, wherein <term>Paradise</term> stands growing and greening. Understand...

### Chapter 4. The Election of Grace.

...in the balance of the single element, which is hidden from the earthly; in summary, we want to indicate to the reader what the <term>Being of all beings<gloss>German: *Wesen aller Wesen*; the fundamental reality or essence underlying all existence.</gloss></term> is.

17. The inner, holy, spiritual world is the Outspoken Word of God, which introduces itself into being and activity according to Love and Wrath. In the impression of darkness, one understands "evil," and yet it is not evil in God; rather, it is only so in its own grasp of <term>Selfhood<gloss>German: *Selbheit*; the state of being focused on one's own ego or independent will rather than the divine whole.</gloss></term>, as in a creature. Yet even there it is also good, so long as the creature remains within the balance <note>original: "Temperatur"; refers to a state of perfect proportional harmony between conflicting forces.</note> of the whole.

18. And in the grasp of the light, one understands the Kingdom as the manifest God with His actual power, which expresses itself in the firing nature into a clear word for Divine Revelation in the Holy Spirit. That same active Word—formed from all powers, from good and evil, as from the light-and-love fire and from the painful and dark nature-fire—which stood in eternity as a real being in two <term>principles<gloss>Latin: *principiis*; the two fundamental realms of Böhme's cosmos: the dark-fire world of potential/wrath and the light-fire world of manifestation/love.</gloss></term> (as in light and darkness), has spoken itself out into a "Time." It has led itself into a being of a beginning and an end, and shaped itself into Creation for its own self-revelation.

19. That is, this external world with its hosts, and all that lives and moves within it, is enclosed in a "Time" of a <term>Clockwork<gloss>German: *Uhrwerck*; Böhme’s metaphor for the physical universe as a temporary, mechanical manifestation of eternal forces that eventually returns to its source.</gloss></term>. This runs from its beginning evermore back to its end, returning into the first source from which it came. It has become manifest for this purpose: so that the eternal Word, in its actual power, might be creaturely and figurative. Just as it formed and shaped itself from eternity in Wisdom, so too it is formed in an individual life, for the glory and joy of the Holy Spirit, within the Word of Life in itself.

20. And therefore, within the eternal <term>Science<gloss>Latin: *Scienz*; Böhme uses this to mean an active, innate "knowingness" or the formative impulse within the divine will.</gloss></term> of the eternal, bottomless will, God created angels out of both fires: from the fire of nature and from the fire of love. Although the love-fire cannot "produce" a creaturely form on its own, it dwells within the creature and fills it, just as the sun fills the world, or as nature fills the time of the world, so that the Holy Spirit may thus have a "play of joy" within itself.

21. And you should understand us correctly and well concerning the angels,

# XV. Concerning Chapter 4.

For here lies the ground upon which the question concerning the <term>Election of Grace<gloss>German: *Gnaden-Wahl*; the theological concept of how God chooses those who receive salvation.</gloss></term> is debated, a matter in which human reason often goes astray.

22. Holy Scripture calls the angels flames of fire and light (Psalm 104:4) and also ministering spirits (Hebrews 1:7). This is indeed the case: and although they have their high-princely regiments <note>Böhme uses "regiments" to describe the ordered ranks or choirs of angels.</note>, they are all together merely a prepared instrument of the one Spirit of GOD in His joy, which He reveals through them; for He reveals Himself through them.

23. Their substance and essence—insofar as they are a possession and are called creatures—is a containment of the <term>eternal nature<gloss>The foundational structure of Böhme's cosmos, consisting of seven qualities or "forms" that move from darkness to light.</gloss></term>, which stands without beginning in Divine activity, for His self-revelation, within the <term>eternal birth-giver<gloss>German: *ewigen Gebärerin*; also referred to as the "Matrix" or "Divine Wisdom," the womb of all existence.</gloss></term>. Understand that, as creatures, they are of the eternal nature of all seven forms, and exist in a great diversity of powers. In the same way that the first three forms in nature <note>The first three properties of nature are typically associated with contraction, friction, and the "fire-flash" of consciousness.</note> introduce and shape themselves into infinite differences, so also is their creaturely being to be understood in many properties, each in his own specific quality.

24. And we are to understand primarily seven high regiments in three hierarchies, according to the fountain-spring of the seven properties of nature; for there, every form of the eternal nature is contained within a throne, as if in a regiment, wherein the distinctions are understood, as well as the will of obedience toward the <term>Throne-Princes<gloss>The archangels or ruling spirits who lead specific orders of the celestial host.</gloss></term>.

25. This they have in their administration as creatures of Divine gifts, since GOD has given them the essence (of which they are an image) as a possession in which they dwell; this essence is the holy spiritual power of the world, the <term>Temperament<gloss>German: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect harmony where all conflicting qualities of nature are balanced and none dominates.</gloss></term>. Their most inward ground, which originates from Divine property from eternity, is the single will of the <term>Abyss<gloss>German: *Ungrund*; the "Groundless" or the nothingness out of which God's will and all existence emerge.</gloss></term> into the ground. Thus, according to their beginning in nature, they originate from the <term>science<gloss>German: *Scientz*; from Latin *scientia*, but here meaning a self-active "spark" or "knowledge-seed" of the will.</gloss></term> of the free will, from which and in which free will GOD brings forth His Word. That same free will has, in the birth of nature—as in the <term>First Principle<gloss>Latin: *1sten Principio*; the realm of the dark fire-flash and potential energy.</gloss></term> of the ignition of fire—introduced itself into distinctiveness. And from that same distinctiveness in the origin of the fire, the angels were introduced into free will (as a particular part of the bottomless free will), to turn themselves with that free will into the first or the second principle, and to reveal themselves therein.

### Chapter 4. Election of Grace.
<meta>catchword: itself</meta>

26. Just as God himself is free within that same free will, and is all, and introduces himself in that same free will into nature—in fire, light, and darkness, in pain and torment, as well as in love and joy; so too the **particular** <gloss>The individual being or specific part of the whole.</gloss> has the power, from the whole free will, to introduce itself into a creaturely property within the three hierarchies or principles, as they choose. That is to say, the **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scienz*, from Latin *scientia*. In Böhme's writing, this is not modern academic science, but the "inner spark" or the root of the will that has the power to know and manifest itself.</term> may grasp and reveal itself in the three hierarchies over which it has power, just as the Divine Science has introduced itself into essence and operation: as one part in the fiery, according to cold; another in the fiery, according to heat; the third in the fiery, according to light; the fourth into **phantasy**, as into a play of **nature’s selfhood**, where she plays with herself in inequality within the properties.

27. The three hierarchies are to be understood by us in three principles, as in three kinds of nature-light: the first **hierarchy** stands in the property of the eternal Father’s essence, according to the fire of strength, as in the **fire-tincture** <gloss>A "tincture" refers to the essential, life-giving quality or spiritual "color" of a substance.</gloss>, in the essence of nature; the second hierarchy stands in the light-fire-tincture, according to the Son’s property in the eternal nature, and is the holiest; the third hierarchy stands in nature’s **selfhood**, where she plays against herself in the properties like the four elements play in the power of the stars: and this is revealed according to the **center** <note>original: *Centro*</note> of darkness, and it also has a nature-light within itself, like the cold and hot fire-flash or glance, wherein transformation is understood, as when the creature may suddenly transform into this or another form; and in nature this is called **false magic**. It is into this hierarchy that Prince **Lucifer** turned himself, and with his **science** departed from the **temperature** <term>temperature<gloss>German: *Temperatur*. This refers to a state of perfect harmony or balance where all the conflicting qualities of nature (hot, cold, dark, light) are reconciled.</term>; his kingdom is called a cave or hell, because it dwells within itself in darkness and has a false light that does not stand within the balance. Instead, it leads a desire and longing of **phantasy** for building and breaking, where now a shape is formed, and very soon—according to the wrestling shapes of nature—it is broken again and changed into something else. This kingdom stands within the location of this world, within the governance of the creation—not indeed according to the four elements and the stars, but hidden within them, and
itself

### XV. Regarding Chapter 4.
<meta>catchword: he</meta>

...penetrating into the creatures themselves, wherein the devils and spirits of **phantasy** <term>phantasy<gloss>German: *Phantasey*. In Böhme's thought, this refers to a state of self-willed imagination or a "false" reality created when a being turns away from God's light to focus on its own ego.</term> dwell within the four elements.

28. If the sun and the water were to cease, then that same kingdom would be revealed: it manifests within various plants, as well as in metals that are not fixed and do not endure in fire; likewise in herbs, trees, and creatures, wherein the false magic of sorcery is understood, and where Christ calls the devil a "prince of this world." <note>original: "Fürsten dieser Welt"</note>

29. For when he [Lucifer] was cast out of the light, he fell into the kingdom of phantasy, into the center of nature, outside of the **temperature** <term>temperature<gloss>German: *Temperatur*. This refers to the perfect balance or "tempering" of all natural qualities (hot, cold, etc.) into a harmonious whole. To fall out of the temperature is to fall into chaos.</term> and into the darkness, where he may open for himself a false light from the hot and cold fire, through the **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scienz*, from Latin *scientia*. Here, it means the innate "knowing" or the creative spark of the will that allows a being to manifest its own reality.</term> of the power of eternity. For this is Lucifer's fall: that by his own will he revealed the kingdom of phantasy in his creaturely being, introducing the eternal will out of the temperature into separation—into the inequality of phantasy. This phantasy immediately seized him and led him into an unquenchable, cold and hot fire-torment, into the adversity of the forms of nature.

30. For the **wrath** <term>wrath<gloss>German: *Grimm*. A technical term for the harsh, raw power of nature when it is not unified by the Light of God. It is the "stern" or "fierce" quality of the first principle.</term> of eternal nature, which is called God’s anger, revealed itself in them, and led their will into phantasy; and in this they still live, and can now do nothing else than what is the nature of phantasy: namely, to practice foolishness, to transform themselves, and to break the essence of things apart. Likewise, they exalt themselves in the power of cold and hot fire, conceiving a will within themselves to go out beyond the hierarchies of God and the Holy Angels, to show themselves in magnificent fire-power according to the **first principle** <term>first principle<gloss>Latin: *principio*. Böhme’s term for the root of nature—the world of fire and strength—which exists before or apart from the "second principle" of light and love.</term>, in their wrath. Their will is pure pride <gloss>German: *Hoffart*.</gloss>, as well as a greed <gloss>German: *Geitz*.</gloss> for a multitude of qualities, a prickly envy from bitter woe, an anger from the fire, and a despairing from anguish.

31. In short, as the first three properties—the spirit of nature in the spiritual **Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury** <term>Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury<gloss>The three "philosophical" principles of alchemy. Sulphur represents the soul/fire, Salt represents the body/substantiality, and Mercury represents the spirit/movement. In Böhme's theology, these are the working forces of the soul's inner nature.</term>—are, so too is their mind, from which the senses come. Understand that as the first three are in their original state outside the light of God, so too is the devil in his will and mind: for his exaltation was according to the first principle, so that he might be a lord over and in all essence, and over all angelic hosts. And therefore he turned...

It defines the demonic state as one of pride, greed, and elemental conflict, rooted in the alchemical principles of Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury.</summary>

# Chapter 4. Election by Grace.

he turned away from the humility of love and wished to rule within the power of fire, which spat him out from itself, set itself as a judge over him, and stripped him of his divine authority.

32. Regarding this self-elevation, it is for us to consider and highly recognize: because the angels were created before the time of the third **principle** <term>principle<gloss>German: *Principium*. Böhme uses this to describe the three distinct realms of reality: 1. The dark fire-world (God's wrath/power); 2. The light-world (God's love/Son); 3. The outer physical world.</gloss></term>, during the first divine movement, we see how the kingdom of **phantasy** <term>phantasy<gloss>German: *Phantasey*. Not mere imagination, but a state where a being creates its own false reality centered on the ego rather than God.</gloss></term> moved so violently within the wrath of nature, impressing and grasping itself. It was in this "grasping" that the earth and stones took their origin—not that the devils caused them, but rather they provoked the Mother of Nature (namely, the wrath of God) so that it locked their essence into a **compaction** <term>compaction<gloss>Latin: *compaction*. The process of spiritual forces thickening or solidifying into material substance.</gloss></term> and brought it into a clump, because they wished to practice their trickery within the **Matrix of Nature** <term>Matrix of Nature<gloss>Latin/German: *Matrix Naturæ*. The "womb" or creative foundation from which all natural forms are birthed.</gloss></term>. This power has now been withdrawn from them, so that they must now lie captive in the spiritual ground, in that same Mother of Phantasy. They are the poorest of creatures, for they have lost God and His essence. He who wished to be far too rich became poor: In humility, he would have possessed everything and worked alongside God, but in his **selfhood** <note>German: *Selbheit*. A key term for Böhme referring to the ego-driven state of being turned away from communal divine love.</note> he is a fool, so that it might be known what is folly and what is wisdom. Thus, through his own self-elevation, God has enclosed him in his own will, in folly, as if in an eternal prison.

33. To this, **Reason** <note>German: *Vernunft*. Here, Böhme refers to human logic or rational intellect when it attempts to understand divine mysteries without spiritual illumination.</note> says: "It was God's will, so that His wisdom might be distinguished from folly, and so that it might be understood what wisdom or folly is; otherwise, one would not know what wisdom was. Therefore, God let him fall and hardened him so that he had to do it; otherwise, it would not have happened." This is as far as Reason gets, and it understands no more than this.

34. Answer: When the **Ungrund** <term>Ungrund<gloss>Literally "the groundless." Böhme's term for the absolute, unconditioned divine source that precedes all created things—an abyss of pure potentiality.</gloss></term> introduced itself with the singular Will into a fiery separation, the **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scienz*, from Latin *scientia*. Here meaning a "spark of knowing" or the innate creative will within a being.</gloss></term> within the fire became free in that separation. There, every "science" divided in that partition into its own will; and the multitude of wills were all placed into the **temperature** <term>temperature<gloss>German: *Temperatur*. The state of perfect balance where all conflicting qualities (hot, cold, dark, light) exist in harmony.</gloss></term>, having the three **hierarchies** <term>hierarchies<gloss>Latin: *Hierarchiam*. Referring to the three spiritual realms: Light, Fire, and Darkness.</gloss></term> attached to them. There, every host—enclosed within its own creaturely form—could introduce itself into any of these first three hierarchies as it willed. And that this is true is manifest in this: for

<summary>Böhme explains that Lucifer's fall resulted from a self-willed rejection of divine humility, leading to his imprisonment in "phantasy" and the materialization of the earth. He refutes the idea that God forced the fall, arguing instead that the "science" or creative will of every creature is free to choose its own path.</summary>

# XV. Regarding Chapter 4.

the devils were originally angels, and stood in the **temperature** <term>temperature<gloss>German: *Temperatur*. This refers to the perfect balance or "tempering" of all spiritual qualities in harmony, a state where no single quality dominates the others.</gloss></term> with free will: Now, whichever way they chose to turn, they were to be confirmed in that direction.

35. If you say: "No, God did with them as He willed." Answer: Then understand it correctly; the **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scienz*. Derived from the Latin *scientia*, Böhme uses this to describe the internal, "magical" knowledge or the root-potency of the will that allows a being to direct its own essence.</gloss></term> is God’s eternal, **unfathomable** <note>original: *ungründlicher*. Literally "groundless" or "bottomless," referring to the divine will that exists before and beyond all created foundations.</note> will itself, which has introduced itself into nature and the creature: but within the science of the creature, the will arose to introduce itself into the **phantasy** <term>phantasy<gloss>German: *Phantasey*. A state of self-delusion where a being turns away from divine reality to create its own ego-centered world.</gloss></term>, as into the center of the **fire-life**; and upon this followed the confirmation and separation, as well as the expulsion from the temperature into the **torment** <term>torment<gloss>German: *Quall* (modern *Qual*). Böhme often puns this with *Quelle* (source/wellspring); it signifies the agonizing, restless activity of the lower spiritual forces when they are not harmonized by love.</gloss></term> into which the science had turned itself with its free will.

36. That same hierarchy of darkness and phantasy accepted that same will and confirmed it within itself: thus, from an angel, a devil was made, as a prince in the wrath of God; therein, internally, he is "good" <note>This is meant ironically; the devil is "good" only in the sense that he is a perfect expression of God's wrath, just as a sharp knife is "good" at cutting.</note>; for as God’s wrath is, so also is his inborn princely throne. He is and remains eternally a prince with his legions, but only in the kingdom of phantasy. For as the kingdom of those powers is within itself, so also is its inborn prince; the torment of the wrathful kingdom is the mother of his **selfhood** <note>original: *Selbheit*. The state of being trapped in the ego or "self."</note> as his God; he must now do what his God wills, and thus he is an enemy of the Good, for Love is his poison and death: even if he were to sit in holy power within the light, he would only draw the torment of poison into himself, for that would be his life and nature. Just as if one placed a toad in a sugar bowl, it would still only draw poison from it, and would poison the sugar.

37. Now Reason speaks: "If God had poured His love into him again, he would have become an angel again; therefore, it lies in God’s purpose." Answer: Listen, Reason: Look at a thistle or a nettle, upon which the sun shines for an entire day, and with its power also penetrates it, and very gladly gives its rays of love into its prickly **essence** <term>essence<gloss>Latin/German: *Ens*. The spiritual substance or core being of a thing.</gloss></term>; this thistle also rejoices in the sun's **essence** <note>original: *Ente*. Likely a contraction of "Entity" or "Essence."</note>, but it only grows into a thistle all the more prickly because of it; it only becomes prouder because of it. Thus it is also to be understood regarding the devil: even if God had poured His love into him, the science of the unfathomable will would have introduced itself in the manner of a thistle,

<summary>Böhme argues that the Fall was a result of the angels' free will directing their internal "science" toward self-centered phantasy rather than divine harmony. He explains that once a being's nature is fixed in wrath, even divine love would be perceived as poison, much as the sun only makes a thistle more prickly.</summary>

# Chapter 4. The Election of Grace.

namely, the eternal will, which, without ground or location, is a will in itself that nothing can break.

38. And yet we are not to understand that the will of the **Abyss** <term>Abyss<gloss>German: *Ungrund*. Böhme’s signature term for the "groundless" or "unfathomable" source of all being that exists before all differentiation, even before God manifests as a Trinity.</gloss></term> performed this act: for that will is neither evil nor good, but is purely a will—that is, a **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scienz*. Derived from the Latin *scientia*, this refers to an innate, "magical" potency or root-knowledge within the will that precedes conscious thought or understanding.</gloss></term> without understanding toward "something" or in "something," for it is only one thing, and is neither desire nor pleasure; rather, it is the welling or willing.

39. Just as the external world also has a will in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original Latin: *Spiritu Mundi*. The vital, animating force of the physical cosmos.</note>, or as the air is a welling and is neither evil nor good: only one understands how the **first three** <note>Referring to the first three "Forms of Nature" in Böhme's system—contraction, friction, and rotation—which constitute the harsh, dark foundation of existence when not illuminated by light.</note> with the sensory ground press into it and take the will into their possession; and though they originate from that same will, they nevertheless seize it as their own property.

40. Likewise, we are to understand the **science**—as the single eternal will from the **Abyss**—which originates from the eternal One and has yielded itself to evil within the creature of **fantasy** <term>fantasy<gloss>German: *Phantafey*. A state of self-deluded imagination where a being turns away from divine reality to dwell in its own ego-centered world.</gloss></term>, as into the **wrath** <term>wrath<gloss>German: *Grimm*. The harsh, consuming, and painful quality of nature when it is separated from the light of divine love.</gloss></term> of eternal nature: that same will is not the cause of the fantasy, but rather the **first three**, in which the creature is understood, as nature in the eternal bond. Out of these and in these the understanding as well as the fantasy originate; this is the cause of the fall. For the unfathomable will is not the creature, for it is no formation; rather, formation originates in eternal nature, as does the creaturely will toward "something" or toward multiplicity.

41. The unfathomable will belongs to God, for it is in the One, and yet it is not God [as a manifest person]: for God is only understood in that [will], or when the will of the **Abyss** shuts itself into a center of the **Trinity** in the birthing process and leads forth into the delight of **Wisdom** <term>Wisdom<gloss>German: *Weisheit*. Often associated with the figure of Sophia, this is the divine mirror or "looking-glass" through which God beholds His own wonders.</gloss></term>.

42. From the will into which the Godhead shuts itself into the Trinity, the ground of nature was also born from eternity; for there is no predetermined plan, but a birth; the eternal birth is the plan—namely, that God wants to beget God and reveal Himself through nature.

43. Now nature shuts itself into its own will, as into a painful and hostile life; and this same hostile life is the cause of the fall, for it has [turned] into the fantasy of nature...
