<summary>Böhme explains that the "Abyss" or groundless will of God is not the cause of evil; rather, evil arises when the natural forces within a creature seize this neutral will for their own "fantasy" or self-centered delusion. He distinguishes between the unfathomable divine will and the "eternal birth" through which God reveals Himself in nature and the Trinity.</summary>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 4.

fantasy (or play of birth) <note>original German: *Spiel der Gebärung*. This refers to the creative, ever-shifting interplay of forces and images within the soul's inner nature.</note>, and made himself the leader or master of that same fantastical nature. The fantasy took that life into itself and fully gave itself over to it. Now the fantasy and the life have become one thing, and it possesses the will of the **Abyss** <term>Abyss<gloss>German: *Ungrund*. Böhme’s term for the "groundless" or "unfathomable" source of all being that exists before all manifestation.</gloss></term> (as the divine **science** <term>science<gloss>German: *Scientz*. An innate, "magical" potency or root-knowledge within the will that precedes conscious thought.</gloss></term> in which God births Himself in God). But God does not birth Himself in this enclosed science; He may birth Himself within it, yet He is not made manifest in that science as far as nature grasps and perceives it. God is unmoving and non-acting within it; He does not birth there a Father, Son, Holy Spirit, and Wisdom, but rather a fantasy according to the properties of the dark world. God is indeed a God within it, but dwelling only in Himself, not in the **creature**; rather, He dwells in the **Abyss** outside the motion and the will of the creature, and outside the life of the creature.

44. So when the creature does anything, it is not God doing it in the will of the **Abyss** (which is also in the creature); rather, it is the life and the volition of the creature’s life that does it. We can see this in the **Devil**: he regrets having become a devil, since he was once an angel. Now, he does not regret this in his life's will according to his creaturely state, but according to the will of the **Abyss**, wherein God is so near to him that he is ashamed before God’s holiness—ashamed that he was once a holy angel and is now a devil. For the **science** of the **Abyss** is ashamed that such an image stands before it in its revelation, and that it has become a fantasy in the outward sense. However, that same will cannot break the fantasy, for it is only One thing; in itself, it contains no torment or sensitivity to the fantasy. Rather, it is a **science** into which the fantasy forms itself. And this same fantasy takes nothing into itself except a **likeness** <note>original: *Gleichheit*. A resonance or "equal-ness" that allows the fantasy to sustain itself through its own self-image.</note>. This likeness is the power of its life; but if anything else were to enter it, the fantasy would have to perish. Thus, the very thing from which it is born—namely, nature—would also perish. And if nature perished, the word of divine power would not be speaking or manifest, and God would remain hidden.

45. Therefore, understand that it is entirely unavoidable that both good and evil exist; for in God everything is good, but in the creature, there is the distinction: the life of the eternal...

<summary>Böhme explains that while God remains present as the "Abyss" within every creature, He is not active or manifest in a soul that has turned toward "fantasy" or self-delusion. This separation explains how a fallen being like the Devil can feel shame from the divine ground within him while remaining trapped in his own dark nature.</summary>

### Chapter 4. The Election of Grace.

45. The **creature** <note>In Böhme’s writing, "the creature" often refers collectively to any created being, such as an angel or a human.</note> was entirely free in its beginning, for it was made manifest in the **Temperature** <note>original: *Temperatur*. This refers to a state of perfect balance where all qualities (hot, cold, dark, light) exist in harmony without one dominating the others.</note>; just as in heaven the angels were created out of that same nature, quality, and property. The dark world, along with the kingdom of the **fantasy** <note>original: *Phantasey*. For Böhme, this is not mere daydreaming, but a powerful, formative force that creates self-centered illusions and false realities.</note>, was present within it, but not manifest in heaven. However, the free will in the fallen angels made this fantasy manifest within themselves, for the will inclined toward the fantasy; thus, the fantasy seized the will and surrendered itself to the creature’s life.

46. Now, that same dark kingdom and the fantasy, and the creaturely nature of the fallen angels are currently one single thing, one will and one essence. Because that same rebellious will did not wish to dwell and rule in the fantasy alone, but also desired to rule simultaneously in the holy power in which it originally stood, the holy power (acting as the **science** <term>science<gloss>Latin: *scientia*; German: *Scienz*. An innate, "magical" potency or root-will that precedes conscious thought.</gloss></term>) in the light of God cast the will out from itself and hid itself from it. That is to say, the inner heaven enclosed itself against him so that he no longer sees God. This is the same as saying he died to the kingdom of heaven and to the good will, and is now in God just as the night is in the day; for the night is not manifest during the day in the sun’s brilliance, yet it exists, dwelling only within itself, as it says in **John 1:5**: *The light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not understood it.* <note>original Latin reference: *Joh. 1: 5.*</note> This is how we are now to understand the Devil and God: for the Devil is in God, but he is enclosed in the Divine Night (in the **Center of Nature** <term>Center of Nature<gloss>German: *Centro der Natur*. The inner core of existence where the different "qualities" or "source-spirits" of nature are birthed.</gloss></term>) with darkness in the **essence** <term>essence<gloss>German: *Essenz*. The fundamental nature or spiritual "substance" of a thing.</gloss></term> of his life. He carries a magical fire-light from the **Ens** <term>Ens<gloss>Latin: *Ens*. A philosophical term for "being" or the fundamental "stuff" that makes up a thing’s existence.</gloss></term> of cold and heat, which appears as a terrifying light before our eyes, but to him, it seems good.

47. Scripture says that the archangel Michael fought with the dragon, and the dragon did not prevail (**Revelation 12:7–8**), and in another place Christ says: *I saw Satan fall from heaven like lightning* (**Luke 10:18**). This prince Michael is a Throne-Angel; he fought against the dragon in the power of Christ—that is, in the Word of holy power—into which Word Adam was also created.

48. That same Word of power is understood in all three **Principles** <term>Principles<gloss>German: *Principien*. Böhme’s system of the three "worlds": the Dark World (Fire), the Light World (Love), and the Outward World (Nature).</gloss></term>. For when Lucifer fell and committed himself to the kingdom of fantasy, he lost the kingdom in the holy power and was cast out. This occurred through the work of the angels, who, acting through Divine...

## Chapter 5.
# On the Origin of Man.
### Summaries.

Man’s body is from the **clay of the earth** <note>original: *Limo der Erden*. From the Latin *limus*, meaning the slime, mud, or dust from which Adam was formed.</note>. §. 1. In Man, the **Mind** <term>Mind<gloss>original Latin: *Mens*. Referring to the higher, spiritual intellect or the seat of the will.</gloss></term> is understood as the spiritual essence; and in the **Being** <term>Being<gloss>original Latin: *Ens*. The essential "stuff" or underlying substance of a thing.</gloss></term> is the life of the seven properties of nature; 2. 3. but the Mind lies within the Being like the soul in the body. 4. 6. Man does not eat gross earth, but desires the **Quintessence** <note>The "fifth essence," a pure spiritual substance superior to the four physical elements.</note>; 7. but because he has gone out into the four elements, he now eats the curse. 8. In his state of innocence, the pure element bloomed for him in the **Temperature** through the four elements, as Paradise: 9. but in the Fall, the four elements woke up, which now sicken and kill him. 10. A proper description of Man. 11. He has been beheld from eternity in the Divine Wisdom as a shadow and an image of God. 12. Thus also heaven and earth are beheld in Wisdom as if in a mirror. 13. What the stars are in their **distinction** <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*. The state of being diverse and separated into different functions.</note>? 14. from which the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spir. M.* or *Spiritus Mundi*. The soul or animating force of the physical cosmos.</note> was opened as the soul of the world; 15. and the whole work is the **formed Word**, which has expressed itself into elemental spirits, 16. which are from the natural life and rule in the elements at every pole according to the order of the stars. 17. The Spirit of the World is the life of the outer world; 18. the higher hungers for the lower, and the lower for the higher. 19. Out of this **Matrix** <note>womb or source-foundation.</note>, God on the fifth day let all living creatures come forth: good and evil; dark and fierce ones that dwell in holes; **phantastical** ones, such as apes and similar animals and birds; and tame ones, which are recognized by their food and dwellings; 20. which outer creatures are all enclosed in time, limit, and measure. 21. In God there is no purpose for evil, but in the Spirit of the World there is, which gives birth and breaks apart. 22. God is Love and can do nothing but love: His anger is only understood in eternal and temporal nature. 23. If a land or a creature awakens the anger within itself, then the Word of Anger speaks out the **Turbam** <term>Turbam<gloss>Latin: *Turba*. A state of terminal confusion, disruption, or the "destroyer" that breaks down a form when it falls out of harmony.</gloss></term>, for sin is the food of anger. 24. 25. Out of the higher and the lower, where everything lies mixed together, God created Adam, in which he was meant to rule. 26. This Word drew together a **mass** <note>original: *Massam*. A lump of substance.</note> from all the essences of the earth, out of all three Principles. 27. The body of the animals is from the element: Adam's body, however...

1. Moses says: **GOD** created man from a clod of earth, Gen. 2:7. Understand this to mean the body, which is a *limus* <note>clay or slime</note> of the earth; and the earth is an **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*.</note> out of all three Principles, a breathed-out, captured, and solidified power from the **Word** of all three Principles, out of the **Great Mystery** <term>Great Mystery<gloss>original: *Mysterio Magno*. In Böhme's thought, the chaos or "mother" out of which all things were produced.</gloss></term>, as if from the first three, from the **seven forms of nature**, which have captured themselves in the ignited desire (as if in the *Fiat* <note>The divine "let there be" command.</note>), and led into a being—each quality within itself toward a compaction. This is what **GOD** has grasped into a lump through the *Fiat* as the essential **Science** <note>Used here in the sense of "knowledge" or "active wisdom."</note>, in which all the powers of the spiritual world lie fixed, according to **GOD’S** love and wrath, and also according to the **fantasy** <note>The realm of imagination or the false mirror of reality.</note>; not after the manner of the **Mind**, but after the manner of the **Being**.

2. In the **Mind** <term>Mind<gloss>original Latin: *Mens*.</gloss></term>, the living essence—which is spiritual—is understood as a completely spiritual being, a spiritual **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*.</note> of the **Tincture**, where the highest power of fire and light introduces itself into a being.

3. And in the **Being** <term>Being<gloss>original Latin: *Ens*.</gloss></term>, the life of the seven properties of nature is understood as the sensitive, growing life—namely, the **spoken Word**, which speaks itself out again in growth, forming and solidifying itself.

4. However, the **Mind** lies within the **Being** just as the soul lies within the body; the **Mental Word** speaks out the **Essential Word**; the heaven encloses the Mind, and the fantasy encloses the Being. Understand it thus: in the Mind is understood the Divine holy power in the containment of the Word, where the Word of powers encloses itself into a spiritual being, where the Word of powers is substantial.

5. Thus, the Mind is the **spiritual water**, and the power within it, which forms itself in the spirit-water, is now the highest **Tincture** which stands in the balance; and the ground of—

### XV. Regarding Chapter 5.

5. Of that same **Tincture** <note>In Böhme’s alchemy, a spiritual life-force or transformative power.</note> is the Divine Wisdom; and the ground of Wisdom is the Trinity of the unfathomable Godhead, and the ground of the Trinity is the single unsearchable Will, and the ground of the Will is the **Nothing** <note>original: *das Nichts*. Böhme’s "Unground"—the absolute void or primal state before existence.</note>.

6. Thus the mind should first learn to distinguish what is understood by "the Earth," before it says, "man is earth," and not look upon the earth as a cow does, which thinks the earth is merely a mother of grass, needing nothing more than grass and herbs.

7. Man, however, desires to eat the best part of the earth; therefore he should also learn to recognize that he himself is the best part of the earth. For every **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*.</note> desires to eat from its mother, from whence it has come: and we clearly see that man does not desire to eat from the coarseness of the earthly essence, but rather from its subtilty; he desires the **Quintessence** <note>original Latin: *Quintam Essentiam*, the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of matter.</note> for his life-force, which he also had for food in Paradise.

8. But when he departed from the **Temperature** <note>A state of perfect balance where all spiritual and physical qualities are in harmony.</note> into the **Science of Diversities** <note>original: *Scientz der Unterschiedlichkeiten*. The knowledge of "Good and Evil" or the fall into fragmented, polarized reality.</note>, God placed the curse between the element of the **Temperature** and the four elements. Because man had entered into the inequality of properties with his desire—which had also seized him in such a beastly, hard, tangible, feeling, and sensitive being of enmity, within the **Phantasy** <note>In Böhme's thought, "Phantasy" is the fallen, self-centered imagination that creates a false, illusory reality.</note> (as in the four-elemental coarseness of heat and cold, and also in the poisonous torment of the dark world, as in deadliness)—he was now forced to eat those same properties within himself. For the state of inequality does not belong to the **Temperature** of the single Holy Element; rather, the four elements belong to it. Therefore, the curse is the **dividing boundary** <note>original: *Scheide-Ziel*.</note>, so that the impure does not enter the pure; for the curse is nothing other than a fleeing of the Good, such that the single Element has withdrawn into itself and hidden itself from the being of wickedness.

9. For in Adam’s innocence, the Holy Element blossomed out through the four elements in the **Temperature**, and through the four elements it bore heavenly fruits, which were lovely to look upon and good to eat, as Moses says. And in that same blossoming, Paradise is understood; for that same fruit stood in its quality within the **Temperature**, and

# Chapter 5. The Election of Grace.

Adam also stood in the **Temperature** <note>original: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect harmony where all spiritual and physical qualities are in balance.</note>; thus man should and could eat of the Paradisiacal fruits.

10. But when Adam, through his desire, led himself into the multiplicity of properties—that is, into the **Phantasy** <note>original: *Phantasey*; here meaning a state of sensory illusion or the fragmented imagination of the material world.</note> of inequality—into the **Center** <note>The core of self-will or the "heart" of his own being.</note>; and wanted to know everything and become clever, and taste how heat and cold and all other properties tasted in their wrestling strife; then those very same properties in their strife captured him, and woke up within him, and through his desire took hold of the essence of the Phantasy. Thus the image of God within the **Temperature** was destroyed, and the light within the essence of the holy element—by which he recognized God—was extinguished in him. In this way, he died to the Temperature and woke up to the four elements and to the unequal **Science** <note>original: *Scienz*; for Böhme, this refers to a fragmented, polarized way of knowing the world through the senses rather than through divine unity.</note>, which now afflicts and finally kills him. And this is the true ground.

11. However, so that we may satisfy the seeking mind—which asks after its homeland and is on the path of pilgrimage—we wish to present man to it: (1) what he actually is, (2) out of what he was created, (3) what his soul and body are, (4) and then also his fall, and (5) his **redemption** or **restoration** <note>original: *Wiederbringung*; the alchemical and theological process of returning to the original divine state.</note>; so that we may fundamentally show him the ground of the Divine Will toward him. Afterward, we will test this with the Holy Scripture and show the same alongside its supposed contradictions, to see if anyone’s eyes might be opened thereby; which we shall faithfully do according to our gifts.

12. Moses speaks quite rightly: God created man in His image, yes, to the image of God He created him; likewise, God made man from the **dust of the earth** <note>original: *Limo der Erden*; from the Latin *limus*, meaning the "slime" or essential extract of the earth.</note>. In saying that God created man in His image, Moses does not mean that God is a physical image, or that He created man according to a physical model; rather, he understands the **Science** within the power, where from eternity all things in the **Science**, in the **Temperature**, and in the powers, were modeled within the spirit of **Wisdom**; not as creatures, but like a shadow or a pre-modeling in a mirror, where God from eternity in His Wisdom saw what could come to be. With which formation the Spirit of God played in Wisdom: In the contained model, where the Spirit of the **Science** in the

### Chapter 5. Election by Grace <note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*; the theological concept of predestination or God's choice of who receives grace</note>.

Adam also stood in the **Temperature** <note>The state of perfect spiritual and physical harmony where no single element or property dominates</note>; thus man was meant to and was able to eat of the fruits of Paradise.

10. But when Adam, through his own desire, entered into the multiplicity of properties—into the **Phantasy** <term>Phantasey: in Böhme’s view, this is not just "imagination" but a state of illusion or a "false" reality where the mind focuses on the many things of the world rather than the unity of God</term> of inequality—and into the Center; and when he wished to know all things and become clever, and to taste how heat and cold and all other properties tasted in their wrestling strife; then those same properties also caught him in their strife. And this is the true ground of the matter.

# XV. Regarding Chapter 5.

12. Wisdom, in the nature of the powers, has modeled a play from eternity (which model was not a creature, but rather existed as a shadow of a creature). In this, God created the creaturely human being, as if in man's own image, yet he was not a man: rather, he was God's image, in which the Spirit of God modeled itself out of all **Principles** <note>original: *Principiis*; the fundamental spiritual foundations of reality in Böhme's system</note> into a shadow of a uniformity of the Being of all Beings. Just as a person looks at themselves before a mirror, where their image is in the mirror but possesses no life; so also we are to contemplate the image of God in man from eternity, as well as the whole creation, just as God has seen all things from eternity in the mirror of His wisdom.

13. When God had grasped all the powers of all three principles in **Science** <term>Scienz: a polarized, sensory way of knowing the world through its divisions and conflicts rather than its unity</term> into one being and gathered them into a lump, which is called Earth—namely, into a fixity of the birthing spiritual powers—He then separated the elements out of the **Temperature** <note>The state of perfect spiritual and physical harmony where no single element or property dominates</note> of the single element into four elements for a weaving life. He further enclosed the spiritual powers of nature (from which the material fixity found in the matter of the Earth arises) into stars: for of whatever essence the Earth is corporal, of that the stars are spiritual. And yet they are not like living spirits, but a spiritual **Being** <term>Ens: Latin for an essence or "thing-in-itself"</term> as powers, a **Fifth Essence** <term>Quinta Essentia: the purest, most refined substance that permeates all things</term>, namely the subtle power from which the Earth, as the coarseness, has separated itself, which God formed in the **Science** of His speaking into a diversity of powers.

14. They are called stars because it is a moving, harsh-grasping, severe **Being** <term>Ens</term> in which the properties of nature are understood. Everything that nature is in itself spiritually in the **Temperature**, that the stars are in their separateness; I set it forth to be understood thus: if all the stars dissolved and returned again into the One from which they came, it would be Nature as it was from eternity, for it would stand again in the **Temperature**, as it shall indeed happen at the end; however, all beings shall be tested through fire and separated into their own principle. With this division and enclosure of the powers of the stars and the four elements, we understand time and the creaturely beginning of this world.

He describes the material world as a coarse separation from a subtle, spiritual Fifth Essence that will eventually return to unity through fire.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of Chapter 5, shifting from the eternal model of humanity to the physical manifestation of the universe. It describes how the spiritual powers previously discussed were organized into the stars, the elements, and the animating "World-Spirit."</meta>

### Chapter 5. The Election of Grace.
D 4

15. When **GOD** had created the earth and the firmament of the stars, and had ordered the planetary wheel of the seven properties of nature in the midst of them, along with the sun as their ruler, the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*; the animating soul of the physical universe</note> then opened itself. This spirit arose from all the properties of the powers, from the stars and the elements, for every power flows outward according to the law of eternal nature, within the "speaking word." This eternal word had here, out of the **Great Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*; the chaotic but fertile "chaos" or womb from which all things were created</note>, enclosed and shut itself into a period of time, as if into a figure of the spiritual Great Mystery—like a great **clockwork** <note>original: *Uhrwerck*; Böhme often uses mechanical metaphors to describe how the universe functions automatically once set in motion by God</note>, in which one understands the spiritual word through its outward workings.

16. The entire work is the formed word of **God** (understand this as the natural word, while the living word of **God**, which is **God** Himself, is understood as the internal power). This word expresses itself through nature into a **World-Spirit**, functioning as the soul of the **Creation**. And within this expression, there is again a separation in the fiery astral **science** <term>Scienz: a polarized, sensory way of knowing the world through its divisions and conflicts rather than its unity</term> within the World-Spirit, where this fiery knowledge leads to a spiritual division. In this division, the spirits within the elements are understood, occurring according to the distinction of the four elements, each spirit within an element according to its own property.

17. For every element has its own indwelling spirits, according to the quality of that specific element; these are a shadow and image of the Eternal. And yet, they possess a true life arising from the "science" of nature belonging to the expressed, formed word out of the **Great Mystery**. They do not arise from the true Divine Life, but rather from the natural life. These spirits rule in the fire, in the air, in the water, and in the earth, organized in the same way that the stars have their own inherent order; this is also to be understood as existing under each **pole** <note>original: *Polo*; referring to the celestial poles and the global structure of the spiritual-physical cosmos</note>.

18. The **World-Spirit** is now the life of the outer world. The stars stand all around it and contain within themselves the **Three Firsts** <term>Sal, Sulphur, Mercurium: Salt (fixity/body), Sulfur (energy/soul), and Mercury (motion/spirit); the three fundamental building blocks of all matter in Böhme’s alchemy</term> in a harsh, fiery science. Indeed, they are made of that very same essence, but in great diversity and distinction. These same distinctions of powers flow out of themselves and are a "hunger" for the essence they once had—namely, for the earth and its materials according to their properties. Conversely, the earth is a hunger for the **World-Spirit**, for the earth was separated out of it.

<summary>This page describes the creation of the physical universe as a "divine clockwork" animated by the World-Spirit, which acts as the soul of the cosmos. It explains that the elements and stars are governed by natural spirits and the three primary alchemical qualities, all of which long for a restoration of their original unity.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 5, specifically moving into the fifth day of creation. # XV. Concerning Chapter 5

19. Thus the upper desires the lower, and the lower desires the upper; the hunger of the upper stands powerfully toward the earth, and the hunger of the earth toward the upper. Therefore, all things that are material fall toward the earth, just as the water is also drawn toward the earth; and on the other hand, the fiery spirit in the upper realm draws the water back up into the height into itself for its refreshment. It brings it forth, and gives it away, and also—after it has been tempered with the earth—draws it back into itself. These two are toward each other like body and soul, or like man and woman, who bear children together.

20. Out of this birth, as the **matrix of nature** <note>original: *Matrice der Natur*; the womb-like source from which natural forms emerge</note>, **GOD**—in the **Word "Fiat"** <note>original: *Verbo Fiat*; Latin for the "Word of Becoming" or "Let there be," the command that sparked creation</note>, which is the essential desire of the powers—commanded on the fifth day that all creatures should come forth from every **science** <term>Scienz: a technical term in Böhme’s thought referring to the specific "knowledge" or "active seed" of a spiritual property that defines a creature's nature</term> according to its own property: the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> from the fixity of the earth, and the spirit from the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note>. This occurred in the **conjunction** <note>original: *Conjunction*; the union or joining together</note> of the upper and the lower; that is, the inner Divine Word spoke itself through the outer spoken word in every science, out of the fiery property of the powers, into a creaturely life. These are now the creatures on earth, in the water, and the birds in the air—each creature from its own science, out of good and evil, according to the property of all **three principles** <note>original: *drey Principia*; the three realms of Böhme's cosmology: the dark fire-world, the light world of God's love, and the physical world we see</note>. Each is an image of the likeness of the **Inner Ground** <note>the spiritual foundation of reality</note>, arising from the realm of **fantasy** <note>original: *Phantasey*; here meaning the playful, shifting, and imaginative forces of the outward world</note> as well as from the original good life. This is why one sees with their own eyes that there are both good and evil creatures, such as venomous animals and worms, which follow the **Center of the Nature of Darkness** <note>original: *Centro der Natur der Finsterniß*</note> out of the power of the **fierce property** <note>original: *grimmen Eigenschaft*; the wrathful or harsh aspect of nature</note>. These creatures only desire to dwell in the dark, such as those that live in holes and hide from the sun. In contrast, one also finds many creatures that the World-Spirit has formed out of the realm of fantasy, such as monkeys and similar animals and birds, which only play pranks and plague and disturb other creatures. Thus, one is the enemy of the other, and everything strives against one another, in the same way that the three principles play with one another in their powers. In this way, **GOD** has introduced that same "play" before Him—with the World-Spirit in its science—into a living creaturely being; just as one also finds good, friendly creatures in—

Böhme accounts for the diversity of wildlife—from poisonous snakes to mischievous monkeys—by describing them as physical manifestations of the different "properties" and "conflicts" within the spiritual world.</summary>

## Chapter 5: The Election of Grace
### D 5

20. An imitation of the **Angelic world** <note>original: *Englischen Welt*; Böhme refers here to the "Angelic" realm, not the country of England</note> is found where the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*</note> has expressed itself in the good, revealed powers, which are the tame animals and birds. Yet many evil animals, representing evil qualities, are also mingled among the tame ones, having been caught up in mixed qualities. From every animal’s food and dwelling, one can see where it originated; for every creature desires to dwell in its "mother" <note>the source element from which it was born</note>, and yearns for her, as is clearly visible before our eyes.

21. The **World-Spirit**, from which all external creatures have come according to the spirit, is enclosed within a time, a goal, and a measure of how long it shall last. It is like a **clockwork** <note>original: *Uhrwerck*</note> made from the stars and elements, in which the highest **GOD** dwells. He uses this clockwork as His instrument and has enclosed His creative work within it. It proceeds freely and brings forth according to its minutes, so to speak. All things lie within it—whatever has happened in the world, and what is yet to happen. It is God’s purpose toward the creature and within the creature, wherein He governs everything through this **governance of nature** <note>original: *Regiment der Natur*</note>.

22. In **GOD** Himself—insofar as He is named and is God—there is no purpose for evil or for anything else, for He is the **Sole Good**. He has no other capacity within Himself but Himself and His Word, which He has spoken from Himself as the **World-Spirit** out of the **Great Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*; the primordial "Great Mystery" or chaos out of which all creation is differentiated</note> of eternal nature. There He conceived His purpose and enclosed it in the free clockwork, in the World-Spirit; this spirit now brings forth and breaks everything according to its inner state and course, bringing about both fertility and infertility.

23. But **GOD** in His essence pours His power of love into it—that is, He pours Himself into it, just as the sun pours into the **science** <term>Scienz: In Böhme’s vocabulary, this is not "science" in the modern sense, but refers to the active, conscious "knowing-power" or "living spark" within a property that gives it form and direction.</term> of the elements and the fruits. That is, the holy Divine Science gives power to the natural science. **GOD** loves all His works and can do nothing else but love, for He is the sole love itself. His **wrath**, however, is understood in eternal and temporal nature: in the eternal, it is in the **center of darkness**, in the cold and hot **source of fire** <note>original: *Feuer-Quall*; a pun in Böhme's German meaning both "source" (Quelle) and "torment" (Qual) of fire</note>; and in the temporal, in the **World-Spirit**, it is also understood as the fiery science that separates all qualities.

<summary>Böhme explains that the physical world is a mechanical "clockwork" governed by the World-Spirit, which contains all past and future events. He clarifies that while God is pure love, the qualities of wrath and evil arise from the "clockwork" of nature and the dark center of fire, not from God's divine essence.</summary>

<keywords>World-Spirit, clockwork of nature, Great Mystery, divine love vs. wrath, science of elements, angelic world, governance of nature, creation mechanics</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 5, focusing on the "clockwork" of nature and how the "Spirit of the World" reacts to human sin. It moves from general natural principles to the specific creation of humanity as the pinnacle of this cosmic system.</meta>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 5.

24. And so, if a city, land, or creature awakens this same wrath within the **fiery science** <note>original: *feurischen Scienz*; Böhme uses "science" here to mean an inner, active knowledge or life-force</note>, within the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note>, it causes the **aversion** <note>original: *Eckel*; a technical term for the moment of tension or revulsion that triggers an outburst of wrath</note> to turn into fury. It is then like wood in a fire, where the fury becomes the defining quality and consumes everything around it, placing the life within the creature's inner science into the highest state of torment.

25. Then the angry, fiery word speaks within the awakened **Turmoil** <note>original: *Turba*; the disruptive power in nature that breaks things apart</note> through the prophetic spirit, in the **Great Turmoil** <note>original: *Turba Magna*</note>: "I will call down misfortune upon city and land, and I will find my satisfaction in it, seeing how the wrath consumes the aversion, and how it devours the evil people." For this is precisely a joy and a strong power of fury within nature: when one feeds it such firewood as blasphemy and other sins and shames, it eats and consumes them, for they are its food. This is especially true when human science breaks away from God’s love and **plays the harlot** with the fury of nature. There, the fury fattens itself and grows strong until the "clockwork" <note>the mechanical system of the universe</note> enters a fiery science where all beings must face the **trial** <note>original: *Proba*</note>. Then the fury ignites within it, once the Turmoil in the wheel of the clockwork is set ablaze, so that a specific quality becomes manifest. Thus the plague goes forth, and thus it is poured out over that land, city, and creature; often as poison, as pestilence, often as infertility, or often as the embittering of the minds of the rulers, from which war originates.

### Concerning Humanity.

26. From this great clockwork—comprising both the upper and lower worlds where everything lies intertwined—man was created in the image of **GOD**. For Moses says the **LORD** spoke: "Let us make man, an image after us, that he may rule over all creatures on earth, over the animals, birds, fish, and over all the earth and creeping things that crawl upon the earth." (*Genesis 1:28*). Now, if humans are to rule over all these things, they must also be made from the same foundation, and moreover, from its very best power. For no thing can rule deeper than the "mother" <note>the source element or origin</note> from which it comes, unless it is **transmuted** <note>original: *transmutiret*; transformed into a higher state</note> into something better; in that case, it rules within that better state, but no further than its own foundation allows.

27. Furthermore, Moses says: **GOD** made man from the clod of earth, and breathed into him the living breath,

<summary>Böhme explains how human sin acts as fuel for the "wrath" of nature's clockwork, leading to disasters, and begins describing how humanity was created from the best powers of this system to rule over it.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 5, shifting from the "clockwork" of nature and its reaction to sin toward the specific creation of humanity. ### Chapter 5. The Election of Grace

...then man became a living soul. *Genesis 2:7.* Here we must not understand that God stood there in a personal, creaturely way like a human, and took a lump of earth and made a body out of it. No, that is not how it was; rather, the Word of God, as the **Speaking** <note>original: *Fiat*; the Latin command "let it be"</note>, was active in all qualities—within the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note> and within the **Being** <note>original: *Ente*</note> of the earth derived from that Spirit—and spoke a life into all **essences** <note>original: *Essentien*</note>. Namely, the **"Let it be"** <note>original: *Fiat*</note>, which is the desire of the Word within the **active knowledge** <note>original: *Scienz*; used here to mean a living, formative power</note>, existed in the eternally envisioned **Model** of man that had stood within Wisdom. It drew the **Being** <note>original: *Ens*</note> of all the earth's qualities, and whatever might be within them, into a **Mass** <note>original: *Massam*</note>. This was a **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the pure, underlying substance of the four elements</note> drawn from the four elements, in which lay the **Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; a spiritual life-essence or transformative power</note> of all powers from all three **Principles** <note>the three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light world of God, and the outer world</note>, as well as the quality of the whole **Creation** of all **creatures**, as the Being of all beings from which all creatures had arisen.

28. Then understand it correctly: the earthly **creatures** of time are made with a **body** <note>original: *Corpore*</note> from the four elements, but the body of man is from the **Balanced Temperament** <note>original: *Temperatur*</note>, where all four elements lie within one another in a single Being, from which earth, stones, and metals, along with all earthly creatures, have their origin. It is indeed from the **mud** <note>original: *Limo*; referring to the "dust of the ground" in Genesis</note> of the earth, but not from the coarseness of the enclosed being of separation in the qualities—where each quality has enclosed itself into a particular being of earth, stone, or metal. Rather, it is from the **Fifth Essence**, in which the four elements lie within the **Balance**, where neither heat nor cold was manifest, but they were all in equal weight.

29. For if man was to rule over all **creatures**, he surely had to have the higher power within him, as the highest **Being** <note>original: *Ens*</note> of the **creature**, compared to which the creatures were one degree more external or lower (or however one might call it, lesser). This was so that the mighty might rule in the powerless, just as God rules in **Nature**, which is also lesser than He. Yet, one should not think that the animal qualities were meant to be creaturely or manifest in man; rather, the **Being** of all creatures lay in the human **Being** within the **Balance**. Man is an image of the whole **Creation** of all three **Principles**, not only in the **Being** of the outer **nature** of the stars and the four elements, as the created world,

<summary>Böhme explains that man was created not by a physical hand but by the Divine Word acting upon the 'Fifth Essence'—a perfectly balanced state of all elements and principles. This unique composition grants man dominion over all other creatures, as he contains their essence in a higher, harmonized form.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 5.

...but also from the inner spiritual world’s <term>Being</term> <note>original: *Ente*, derived from the Latin *Ens*; it refers to the essential, spiritual substance that underlies physical matter</note>, out of Divine Essence; for the holy Word in its <term>Being</term> took hold of itself within the spoken word: namely, just as Heaven took hold of itself within the essence of the outer world, so too did the greening life of the inner world’s essence—as Paradise, the holy element—exist within the surging dominion.

30. In summary, the human body is a <term>Slime</term> <note>original: *Limus*; a reference to the "dust of the ground" in Genesis 2:7, often interpreted by alchemists as a "muck" or extract containing all cosmic powers</note> drawn from the Being of all beings. Otherwise, it could not be called a Likeness of **GOD**, or an Image of **GOD**: the invisible **GOD**, who has introduced Himself from eternity into Being, and also into time with this world, has modeled Himself into a creaturely image through the human form, fashioned from all beings as a figure of the invisible Being. For this purpose, He did not give man the creaturely, animal life from the <term>Active Power</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; from the Latin *scientia*, meaning a formative, conscious power or "know-how" inherent in nature</note> of the creature, for that life had to remain undivided in the <term>Perfect Balance</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state where all opposing qualities or "complexions" exist in perfect harmony without one dominating the others</note>; rather, He breathed into him the living breath as the true, intelligible life in the Word of Divine power. That is, He breathed into him the true soul drawn from all three <term>Principles</term> <note>the three realms of Böhme's cosmology: the dark fire-world, the light-world of God, and the outer physical world</note> in their <term>Perfect Balance</term>.

31. (1) First, from within, the magical <term>Fire-World</term>, as the <term>Center</term> of nature, as already mentioned above; this is the true creaturely <term>Fire-Soul</term>, because of which **GOD** calls Himself a strong, jealous **GOD**, and a consuming fire—which is the eternal nature.

32. (2) And with this, simultaneously, the <term>Light-World</term>, as the realm of the power of **GOD**; just as fire and light are undivided within one another, so also is it to be understood here.

33. (3) And from without, He also simultaneously breathed into him the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: *Spiritum Mundi*</note> along with the <term>Air-Soul</term>. The entire speaking Word breathed itself into all of nature, according to both time and eternity, for man was an image of **GOD** in whom the invisible **GOD** was manifest—a true temple of the Spirit of **GOD**. As it stands in John 1:4: the life of men was in the Word, and was breathed into the created image; thus the Spirit of **GOD** breathed into him the life of nature in <term>Perfect Balance</term>, as the spirit of Divine revelation, wherein Divine <term>Active Power</term> introduces itself into a natural life. That same Divine natural life is man—equal to the angels of **GOD** according to the soul, as the spiritual world—as written in Matthew 13:43 and 22:30:

# Chapter 5. Divine Election.

In the resurrection they are like the angels of **GOD**; Now we only return again into the first created Divine Image, and not into another creature.

34. Thus, we are to rightly recognize man: first, what he was in his innocence, and secondly, what he became afterward. He was in Paradise; this is the **Perfect Balance** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect harmony where no single element or "quality" dominates another</note>. He was placed in a certain location where the holy world blossomed forth through the earth and bore Paradisiacal fruits, which in their essence also stood in the **Perfect Balance**. These were good and lovely to behold, and also good to eat in a heavenly manner: not into a **maggot-sack** <note>original: *Madensack*; Böhme’s characteristic term for the corruptible, digestive physical body of fallen man</note>, as is the case now in the awakened animal property; rather, in a magical way, certainly into the mouth, but within the mouth were the centers of separation, each principle into its own, in the manner that may exist in eternity. Just as the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*; the animating soul of the external cosmos</note> gives birth to and releases water from the first three—namely, from fiery **Sulfur**, **Mercury**, and **Salt**—as in the **Saltpeter** <note>original: *Salniter*; Böhme uses this to describe a volatile, formative "divine salt" or explosive creative power</note> of separation, and yet draws it back into itself from the earth and never becomes full; so too is it to be understood regarding man.

35. Adam was naked, and yet clothed with the greatest glory, as if with Paradise: a completely beautiful, bright, **Crystalline Image**, neither man nor woman, but both, as a **masculine virgin** <note>Böhme’s concept of the original androgynous human who contained both male and female spiritual principles in harmony</note>, with both **Tinctures** <note>original: *Tincturen*; the spiritual "stains" or essential qualities of fire/strength and light/love</note> in the **Perfect Balance**, like the heavenly **Matrix** <note>original: *Matrix*; the receptive, mother-principle of nature</note> in the birthing fire of love. And then there was also the **Seed-Extract** <term>Limbus</term> <note>original: *Limbus*; the masculine, formative "extract" of the divine earth</note> from the nature of the essential fire; within these two, the first and second principles of the holy Divine nature are understood. There, the **Tincture of Venus** (as the birthing and giving, from the property of the Son) is and is understood as the woman, the mother of the birthing-woman; and the fiery property, from the property of the Father, is understood as the **Active Power** <term>Scients</term> <note>original: *Scients*; the conscious, formative power of the soul</note>, the man. These two properties later separated themselves into man and woman.

36. For had Adam been able to remain steadfast, the birth and multiplication of humans would have been magical—one out of the other, just as the sun penetrates glass and yet does not break it. But because **GOD** well knew that Adam would not remain so, He ordained for him the Savior and Re-generator before the foundation of the world,

XV. Of Ch. 6.

...[origi]nally created him in the true, right image and placed him in Paradise, where he was meant to remain eternally. ### Chapter 6.
## Of the Fall of Man, and his Wife.

->Summaries.<-

A reminder to the reader. §. 1. Just as Father and Son are one **GOD**, inseparable: so the masculine and feminine **tincture** <note>the inner spiritual essence or "color" of a soul</note> cannot exist as two separate lives in eternity. 2. 3. Therefore **GOD** created His likeness in a single image; 4. but once it was divided, it could no longer exist eternally. 5. A rational objection: was it not **GOD'S** intent, since He foresaw it? 6. Answer: to provide/ordain and to foresee are not the same thing. 7. **GOD** created Adam according to His purpose, and His will is singular; 8. but the dark fire broke forth with the formation of the **phantasms** <note>original: *Phantasen*; the realm of shadows, false imaginations, or the dark mirror of reality</note>; 9. which the fiery science took into itself, so that a princely throne separated into the **ruling principle** <term>Archiam</term> <note>original: *Archiam*; the formative, archetypal power or governing center</note> of the phantasms. 10. The kingdom of phantasms is from eternity, 11. and **Lucifer** entered into it by his own will. 12. But man was created in his place; yet he was deceived by the devil: ibid. therein the name **JESUS** was immediately incorporated into man. 13. Accordingly, souls, angels, and devils come from one **Ground** <note>the fundamental source of being</note>; and both man and devil have a share of the external world within them. 14. The **Spirit of the World** <term>Spir. M.</term> <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*; the animating soul of the physical cosmos</note> as well as the kingdom of phantasms with its fierce nature pulled at Adam; 15. there he stood in the test: his **science** <note>his capacity for spiritual knowing</note> was free. 16. The speaking Word of Love gave a command to the fiery science, 17. for the Spirit of **GOD** manifests neither in an animal property nor in the kingdom of phantasms. 18. Why did Almighty **GOD** not prevent such a thing? 19. The fiery science was from the will of the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund</term> <note>original: *Ungrund*; the absolute abyss or "nothingness" before all creation from which the divine will emerges</note>; 20. the same is eternal, and a part of the highest omnipotence of all beings in the soul; 21. the command was given to it, and it was the power that could do it. ibid. But the science broke itself off from **GOD’S** power and light and wanted to be its own thing: which was a **counter-will** <note>original: *Wiederwille*; a will acting in opposition to the divine harmony</note> in **GOD’S** power. 22. Therefore **GOD** has set a day of separation. 23. For the **Last Judgment** is a harvest of all created beings. 24. **GOD** made nothing to oppose Himself: but as long as it runs in a "foreign mother," it is in counter-will. 25. Hence the conflict in the **elements**. ibid. Now **GOD** indeed governs it, but in His particular government, namely in the **Spirit of the World**, in which many evil effects come forth. 27. **GOD**, as **GOD**, always gives good: but in the manifestation of his...

It explains that while God is entirely good, the Fall allowed for a separation into a realm of phantasms and conflicting elements.</summary>

### Chapter 6. The Election of Grace.

From His Word arises the operation of good and evil, 28. for this is contained within a time in which the making of good and evil stands. 29. The will toward evil thus arises in the creature; just as accidental things can corrupt one's own will. 30. Therefore God gave teaching and law to resist evil influences, since the soul's **Science** <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; a spiritual capacity for knowing or active perception</note> can indeed grasp both good and evil. 31. Adam stood in the **Temperament** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect balance and harmony between opposing spiritual forces</note>, but the influences from the Devil and the dark world led him astray, 32. so that the soulful Science stared gapingly <note>original: *vergafte*; to look at something with foolish or misplaced wonder</note> at the Creation; the Devil was the primary cause of this. 33. Because he [the Devil] had departed from the Temperament, he led his desire into the soulful Science. 34. God therefore did not will the fall of the Devil or of Adam. 35. He leads His Only Will into the forming of His Word toward **differentiation** <term>Schiedlichkeit</term> <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the quality of being distinct or separable from the unity of God into individual forms</note>; therein differentiation stands in free will. 36. Whatever has then differentiated itself into the good, in that He wills good, as in the angels, and in that which has differentiated itself into evil, as the devils, there He wills evil. ibid. Objection: If God, in His formed Word, is everything Himself, where does the human struggle come from? 37. Answer: Reason, which is blind to God, does not understand that all beginnings and purposes **originate** <term>urständen</term> <note>original: *urständen*; to have one's primordial source or "original standing" in something</note> in the differentiation of creation, so that all evil comes from nature and the creature. 38. 39. Explanation of the verses John 12:40, Psalm 91:8, 64:8. ibid. Thus there is no devilish will in God; rather, in the center of nature, personal Science grasped itself in its own will, 40. when Adam and Lucifer put **Fantasy** <note>original: *Phantasey*; here meaning a false, self-created reality or illusory imagination</note> in God’s place and became self-willed spirits, so that the Holy Spirit departed from them; 41. whereupon Adam fell asleep, that is, the Divine Light in love was extinguished within him. 42. 43. The time of Adam's temptation is revealed through the 40 years of Israel in the desert; the 40 days of Christ's temptation in the desert, the 40 days of His walking after the Resurrection, and the 40 hours in the grave. 44. Whoever reflects on this finds the entire process of Christ. ibid. Adam is the spoken word; Christ is the speaking Word. 45. In the division of the **Tinctures** <term>Tincturen</term> <note>original: *Tincturen*; the spiritual essences or "colors" that constitute the soul’s inner life</note>, God's image was broken, and they already had animal limbs. 46. The tree of knowledge was the very trial <note>original: *Proba*; a test or ordeal of one's nature</note>. 47. Then the Science of the soul desired the tree of temptation, 48. and that was Adam's fall; in this, the Devil was concerned with the **Matrix of Venus** <term>Veneris Matrix</term> <note>original: *Veneris Matrix*; the generative, feminine principle of desire and love within the spiritual nature</note>. 49. What was the serpent? 50. Satan was one of the most beautiful in heaven, 51. and just as he poisoned himself, so he also poisoned nature, 52. in which the serpent was close to the devil, 53. whereby he presented his cunning **outpouring** <term>Egeßt</term> <note>original: *Egeßt*; a discharge, waste product, or externalized expression of one's inner nature</note> to Eve's desire, and introduced to her the serpent's **essence** <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; the essential being or core substance of a thing</note>, in a magical way. 54.

Here we now wish to admonish the lover of truth to grasp our meaning correctly, for we wish to show it to him in such a way that he shall have enough, if only he can understand us, as to where the Divine will toward good and evil originates, where the Scripture says: *He hardens their hearts, so that they do not believe and be saved:* John 12:40. and also, on the other hand: *God does not want the death of the sinner.*

### XV. From Chapter 6.

<margin>Ezekiel 33:11</margin> This is so that one does not remain stuck in the delusion that God had formed a decree <note>original: *Vorsatz*; often used in the context of predestination</note> to damn one crowd and to save the other in His decree out of grace; rather, that one might learn to understand thoroughly how the Scripture, which speaks in this way, actually intends it.

2. Now, consider the image of God in Adam before his Eve, which stood in the **Temperament** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>the state of perfect spiritual balance where all internal forces are in harmony</note> in Paradise; for Moses says: "God looked at everything He had made, and behold, it was all very good." But afterward he said: "It is not good that the man should be alone"; and He also cursed the earth for the sake of man.

3. Dear reader, tell me: why did God not make male and female right at the beginning, as He did with the other creatures? What was the reason that He did not create them together from one single mass? Answer: the reason was that the life of both **Tinctures** <term>Tincturen</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; in this context, the two fundamental spiritual essences—the masculine "fire" and the feminine "light"—that must be unified</note> is only a single human being in the image of God. In eternity, life cannot stand in a twofold way as male and female, but rather according to the nature of the Father’s and Son’s property, who are only one God within each other, and not divided <note>original: *entschieden*; separated into distinct, disconnected parts</note>.

4. Thus, He also created His image and likeness into a Single Image. For perfect love does not reside in one Tincture alone, but it resides in both when one enters into the other. There arises the great fiery desire of love, just as the fire gives the light, and the light gives the fire power, radiance, and essence for its life. These two make only one Spirit <note>original: *Spiritum*</note>, like air; and the Spirit gives essence, like water. However, because and as long as these four—fire, light, air, and water—are separated from one another, there is nothing eternal there; but when they generate one another in the Temperament and do not flee from one another, then it is an eternal thing.

5. This is also how it is to be understood with Adam: when the Tincture of light and water was separated from him into a woman, he could no longer subsist eternally in this image that he afterward became, for his inward "Paradise-Rose-Garden"—in which he loved himself—was taken from him.

6. So **Reason** <term>Vernunft</term> <note>human logic unilluminated by divine spirit</note> says: Why did God do that, dividing Adam and bringing him into two images? It must have been His decree <note>original: *Vorsatz*</note>, otherwise He would not have done it; moreover, He saw before the foundation of the world that he [Adam] would

### Chapter 6. The Election of Grace.

...will and shall do. And here reason lies dead, and cannot go further without God's knowledge in the Holy Spirit; and from this comes all dispute and strife.

7. God’s decree and ordaining, and God’s foreseeing and knowing, are not the same thing. All things have occurred from eternity in the outgoing Spirit, from the **science** <term>Scienß</term> <note>original: *Scienß*; Böhme uses this to mean the "root-knowledge" or the essential, living potential within the divine wisdom</note> of fire and light, within the Wisdom of God—whatever might come to be, should God’s being move according to the birth-giver of nature.

8. For instance, in the property of the fiery science according to the darkness, it was clearly seen what a devil would be; likewise, in the science of the light-fire, what an angel would be, should the fiery science separate itself from the light. However, God did not create a devil; had there ever been a Divine decree <note>original: *Vorſatz*; here meaning a predetermined plan or predestination</note>, then a devil would have been created in that very decree. The single will of God gave itself only into the angelic figure, but the fiery science—according to the property of the dark world—pressed forward, took hold of itself in a decree, and also wanted to exist as a creaturely being.

9. When the light and the shining fire became creaturely, the dark, cold, painful fire also pressed forward with the formation of the **Fantasy** <term>Phantaſey</term> <note>in Böhme's thought, "Fantasy" refers to a state of self-delusion and illusory imagination that exists outside the divine light</note> and appropriated itself into the fiery science. The fiery science took this into itself as a joyful surge <note>original: *Freuden-Uffen*; likely referring to a passionate, prideful eruption of energy</note> and broke out of the **Temperament** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>the state of perfect spiritual balance where all internal forces are in harmony</note>; thus the new will was born against the Temperament, which was then cast out from God.

10. One must understand that the beginning of **separation** <term>Schiedlichkeit</term> <note>the process of spiritual properties becoming distinct and independent from their original unity</note> did not originate in God, as if God had formed Himself into a will toward the devil. Rather, it originated in the fiery science within eternal nature, in the speaking of the Word, according to fire and light. It happened from the **first three** <note>referring to the first three properties of eternal nature: desire, mobility, and anxiety/sensibility</note> that a princely throne in the fiery science separated itself into the kingdom—that is, into the **Archia** <term>Archiam</term> <note>from the Greek *arche*; the ruling principle or essential foundation of a realm</note> of the Fantasy.

11. The kingdom of the Fantasy according to the darkness, however, has existed from eternity, which is also a cause of the devil’s fall; although the fiery science of Lucifer stood in its own will and entered into it without any compulsion or pressure.

12. But man was deceived by the devil so that he fell: for when Prince Lucifer, before the foundation of the world, in the first...

...[from the fall of Lucifer's] movement or encompassing of nature, he was expelled from his royal **Place** <term>Loco</term> <note>original: *Loco*; Latin for place or station</note>; thus Adam was created in his stead. And because Lucifer had not endured, God created Adam according to the body as a material being, as a water-source <note>original: *Wasser-Quall*; Böhme uses this to describe a bubbling fountain or the fluid source of life-force</note>, so that He might help him.

13. And from this also arises the great envy, that the devil is hostile toward man; here also originates Christ's temptation in the wilderness, since He was to take the devil's throne and break his power in the **Creation** <term>Creation</term>, and become his judge who would cast him out eternally.

14. By this, Adam's free will in the fiery **Science** was **infected** <term>inficiret</term>, just as a poison enters the body and begins to **mingle its influence** <term>qualificiren</term> <note>original: *qualificiren*; Böhme’s term for how qualities or "qualities" interact and operate within a being</note>, from which an initial will toward self-lust arose. Then it was all over for the **Temperament** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect harmony where all conflicting qualities are balanced</note>, for the properties of **Creation**, which all lay in Adam in the **Temperament**, woke up—each in its own peculiarity—and drew the free will into themselves, and wanted to be manifest.

# Chapter 6. Election of Grace.

The will of the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund</term> <note>original: *Ungrund*; Böhme’s term for the absolute, fathomless void or primal nothingness from which God and all existence emerge</note> was free; it stood in three **Principles** <term>Principien</term> in a state of **Balance** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; meaning a perfect equilibrium where no single quality dominates another</note>, and it could turn toward whichever way it wished: not that it was free within the creature, for the command was given to the creature not to turn away from God into the desire for Evil and Good; but the foundation of the creature, as the fiery **Science** <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz* or *Scientia*; not "science" in the modern sense, but a spark of innate, active knowledge or a "knowing-drive" that powers the will</note>, as the root of the souls, stood in the groundless will of the beginning of all beings, and was a **Particular** <note>original: *Particular*; a specific, individual portion</note> of the eternal will; which eternal will, in the fiery Word of the division of nature, had divided itself into distinct "Sciences." Thus the soul was a part of the **Divisibility** <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the capacity for the unified divine power to differentiate into various qualities</note>, which divisibility was figured into the creatures of the angels and high eternal spirits in the Word of powers in nature (namely in the first three and in the seven forms of nature, and their expansion), wherein one also understands the fiery, breathed-in soul.

17. But the whole, holy, speaking Word of God according to Love—that is, according to the Trinity of the groundless Deity—gave a command to the fiery Science of the souls and spoke: *Eat not of the growth of the knowledge of Good and Evil, for if you do so, you shall die to the image of God on that very day*, Genesis 2:17. <note>The author translates the Latin Vulgate concept into German: *wirst du desselben Tages des Bildes Gottes ersterben*</note> That is, the fiery soul will lose the Light; and thus the Divine power in the holy **Essence** <term>Ente</term> <note>original: *Ente* or *Ens*; a spiritual "substance" or being-ness that gives the soul its heavenly form</note> will be extinguished within the second **Principle** <term>Principio</term> through the working of the Holy Spirit.

18. The Spirit of God reveals itself in no animal property, much less in the kingdom of **Fantasy** <note>original: *Phantasey*; for Böhme, this is the realm of selfish delusion and unstable mental images divorced from divine truth</note>: therefore God told him that he should not step out of the Balance into the lust of the properties, nor taste them in their distinctions; otherwise death would wind itself forward and reveal itself in him as the property of the dark world, emerging from the **Center** <term>Centro</term> of the first three [forms of nature]; and the Kingdom of God would be swallowed up within him, as indeed came to pass.

19. Thus Reason speaks: Why did God not prevent him with His holy power? Is He not almighty, such that He might break the fiery Science (from which the will toward lust arose)?

20. Hear, Reason: The fiery Science is from the will of the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund</term>, which will is called a Father of all beings,

# XV. From Chapter 6.

in which God is born (as the Son from the Father); this will leads itself through powers into the Word, as if into the act of Out-speaking.

21. So know now, that a **Particular** <note>original: *Particular*; a distinct, individual portion</note> of the highest Omnipotence—the Essence of all essences—is understood within the soul as the **Science** <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; not modern science, but an innate, active "knowing-drive" or spiritual spark that powers the will</note>, which has existed from eternity. Through the movement of the Word of all powers, this **Science** shaped itself into an image within the "three first" <note>Referring to the first three of Böhme's seven properties of nature: contraction, friction, and anguish</note>. Thus, this same **Science** is a unique property (arising from the will of the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund</term> <note>original: *Ungrund*; the absolute, fathomless void or primal nothingness from which God and all existence emerge</note>), for nothing exists before it that could break it. The creature is indeed fashioned after it, but the **Science** belonging to the creature is from eternity; in the "three first" properties, this **Science** introduced itself into the creaturely nature as a desire against the **Balance** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect harmony or equilibrium where no single quality dominates another</note>. The command was given to it that it should keep the creature within this **Balance**; that is, it should hold the properties of nature in equality. For it was the power that could do so, being a spark of the "All-possibility." Furthermore, it possessed within itself the kingdom of holy power in the Light of God. What more should God have given it to tame it? He had given Himself to it, just as He had also given Himself to King Lucifer.

22. But the **Science** <term>Scienz</term> broke itself off from God’s power and Light and wished to be its own thing; it wanted to be a self-acting God according to the properties of nature, working in both Evil and Good, and to manifest such workings within the kingdom of holy power. This was a "counter-will" against Divine power and property. For this reason, King Lucifer—and also Adam—were cast out of the kingdom of holy power: Lucifer into the kingdom of fantasy <note>A state of delusion and shifting, unstable shadows</note> in the darkness, and Adam into the inequality of **Creation**, into the animal property, into the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spiritum Mundi</term> <note>original: *Spiritum Mundi*; the governing spirit of the physical, temporal universe</note>. Thus, the qualities of all creatures, both Evil and Good, immediately woke up within him. Because of this, God has established the final judgment within the **Spirit of the World** to separate the Evil from the Good, and to harvest all things, each into its own **Principle** <term>Principium</term> <note>original: *Principium*; the fundamental spiritual realm or "ground" to which a soul or substance belongs</note>.

23. Therein all things (whatever the great clockwork within the **Great Mystery** <term>Mysterio Magno</term> <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*; the vast, chaotic potentiality or "great workshop" of the universe</note> in the **Spirit** has brought forth, both in the inner spiritual world and the outer) shall be put to the test of fire. That is, it shall be tested through the fire of eternal nature (for God calls Himself a "consuming fire"). For how else would God judge the creature, if it...

### Chapter 6. Divine Choice <note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*; the theological concept of election or how God chooses those who receive grace</note>.

...only do that which it inevitably had to do, if it had possessed no free will?

24. The Last Judgment is nothing other than a harvest of the Father of all beings, and of everything that He has brought forth through His Word. Into whichever state each thing has separated itself through its own free will, into that same state it shall go; for within that same eternal container, according to the property of that **Principle** <note>original: *Principii*</note>, it is considered "good" <note>Meaning it is consistent with the realm it has chosen for itself, whether light or dark</note>.

25. God has birthed nothing in opposition to Himself; in Him, everything is good, but each thing is good within its own "mother" <note>The "mother" refers to the source or natural environment from which a thing arises</note>. As long as a thing runs in a foreign mother, it exists in **Opposition** <note>original: *Wiederwillen*; literally "counter-will"</note>. We give you a likeness of this: Look at heat and cold, or fire and water. These come from a single original state, yet they divide from one another, and each enters its own will as if into its own **Source-quality** <term>Quall</term> <note>original: *Quall*; a Böhmean term combining the meanings of "quality," "source," and "tormenting-drive"</note>. Now, if they are forced to enter one another again, there is enmity, and one kills the other. This is caused by the individual will of each property. While they lie together in the **Balance** <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect harmony where no single quality dominates</note>, they have great peace; but as soon as they depart from one another, each wants to be its own master and rule over the other. From this arises the strife in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note> between the four elements, such as between heat and cold. Each wants to rule, and soon one triumphs, then the other: now it rains, now it becomes cold, then hot; now the air rages—now one way, now another. This all happens according to the power of the seven properties of nature and their out-flowings in the "three first" <note>The three primary qualities of contraction, friction, and anxiety</note>, from which everything that moves is drawn.

26. So Reason speaks: "Does God govern this, so that it goes this way?" **Answer:** Yes, that is true, but Reason is blind and does not see *how* God governs, or how it happens. It does not understand the **Expressed Word** <note>original: *entschiedene Wort*; the Word of God as it has been spoken out into differentiated, physical existence</note> within the properties, in which this governance resides.

27. For in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note> many evil effects come forth, which seem to be against God: likewise, that one creature strangles and harms another; likewise, that war, pestilence, thunder, and hail occur. All of this lies in the **Spirit of the World** and arises from the "three first"—**Salt**, **Sulphur**, and **Mercury** <term>Sale, Sulphure, und Mercurio</term> <note>These are the *Tria Prima* of alchemy, representing the fundamental spiritual forces behind physical matter</note>—in which the properties draw themselves together in their opposition.

28. For God can give nothing but good, for He is

<meta>This page continues the theological exploration of God as the ultimate Good and the origin of evil within the "Word of Revelation." It builds on the previous page's discussion of the human soul's free will and its relationship to the divine Balance.</meta>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 6.

...alone the only Good, and never transforms into any evil. He cannot do so, otherwise He would no longer be God; but in the Word of His revelation, where the formative states originated—as when nature and the <term>Creature</term> <note>original: *Creatur*; refers to any created being, especially humans</note> originated—there the effect in evil and good arises.

29. That same Word has contained itself within a "clockwork" of time, and therein now stands the making of evil and good, according to the distinction of the powers in the Word, just as the powers of <term>Divine Revelation</term> <note>original: *Göttliche Offenbarung*</note> separated themselves in the beginning into distinct <term>Principles</term> <note>original: *Principia*; the fundamental source-states of reality</note>: such as into pain and into joy, into darkness and light, into a love-fire of light, and into a painful fire of nature, as has already been mentioned above. Within this, the entire ground of the Divine Will is understood in its distinctions.

30. No creature may say that a will is given to it from the outside; rather, the will to evil and good arises within the creature. However, through external accidents of evil and good, the creature is infected, just as when an external poisonous air infects and poisons the body; so too do external things corrupt the creature's own will, so that the individual will occupies itself with evil and good.

31. And for this reason, God has given man teaching and laws, so that he should take cause from the commandment to reject evil influences, and not say: "If I do something evil, I must do it, for I am of an evil inclination." Rather, he should know that the soul’s <term>Innate Knowledge</term> <note>original: *Scientz*; from the Latin *scientia*, referring here to the soul's internal power or "science" of being</note>, which was able to occupy itself with evil, was also just as able to occupy itself with good. God is not the cause of man’s fall, nor the devil’s fall, nor did He draw them into it, insofar as He is called "God."

32. Rather, the distinction of the revealed Word of powers—after they led themselves into specific properties—those are what drew him. He stood in the <term>Balance</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect harmony or equilibrium between the various qualities of the soul</note>, but the external influences from the devil and from the dark world, as well as those in the creation within the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*; the animating spirit of the physical universe</note>, breathed into him as the image of God. They awakened the distinction within the image of God in his balance, so that the soul's eternal <term>knowledge</term> committed itself to a desire for the revelation of the properties <note>Meaning the soul chose to experience the individual, clashing qualities of nature rather than staying in the divine harmony</note>.

<summary>This page explains that while God is purely good, evil arises through the "Word of Revelation" and the separation of natural properties. It emphasizes that human beings possess an internal "science" or power to choose between good and evil, and the Fall occurred when the soul's internal balance was disturbed by external influences.</summary>
