...a godly man [finds] a godly man, and so forth. In the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note>. This is the pull of the outer life, where wrath and love stand against each other. 44. This clockwork, however, is not God, but an image after Him. 45. The inner quality of the soul is not formed within the outer constellation <note>Böhme uses "constellation" to mean the external astrological and physical influences of the stars on a person's temperament.</note>: for its foundation lies in the eternally-beginning ground, and it constellates itself either into an image of angels or of devils. 46. Here the Divine Election certainly sees what kind of angel will emerge there; however, no decree is made over it, for the door of grace stands open as long as wrath <note>original: "Grimm." In Böhme's theology, "Grimm" refers to the harsh, fiery, and wrathful aspect of nature or God that exists before it is tempered by Light/Love.</note> and love struggle for the soul. 47. The constellation indeed draws man and woman together: yet most are drawn together through their own will, 48. such as the rich with the rich; so that in this matter, it mostly follows the soul's constellation, which is mightier than the outer one. 49. But when the soul yields itself to God, and does not constellate itself into this order: then God fits it into a true equality. 50. But in the world, things go crookedly: hence also come such tyrants. 51. For the tinctures <term>Tinctures</term> <note>original: "Tincturen." The light of nature, in which God's voice has become embodied, stands in the inner ground: but the soul-fire is in the wrath of the struggle; and here is the pull into wrath and love. 54. 55. In its own nature the soul can indeed do nothing, but Christ has given it His love as a helper: whichever quality triumphs, love or wrath, that one leads the way upward. 56. If the soul yields itself to Christ, He takes hold of the will and draws it up into Himself; there, the capability originates <note>original: "urständet." A favorite word of Böhme's, meaning to take its origin or "stand" into existence.</note>. 57. The penetration of the Spirit is the call. 58. Now God indeed calls everyone: but not all are capable of the call. 59. For the soul's quality is seized in the darkness, and love finds no essence within it; <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens." A Latin term for "essence" or "spiritual substance."</note> 60. these are hardened by the wrath in the center of nature, 61. to which the inequality of both marital wills contributes, which come together only in beastly lust. 62. Now the child may not want this; but how can the sun also change a branch on a sour tree so that it becomes sweet? Shall God then change Himself for the sake of the thistle? 63. The will toward destruction indeed originates in the essence <note>original: "Ente"</note> of the creature: but the will toward a holy life originates alongside it, from God in Christ. 64. But the false essence draws evil influences into itself from the outside and darkens the image of God, 65. and if then the guest does not have a wedding garment on, he is bound by wrath: 66. whereas those who put on Christ are chosen; 67. not the Christians-in-name, but those who give ear to the voice and enter into repentance, not waiting until God overcomes the false will. 68. The soul must reach out, eat and drink Christ’s flesh and blood; 69. which is His calling and knocking, His pulling and willing. 70. But Christ also comes to the soul's aid in the mother's womb, and has established the baptismal covenant <note>original: "Tauff-Bund"</note>, 72. and calls them afterward through the preaching of His word from the mouths of the children of God. 73. ...becomes crooked and hunched: thus the soul presents itself in a shapeless figure before God, 75. where the wedding garment is then transformed into an animal-like mask, 76. and the devil constantly sows his weeds into it. 77. False branches that held the light for their wickedness, 78. are not born of God, but in their nature's own will; although in the world they become high-ranking and learned people. 79. But where the soul has grown from the will of pious parents: there good fruit comes. 80. The soul is born of the soul. 81. Christ possesses the soulful foundation in the inward center <note>original: "Centro"</note>, in the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note> of which He speaks: he who is of God, hears God's word. 82. Thus a good grain is also sown in an evil field, which sometimes brings forth fruit. 83. So it is also with man. In the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note>. Many a one is poisoned by the torment of sin <note>original: "Sünden-Quall." In Böhme's thought, "Qual" refers to both the pain of sin and the "source-quality" or vibrating energy of being.</note>, so that he has evil inclinations in him: but Christ's essence <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens." A Latin term for "being" or "essential substance."</note> draws him away; 84. although no man is without the torment of sin. 85. Just as a tree must grow up in conflict: so must man. In the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note>. The Divine essence <note>original: "Ens"</note> sows itself into the soulful essence of eternal nature, and gives itself in the conflict: for in God there is only a single will, which reveals itself to him in the conflict. 86. Therefore, the Divine power gives itself into the soul, so that it may reveal its virtue in the fiery differentiation <term>Schiedlichkeit</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit." This refers to the spiritual process where the undifferentiated unity of God separates into distinct qualities or "distinguishability."</note>. 87. The soul, however, must eat Christ's flesh, if it is to give a beautiful and powerful light; for out of the soul-fire, God's Spirit is revealed. 88. Just as the tree cannot grow without fire, light, and sun: 89. so also man cannot, unless the soul eats of the Divine essence <note>original: "Entis"</note> into its fiery property. 90. In the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note>. This happens when the soul accepts His word; then Christ pours His essential power or tincture <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." In alchemy and Böhme's theology, a tincture is a spiritual catalyst that transforms or "colors" the base nature of a substance into something noble.</note> into its fire, and transforms it into love. 91. The earthly body must help to bear Christ within itself: it is Christ's yoke, which the soul must carry. In the same place <note>original: "ibid."</note>. Thus we must now be born again: otherwise no purpose <note>original: "Vorsatz." Refers to human intention or divine decree.</note> helps. 95. The soul must lead its desire toward Christ's desire, and open wide its fiery palate; then Christ penetrates into the soul's essence: this is called believing and receiving. 96=97. In this time, it must put on Christ: just as the body from the earth shall first put on Christ essentially <note>original: "essentialiter"</note> in the resurrection. 98. Whoever puts on Christ is predestined <note>original: "versehen." In this context, it refers to being "provided for" or chosen in the divine plan.</note>. 99. The inner foundation of the soul is neither evil nor good; but in the differentiation <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit"</note> of the fire, the will separates. 2. Look at a tree, which grows from its essence and seed, in which seed the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." Thus we see how the innermost has poured itself out into an external; and just as the internal has its birthing and effect, so also does the external.

5. Primarily, three <term>principles</term> <note>original: "Principia." But if these three kinds of life are manifest in one thing at the same time, so that one sees and grasps the other within itself, then the thing is Divine, for it stands in the <term>balanced state</term> <note>original: "Temperatur." But in its unfolding, as it leads itself into distinction, it becomes a **Conflict** <note>original: "Contrarium." An opposition or tension between different qualities.</note> of properties, where one overcomes the other and casts it off from the community; therein we understand the great secrets of **God**, how it stands with the whole **Creation**.

# XV. Of Chapter 8.

10. Now we must consider the ground of the Earth: whether the three first, at a place where the kernel is sown, are similar in their worked manifest **Ens** <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens." And even if it receives **Ens** from the unlikeness, yet it stands in great danger before it can transform itself into a foreign **Ens** with its essence <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Essenz." 12. If we now observe the growth of this same tree, we first find the hidden ground of all secrecy: For first, it takes on the essence of the step-mother <note>Böhme uses "step-mother" to describe the Earth when its qualities do not naturally match the seed’s specific internal nature.</note>, and yields its own essence to the step-mother, who also accepts the essence of the seed—but not with such joy as if it were a similar essence. She indeed draws the essence of the seed to herself, in which the root arises; but there is soon a resistance in the three first <note>Referring to Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, the foundational alchemical properties Böhme believes constitute all matter.</note> of the mother, which causes the root to become gnarled and bumpy.

13. In this conflict, the fire now ignites in the essence of the seed through the sun's power, in which ignition the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhme's philosophy, this is the chaos or "Great Morning" of all potentiality from which the world was created.</note> becomes manifest in the Spirit of the World <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi." The vital, animating breath or life-force of the physical universe.</note>: the essence of the sun takes hold of this and rejoices in it, for the sun's power becomes substantial <note>That is, it takes on "substance" or physical reality.</note> within it, and draws the essence of the seed up from the root into the heights, so that a fruit may be born therein.

14. The sun gives itself with its power without distinction; it loves every fruit and plant and withdraws itself from no thing: it wants nothing else but to raise a good fruit in every herb, or whatever it may be; it accepts all, whether they be evil or good, and gives them its love-will; for it cannot do otherwise; it is no other being than what it is in itself.

15. But we must consider this rightly: how it is also a poison to the evil, and a good to the good; for in its power the growing soul arises, and in its power it also perishes; understand it thus: If the forms of nature in the three first in the root of the tree are of the same will as the mother of the earth, then the earth gives its power and sap to the root with great desire; then the sun's power rejoices therein and hastens the growth. But if the earth and root are contrary to one another, then the power and sap of the earth are withheld from the root; so when the sun then ignites the root and the tree with its light-rays, the three first—namely **Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury** <term>Sal, Sulphur, Mercurius</term>—ignite within their malice,

## XV. Regarding Chapter 8.

...and burn the <term>Essence</term> <note>original: "Ens." The fundamental inner substance or vital principle of a thing.</note> of the Sun and dry up the water, so that the trunk or the branches wither. But if the <term>three first</term> <note>Referring to Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—the foundational alchemical properties Böhme believes constitute all matter.</note> are able to possess the sap of the earth, they remain in <term>balance</term> <note>original: "Gleichheit." A state of harmony or "equality" where no single quality dominates the others.</note>, and do not awaken in conflict, but rather harmonize with the Sun’s rays of light, as we also see this in the <term>Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterio." The hidden divine order or potentiality underlying the physical world.</note> within the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi." The vital, animating breath or life-force of the physical universe.</note>, when the fiery quality winds itself upward so that it can ignite the Sun, just as a parching heat then arises, and the herbs and grass are pressed down.

16. Furthermore, we see in this figure how it happens in the growth of the branches: When the trunk rises, the conflict in nature rises with it; for when nature is ignited in its <term>proper temperament</term> <note>original: "Temperatur." The balanced state where all qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) work together harmoniously.</note>, it stands without ceasing in the <term>differentiation</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit." The capacity for nature to divide itself into various distinct qualities and forms.</note> of the Sun’s power; it always wants to cast off the malice of the three first, and they themselves also hasten in their own will. From this splitting and moving apart, the branches break out from the trunk; in winter, the cold shuts them in with their conflict: and when the spring comes, so that they can only reach the heat, they enter again into the conflict; then the conflict breaks out again into limbs and branches, as one then sees the annual growth in every tree.

17. Now, however, we must consider the internal ground regarding the sprouting of the branches: for we see that one branch grows large and bears fruit, and the other withers; we understand this now through the <term>differentiation</term> of nature by the <term>Spirit of the World</term>, as the qualities each want to take hold of a specific <term>individuality</term> <note>original: "Eigenheit." A self-centered or singular nature that has broken away from the communal whole.</note> in the <term>essence</term> of the tree, and thus abandon the balance. This individuality, having now emerged from the balance in its **pride** <note>original: "Hoffart." In Böhme's view, this is the root of evil—the desire of a part to be greater than the whole.</note> over the others through the power of fire, does not want to remain within the Sun’s will in the <term>proper temperament</term> as it draws the same up into itself; it suffocates once it has broken out of the trunk. For that same <term>active impulse</term> <note>original: "Scienz." From the Latin *scientia*, here referring to the innate "consciousness" or "driving knowledge" within a specific natural quality.</note> within that same quality has introduced itself into its own will, and in its pride wants to break out sooner than the others in the balance, yet it does not have enough strength. When a strong <term>constellation</term> <note>original: "Constellation." The external influence of the stars and planets that "tests" the internal qualities of living things.</note> of the stars then penetrates these proud branches from the outside, and sifts and tests them to see if they are from the balance, they become poisoned and wither, for they are **renegade branches**; the Sun’s heat in the Spirit of the World also dries them out.

### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.

18. The other branches, however, come from the <term>balanced temperament</term> <note>original: "Temperatur." In Böhme’s thought, this is the state where the conflicting qualities of nature (hot, cold, etc.) are in perfect harmony.</note>, and they emerge from the powerful drawing-out of the Sun, where the Sun rejoices in the qualities, balances them, and draws itself out within them: the Sun raises these same branches in its power, for the qualities stand within its will. Furthermore, we see how the qualities of nature in the branches, as they grow out, are corrupted by external accidents—such as by the stars or by the impure air—where the Sun cannot reach them with its rays, so that they become gnarled, crooked, and hunched; through this, many a branch becomes stunted and is cast off, so that it withers.

19. And just as it happens with the <term>original state</term> <note>original: "Urstand." The fundamental, primordial beginning or "ground" of a thing's existence.</note> and growth of the tree, so it also happens with the original state and growth of man. Although man is higher in the quality of nature and light than the plants of the earth, it is nonetheless all within one order; for it proceeds from a single Ground, namely through the spoken Word of **God**, wherein the Divine Speaking works together in the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterio Magno." The hidden, chaotic potentiality or "womb" of the universe from which all things are called into being.</note>. The only difference is that man, in the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ente." The inner substance or being.</note> of his body, is one degree higher than the earth and its fruit; and in his soul, he is even higher than the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." The animating, vital life-force of the physical universe.</note>. But otherwise, everything in its origin proceeds from one Ground, though it divides itself and takes hold in distinct beginnings within the <term>Creation</term> <note>original: "Creation." The act of making the physical world.</note>.

20. **God’s** sole purpose is his eternally-speaking Word, which He speaks through Wisdom from his power in <term>Active Knowledge</term> <note>original: "Scienz." Not science in the modern sense, but a formative, "drawing" power or the internal root of a thing's will.</note>, into <term>differentiation</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit." The capacity for the unified divine power to distinguish itself into various forms and qualities.</note> for his own revelation. He has no other purpose within Himself, and it cannot be that He has more purposes; for if that were so, there would have to be something before Him from which He took the cause for a purpose.

21. Thus, the speaking of his power toward his self-revelation is the only Divine purpose—not an initial purpose, but a generative one; and the purpose of the Word is the <term>Active Knowledge</term> of the differentiation and form-giving of the single Divine power. This differentiation and form-giving is what the one **God** in his **Trinity** has from eternity, spoken through the Word into a beginning, as into an <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens." The vital, fundamental substance of a thing.</note> of all the qualities of differentiation, where all differentiations lie within one another. And this "Spoken Thing" is...

### XV. Regarding Chapter 8.

...the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhme’s cosmogony, this is the "chaos" or the "womb" containing all possibilities of existence before they are spoken into being.</note>, and a true, singular purpose of the Word.

**22.** The Word desires nothing more than to reveal its holy power through <term>distinction</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit." This refers to the capacity for unity to divide into specific, individual parts or qualities so they can be perceived.</note>; and in the Word, the Godhead becomes manifest in distinction through fire and light. Thus, the two—the Word and the Great Mystery—are within one another like soul and body. For the Great Mystery is the Word's essential substance, in which and through which the invisible **God** is revealed in His Trinity, and is revealed from eternity to eternity. For that which the Word is in power and sound, the Great Mystery is as a being; it is the eternal, substantial Word of **God**.

**23.** Understand us correctly: the spiritual, sounding Word is the Divine Understanding. Through the Great Mystery—as the eternal essence of the Word—the Understanding has spoken itself into a "formality" <note>a structured, perceptible form</note>, as a beginning and into time. The Spirit who speaks eternally has made manifest the <term>distinction</term> that lies within the Great Mystery as an active <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ente," from the Latin "Ens." It refers to the internal "being" or "substance" of a thing.</note>, so that it may be a surging, grasping, birthing life. And this very thing is now the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." The vital, animating soul of the physical universe.</note>; its activity is the life of all creatures, its substance consists of the four elements, and the "science" <note>original: "Scienz," meaning the inherent knowledge or structural logic</note> of distinction within the Spirit of the World is the constellations of the stars, in which all growing life is rooted.

**24.** At the beginning of its distinction, through the speaking of the Word of the Godhead, this eternal Great Mystery divided itself into a subtle essence and a coarse, "clotted" essence. The subtle essence is the stars, acting as a <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: "Quinta Essentia." In alchemy, the purest, most concentrated element that transcends the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note>, and the coarse, clotted essence is the <term>residue</term> <note>original: "Abwerfung," literally the "throwing-off" or "dross."</note>, which is the earth, stones, and metals. This casting off occurred so that a purity—a visible, sensory life—might exist within the Spirit of the World. This residue also possesses a twofold quality: a subtle one derived from the Light's power in the Word, and a coarse one according to the "enclosure of darkness" in the <term>original state</term> <note>original: "Urstand." The fundamental, primordial ground of nature.</note> leading toward fire. By the coarse quality, we understand the physical earth; by the subtle quality, we understand the power within the essence of the earth, from which herbs, trees, and metals grow in their various distinctions. Furthermore, all flesh comes from this subtle essence of the earth. Everything that exists solely within time and stands within the life of the Spirit of the World has its body from the essence of this subtle earth.

<summary>Böhme explains the relationship between the Divine Word and the "Great Mystery," detailing how the spiritual Word manifests as the "Spirit of the World" and differentiates into the stars and the physical elements of the earth.</summary>

### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.

**25.** This <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." The vital, animating soul of the physical universe that bridges the gap between the spiritual and the material.</note>, with the constellations of its <term>science</term> <note>original: "Scienz." For Böhme, this is not "science" in the modern sense, but an inherent, active "knowing-power" or sensory intelligence within nature.</note>, and with the subtle bodies of fire, water, and air—along with its stability in the earth and all that is within it—is now the "spoken" life and being. It arises out of the inner, eternal Mystery, as if from the inner, essential Word of **God**. This eternal Word of **God** works and dwells in the inner ground in holy power; and at the beginning of this world, through the inner Mystery, it "expressed" itself into an outer Mystery. From that same outer Mystery, the entire creation of the external world proceeded, and it remains enclosed within it as if in its mother’s womb. In this womb, the eternal Word, using the <term>science of distinction</term> <note>original: "Scienz der Schiedlichkeit." The process by which the unified power of God divides into many specific, individual qualities.</note>, has introduced itself from these powers into a figurative, perceptible life.

**26.** This outer Mystery of the formed Word is now enclosed within a wheel—much like a clockwork—containing its own birthing life, wherein the properties are in a struggle for <term>primacy</term> <note>original: "Primat." The constant shifting of which natural force is dominant at any given moment.</note>. Soon one property is on top, then quickly the second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, and seventh. This is how the seven properties and their outcomes are to be understood: for soon the spirit triumphs in the fire, from which heat arises; soon in the water, from which it rains; soon in the air, from which it rises; soon in the earthiness, from which cold originates. What one property builds, another breaks; one property gives, another blocks the giving so that it perishes; one gives a good <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens." The internal spiritual "being" or "substance" of a thing.</note> and will, while another adds evil into it and hinders the good, so that one may be revealed within the other.

**27.** Into this outer Mystery of properties—in which the distinction of the "spoken" Word is understood—**God** has now spoken the <term>Light of Nature</term> <note>A traditional hermetic concept referring to the divine wisdom embedded within the physical world, which allows humans to understand the cosmos through observation.</note> from the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." The primordial "womb" of all possibilities.</note>, through and out of the power of the eternal light. Thus, in every "evil" essence, there lies within it a good ground, acting as a good power from the holy Word, and no evil exists entirely without the good.

**28.** Furthermore, **God** has given the sun as an active life into the properties of the outer world, so that all things may find themselves within it and be brought into an "equality of the struggle," wherein they may grow and bear fruit. And although the Light of Nature, out of Divine power, works within all things, and the sun also works from without in

<meta>catchword: all</meta>

<summary>Böhme describes the physical universe as a "clockwork" or "wheel" of seven properties that emerged when God's inner Word was spoken into an outer form. He emphasizes that despite the chaotic struggle of these natural forces, a "Light of Nature" and the "Sun" provide a balancing goodness and life-giving power to all things.</summary>

### XV. Regarding Chapter 8.

...gives itself and presses into all living and growing things; yet the fiery quality in the <term>wrath</term> <note>original: "Grimme." In Böhme’s cosmology, this is the harsh, consuming, and contracting force of nature that exists when it is not tempered by divine light.</note> is so strong, and these qualities impress themselves so hard through the power of darkness, that many creatures and plants must live in bitterness; for the hunger in the dark <term>impression</term> <note>original: "Impression." The process by which spiritual forces condense or "press" themselves into material, physical form.</note> is so strong that it holds all creatures in its power.

**29.** This active being in the qualities of light and darkness, which now encompasses the entire creation, is the sole purpose of **God’s** Word: namely, that He might give birth to life and creatures, and bring the expressed Word into a figurative form—so that every power in the <term>science of distinction</term> <note>original: "Scientz der Schiedlichkeit." The inherent "knowing-power" or potential within nature that allows the unified divine power to divide into specific, individual forms.</note> might stand as a life and an image, according to both the light-power of the Holy Word and the qualities of the fire-power. The light, however, is given to all things for a <term>balanced temperature</term> <note>original: "Temperatur." A state of harmony where all competing natural forces are perfectly balanced.</note>; not that the light alone shines upon the being from the outside, but it is co-active within every <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Enti." The inner, essential being of a thing.</note> in everything that lives and grows.

**30.** Therefore, no creature has cause to complain against its **Creator** that He created it for evil. It is only the <term>wrath</term> within nature that hardens a thing and blocks the power of the light; furthermore, it is prevented by the curse, because the holy <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." A vital spiritual essence or "dye" that transmits the qualities of the divine light into the material world.</note> of the Holy Ground of the speaking Word—dwelling in the eternal light—has withdrawn back into itself (on account of the vanity of the devil, as well as of man and the creatures). It gives itself only to that which leads itself into an image of the light-power, and it will not work with the <term>science</term> that gives itself over to the wrath of darkness. The reason for this is that the darkness would otherwise seize the holy power and lead it into its own malice. Thus it is said according to the Scripture in *Psalm 18:26-27*: <note>original: "Bey den Verkehrten bist du verkehrt, und bey den Heiligen bist du heilig."</note> **With the perverse you are perverse, and with the holy you are holy.** Just as the sun must endure having the thistle swallow its good essence into its own prickly nature and use it for its thorns, so the highest <term>tincture</term> will not give itself into the falseness of that <term>science</term> where the eternal, bottomless will has turned itself into an image of the dark world's quality.

**31.** The second purpose of **God**, through the out-speaking Word of **God** by which **God** wished to reveal Himself through the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." The primordial chaos or "mother" of all things from which the universe was birthed.</note>, is the most precious Holy Name **JE-**

He uses the metaphor of a thistle using sunlight to grow thorns to explain how a holy power can be misused by a perverse nature.</summary>

# Chapter 8. Grace-Election.

...sus: <note>The syllable "sus" completes the name "Christus" or "Jesus" from the preceding page.</note> after man had turned himself away from GOD and toward the creaturely, he had lost GOD’s voice; therefore GOD spoke it back into him in grace through the woman’s seed, with the in-imagined name JESUS, as a second purpose originating from the Divine Ground.

**32.** The first purpose regarding nature and the creature arises from the Father’s property <note>In Böhme’s thought, the "Father" represents the raw, fiery power of nature and justice, while the "Son" represents light and love.</note>: the second purpose—to redeem nature and the creature from the curse and the torment—is the name JESUS, acting as the highest **tincture** <term>tincture: an alchemical term for a spiritual essence that can transform and "color" a substance, turning leaden nature into spiritual gold</term> of Divine power, to manifest that same power through the formed, spoken Word in the quality of the Good, which is currently held captive within the Evil.

**33.** This name JESUS, as the purpose of His love, GOD spoke into the mother of all humanity <note>Referring to Eve, the "mother of all living."</note> and incorporated it as a living power into an eternal covenant. He fulfilled that same covenant by introducing the Divine **Being** <note>original: "Entis." A Latin term Böhme uses to describe the essential, spiritual substance of God.</note> into human nature; so that, just as all people now bring the curse and corruption with them into the world—wherein they are all children of God’s wrath and are enclosed under the curse—so too they all bring the Covenant of Grace in the embodied name JESUS into the world. GOD has confirmed this covenant in Christ with the seal of infant baptism, and among the ancients with the circumcision of the foreskin.

**34.** Know then, that GOD has revealed no other purpose through His Word than the ground of Creation, which is the nature of **distinction** <term>distinction: (Schiedlichkeit) the capacity for the one divine light to split into different individual qualities, colors, and beings</term>; within this, the purposes toward evil originate when the **science** <term>science: (Scienz) not modern science, but an inherent, creative "knowing-urge" or "root-will" within a being</term> of the foundational will leads one part into the fiery distinction, one part into the light-power, another part into the fiery property of torment, and a third part into the **fantasy** <note>original: "Phantasey." For Böhme, this is a false or distorted imagination that leads away from God's reality into self-centered pride.</note>—according to fire, light, and darkness—as into the property of pride, just as Lucifer and Adam have done. But whatever is distinguished into the power of the light is Good; and whatever remains standing in the **temperature** <term>temperature: (Temperatur) the state of perfect harmony and balance where no single quality (like anger or heat) dominates the others</term> within the fiery distinction, to that the highest tincture of powers is assigned. To the others, however, in their state of distinction, the tincture of the sun and of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spir. Mundi." The breath or soul of the physical universe that governs natural life.</note> is assigned.

# XV. Of Chapter 8.

...and evil, to carry out and demonstrate what the **purpose of God** is—as well as the **drawing of the Father** <note>original: "Zug des Vaters." A reference to the biblical concept in John 6:44 where God "draws" the soul toward the divine.</note> in both good and evil—and what the **election** over humanity is, and subsequently to compare it with the sayings of Scripture.

**36.** Man was brought into an image from the **speaking and spoken Word** <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "speaking Word" is God’s active creativity, while the "spoken Word" is the manifested creation.</note> out of the purpose of the eternal and temporal essence’s beginning, in which the "speaking Word" of differentiability itself lies within. For according to the outer body, he is a **being** <term>being: original "Ens," referring here to the essential substance or "stuff" of a thing</term> of the four elements; and according to the outer life, he is a being of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." This refers to the animating soul of the physical universe in Renaissance philosophy.</note>. According to the inner body, he is a being of the eternal Word of God, as the highest **Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterii."</note> of God’s essential powers. According to the inner spirit, however, he exists in two properties: first, the creaturely soul is from the Father’s nature, out of the eternal differentiation of God’s Word into light and darkness; this property is the creaturely soul’s own possession, originating from the ground of the eternal will. The other property is the truly Divine one, in the power of the light—which is **Christ**, in whom the name **JESUS** has been revealed. This is the true eternal purpose of God before the foundation of the world, when the soul was not yet a creature, but only a being in the **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterio Magno." Böhme’s term for the vast, unmanifested potential of God.</note>.

**37.** This second property was manifest in man in the beginning before sin, in **JEHOVAH**. But when the soul broke away from it and turned toward **Creation**, the creaturely soul became silent to God; there, the purpose in the holy **Jesus** stepped forward as a **gift of grace** and entered into the light of life. This gift of grace is now not the creaturely soul’s own possession: she <note>The soul (die Seele) is grammatically feminine in German and often personified as a bride in this period.</note> does not have it by natural right, nor does she ever receive it by natural right; rather, it stands within the soul in its own **center** <note>original: "Centro."</note> and calls to the soul, offering itself to manifest within her.

**38.** The soul should stand still from the imagery of earthly creatures and not introduce earthly **being** into her **fire-life**—from which a false light arises. If she does this, then this Divine purpose, in the highest **tincture** <term>tincture: an alchemical term for a spiritual essence that can transform and "color" a substance, turning the "lead" of human nature into spiritual gold</term>, wishes to manifest itself out of the holy **love-fire** with the holy light, in the same way that a fire glows through iron so that the iron appears to be pure fire. Thus also the love-fire of this purpose of the gift of grace transforms the soul into its own property...

### Chapter 8. Divine Election.

...property, and yet the soul retains its own nature, just as iron in the fire retains its nature. <note>Böhme frequently uses the "iron in the fire" metaphor to explain how the divine light can penetrate and transform a human being without destroying their individual essence.</note>

**39.** Every child born from the seed of man and woman has this gift of grace standing within its innermost ground, facing the light of life; it offers itself to every soul and extends its longing toward the soul throughout the entirety of a person's life, calling out: "Come here to me, and depart from the earthly imagery in the **wrath** <note>original: "Grimme." In Böhme’s theology, this refers to the harsh, severe quality of nature when it is not tempered by God's light.</note> and from the **fantasy**." <note>original: "Phantasey." This refers to the illusory or "imaginary" world of self-centered desires created after the Fall.</note>

**40.** Conversely, in every soul, as soon as its life begins, there also stands the awakened **grim wrath of God** in the essence of **differentiability** <term>differentiability: original "Schiedlichkeit," the process by which the unified divine power divides into specific, distinct, and often conflicting forms</term>, within which also lies the introduced **serpent's venom**, along with the devil's desire.

**41.** Thirdly, every seed of the body, according to the outer world, stands under the power of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." The animating force of the physical universe, often associated with the influence of the stars.</note>, in the **constellation**: as the great clockwork stands in its configuration at that time, so the Spirit of the World gives the person a similar configuration in the property of their outer life. It fashions a certain **animal** into the outer life-property, for the spirit of the outer world from the elements can give nothing other than an animal; and such an animal arises from the fact that the entire creation lies within man, and that through the Fall he has led himself out of the **balanced temperament** <note>original: "Temperatur." This refers to the original state of harmony where all qualities were perfectly balanced; the Fall broke this balance.</note> into earthly desire and imagery, so that the Spirit of the World with its differentiability has become manifest within him.

**42.** And so, it always separates itself into such a figure at the beginning of every child's life; as the stars stand in their wheel, it makes such an image into the property from the **slime of the earth** <note>original: "Limo der Erden." Derived from the Latin "limus," referring to the biblical "dust of the ground" from which Adam was formed.</note>, as in the four elements. Thus, from the mother's womb, according to the outer man, one person is of the nature of an evil, venomous serpent, another a wolf, a dog, a toad, a cunning fox, a proud lion, a filthy sow, a vain peacock, or even a spirited horse, or other good tame animals—all according to the figure in the Spirit of the World. Likewise, that same constellation, out of the outer purpose of the **formed Word** <note>The "formed Word" refers to the physical manifestation of God's creative speech in the material universe.</note>, imparts to one person good reason and senses, along with honor and worldly fortune, and to another misery, misfortune, folly, malice, and a wicked will toward all kinds of vices. ### XV. Regarding Chapter 8.

...and evil, to carry out and demonstrate what God’s purpose is—specifically, the "drawing" of the Father in both good and evil—as well as what the choice concerning mankind may be, and thereafter to compare it with the passages of Scripture.

**36.** Man was brought from the purpose of the eternal and temporal essence's beginning into an image formed from the speaking and the spoken Word, within which the "speaking Word" of **differentiability** <term>differentiability: original "Schiedlichkeit," the capacity of the unified divine power to divide into distinct, individual, and diverse forms</term> itself resides. For according to his outer body, man is an **entity** <term>entity: original "Ens," a fundamental substance or "being-ness"</term> of the four elements; and according to his outer life, he is an entity of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." According to the inner body, however, he is an entity of the eternal Word of God, serving as the highest **mystery** <note>original: "Mysterii." The hidden, essential spiritual power of God.</note> of God’s essential forces. Yet according to the inner spirit, he exists in two properties: first, the creaturely soul is from the Father’s nature, arising from the eternal separation of the Word of God into light and darkness. This property is the creaturely soul’s own "selfhood," originating from the ground of the eternal will. This is the true eternal purpose of God from before the foundation of the world, when the soul was not yet a created being, but only an essence within the **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterio Magno." The vast, unmanifested potential or "womb" of the universe from which all things were called into being.</note>.

But when the soul broke away from it and turned toward the Creation, the creaturely soul became "mute" to God. This gift of grace is not the creaturely soul’s own property by natural right; she does not possess it by nature, nor will she ever receive it as a natural right. Instead, it stands within the soul in its own **center** <note>original: "Centro." A spiritual focal point or "hub" within the soul's internal structure.</note>, calling to the soul and offering itself, desiring to reveal itself within her.

**38.** The soul must stand still and turn away from the **imagery** <note>original: "Bildlichkeit." The soul's tendency to form mental "images" or attachments to worldly, material things.</note> of earthly creatures, and must not introduce earthly essence into its "fire-life" (from which a false light arises). If it does this, then this Divine purpose—in the highest **tincture** <term>tincture: original "Tinctur," an alchemical term for a spiritual or medicinal agent that can permeate and transform the inner quality of a substance</term>—will reveal itself from the holy fire of love along with the holy light. ### Chapter 8: Election by Grace

...property, and yet the soul retains its own nature, just as iron in the fire retains its nature.

**39.** Every child, born from the seed of man and woman, has this **gift of grace** <note>original: "Gnaden-Geschencke"</note> within its innermost ground, standing opposite the light of life. It offers itself to every soul and reaches out its desire toward the soul throughout the entire span of a person's life, calling to it: "Come here to me, and depart from the earthly imagery in the **wrath** <note>original: "Grimm." In Böhme’s thought, this refers to the harsh, fiery, and judgmental quality of nature when it is separated from God’s light.</note> and from the **fantasy**." <note>original: "Phantasey." Here, it refers to the deceptive, shifting images of the fallen world that distract the soul from God.</note>

**40.** On the other hand, in every soul, as soon as its life begins, there also stands the awakened **fierce wrath of God** in the essence of **differentiability** <term>differentiability: original "Schiedlichkeit," the capacity of the unified divine power to divide into distinct, individual, and diverse forms</term>, within which also lies the introduced **serpent-venom** <note>A reference to the "poison" introduced into human nature by the Fall in the Garden of Eden.</note>, along with the devil’s desire.

**41.** Thirdly, every seed of the body, according to the outer world, stands under the power of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi." The animating spirit of the physical universe and the stars.</note> and the **constellation** <note>original: "Constellation." The astrological arrangement of the stars and planets at the moment of birth.</note>. Just as the great **clockwork** <note>original: "Uhrwerck." Böhme uses this as a metaphor for the mechanical, predetermined movements of the heavens.</note> stands in its arrangement at that time, the Spirit of the World gives the person such a figure in the property of their outer life. It models the person into the property of an **animal** <note>original: "Thier." Böhme believes the outer, "astral" man takes on the characteristics of various animals based on cosmic influences.</note>; for the Spirit of the outer world, drawn from the elements, can give nothing other than an animal. And such an animal arises because the entire **Creation** lies within man, and because through the Fall he has led himself out of the **temperament** <note>The state of perfect balance between all spiritual and physical forces.</note> into earthly desire and imagery, so that the Spirit of the World has become manifest in him with its differentiability.

**42.** And thus it still divides itself even now, at the beginning of every child's life, into such a figure. As the stars stand in their wheel, so the Spirit makes an image in that property from the **slime of the earth** <note>original: "Limo der Erden." Referring to the "dust of the ground" from Genesis, the physical material of the body.</note>, that is, into the four elements. Because of this, many a person from their mother’s womb—according to the outer man—is of the nature of an evil, poisonous snake, a wolf, a dog, a toad, a cunning fox, a proud lion, a filthy sow, a haughty peacock, or even a spirited horse, or other good, tame animals, all according to the figure in the Spirit of the World. Likewise, that same constellation, from the outer purpose of the **formed Word** <note>The physical world as a "spoken" or "expressed" manifestation of God's creative power.</note>, instills in many a person good reason and senses, along with honor and worldly luck; and in others, it instills misery, misfortune, folly, malice, roguery, and a bad will toward all kinds of vices, which many a person—if they do not constantly [overcome] the implanted earthly animal—

...kills that, and breaks the evil will with the **divine gift of grace** <note>original: "Göttlichen Gnaden-Geschencke"</note>, comes into the hands of the executioner.

**43.** Now look, O Man, this is brought to you by the outer purpose of the **formed and spoken Word** <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Spoken Word" refers to the entire created universe as an expression of God’s inner "Speaking."</note>, wherein both good and evil lie within, since the **science** <term>science: original "Scienz," referring to the inherent knowledge or "blueprint" within a seed or essence</term> of the seed divides itself into a specific property at the beginning of life. And within this lies the **pull** <note>original: "Zug." Böhme uses this to describe a spiritual magnetism or attraction.</note> from the Father’s property toward either evil or good: and into whatever **being** <term>being: original "Ens," a technical term for the fundamental essence or substance of a thing</term> life has **constellated** <note>original: "constelliret." This refers to the specific alignment of spiritual and astral forces at the moment of a thing's beginning.</note> itself, that same constellation pulls itself toward its own likeness. Like always desires to dwell with and in like: just as a pious man gladly dwells with the pious, and a mocker with mockers, a thief with thieves, a glutton, a drunkard, a gambler, a fornicator, and the like also with their own kind; toward this, his nature pulls him out of the property of the **wrath of God** <note>original: "Zornes GOttes"</note>. Thus, the actual sins of the parents also enter into the property, for every child is born from the seed of the parents; whatever the parents are, so too is the child. Nevertheless, the constellation often changes with force and compels it into its power, provided it is strong.

**44.** Now see, that is the pull of the outer life, where God says: *Whomever I harden, I harden.* In this way, the outer man is hardened, and also drawn as a pious and sensible person toward humility or pride. That is now God’s purpose according to His **wrath**, which man has awakened in himself, for he is the outer, birthing Word of God, through which God acts with the outer creature as He grasps it in His **clockwork** <term>clockwork: original "Uhrwerck," a metaphor for the mechanical, predetermined system of the physical cosmos and nature</term>. Through this clockwork, He also reveals His glory according to both fire and light, according to understanding and folly, so that one might be revealed in the other, and it may be known what is good.

**45.** Now, however, this clockwork of the **spoken word** <note>original: "ausgesprochenen Worts"</note> is not God Himself; it is only an image after Him, namely the outward substantial word into which He has enclosed the **Creation** <note>original: "Creation"</note> and out of which He has created. For no creature can come from the purely divine property, because that property has no ground or beginning; thus, the creature cannot form itself into any beginning other than through the Word of powers, through **differentiability** <term>differentiability: original "Schiedlichkeit," the capacity of unity to divide into diverse forms</term>, and out of the differentiability of the Speaking, since the Speaking must introduce itself into **Nature** <note>original: "Natur"</note>, otherwise the Word would not be revealed.

# Cap. 8. Election of Grace.

H 2

...first created **constellation** <note>original: "Constellation." In Böhme's thought, this is not just the stars in the sky, but the spiritual alignment of forces within a being.</note>, in the eternal original ground, which is not formed within the outer animal constellation: For the soul’s **science** <term>science: original "Scienz," referring to the inherent knowledge, active blueprint, or potentiality within the soul's essence</term> has one single form, like a magical **fire-wellspring** <note>original: "Feuer-Quall." An archaic term describing the soul as a bubbling, agitated source of life-force.</note>, and separates itself in life into the figure of the body; within this now lies the ground of eternal nature, and it is capable of both good and evil: for it is the cause of both fire and light, yet it lies hard and heavily imprisoned in sins. For here lie the **inherited sins** <note>original: "Erbsünden," usually translated as "original sin," but here emphasizing the generational inheritance.</note> in the center of nature, where the devil has gained a seat; likewise, here now lie the inherited sins from parents and grandparents, like an evil poison, of which God says He would punish them in the children unto the third and fourth generation; also herein lie the good deeds of parents and God’s blessing, which pass over to the children. **Exodus 20: 5, 6**. These properties now also **constellate** <note>align themselves spiritually</note> into a figure according to their kind; thus the soul figures itself either into an image of the angels or the devils.

**47.** And here now lies the heavy ground where the **Election** <note>original: "Wahl," the choice or predestination</note> of God sees what kind of angel will emerge there; yet no final decree is made upon it: for the **gift of grace** <note>original: "Gnaden-Geschenck"</note> stands in the inner ground and appropriates itself to the center of the **science** <term>science: the soul's internal blueprint</term> of the **Abyss** <term>Abyss: original "Ungrundes," the Groundless or the Nothingness from which the divine manifests</term> of the soul, as the will of the eternal Father. Here Christ pleads for the poor imprisoned soul, as the Scripture says, for the soul lies in the bonds of God’s wrath and is hardened in its sins; here life draws itself through death and sifts to see if any good little spark might be within it that is capable of receiving divine power, so that it might be drawn. For just as Christ wills to be manifest, so the **fury of nature** <note>original: "Grimm der Natur," the harsh, wrathful aspect of the natural world</note> also wills to be manifest: so these two purposes stand in the **formed word** <note>the created universe as the physical expression of God's "speaking"</note> in a struggle over the human being, as over the image of God; the kingdom of grace in the light wants to possess that and manifest itself within it: so too the kingdom of nature, in the fury of the fire, wants to have it in the **separability** <term>separability: original "Schiedlichkeit," the capacity of nature to divide into distinct, conflicting properties</term> of nature and manifest itself within it; and both of these lie in the formed word, as the Father’s property in the fury, and the Son’s love-property in the light.

...desires to have? Answer: Yes, rightly so, but through His **purpose** <note>original: "Vorsatz," referring to God's divine plan or intentional resolution.</note>, which He has set into a governance within the **Word** <note>The "Word" or "Logos" is the creative power of God that structures the universe.</note> through the **great clockwork of nature** <note>original: "grosse Uhrwerck der Natur." Böhme uses this mechanical metaphor to describe the complex, lawful, and interconnected systems of the physical and spiritual world.</note>. The **constellations** <note>original: "Constellationes." In Böhme's thought, these are not just stars, but the spiritual and energetic alignments of forces that influence a person's character and destiny.</note> in the clockwork draw them together, but most people are drawn together by their **own will**, for the human will—which originates from the eternal ground—**constellates itself** <note>forms its own internal alignment of desires and qualities</note>, whereby the outer constellation of the stars is broken.

**49.** We see this in how the rich align themselves with the rich, and likewise the noble with the noble; otherwise, if the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spirit of the World: original "Spiritui Mundi," the animating soul or vital force of the physical universe and its astrological influences</term> did not have its alignment broken, many a poor servant-maid would be joined to a nobleman, as they might outwardly align with one another in the Spirit of the World. But this self-made human and soulful alignment, coming from that high ground, is more powerful than the constellation in the Spirit of the World: therefore, things often and mostly follow the alignment of the soul, which surpasses the outer world in power and majesty, just as it depends on the sower where he sows his grain, even if another field might be better suited for it.

**50.** But if the soul yields its will to God, and does not align itself within this worldly order, but instead commits itself to the **purpose of God**, then the male and female **tinctures** <term>tincture: original "Tinctur," the spiritual essence or "color" of life-force that gives a being its specific quality and vitality</term> are enclosed within the Word, and aligned in the right Divine order—according to the soul in the **Great Mystery** <term>Great Mystery: original "Mysterio Magno," the divine source or "chaos" from which all creation proceeds and into which it returns</term>, and according to the body in the Spirit of the World. There, a love is awakened within the person according to the true equality of their nature: and if the person then follows that love, and does not look upon wealth, nobility, or beauty and outward skill, then his own constellation, which he has by nature, receives its true and right equality. It is then like a field that is matched and pleasing to the grain: thus, strife does not so easily or quickly arise in the **fruit** <note>the children or the outcome of the union</note>, for they stand together in equality, and there the inner and outer **Sun** <note>the divine spiritual light and the natural light/vitality</note> can better align themselves within the fruit.

**51.** But how it goes in the world is plainly seen; nature brings things together and binds them, so that often two young people align themselves in the highest love (which happens out of the great purpose of the true constellation in the Spirit of the World, within the **formed Word** <note>the Word as it manifests in physical creation</note>). This is then broken by parents and friends for the sake of poverty or status; so then speaks

### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.

God said to Noah: Humans will not let my Spirit guide them; instead, they take wives and sleep with the daughters of men according to their beauty (Genesis 6:2–3), wealth, and nobility. All of this is merely human vanity <note>original: "Gedichte," literally "poems" or "fictions," implying something fabricated by human imagination rather than divine will.</note>. From these unions come powerful men and tyrants, against whom God sets the Deluge of His wrath into their self-made **constellation** <term>Constellation: The internal and external alignment of spiritual and celestial forces that shape a person's destiny.</term>, hardening their own wills. For many people are forced and coupled together because of status or wealth, only to become enemies later, wishing for death and separation in their souls for as long as they live.

52. These couples are meant to bring their **tinctures** <term>Tincture: A spiritual quality or "essence" that gives life its specific character and color.</term> within the seed into a **conjunction** <note>original: "Conjunction," a union or joining together.</note> to form the human life of a child. The woman is the field, and the man sows the grain. Now, when the two **tinctures** should enter into one another and be transformed into one—as in the female and male seed where the **essence** <term>Essence: original "Ens," the fundamental spiritual substance or being of a thing.</term> should lead itself into a joyful equality—they are instead unequal in their will. In this case, the field receives a "step-son" along with the grain; it must indeed accept the grain, for it forces itself in and draws the **essence** out of the field into itself, but the field does not give its good will. Thus, the **essence** of the seed must seek its equality in the female seed, but finds it locked too deeply within the **constellation**, making it difficult to reach. From this arises infertility and a "disgust" within nature. And even if the grain takes root in the female **tincture** of the field, the outer **constellation** within the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spirit of the World: original "Spiritu Mundi," the animating force of the physical universe.</term>—in the true order of the formed, spoken Word—is hostile to it. For it does not stand in a joyful form within the great clockwork of nature; instead, it immediately carries its hostile rays from the **Great Turmoil** <term>Great Turmoil: original "Turba Magna," the disruptive force of divine wrath that breaks apart natural forms.</term> into the formation of the creature, whereby much fruit perishes before it even receives life.

53. I leave it to the reader's reason to contemplate what kind of effect this has within the center of nature for the sake of life, and how nature hardens itself in its own resistance. One can well imagine what kind of soulful fire it awakens and gives birth to within itself, of which the Scripture says: "God’s wrath hardens them so that they do not come to the true, holy Light." For

### XV. Concerning Chapter 8.

53. Whatever the quality of the soul-fire is, such a light also originates from it; and life now exists within this soul-light. Therefore, the Scripture says: *with the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse* <note>original: "Ps. 18: 26. 27." This refers to Psalm 18:25–26 in modern numbering.</note>. Whatever kind of people they are, they will perceive God in that same way.

54. The Light of Nature—in which the voice of God in Paradise, through the seed of the woman, has re-embodied itself (and in which **Christ** is conceived and born)—now stands in the innermost ground. It is meant to reveal itself through the ignited soul-fire and to enter into and work within the creature. The soul should now stand still before the Spirit of **Christ**, so that He may work within it; but the soul (meaning the soul’s property, in which the soul-fire burns and becomes living) is caught in the fury of the conflict.

55. Here now is the pull of wrath, and also the pull of **Christ** through the Light of Nature. Here it is rightly said: wherever the **science** <term>Science: original "Scientz," referring here to the active, directed consciousness or "will-knowledge" of the soul.</term> of the groundless <note>original: "ungründlichen," referring to the *Ungrund*, the fathomless or "bottomless" abyss of the divine will before creation.</note> will turns itself from the ground of eternal nature into the soul’s property, and gives itself as a servant in obedience, it becomes that thing's servant—either to the wrath of God in the fury of eternal nature, or to the life of **Christ** in grace, as Saint Paul says in *Romans 6:16*.

56. So Reason speaks: "The soul’s **essence** <term>Essence: original "Essenz," the spiritual substance of the soul.</term> cannot do this; it must suffer whatever God does with it, for it is corrupted and inclined toward wrath." **Answer:** Yes, it cannot do it by its own power; but **Christ**, when He took on the soul’s property, shattered the wrath and the **turmoil** <term>Turmoil: original "Turbam," the disruptive, chaotic force of wrath that awakens in the soul when it turns away from God.</term> of the false will with His love. He introduced His love into the created Word and gave it to the soul’s **being** <term>Being: original "Enti," the fundamental spiritual existence of a person.</term> as a helper. It depends entirely on which property outweighs the other: the light-fire or the wrath-fire, God’s love or His wrath. For the **essence** <term>Essence: original "Ens."</term> of the soul does not yet have understanding, but it possesses the ground of the will from the groundless eternal will, for the purpose of birthing God’s dwelling-place—where the Father’s groundless will gives birth to the Son as His power.

57. The soul’s **essence** stands within this groundless will and desires to have God within it. It is meant to give birth to divine power, even though it cannot do so by its own ability after its Fall. Therefore, He has embodied the kingdom of His grace within it and revealed it in the name **JESUS**; so if...

->### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.<-

->H 4<-

Now, when the fathomless will of the soul <note>original: "seelische ungründliche Wille," referring to the will arising from the *Ungrund*, the bottomless abyss of the divine will before creation.</note> makes itself home to the Spirit of **Christ** in the innermost ground, then **Christ** seizes it and draws it up into Himself. There, the "ability" or "potential" originates, for the essence of wrath is shattered by the indwelling voice of Divine Love; the Spirit of **Christ** penetrates the Light of Nature within the soul’s property and works within it, just as the light of nature in the earth works within the seed to produce a tree, pressing inward so that the seed may take root.

58. And this pressing-in of the Spirit of **Christ** into the *ens* <term>Ens: the essential spiritual substance or "being" of a thing.</term> of the soul is the Divine Calling, of which the Scripture says: *Many are called*, etc. For in this way, they are called in the ground of the soul before the soul has [true] life.

59. **Question.** But why does the Scripture say *Many*, and not *All*? **Answer.** **Christ** stands before all and calls them all, for the Scripture says: *God desires that all people be helped* <note>original: "1. Tim. 2: 4." Most modern translations say "saved."</note>. But they are not all capable of the calling, for some *ens* is more devilish than human; the wrath has overwhelmed and hardened it.

60. There the light now shines within itself in the darkness, and the dark essence of the soul has not comprehended it. The calling passes by this soul-essence because the soul’s property is caught in the darkness; the light indeed penetrates it, but it finds no *ens* of love within where it could ignite itself. Therefore, the *ens* of the created soul remains dwelling in itself outside of **God**, and **Christ** also remains dwelling in Himself; and yet they are near to one another; but a principle <term>Principle: original "Principium," referring to a distinct realm of existence or a fundamental state of being.</term> separates them, like the great gulf between the rich man and poor Lazarus <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 16:19–31.</note>, for they are toward one another like life and death.

61. Concerning these, it is now understood that **God** makes known His wrath and hardens them, but not out of some alien or Divine will or purpose; rather, out of that by which He has introduced His Word into nature and differentiation <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit," the capacity for things to be distinct or separate from one another.</note>. It is not that the holy will of **God** withdraws itself from them so that they must remain hardened—as [human] reason mistakenly thinks here—for He is in them and would gladly have them, wanting to reveal Himself in them as in the image of **God**. However, the fury in the center of nature, where the will of the fathomless abyss <note>original: "Ungrundes"</note> divides itself into the darkness, has seized them and filled the shattered gates of Divine Love with the abominations of inherited sins.

->### XV. Concerning Chapter 8.<-

62. To what end does the unfavorable constellation of inequality lead, when a man and a woman, in their mutual will, sow nothing but hatred, curses, and a vain will-to-death into one another? They capture their Life-Tincture <note>The "Tincture" is a term from alchemy used here to describe the subtle, spiritual life-force or "color" of a person's soul.</note> in a hostile will and only come together in the mixing of their seed through animalistic lust. No will is faithful to the other; they intend only poison and death, cursing one another at every hour, and living together like dogs and cats. As their life and constant will is, so too is the soul-tincture in their seed. Therefore, Christ says: *A diseased tree cannot bear good fruit* <note>original: "Ein arger Baum kann nicht gute Früchte tragen," referencing Matthew 7:18.</note>. For in the tincture of their seed, the hardening of the heart is already present; what can God do about the fact that they are planting a thistle?

63. So you may say: "But what can the child do about this?" **Answer:** The child and the parents are One Tree; the child is a branch on that same tree. Therefore, listen, Reason: When does the sun ever change a branch on a sour tree so that it becomes sweet? Shall God then act against His own purpose, against His spoken Word and Will, for the sake of a thistle? Yet even the kingdom of darkness requires creatures; they are all useful to God. To God, the ungodly are a fragrance of death leading to death, and the holy are a fragrance of life leading to life. <note>original: "2. Cor. 2: 15. 16." This refers to 2 Corinthians 2:15–16, describing how the Gospel affects different people differently.</note>

64. Therefore, the will toward destruction originates in the *ens* <term>Ens (or Entis): the essential spiritual substance or "being" that forms the foundation of a creature.</term> belonging to the creature. But the will toward a holy life originates from God in Christ. These two are within one another as one thing, yet they are understood in two "Principles" <note>In Böhme’s thought, "Principles" are the fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world (wrath), the light-world (love), and the outer world (nature).</note>. Because they are both active within the creature, the creature is pulled by both. However, if it happens that Christ can find no place of rest, then the Devil possesses the place where Christ should have worked.

65. And here Christ now says: *Few are chosen.* <note>original: "Matth. 20: 16."</note> Why? Many of them still have a tiny spark of the good *ens* <term>Ens: the essential spiritual being or "seed" of the soul.</term> within them, in which Christ works and warns and calls them without ceasing. But the false *ens* is so great and strong that it attracts a mass of evil impulses from the outside; it darkens the image of God and kills the good *ens* and will. Thus, the person crucifies the image of Christ—which Christ, in His breakthrough <note>The "breakthrough" refers to Christ's victory over death and the opening of the path to the divine light.</note>, sprinkled with His blood and redeemed with His death—he crucifies it within himself through sin and kills Christ in his own members.

66. And when the House-Father <note>A reference to the Parable of the Great Banquet, where the master of the house invites guests.</note> then comes to His invi—

### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.

...to behold the guests at the marriage of the Lamb, He sees that this redeemed image of Christ, which is invited to the wedding, has no wedding garment on; so He commands His Servant of Wrath <note>The "Servant of Wrath" (*Zorn-Knecht*) represents the judgmental aspect of God's nature that reacts to spiritual impurity.</note> to bind this guest's hands and feet in the essence <note>original: "Ente," likely a derivation of the Latin *ens*, meaning being or essence.</note> of life, instead of Christ’s place, and to cast him out into the darkness, where there is wailing and gnashing of teeth, as Christ says in the Gospel, Matthew 22:12-13.

67. This evil wedding guest, although he boasts of Christ's name, is not chosen for the eternal Supper of the Lamb; rather, only those whose souls put on Christ, and who crucify and continually kill the will of sin in the flesh, are chosen.

68. Therefore Christ says: *Few are chosen*; for only those are chosen to be children of God in Christ who obey the voice of Christ within them, who in their "good little spark" <term>Füncklein: a mystical term referring to the residual spark of divine light within the human soul.</term> listen to the voice of the Bridegroom when Christ speaks within them: "Turn back, repent, step into the vineyard of Christ." If they accept, hear, and do this—and do not wait until God overcomes the false will and breaks it with force to make them blessed—then they are chosen. For human reason incorrectly cites the passages regarding the Election of Grace in a way that contradicts all the parables in the words of Christ.

69. For Christ spoke to His disciples when He offered them His body as food: *Take, eat; take and drink, this is my flesh and blood.* <note>original: "Matth. 26: 26. 28."</note> He commanded the soul to reach out and take. So it is also in the inward ground: when He offers Himself to the soul in the Light of Life, He says: "Come to me, I will refresh you; receive me, only open your desire toward me, and open the door of your will, and I will enter into you."

70. He stands before the door of the soul’s essence and knocks, and whichever soul opens to Him, He enters in and holds the Supper with it: His calling and knocking is His "drawing" and "willing," but the soul also has an eternal willing and an unfathomable <note>original: "ungründlichen," meaning without a ground or bottom—a central concept in Böhme’s philosophy regarding the freedom of the will.</note> will.

71. In summary <note>original: "In Summa"</note>, the soul is the eternal Father's natural <term>Fire-Will</term>, and Christ is the eternal Light's <term>Love-Will</term>; these stand within one another. Christ desires to form Himself into the soulish creature, just as the Fire-Will desires...

<summary>This page discusses the "Election of Grace," arguing that salvation requires the soul's active cooperation with Christ's internal call rather than a forced, mechanical selection by God. Böhme uses the parable of the wedding garment and the "Fire-Will" vs. "Love-Will" to explain how the soul must choose to "clothe itself" in Christ's nature.</summary>

Jacob Böhme uses the metaphor of botanical growth—seeds, soil, and trees—to explain how the human soul develops its character and how divine grace (Christ) constantly works to counter the "wild" or "wrathful" tendencies of human nature from the moment of birth.</meta>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 8.

to form its own uniqueness; whichever now triumphs, therein stands the formation. This struggle of formation begins immediately in the seed with the formation of creatures, in the inequality of the seed and the field <note>The "field" refers to the inherited nature or environment into which a soul is born.</note>, where many a branch immediately becomes a wild thistle in the contrariness and hostility of the <term>tinctures: spiritual qualities or "colors" that define the inner nature and health of a soul or substance</term>. From this thistle-child, the <term>Light of Nature: the innate divine guidance and spiritual intelligence present within the natural world</term> (wherein Christ dwells in the innermost ground) does not withdraw itself, until the will of the soul itself becomes darkened with the poison of wrath in its natural light.

72. Just as the struggle ignites itself in the root of the tree within a contrary field, causing the branch from the root to perish before it even grows up; and just as the sun comes to the aid of the tree's branch with its light and power as soon as it sprouts from the root; so also Christ comes to the aid of the soul as soon as it emerges from the mother’s womb, because of the evil accidents <note>In this context, "accidents" refers to external misfortunes or the corrupted state of the world.</note> from without. He has established a bath of rebirth through baptism in His covenant, wherein He shines upon the little children with the eternal sun, and works through them, pouring Himself into them in His covenant, to see if the soul’s essence might be capable of receiving the offered grace.

73. Afterward, when the soul reaches the age of reason, He draws and calls it through His revealed, taught Word from the mouths of the children of God, and offers Himself to the soul throughout the time of its entire outward life. Every day and hour, He echoes into the soul with His Word and power, to see if it will stand still and cease from its animal-like imagery, so that He might give it new birth.

74. Just as the sun’s power draws itself up within the <term>essence: original: "Ens," the fundamental being or "stuff" of a thing</term> of the wood in the tree and tempers the quality of the struggling nature; so also Christ turns with His power from the inner ground without ceasing into the soul, and tempers the forms of life so that they should not separate into ill-will and enmity, nor depart from equality into false lusts—through which false lusts the soul's nature introduces the <term>poison-source: original: "Gift-Quall," referring to the agonizing or bitter root of a self-centered existence</term> into itself.

75. And as the trunk with its branches on the tree becomes gnarled and crooked through the internal struggle of nature, and through the external influences of the <term>constellations: the astrological and cosmic forces believed to influence human temperament and physical health</term>; so also the soul leads them, through the internal adversity...

<summary>Böhme uses the analogy of a tree growing in poor soil to explain how Christ’s grace acts like sunlight, constantly attempting to "temper" or balance the soul's naturally "wild" or "thistle-like" tendencies from birth through adulthood.</summary>
