### Chapter 8: The Election of Grace

...ness <note>original: "keit," the suffix of the word "Ungleichheit" (inequality) from the previous page</note> of the inequality of the natures of father and mother, and then through the external influences of the world's wickedness, into a misshapen figure before God.

76. Then the wedding garment of Baptism is turned into an animal-like mask <note>original: "larve," meaning a mask, disguise, or a distorted outward form</note>, so that the election passes by as long as the soul wears this hunchbacked mask-image upon itself.

77. This mask prevents the <term>Being of Christ: the divine substance or spiritual essence of Christ within the soul (original: "Ens Christi")</term> so that it cannot work fruit for the praise of God; for the Devil constantly sows his desire into this mask, so that false young branches grow out of it with false, rebellious wills. These introduce themselves into pride and into the Devil’s will, breaking away from humility, just as young branches on a tree break out from the <term>Temperament: the state of perfect balance between opposing spiritual qualities</term> and wish to be their own trees. And when they have thus broken out, they stand in the <term>Constellation: the collective spiritual and astrological influence of the physical world</term> of the world, like sprouts on a tree. Then the constellation of the stars sifts them through subtle, clever people and leads them from one idle curiosity to another. Then pride falls in, and soon avarice, then envy, wrath, lies, deception, and everything that rules in the world; there the young, proud branch wishes to rise up in the arts and burns itself in all things. If it then happens that the Divine Sun shines into it and wishes to come to the aid of the rebellious branch, and the fiery life feels this, it swings itself upward like <note>original: "Lucifer"</note> the Light-Bringer, and attributes to itself its own cleverness and understanding, despising that which is simple. From this come those people wise in their own reason, who are full of pride and the lust of self-honor; they only burn themselves through the light that shines in them out of grace, and they use it for the lusts of the flesh. Thus, Christ must be the "cloak for their wickedness" <note>An allusion to 1 Peter 2:16, where faith is used as a cover for sin</note>.

78. These are all false branches over whom the election of the harvest time passes by; for they are called in Christ’s spirit, He has given Himself into them, worked with them, and enlightened their reason; but they have not been born of Christ’s spirit. Instead, they were born in the world’s lust; they have only trodden Christ underfoot and never served Him. Christ has been hungry, thirsty, sick, imprisoned, naked, and miserable to them, and they never served Him <note>A reference to the Judgment in Matthew 25</note>. His name indeed hovered in their mouths, but their soul always turned itself into the own lust of the world and of the Devil; they let Christ stand there and held the light only to serve their own malice.

### XV. Concerning Chapter 8.

79. These have strayed from the stem of the <term>Temperament: the state of perfect balance between opposing spiritual qualities (original: "Temperatur")</term>, and have not grown up in the true Sun, Christ, nor been born of God, but in the self-will of their own nature. Therefore, their fruits are merely human trifles <note>original: "Menschen-Tand," referring to worthless ornaments or vanities</note>. And even if they become high-ranking people in the world and learn many arts and languages, it is all born of the vanity of nature; all their works before God are like a stained and filthy cloth <note>original: "ein beflecktes und besudeltes Tuch," an allusion to Isaiah 64:6 describing human righteousness compared to God's holiness</note>.

80. However, that soul which takes its origin in a good field—where the parents place their will in God and stand in the true bond of love as in the true <term>Constellation: the collective spiritual and astrological influence of the physical world</term>, placing their hope in God where Christ works, lives, and exists within them—from such people spring streams of living water, as Christ says. And even though <term>Adamic corruption: the inherited fallen nature of humanity</term> is in their flesh, and often an evil constellation falls into the flesh as a <note>source or torment of sin (original: "Sünden-Quall")</note>, Christ nevertheless remains in the innermost ground of the soul within them.

81. Thus the soul is born of the soul, and the body from the seed of the body. Even if the external seed is earthly and evil, and is poisoned in such a constellation, Christ nonetheless possesses the soulful ground in the inward center. He is and remains in the <term>Being: the essential nature or spiritual substance (original: "Ente," from the Latin "ens")</term> of the soul, the <term>Being of Christ: the divine substance or spiritual essence of Christ within the soul (original: "Ens Christi")</term>; the soul is conceived and born within this Being of Christ.

82. And here Christ says: "He who is of God hears God’s word" <note>original Latin reference: Joh. 8: 47</note>. And to the proud Pharisees He said: "Therefore you do not hear, because you are not of God." That is to say, even though they carried His word and laws in their mouths, their soul was not born in the Divine <term>Being: essential substance (original: "Ente")</term>. Though they had the <term>Light of Nature: the natural reason and insight given to all humans</term>, it nevertheless shone out of a foreign fire; although Christ indeed shone through it, they were not capable of receiving Him, for their ground was false.

83. Thus a good seed is sown, sometimes even into an evil field, yet the ground of the seed remains good. But where a false grain is sown into an evil field, there grows from it the likeness of its own essence. And just as a good grain must often stand in an evil field and yet bears fruit—provided the external influences do not ruin it—so also a seed of faith from the one <term>Tincture: the spiritual life-force or transformative essence (original: "Tinctur")</term> is often produced, either of the

### Chapter 8. The Election of Grace.

<note>original: "Gnaden-Wahl," referring to the theological concept of predestination or how God chooses those who receive grace.</note>

...sown by man or woman, and the other <note>the adversary or the worldly influence</note> sows his poison into it, whereby the outer person becomes wild and inclined toward evil; but the inner ground is still good; he soon does something evil, which he also immediately repents, and he enters into restraint <note>original: "Abstinentz," implying a turning away from sin or a state of self-denial.</note>

84. Similarly: many a person is so poisoned in one part by the torment of sin <term>Torment of sin: the internal source or spring of sinful urges (original: "Sünden-Quall")</term> that he has an evil inclination within him toward stealing, robbing, and murdering; likewise: toward unchastity, false slander, etc. But the other part in Christ's essence <term>Essence: the fundamental spiritual nature or substance of a thing (original: "Ente" or "Ens")</term> always pulls him away from it: and although he oversteps in weakness (through the devil's interference), the Divine Essence still comes to his aid, provided he does not remain lying in sin and death, as happened to the thief on the cross, Mary Magdalene, and other great sinners.

85. For there is likely no person who does not have a torment of sin in the flesh, arising from the desire of his animal flesh: and just as a tree must grow up in conflict and opposition, where adversity falls upon it from all sides—now heat, now cold, now the wind presses it so that it might break, now a poison falls upon it from the stars—yet it grows in the power of the sun and in its internal light-essence <note>original: "Lichts-Ente"</note> of nature, and bears good fruit which does not have the taste of the earth; rather, the noble tincture <term>Tincture: in spiritual alchemy, the highest medicinal or transformative power that changes base nature into something divine (original: "Tinctur")</term> has introduced itself into a good, well-tasting body; so it is also to be understood with the human being.

86. The Divine Essence, which is spiritual, cannot become manifest except through the conflict of nature; it sows itself into the soul's essence of eternal nature and gives itself into the conflict of the fire's distinction <term>Distinction of the fire: the point where the spiritual light separates from the raw heat of the soul's natural fire (original: "Schiedlichkeit des Feuers")</term>, where it then receives its light and leads itself out of the fire into the power and qualities of love-desire. In the fire of the soul, it receives qualities and will: for in God it is One, and only a single will, which is the eternal Good, but in that way He is not manifest to Himself; however, in the fiery distinction of the soul He becomes manifest to Himself, so that the power emerges in many powers of working virtues, into a form and shaping: just as the tree becomes manifest in its branches and fruits through conflict, so that one sees what lay within the mystery <note>original: "Mysterio," referring to the hidden potential or divine blueprint</note> of the seed for the tree.

87. And therefore the Divine power appropriates the souls—

...of man or woman is sown, and the other <note>referring to the adversarial or "dark" influence</note> sows his poison into it, whereby the outer man becomes wild and inclined toward evil; but the **inward ground** is still good; he very quickly does something evil, so that he also immediately repents, and he enters into **abstinence** <note>original: "Abstinentz," meaning a turning away from worldly desires or a state of spiritual restraint</note>.

85. For there is truly no human who does not have a **torment of sin** in the flesh, arising from the desire of his animal flesh. 86. ### XV. On Chapter 8.

...souls of man, so that it may grow up within them and reveal its virtue in the **fiery distinctness** <term>Fiery distinctness: the state where individual qualities become clear and active through the "fire" of existence (original: "feurischen Schiedlichkeit")</term>, where evil and good work within one another: thus the Spirit of God in Christ presses forward in that which is good and works toward fruit, as a **Divine Formality** <note>original: "Göttliche Formlichkeit," meaning the realization of God's image or likeness</note>. This could not or may not happen unless the soul-fire eats of the **Divine Substance** <note>original: "Göttlichen Entis," from the Latin *Ens*, meaning being or essence</note>; from this "fire-eating," a true power goes forth into the **Light of Nature**.

88. The fire of the soul must have the right wood if it is to give a beautiful, powerful light; for through the soul-fire, God’s Spirit becomes distinct and manifest in its power within the nature of the soul. This is just as light is made manifest from fire, and air from fire and light, and from the air a subtle "little water" <note>referring to a spiritual moisture or essence</note> goes forth, which becomes substantial after its departure. From this, the light draws power back into itself for its food. Therefore Christ said in **John 6:53**: *Unless you eat the flesh of the Son of Man and drink his blood, you have no life in you.* <note>original Latin reference: "Joh. 6. 53."</note>

89. Just as a tree could neither grow nor bear fruit without the **Light of Nature**—which the sun, pressing into it, makes living—and just as the Light of Nature (as well as the sun’s power) could not become manifest and active in the tree without the **fiery science** <term>Fiery science: the inner, active "knowledge" or potency that drives natural processes (original: "feurische Scienz")</term>, namely the fiery ground of nature, which is the tree’s soul;

90. So also, in the same way, Christ cannot become manifest in a person, even though He is within them and draws and calls them, and even presses into the soul, unless the soul eats of the **Divine Substance** <note>original: "Göttlichen Entis"</note> into its own fiery property. This is difficult for the **Pride-Fire** <note>original: "Hoffart-Feuer," the egoistic or haughty nature of the unrefined soul</note> to accept—that it should eat from the **Water-Source** <note>original: "Wasser-Quall," referring to the cooling, life-giving quality of divine grace</note> of the Life of Love and Gentleness. It would rather eat of **Sulfur and Mercury** <term>Sulfur and Mercury: alchemical terms here representing the harsh, bitter, and restless qualities of the lower nature</term>, as of its own likeness. But if it does eat [of the Divine], then the Spirit of Love and Humility (as the Divine Substance) becomes fiery and attacks the **Fire-Root** composed of the "Three Firsts"—namely **Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury** <note>original: "Sal, Sulphur, Mercurium"</note>—and **transmutes** <term>Transmute: to fundamentally change the nature of a substance, as in alchemy</term> them into itself, just as a **tincture** <note>A potent alchemical liquid believed to transform base metals into gold</note> falls upon a glowing iron and changes the iron into gold. So also here: the soul's center is changed from the Father’s fire-nature into a **Love-Fire**, in which Love-Fire Christ is made manifest and is born in the soul. There, out of the soul-fire, the true Divine Spirit of Delight arises from the fire and...

### Chapter 8. On the Election of Grace.

...light proceeds, and leads forth its spiritual water from itself out of the light, which becomes substantial; from this, the power of the light eats, and introduces itself through love-desire into a holy being <note>original: "Wesen," meaning essence or substance</note>, as into a spiritual corporeality, in which the Holy Trinity dwells. This being is the true temple of the Holy Spirit—indeed, it is **God** Himself in His revelation.

91. And this is what Christ meant when He said He would give us the water of eternal life, which would well up within us into a fountain of eternal life: **John 4:14**. This happens when the soul accepts His Word, which He is Himself. Then He pours His substantial power—which He made manifest in our humanity—into the soul; this is the soul's **Tincture** <term>Tincture: a term from alchemy used by Böhme to describe a spiritual medicine or essence that transforms the "base" or "fiery" nature of the soul into something divine and golden</term>, which transforms the soul's hostility of the fiery property into a love-fire. For there, Christ rises from death in the deceased soul-property, and the soul becomes a member of Christ’s body and puts on Christ; indeed, according to the property of love, it is planted entirely into Christ. Therefore Christ says: *Whoever eats my flesh and drinks my blood remains in me and I in him*; **John 6:56**. This is how it happens. Likewise, He says: *We will come to you and make our dwelling within you*; **John 14:23**. That is to say, the whole of **God** becomes manifest in the soul through this new birth in Christ, and works good, Divine fruits.

92. Just as the sun's power becomes manifest in a tree—igniting the light within the essence of the sulfur-spirit and in the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio," referring to the dynamic, moving principle of life</note> (the resinous property) so that the tree grows and bears fruit—so too does **God** become manifest in His formed, spoken Word. This occurs in man, into whom He has introduced His highest love-tincture in the name of **JESUS**. This tinctures the fiery soul as the spiritual **Sulfur** and **Mercury** <term>Sulfur and Mercury: Böhme uses these alchemical terms to describe the "burning desire" and the "active consciousness" of the soul</term>, in which the light of eternal nature becomes manifest and shining. Within this, Christ is born in His formed Word and grows into a glorious Divine tree, which grows into the image of **God** and bears much good Divine fruit.

93. Then this person speaks the Word of **God** out of **God**, and these are then Divine fruits; for then **God’s** formed word (which is the creaturely soul) speaks the fountain-well of Divine speaking out of itself. ### XV. On Chapter 8.

The one **God** speaks His Word out of Himself, and generates it forever; yet the speaking remains within Him, and He is both the speaking and the spoken.

94. And although the corrupt nature of the earthly, animal property still clings to this person in their flesh, and opposes the soul; it does not harm them, for the soul has now, in Christ, overcome the fierce, corrupt, fiery property. Christ within the soul constantly treads upon the head of the serpent's poison in the earthly flesh, and works through the flesh, and raises Himself up in the flesh into a new body, in the same way that precious gold lies and grows inside a coarse stone. In that case, the coarseness must help in the process of growth, even though it is not equal to the gold: so too must the earthly body help to give birth to Christ within itself, even though it is not Christ, nor will it ever be in eternity, and is of no use for the Kingdom of **God**. Nevertheless, it must help as an instrument <term>Werckzeug: an organic tool or vessel used by God to accomplish a task</term>; even though it has a completely different false will and desire, and is a stronghold of the devil <note>original: "Raub-Schloß," literally a robber's castle or den</note>, **God** still uses it as His instrument. 

And concerning this, Christ said it was His yoke—namely our earthly body, which He helps us to carry; that is His yoke in us. The holy soul should take this upon itself in patience, and let all external misfortune—including the temptations of the flesh from the devil and the malice of the world—pass over it. The soul must bow under the birth of the cross <term>Creutz-Geburt: the spiritual process of being "born again" through the suffering and sacrifice of the Cross</term> of Christ under His yoke, and take hold in patience. Thus, in tribulation, it shall grow up with the noble little pearl-tree <note>Perlen-Bäumlein: a favorite metaphor of Böhme’s representing the fragile but precious divine nature growing within the "soil" of the human soul</note> of Christ amidst all evil things. This tree produces and bears purely good, holy, heavenly fruits, which are not of this world—that is, not from the four elements according to the spirit of the world <note>original Latin: "Spiritu Mundi"</note> from without; but as Paul says: *Our citizenship is in heaven.* Phil. 3: 20. 

Likewise: *I have called you out of the world, so that you may be where I am; and for this reason the world hates you, because it recognizes neither me, nor you, nor my Father; but be of good cheer, in me you have peace, in the world you have anxiety,* John 16: 33. That is to say: in me, in the inward ground of the new birth <term>neue Geburt: the radical internal transformation where the soul is reunited with God</term>, you have peace with **God**, but in the outer flesh, in the world, you have anxiety; but I will come to you again, and take you to myself where I am, says Christ. That is: He will return to the human being who was created from the slime <note>original Latin: "Limo," referring to the "dust of the ground" or clay from which Adam was formed in Genesis</note> of the earth, and will take them to Himself again,

95. This, my dear reader, is the true ground of the new rebirth; it happens in no other way than what Reason imagines—namely, that we are merely accepted as children of grace from the outside; likewise, that we are acquitted of sins through a Divine decree; no, one must be born anew out of this aforementioned water and the Holy Spirit.

96. The soul must, by its own will, turn around in the "drawing" of Christ, and lead its desiring will toward the desire of Christ (which powerfully presses into it, and within it). It must open wide its fiery maw—that is, the spiritual sulfur-worm <term>Schwefel-Wurm: a term for the fierce, consuming fire of the soul's own self-will</term> in the Mercury <term>Mercurio: the dynamic, moving life-force or "wheel" of nature</term> of the spirit-life; then Christ’s Spirit penetrates into the essence of the soul. And that is called believing and receiving: not just knowing, comforting, tickling, and wrapping oneself in Christ’s mantle from the outside, always speaking of grace while wanting to remain children of grace in the malice of the devil; rather, one must become in the Spirit of Christ like a child at its mother’s breast, which desires only to suck the mother’s breasts and nothing more; for only in Christ’s essence <term>Ente: Böhme's Germanization of the Latin *ens*, meaning the "being" or "inner substance"</term> does the true new human grow.

97. But when Reason says: "We will only be born anew in the resurrection, and put on Christ in the flesh," that is Babel <note>Babel: Böhme's name for the confused, institutional church that relies on external rituals rather than internal transformation</note>, and shows no understanding of the words of Christ.

98. The body from the earth shall only put on Christ essentially <note>original: "essentialiter," meaning in its very substance</note> in the resurrection; however, during this [earthly] time, the soul must put on Christ in His heavenly flesh. ### XV. From Chapter 8.

A single God speaks forth His Word from Himself, and perpetually gives birth to it, and yet the speaking remains in Him, and He is both the speaking and the spoken.

94. And of this Christ said it would be His yoke—namely, our earthly body, which He helps us to carry; it is His yoke in us. ## Chapter 8: The Election of Grace

...accept him as the new spiritual man and keep him eternally; but he must first lay aside into the <term>putrefaction</term><note>The alchemical process of rotting or decay, viewed as a necessary stage of purification where the old form must die for the new to be born.</note> of the earth the serpent’s essence <note>original: "Schlagen Ens"</note>, along with the animal-like form and all practiced falsehood. Then He will come to him again and awaken the Adam-like body from death, taking it to Himself, wiping away all his tears, and walking in joy. John 14:3. <note>original: "Joh. 14:3"</note>

95. This, my dear reader, is the true ground of the new rebirth. It does not happen at all in the way that human reason imagines—namely, that we are merely children of grace adopted from the outside, or that we are acquitted of sins through a divine decree. No, one must be born anew from the aforementioned water and the Holy Spirit.

96. The soul must, by its own will, turn itself within the "drawing" of Christ <note>The magnetic spiritual pull or attraction of God's love.</note> and direct its desiring will toward the desire of Christ (which presses powerfully toward and into the soul). It must open its fiery maw, like the spiritual <term>sulfur-worm</term><note>In Böhme’s chemistry of the spirit, this represents the restless, consuming fire of the soul's own selfish drive.</note> in the <term>mercury</term><note>The active, moving principle of life and consciousness.</note> of the life-spirit. Then the Spirit of Christ penetrates the essence of the soul. And this is called "Faith" and "Taking"—not just knowing, comforting oneself, tickling the senses, or wrapping oneself in Christ’s cloak from the outside while speaking of grace while remaining in the devil’s wickedness. Rather, one must become in the Spirit of Christ like a child at its mother’s breast, desiring to suck only from the mother’s breasts and nothing more. For the true new man grows only in the <term>essence</term><note>original: "Ente" (from the Latin "entia," meaning substance or being).</note> of Christ.

97. But when Reason says, "We will only be born anew at the resurrection and put on Christ in the flesh," that is <term>Babel</term><note>Böhme uses "Babel" to refer to the "City of Confusion," specifically the established churches and scholars who argue over external dogmas without inner spiritual experience.</note> and shows no understanding of Christ's words.

98. The body from the earth shall only put on Christ <term>essentially</term> at the resurrection; but the soul must put on Christ in His heavenly flesh during this present time. Within Christ, the soul must be given the new body—not of man's blood nor of the flesh, but of the Word and the divine essence. This happens in that "faded" part of the divine essence that withered in Adam and became mute and unfeeling toward God's working. In that very place, Christ must be born anew,

### XV. On Chapter 8

...and become a GOD-man, just as the human being becomes a man-GOD.

**99.** Therefore, dear brothers, understand that on one hand Christ is the Divine Purpose and the Will of Grace; whoever is born from this and clothes himself in Him is "foreseen" or elected in Christ and is a child of grace. On the other hand, the purpose of God is the fiery will of the soul from the center of eternal nature. Where light and darkness separate, one part goes into the center of darkness—namely, the coarse, illusory <term>Sulphur</term><note>In this mystical chemistry, Sulphur represents the soul's inner fire and drive; "fantastic" or illusory sulphur refers to selfish, earthly desires.</note>; but the subtle and pure part goes into the light. Now, into whichever direction the <term>Scienz</term><note>From the Latin *scientia*; Böhme uses this to mean the "root-knowledge" or the sensitive, choosing point of the will that determines its direction.</note> of the unfathomable will toward nature divides itself, therein it becomes a creature, either in the light or in the darkness.

**100.** The purpose of God proceeds entirely from the ground of the soul; for the inner ground of the soul is the Divine Nature belonging to the eternally speaking Word, and it is neither evil nor good. But in the <term>Schiedlichkeit</term><note>The state of being distinct or divisible; the capacity for the one unified source to manifest as many different qualities.</note> of the fire—that is, in the ignited life of the soul—that same will divides itself either into God’s anger or into God’s love-fire. This happens in no other way than through the specific quality that the soul’s essence contains within itself: the soul is its own ground for evil or good, for it is the center of God where God’s love and wrath lie unwound in a single ground.

**101.** Thus, this is the purpose of God: that He wills to reveal Himself through the spoken, formed Word (of which the soul is a living being in the speaking of that differentiation). In this process, the coarseness hardens itself within its inherited as well as its actually committed abominations.

**102.** For there is otherwise no other will of God in the essence of this world than that which becomes manifest from the eternal ground in fire and light, as well as in darkness. The soul is "elected" within itself as a child of grace when it is born from Christ, from the Divine <term>Ente</term><note>original: "Ente" (Latin *ens*). A philosophical term for "being" or "essence."</note>, which is the only purpose of Divine grace, from which God’s grace becomes manifest in the soul. And it is also elected within itself to damnation from the ground of its own essence, which is a false <term>Ens</term><note>A false "being" or substance that has turned away from the light.</note> in which no light can be born.

**103.** God’s purpose toward "hardening" <note>The biblical concept (as with Pharaoh) where a heart becomes calloused and resistant to God.</note> is in the soul’s own essence, as the unfathomable will toward Na-

<meta>catchword: Na- (Nature)</meta>

<summary>This page explains that "election" is not an arbitrary divine decree, but a result of the soul's own internal alignment. The soul contains the potential for both God's love and wrath, and its "choice" or internal "Scienz" determines which quality becomes its reality.</summary>

Nature reveals itself in every being according to the specific quality of that being; just as we consider that God, in His containment of coarseness, has contained and separated Himself into the dark world or Hell. For the will that is in Hell, and the will that is manifest in Heaven, are—in the inward ground, outside of manifestation—one single thing. It is only in the "speaking of the Word" <note>Böhme refers to the *Fiat* or the creative act where the unified God expresses Himself into diversity.</note> that the separation first occurs. Yet Heaven and Hell are within one another like day and night, and Hell is a ground of Heaven; for God’s wrath-fire is a ground of His love, which is the light.

104. Therefore, dear brothers, become seeing at last! Never argue about the will of God: we ourselves are God’s will toward both evil and good. Whichever becomes manifest in us, that is what we are, either Heaven or Hell. Our own Hell within us hardens us—namely, that same specific quality—and our own Heaven within us also makes us blessed, if it may become manifest. It is all one "territory" <note>original: *Land*; here meaning a single reality or state of being</note> over which people have argued for such a long time. Christ has been found; for this may there be eternal praise and thanks to Him, as well as power, honor, and riches, along with all authority in heaven and on earth. <note>original: *Matth. 28: 18.*</note>

# The 9th Chapter.
## On the Opposition of Passages in Scripture; that is, on the Right Understanding of Scripture.

->Summaries.<-

1. What the "lump of clay" signifies in the passage Romans 9:21. 2. As the <term>Ens</term><note>From the Latin for "being" or "essence." Böhme uses it to describe the fundamental spiritual substance of a thing.</note> of the soul is, so is the will toward the making. 3. An evil essence produces an evil life, just as a good essence works a good life. 4. God’s Word is the life of all things: but because the "wrath-zeal" <note>original: *Zorn-Eifer*; the aspect of God's power that consumes and judges.</note> is also within it, it also leads itself into a life; in contrast, Christ came from the Word as a helper to mankind. 5. The godless person cannot say that God makes him evil: rather, God *within him* makes him according to what he himself wills. 6. Just as love introduces its will into its children, so does wrath introduce its own into the godless. 7. God, without nature, is only One; but in the <term>Schiedlichkeit</term><note>Divisibility or the state of being distinct; the process by which the "One" becomes "Many."</note> two wills arise. 8. Adam led himself into this divisibility; thus his "branches" <note>Meaning his descendants, all of humanity.</note> now stand within it as well. 9. Nature, as divisibility, is from the eternally-speaking Word, 10. and has made itself graspable; however, that which is ungraspable is God’s Word, God Himself. 11. The purpose of the Word is to dwell in that which is "grasped" <note>The manifest, physical, or tangible world.</note> so that graspability might make the ungraspable tangible, just as fire makes light. 12. Should...

Now, for God’s holiness and love to be revealed, there must be something that stands in need of love and grace. 12. Thus the Holy Life is revealed as a co-acting life; 13. and this revelation is called Power, and the tangibility is called <term>Tincture</term> <note>In Böhme’s mystical chemistry, the "Tincture" is the spiritual essence or "color" that transforms a substance, representing the power and radiance of fire and light.</note>, as the power and radiance of the fire and the light. 14. This Divine Life stood manifest in Adam without his knowledge; he knew no evil, nor did his own will know what was good. 15. Joy exists when the own-will is redeemed from its pain; but because it cannot take this by its own power, it rejoices in grace. 16. Therefore Nature stands in pain so that love may be revealed. 17. Adam stood in the <term>Temperature</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; referring to a state of perfect balance where all spiritual qualities are in harmony, before they were "split" by the Fall.</note>; he knew not what was evil or what was grace, until the free will entered into <term>separation</term> <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the state of being divided into opposing, distinct qualities.</note>: then it was necessary for grace to be revealed and to "tinge" <note>To permeate or transform with a spiritual quality.</note> the painfulness. 18. Therefore, Love gave itself into the properties of the soulful nature as a helper. 19. 20. This was the <term>Serpent-Crusher</term> <note>original: *Schlangen-Treter*; a reference to the "Seed of the Woman" in Genesis 3:15 promised to crush the serpent’s head.</note>, who intended to crush the head of the will of painfulness. 21. The figure of this image was Abel, until the Godhead itself became man; just as Cain was Adam’s image after the Fall. 22. 26. Grace gave itself into the painfulness so that the own-will might lose its power. 27. If the "spoken word" is now to become substantial, the own-will must surrender its right. 28. Who can say that God’s voice did not lie within Cain, since it spoke to him: "Rule over sin"? <note>Reference to Genesis 4:7.</note> 29. But his own-will, which he had from Adam, hardened him, 30. and made him deaf, so that he could not hear the voice of God in the essence of the soul. 31. Just as current reason also only wants to be a child adopted from the outside. 32. Objection: Did God’s voice of grace not move Cain? 33. The voice that spoke to Cain was God’s Justice; this demands one’s own ability, but he does not reach grace, nor does he even feel he needs it; rather, grace must surrender itself into him. 34. Christ had to suffer in our humanity so that grace might be revealed within Justice. 35. Justice lay in the soul; thus God demanded His justice from it, that it should rule over sin: but the "ability" was lost, and now it depended on grace, to which the own-will should surrender itself; 36. for the instrument was broken, therefore God’s mercy must perform it. *Ibid.* Thus it depends on the will-to-grace, 37. and grace only has "willingness" in that which is born of God. 38. It also wanted to crush the serpent’s head in Cain: but the serpent’s venom had poisoned him, so that he surrendered himself to the wrath of Justice. *Ibid.* Thus, through human works, sin came into the world and was also put to death in grace. 39. Therefore, Abel and Christ had to die through human works. 40. Through the opposition, grace is revealed, 41. in which, as in Christ, we are all only One, from which also the adoption as children comes. 42. 43. Natural Justice demands that man be in the order in which he was created; where it does not find him therein, it takes him into its property of the <term>separation</term> of the Word, of which the soul is an essence. 44. God withdraws Himself from the godless and lets him make of himself what he will (Prov. 20:20). 45. Because he misuses God’s grace, as seen in Lucifer. 46. The root of darkness surged up in Adam, from which Cain came. 47. The true soulful

<header>Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.</header>

The will which died in Adam, and into which God spoke His voice, was the root from which Abel came: 48. just as from the other sinful-will, which arose during the Fall, came Cain. 49. Both, indeed, came from one essence; but according to the will, they separated. 50. Abel, too, had sin clinging to him in the flesh, for which reason he also had to die: but the Voice of Grace lived in him. *ibid.* God, however, does not "harden" <note>original: *verstocket*; referring to the biblical concept of "hardening of the heart," where a person becomes unreceptive to God’s grace.</note> out of His own will: rather, the new will, having arisen from the center of the soul, hardens itself; that will is false and a murderer. 51. Cain was of Adam’s body and soul, not of the "serpent-seed"; but Adam’s natural, false will governed him. 52. If God had hardened him, then His Justice could not have judged him. 53. Justice is the order of the <term>Spoken Word</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Spoken Word" (*ausgesprochene Wort*) is the manifestation of God’s internal thought into the created universe; it represents the natural laws and moral order of existence.</note>: whatever stands within it does not fall into judgment. 54. Now, if God Himself had hardened Adam and Cain, they would have been standing within the Divine Order. 55. Therefore, the will toward hardening arose within Adam and Cain themselves. 56. Thus, God’s "purpose" is the center of the human <term>Ground</term> <note>original: *Grund*; the deepest, most fundamental essence of a person's soul where they meet the divine.</note>, which lies within the human being himself. 57. For if God had a purpose for a devil, then a devil would have surely been created immediately; 58. also, the verses Psalm 5:5 <note>original: "For thou art not a God that hath pleasure in wickedness."</note>, Ezekiel 18:23, and 33:11 <note>original: "As I live, saith the Lord God, I have no pleasure in the death of the wicked; but that the wicked turn from his way and live."</note> would not be true; 59. indeed, God’s commandment would be in vain, and His judgments would be false. 60. Thus, we should condemn no one, except for manifest sins for which the devil is to blame, 61. and not the poor captive soul, in which the Voice of Grace may well be awakened. 62. For Christ stands in the inward ground as long as the soul carries the body. 63. It depends only on "asking and knocking": not on "running and racing" <note>A reference to Romans 9:16, emphasizing that while effort is needed, salvation is a gift of mercy.</note>. 64. **Question:** Who then are the lost? **Answer:** All poor sinners; these are the ones Christ came to seek and to save; but not the "self-born" and false ground, but rather the true ground of the first-born soul. 65. God casts out Ishmael the mocker, 66. as the departure with Hagar demonstrates. 67. By which is represented how Adam ran out from God into animal-like properties. 68. 69. God gifted Adam His Word of Grace; that is the inward ground. 70. Nature shall not perish, but only the false will. 71. Further explanation of the history of Ishmael. 73. He is wrongly condemned. 74. What are circumcision and baptism? 75. God blessed the inward ground of nature, which is God’s Spoken Word. 76. Application to Japheth. 77. Why is Ishmael not to be condemned? *ibid.* Ishmael is an image of the "old man," just as Isaac is the "new man." 78. Jesus took on Adam's nature, but not the false will. 79. 80. The figures of Jacob and Esau are the counter-play. 81. The justice of the external order would not tolerate Christ within itself, because He had sprouted from the heavenly order. 82. Therefore, He had nothing in this world: but in the inner ground, all power was given to Him. 83. The figures of Jacob and Esau are fulfilled in Christ and the Pharisees. 84. Jacob's gifts shattered Esau’s wrath; 85. Within our humanity lay the Father’s wrath, which Christ shattered with His blood, 86. brought back the inheritance lost in Adam, 87. and accepted Esau back into the inner ground. 88. # XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

...predestined to damnation: but not the soulful <term>Ground</term> <note>original: *Grund*; the deepest, unmanifested center of the soul.</note>. 91. The voice of God lay in the inner ground of Esau, but not in the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a Latin term used by Böhme to mean the "essence" or "being" that gives rise to a person's character.</note> of his life, as it did in Jacob. 92. Jacob, Isaac, and Abel were the righteous ones, in whom Grace had revealed itself. 93. Cain, Ishmael, and Esau were the image of Wrath, in whom God’s purpose—as the order of His <term>Spoken Word</term>—was to persist. 94. Adam stood in the <term>Temperatur</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect balance and harmony between opposing qualities (like heat and cold, or wrath and love) before the Fall.</note>. If Grace were to be revealed, the Wrath had to be revealed first. 95. For a thing that stands in equal weight is without motion as long as it is One; but if it departs from its equality, it becomes many, fragile, and a thing of its "own": and then it stands in need of help. 96. Therefore, the final body is better than the first. 97. In Adam, the image of God stood in equality: but when he went out into "lust" <note>meaning worldly desire or self-will</note>, the properties separated; then God came to his aid. 98. Now the <term>In-spoken Word</term> <note>original: *eingesprochene Wort*; the inner call of God’s grace placed within the soul after the Fall.</note> became substantial, and was called Faith, which gives birth to a new body. *ibid.* The In-spoken Word was revealed out of Adam in Abel’s faith, and his broken will was transformed into a whole one, which is the figure of Christ, who was a different person than Adam. 99. This ground in-spoken in Adam was divided among his children. 100. For the name **Jesus** revealed in Adam took hold of itself within him as a desire, which was a beginning of faith, from which Abel came: 101. In Cain, this sound of the Word also lay, but his essence was not capable of it, because God’s fierceness <note>original: *Grimm*; the harsh, judging aspect of God’s nature.</note> had been revealed in him. 102. Now no "new speaking" could happen in him, unless it happened from a Divine resonance. 103. Therefore, God placed Abel’s lineage alongside Cain’s lineage, and revealed **Jesus** in Abel’s line, to speak the same into Cain. 104. The image of Cain, etc., are all unregenerate people whom God calls with His in-speaking Word; 105. Abel, Isaac, etc., are all those who allow themselves to be awakened by that in-speaking: the Word itself, however, is Heaven and the Temple of God. 106. Thus Adam’s corrupted image was manifest in Esau, and Christ’s image in Jacob. 107. But the in-spoken Word had to surrender itself into God’s Wrath, and transform the Wrath-fire into a Love-fire, 108. as is to be seen in Jacob and Christ. 109. God led such a process so that man would have cause to love and thank God, 110. which could not have been in Adam, because he stood in the balance; 111. And this is Christ’s resurrection in man. 112. Thus God did not hate Esau; rather, the Justice in the distinction of beings hated the false image in Esau, because it was in Wrath and not in Love, 113. and Esau was himself the image of hatred. 114. For the creaturely life, without the revelation of Light, is nothing but hatred, envy, and wrath. 115. So indeed God’s Justice hated Esau unto destruction: but the Love-Will did not, 116. for God wills in Christ that all men be helped: but His Wrath wills to swallow all in whom it is manifest. 117. A thistle does not allow itself to be easily trained. *ibid.* This comes not only from the mother’s womb, but also through external accidents. 118. For sometimes there is still a tiny spark of Divine drawing in them, but external influences corrupt it, so that it leads itself into the <term>Serpent-Ens</term> <note>the "serpent-essence" or the poisonous, selfish nature inherited from the Fall of man.</note> before Christ can be born in them. 119. Was Esau saved? 120. He is not to be condemned.

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

...attained, 121. the Gate of Grace stood open for him as well as for his parents. 122. Also, God did not harden him; rather, God’s Justice [hardened] him within himself. 123.

Has not a potter power, from one lump of clay, to make one <term>vessel</term> <note>original: *Gefäſſe*</note> for honor, and the other for dishonor? Romans 9:21. Answer: The lump of clay signifies the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*; Böhme’s term for the primal, unmanifested source from which all duality and existence emerge.</note>, where the eternal God has expressed Himself through the Word; where out of one Being, two beings proceed: one into the darkness according to the fiery separation and the coarseness of the <term>Impression</term> <note>original: *Impreſſion*; the process by which spiritual desire "compacts" or "hardens" into a form or state of being.</note>, and the other into the Light, according to the Divine quality of Being. These two come from a single ground. Likewise, the false soul and the holy soul both come from Adam’s soul, as from one lump of the ground, though one should understand this only as Spirit in the <term>Great Mystery</term>; yet one soul separates itself into the Light, and the other into the darkness.

2. This potter makes a vessel out of every distinction, for whatever the separated material is useful for. He does not take a holy <term>Essence</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a term used to describe the essential, underlying substance or "seed" of a soul's character.</note> and make a devil out of it Himself; rather, as the <term>Essence</term> of the soul is, so also is the will to the making. God does not sit above the will and shape it as the potter shapes the clay; rather, He generates it out of His own property. Why then should the godless say: "Why did You make me so, that I am evil?"

3. God works a life out of all things: out of an evil <term>Essence</term>, an evil life; and out of a good <term>Essence</term>, a good life—as it is written: *With the holy You are holy, and with the perverse You are perverse.* Psalm 18:27. Therefore, no one can accuse God of having worked an evil life within them. Had the clay been better, He would have made a vessel for honor out of it; but since it served Him for dishonor, He makes of it a vessel of His wrath.

4. For God’s Word is the life, being, and beginning of all things. But because the <term>Wrathful-Zeal</term> <note>original: *Zorn-Eifer*; the aspect of God’s nature that acts as a consuming fire or stern justice.</note> is also within it, it also leads itself into a life—for who shall prevent Him? But for mankind, **Christ** has come as a helper out of the eternal Word, and says: *As truly as I live, I do not desire the death of the sinner, but that he convert and live.* Ezekiel 33:11. But if the soul's <term>Essence</term> were so evil and unfit, and incapable of the Divine <term>Essence</term>, what could **Christ** do? God’s...

<summary>Böhme explains the metaphor of the Potter and the Clay, arguing that God does not arbitrarily create evil, but rather that the "lump" (the soul's essential nature) determines whether it becomes a vessel of honor or wrath.</summary>

# XV. Regarding Chapter 9.
<meta>catchword: Purpose</meta>

Wrath creates no further will outside of the creature, for Christ said (Matthew 28:18): *All power in heaven and on earth has been given to me*; thus Christ now alone has all power in all things. So He also says (John 3:16): *God did not send his Son into the world to judge the world, but that the world might be saved through Him.* Since He now has all power, there is no other maker of dishonor present except that which arises in the <term>Essence</term> <note>original: *Ente*; referring to the underlying substance or "being-ness" of the soul.</note> of the soul from its own center. For it is precisely the wrathful God Himself who creates for Himself an image out of His own being that is like Himself. Therefore Paul says: *Does the potter not have power to make what He will?* This potter is God in the speaking of His <term>Distinction</term> <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; Böhme’s term for the process by which the unified Divine Will differentiates into specific, individual qualities or "distinctions."</note>, through which He reveals His glory, as has been sufficiently proven earlier.

5. Because Christ alone has all power, no other will to "make" anything can exist outside of Him. Therefore, the godless person must not say, "God makes me evil"; rather, the God within him—in whose ground he stands—makes him into whatever he can be, according to the possibility. The ground of his being, which belongs to him, is the beginning; as soon as life is born from it, the "Maker" is in that life as the wrathful God, who becomes manifest to him there and makes him.

6. Just as Christ introduces His will into His children who are born in Him, so also God’s wrath does with its children who are born from it. For God is manifest in the soul, either in Love or in Wrath. Nature is the soul, and the working life is God Himself—understood according to the Word of <term>Distinction</term>.

7. For the pure, clear God without Nature is not a maker of wills, for He is only One. But in His Word, as it introduces itself into Distinction, there the wills toward evil and good <term>originate</term> <note>original: *urſtänden*; a Böhmean verb meaning to take one's stand, arise, or find a foundation.</note>. From every distinction of that which is separated, a will originates according to that same property. Into whatever <term>quality</term> <note>original: *Qual*; Böhme plays on the German words for "quality" (Qualität), "source" (Quelle), and "torment" (Qual), implying a vibrating, active state of being.</note> the groundless will has introduced itself within the Distinction, such a will arises.

8. But Adam led himself out of the <term>Temperature</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; the state of perfect harmony or "tempered" balance between all divine properties before the Fall.</note> and into Distinction. Now his branches <note>meaning his descendants or the lineages of humanity</note> stand in Distinction, from which comes a "new-making" will; every <term>Essence</term> receives a will according to its being; the...

<summary>Böhme explains that God does not "make" people evil from the outside; rather, when a soul moves away from the divine harmony (Temperature), the wrathful property of God’s Word becomes the formative power within that person. He reconciles Christ's universal authority with the existence of evil by describing the "Potter" as the process of divine differentiation.</summary>

It distinguishes between the "Fiery Word" of nature and the "Love-Word" of grace, explaining how God's unified will differentiates into the many properties of the natural world.</meta>

<page-num>137</page-num>
### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

**Purpose**, however, holds the sovereignty—namely, the **fiery word** of nature and the **Love-word** of grace. These two are the makers of the vessel's honor and dishonor, and both are within man.

->The highest portal of Cain and Abel; likewise, of Ishmael and Isaac, and of Esau and Jacob.<-

9. The realm of nature is the ground of the **speaking Word**; for if a creature is to exist, nature must first be present. Thus, the Word of God is the ground of all beings, the beginning of all properties. The Word is the *speaking* of God and remains in God; but the *out-speaking* <note>original: *Aussprechen*; the external expression or manifestation of the internal Divine Word.</note>, as the departure from the Word (where the **groundless will** <note>original: *ungründliche Wille*; Böhme’s term for the *Ungrund*, the absolute, undifferentiated depth of the divine before creation.</note> introduces itself into **distinction** through this out-speaking), that is Nature and Property, and also a self-will. For the groundless will separates itself from the speaking and grasps itself into its own self-speaking, into a state of distinction, as an initial will. From the single, eternal, whole will, the properties have arisen, and from the properties, the creation—as in all creatures.

10. This is now the first **purpose** of God: that the Word of power has set itself forth from itself, as the groundless, inconceivable Word of life, into a **tangibility** <note>original: *Faßlichkeit*; the quality of being graspable, finite, or contained.</note> wherein it may live. This tangibility is Nature, and the inconceivable life within Nature is God’s eternally speaking Word, which remains in God and is God Himself.

11. The other **purpose** of the Word is this: that the tangibility, as the self-contained will, should allow the inconceivable One Will of God to dwell within it. For in this way, the single life has placed itself into tangibility and desires to become manifest within that tangibility. The tangibility must grasp the inconceivable life within itself and make it graspable, just as one has an example of this in **fire and light**. For the fire is nature, acting as the tangible life; it grasps within itself the "un-natural" <note>By "un-natural," Böhme refers to that which is beyond or above the physical laws of nature—the supernatural or divine.</note> life, namely the light. For in the light, the powers of the un-natural life become manifest through the fire; thus, the light then dwells in the fire, and the un-natural life is introduced into the light in power, as in **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; in alchemy, a spiritualizing essence that transforms and perfects matter; here, the spiritual life-force.</note>, air, and water.

12. Therefore, understand also that God’s holy life would not be manifest without nature, but would exist only in an eternal stillness,

<summary>Böhme explains that God's "Purpose" involves two modes of the Divine Word: the fiery Word of nature and the Love-Word of grace, which together form the "vessels" of human character. He argues that nature is the necessary tangible medium through which the otherwise silent and inconceivable Divine Will becomes manifest and active.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

for nothing could be contained within it without the Out-speaking <note>original: *Aussprechen*; the external manifestation of the internal Divine Word.</note> and the **Tangibility**: God’s holiness and love would not be revealed; but if they are to be or become revealed, there must be something that stands in need of love and grace, and which is not identical to that love and grace. This is the will of nature, which stands in opposition within its own life: to this will, love and grace are necessary, so that its painfulness might be transformed into joy.

13. And in this same transformation, the holy, unfathomable life becomes revealed in the Word as a co-operating life within nature: for the painfulness causes the will of the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund: Böhme’s term for the absolute, undifferentiated depth or "abyss" of the divine before it expresses itself in creation.</term> (which has separated itself into its own particularity through the Out-speaking) to once again appropriate itself to the holy, groundless life, so that it may be softened; and in this softening, it becomes revealed in the life of God; for it takes the same into its own desire, and thus the holy life of the **Groundless** also becomes revealed within it.

14. And in this revelation of the holy life within nature, the holy life is called **Power**, and the capacity of nature to grasp it is called **Tincture** <term>Tinctur: Derived from alchemy, this refers to the spiritual essence or "inner light" that can transform and "color" base nature into a divine state.</term>; for it is the power from the radiance of the fire and light: and if this were not so, no fire would be visible, for the self-will of nature is not visible, because its tangibility is a closing-in, and is the ground of darkness.

15. Thus we trace our deep ground to Adam, and further to Cain and Abel. In Adam, the Kingdom of Grace stood as the revealed Divine life, for he stood in the **Temperature** <note>original: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect equilibrium where all the conflicting qualities of nature are balanced and harmonious.</note> of properties: but he did not know that God was revealed within him, for he had known no evil; thus the self-will did not know what was "good," for how could there be joy if there were no knowledge of pain or sadness?

16. This is joy: when nature, as the self-will, is redeemed from its pain; then it rejoices in the Good when it encounters it. But if it had the power to take that same Good by its own strength, it would be no joy, for the self-will would then live as it pleased, and it would have no hope if it were capable of everything itself. But because it is not capable itself, it rejoices in that which happens to it out of grace, or in that which it hopes shall happen to it. All joy stands

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the "Election of Grace," specifically focusing on how the painful struggle of nature is necessary for the manifestation of God’s love. It transitions from the general principle of contrast to the specific historical-mystical narrative of Adam's fall and the promise of the "Serpent-Bruiser" (Christ) as foreshadowed in Abel.</meta>

<page-num>139</page-num>
# Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

in the hope of grace, which is granted to him continually (without the power of his own ability or his own taking).

17. And for this reason, nature exists in pain and strife so that the kingdom of grace, which is love, might be revealed within it, and that nature might become a kingdom of joy. This joy arises from that which is continually granted to it, as **God's** life is revealed within nature. Through this, nature attains a holy <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; in Böhme’s thought, a spiritual essence or catalyst that transforms and "colors" the base, painful qualities of nature into a divine state.</note>, which "tints" or transforms the pain, changing it into joy, as an image of the holy life.

18. When Adam stood in "equality," he did not know this; he did not know what evil was within nature, and thus he also knew nothing of the kingdom of grace, for they both stood in the <term>Temperature</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect balance or equilibrium where no single element or "quality" dominates another.</note>. But when his free will introduced itself into the <term>separability</term> <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the state where the unified powers of God's Word begin to differentiate and pull apart from one another.</note> of the Word of powers, then the painfulness of the kingdom of nature was revealed within him. Here it became necessary that the power of grace also move within him, which the kingdom of nature could not do on its own: for there is no such possibility within its own will, because its will is **tangible**, whereas the kingdom of grace is **intangible**. Therefore the soul, as the tangible will, could take nothing from the intangible life; but in that case, **God** would have remained hidden in this image and would not have been revealed.

19. Therefore, the intangible holy life spoke itself—in its love—into the tangible life of the soul, so that it might have something to love; and it formed itself into the properties of the soulful nature to be a helper.

20. And this was the **Serpent-Bruiser** <note>original: *Schlangen-Treter*; a reference to Genesis 3:15, the "seed of the woman" who crushes the serpent's head, interpreted here as the divine love-principle entering the soul.</note>, who intended to crush the head of the serpent’s introduced poison and the will of painfulness with the desire of love. This same intangibility came to the aid of the kingdom of nature and placed itself within the "figure": and now nature, hungry for grace, allowed itself to be enclosed within an image of the natural soul and body.

21. And of this image, Abel was a figure in the likeness of Christ, until the fullness of time when that same intangibility of love moved once more and enclosed itself in an <term>Ens</term> <note>Latin: *Ens*; "being" or "essence." Böhme uses it to describe the spiritual substance that takes on human properties.</note> of the substance (in human property), so that the Godhead itself might be a substance within the human substance. This substance was indeed already lying within Adam, but he did not know it; and when he led himself away from this substance with the self-will of nature, the soul became blind to **God** and lived only in itself.

<meta>catchword: 22. Thus</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that Adam's fall from a state of balance (Temperature) into the "painfulness" of nature was necessary for God's love to be revealed as Grace. He introduces the "Serpent-Bruiser" as the divine love-essence that enters the tangible soul to rescue it, a process prefigured in the biblical Abel.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the "Election of Grace" by applying the cosmological principles of Chapter 9 to the biblical figures of Cain and Abel. It moves from the abstract necessity of contrast within nature to the historical struggle between the "natural man" (Cain) and the "spiritual man" (Abel) as a precursor to Christ’s incarnation.</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

22. If we now wish to see, and not make ourselves blind, we can see Cain and Abel: Cain must be the firstborn, for he is the image of Adam after the Fall; for Adam had been created into the Kingdom of **God**.

23. Cain is in the Kingdom of Nature, as a true image of what Adam was in himself, apart from grace: and Abel is the image of what Adam was in the re-spoken grace <note>original: *wieder-eingesprochenen Gnade*; Böhme views God's promise of salvation as a "speaking" of divine power back into the corrupted human soul</note>. This points toward Christ, who wished to give Himself into a human nature, and to "speak" the grace of the corrupted nature into Cain's image.

24. Therefore Christ said that all power had been given to Him by His Father, so that He might have the authority to speak grace into the will of nature.

25. Thus **God** presented the figure with Cain and Abel, as well as with Ishmael and Isaac, and also in Esau and Jacob: how **God** intended to send Christ into the flesh—whom He had here in Adam and Eve, in the voice of His Word, spoken into them with power as a fountain for life.

26. He wished to fulfill that same power with a human being, which happened in Christ; to which man, Christ, in that same power and voice, authority was given to abolish sin through His own voice, and to make nature alive again within Him with a Divine Life.

27. But for this to happen, grace—in the power of love—had to enter into the adversity of the painful nature <note>original: *peinlichen Natur*; referring to the state of nature when it is in conflict and lacks the balancing light of God</note>, and surrender itself within its own will, so that it might take hold of nature: and in this taking-hold by the high love, nature was transmuted into the Divine Love-Will, and died to its own self-centered will; not as a death of perishing, but as a losing of its own will, which happened in Christ within our humanity.

28. Now, when the self-will loses its right, the "spoken word" becomes substantial <note>original: *wesentlich*; meaning it takes on actual essence or being rather than remaining a mere sound</note>, which cannot happen before the self-will of the <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; from the Latin *scientia*, but used by Böhme to mean an "active knowledge" or the formative, creative "drawing" power of the soul's will</note> of the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>original: *Ungrund*; the "Groundless" or "Abyss"—God’s existence prior to all manifestation, which has no foundation or beginning</note> surrenders its right: otherwise, it draws the Divine <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; Latin for "being" or "essence"</note> into selfhood and changes it into its own malice, as Lucifer and his followers did; they were angels and had the Divine Essence within them, in which their light was a radiance, but the Self-Will from the <term>Scienz</term> of the <term>Ungrund</term> corrupted it.

<meta>Catchword: 29. Who</meta>

<summary>Böhme interprets the biblical figures of Cain and Abel as archetypes of the "natural" and "spiritual" states of humanity, explaining that Christ's role is to "re-speak" divine grace into the corrupted human will. He details the mystical process by which the human self-will must surrender to divine love to be transmuted into a holy essence.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Election of Grace" by examining the specific case of Cain. It argues that Cain possessed the internal voice of grace but was rendered "deaf" to it not by God’s decree, but by the corruption of his own will and the "serpent’s poison" inherited from the Fall.</meta>

<page-num>141</page-num>
<header>Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.</header>

29. Who can now tell us with any foundation that the Divine voice of grace (which echoed within the Seed of the Woman <note>The "Seed of the Woman" refers to the promise in Genesis 3:15, which Böhme interprets as a spiritual potency or "voice" of Christ implanted in humanity after the Fall.</note>) did not lie within Cain? Which scripture says that? Answer: Truly none. For when **God** looked upon his offering without favor, he raged within himself against Abel, who was the figure of Christ that had separated from him out of Adam’s essence <note>original: *Ente*; likely a contraction of *Entität* (entity) or *Essenz* (essence), referring to the biological and spiritual lineage of Adam.</note>. Thus, the embodied voice of grace spoke within him: *Rule over sin, and do not let it have power over you.* For **God's** purpose according to His wrath would not say this within him, but rather the embodied voice of grace.

30. But how did it happen that Cain did not rule over sin? Was he then unable to? Answer: No, he could not. Why could he not? Had **God** hardened him so that he was unable? Answer: **God** had not hardened him; rather, the Adam-like self-will, arising from the knowledge <term>Scientz: the inner cognitive power or "perceiving" of the soul</term> of the Groundless <term>Ungrund: Böhme’s term for the absolute, undifferentiated divine abyss before creation</term>, had led itself—through imagination—into animal vanity. It entered into a self-made formation of good and evil, into which the Devil had cast the serpent's poisonous essence <term>Ens: a Latin term for "being" or "substance"</term>, which Eve had taken in.

31. This was the hardening within his own will. For the purpose of **God** according to fierce nature <note>Böhme distinguishes between God's "Light" (love/grace) and His "Fierceness" (the wrathful foundation of nature).</note> had taken hold within Cain and made him deaf, so that he could not hear the embodied voice of grace. For even though he heard it from the outside, he did not hear it in the essence <note>original: *Ente*</note> of the soul; otherwise, grace would have been stirred, so that the soul would have ruled over the serpent's poison. He thought he would and should rule over sin from the outside, which is why he exalted himself over Abel.

32. Just as modern Reason <note>original: *Vernunft*; Böhme uses this term to describe human intellect divorced from spiritual revelation.</note> thinks it can reach divine sonship from the outside in an assumed manner—as if through external works, or through a "cloak of grace" <note>original: *Gnaden-Decke*; a critique of the theological idea that grace merely covers sin without inwardly transforming the person.</note> under Christ’s suffering and death, as an external satisfaction for sin. People think they need only comfort themselves and accept this from the outside, even while the self-will remains a lodging-place for the serpent’s poison. But this is worth as much as it was for Cain, unless the inward ground is stirred, so that grace becomes active in the soul as the embodied voice of **God** in the Seed of the Woman—which is Christ in us—so that the soul hears **God’s** voice moving within its own essence <note>original: *Essentz*</note>.

33. Thus Reason speaks: If the voice of grace in

<summary>Böhme argues that Cain's failure to rule over sin was not due to a divine decree of hardening, but because his self-will and the "serpent's poison" made him spiritually deaf to the internal voice of grace. He warns against a "cloak of grace" that seeks salvation through external belief rather than internal transformation.</summary>

<page-num>242</page-num>
# XV. Regarding Chapter 9.
<meta>catchword: for</meta>

If Cain lay under the shroud of sin, did not **GOD’s** "speaking-into" move him when He said: *Rule over sin, and do not let it have power over you*? For if He had moved the inner ground of the soul with the embodied voice of grace, and if he had heard it within the soul (which is the master of the body), would not the external ground have been unable to rise up?

34. **Answer.** This voice, which came to Cain—*Rule over sin and do not let it have power over you*—was **GOD’s** Justice in His purpose; namely, in the speaking Word, where the Divine voice wills that the self-will of the *science* <term>Scientz: the soul's inner power of attraction and sensory perception</term> of the groundless eternal will should lead itself into a Divine birthing toward the Good. That same Word is demanded by **GOD’s** Justice, for He does not will evil; it is the true ground of the Law in the Old Testament. However, it does not reach Grace, for it demands one's own ability <note>Böhme argues that the Law demands human effort, which the fallen soul cannot provide on its own.</note>. Nor does it surrender itself to Grace, for **God** has no need of grace; rather, Grace must surrender itself into Justice, as into **GOD’s** Justice. Just as Grace—which was revealed in Christ as the embodied voice of grace—had to surrender itself into **GOD’s** Justice, namely into the eternal, single purpose for the revelation of the glory of **GOD** in His speaking Word, as in the *differentiation* <term>Schiedlichkeit: the process by which the unified Divine Will divides into a variety of forms or "distinctions"</term> of the Father. It had to lead the will of man (which had deviated from the purpose of Justice) into the wrath-fire of **GOD** in and with itself. It had to lead the Father, as the purpose of **GOD**, into His Justice as the original state of the soul. And it had to drown the soul's will (which had deviated from Justice) in his blood, out of the Divine holy essence <note>original: "Ente"; likely a contraction of "Entität" (entity) or "Ens" (essence)</note> of love, so that the soul might be revealed in Grace, in that same blood of love, within the purpose of Justice.

35. And for this reason, Christ had to suffer and die within the Justice of **GOD**, in our humanity, and in us, so that Grace might be revealed within Justice. For in Cain, Grace was not yet revealed in the Justice of **GOD**, because it had not yet taken a soul into itself—not until Grace in Christ accepted the soul.

36. Thus, the Justice of **GOD** lay in the soul,

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.
<meta>catchword: soul</meta>

for the soul was **GOD’s** image: thus **GOD** demanded His justice from the soul, requiring that it should rule over evil, just as **GOD** ruled over the rebellious will of the devils and cast them out from the good order of justice when they became apostate. In the same way, Cain was supposed to cast out the torment of sin from himself; but it was not possible for him, for sin had possessed him as a free-will agent. Human ability was lost: and it now lay in the other decree of the "spoken-in" justice <note>Böhme refers to the "Ingrafted Word" or the presence of God's voice within the soul.</note> within Grace, that the soul should give its will over to that Grace and stand still before that same "Speaking-in." For in the speaking of **GOD’s** justice, there was now nothing but distress and reluctance within the soul. For Justice demanded the *temperature* <term>Temperatur: the perfect, balanced state of all inner qualities, which Böhme believes was lost at the Fall</term>; namely, that man should stand still as **GOD’s** instrument, through which He wished to manifest His voice. But the instrument was broken and had departed from Divine Harmony; therefore, it no longer depended on Cain’s willing, running, or racing, but on Grace, as on Mercy <note>A reference to Romans 9:16: "It does not, therefore, depend on human desire or effort, but on God’s mercy."</note>.

37. Thus says St. Paul: *He has mercy on whom He will, and hardens whom He will.* <note>original: "Er erbarmet sich welcher Er will, und verstocket welche Er will." See Romans 9:18.</note> In this lies the entire ground of the error in human Reason; for Reason does not understand the "Willing of Grace" or how it comes to pass. For whatever Grace wills, that is also one single Will with Grace itself.

38. For Grace has no "willing" in the devil, or in hell, but rather in that which is born of **GOD**: the "Willing of Grace" is not in the will of flesh and blood, nor in the will of man’s own seed; but in the Divine *Essence* <term>Ente: from the Latin 'Ens', meaning the spiritual substance or essential being of a thing</term>. Grace did not wish to "speak itself" into the serpent-seed introduced into Cain, but rather to crush the head of that seed; it did not wish to crush the head of the poor captive soul in Cain (for that soul was also sprouted from Adam’s soul), but rather the serpent’s seed within Cain's soul. However, the serpent’s venom had so hardened and overtaken the soul that the soul stirred itself within that venom and surrendered itself to the Wrath of Justice, so that Justice took hold of it and used it as an instrument. Thus, Justice—acting within Grace—slew the man Christ, as prefigured in His archetype, Abel.

39. For through human works, sin had entered into the soul...

come from the soul: thus it also had to be killed through human works, within Grace, and within **GOD’s** Justice, just as occurred in the humanity of *Christ* through the act of killing by the *Pharisees* <note>original: "Pharisern"</note>, who carried and possessed the Law of **GOD’s** Justice.

40. Therefore *Abel*, as a prototype of *Christ*, and also *Christ* himself, had to die within **GOD’s** Justice through the human works of the self-centered Adamic will. Those whom **GOD’s** Justice had seized in the wrath <note>original: "Grimme," referring to the stern or "dark" side of God's nature in Böhme's cosmology</note> of His purpose had to be the instruments so that the Grace of **GOD**, within the Justice of His decree, would be revealed in the midst of that anger. For it is written in *Matthew 18:7*: Woe to the person because of offenses, yet offenses must come, so that Justice and Truth may be revealed in the midst of untruth.

41. For Grace would otherwise not be revealed if the False were not an opposite to the Truth. Just as the free will could not have been revealed in Grace if Justice had not killed that same will—which Grace then made alive within itself (after the will lost its self-chosen direction)—so that it no longer wills or lives for itself, but lives and wills for Grace, which was revealed in *Christ*.

42. Therefore, in *Christ* (in the life of Grace) we are all but One; for we have lost the natural life of **GOD’s** Justice in His eternal decree and received the sonship <term>Kindschaft: the state of being a child of God, often translated as "adoption" or "divine sonship"</term> in Grace.

43. Therefore the Scripture says: **GOD** wants all people to be helped <note>A common 17th-century rendering of "saved."</note>. *1 Timothy 2:4.* Namely, Grace wills this, for it can will nothing other than mercy; otherwise, it would be nothing in its own essence.

44. But natural Justice in the decree of **GOD** demands the soul's obedience to the Divine Order without Grace; for the soul was not created into Grace, but into the Order. Now, where Justice does not find the soul within that order, it claims the soul in its own quality of the distinguishability <term>Schiedlichkeit: Böhme’s term for the way the unified "Word" of God differentiates itself into various distinct qualities or "voices"</term> of the Word, of which the soul is a being <note>original: "Wesen"</note>. For instance, if the soul is a false *entity* <term>Ens: a Latin philosophical term meaning a "being" or "essence"</term>, Justice takes on that same likeness; this is also to be understood in *Cain*, where the deviated Adamic will introduced itself into a creaturely self-interest: and the intro-

45. Just as the essence of the sun must allow itself to be pricked by the thistle, yet the sun withdraws its "good will" from it—that is, the holy life which it would otherwise manifest in a good herb—and lets the thistle make what it will of its essence. So it also goes with the godless thistle-essence of mankind, as the Scripture says in *Proverbs 20:20*: He lets their light be extinguished in the midst of darkness <note>Böhme’s reading of Proverbs 20:20 emphasizes the withdrawal of divine light from the self-hardened soul</note>, referring to the holy life within the indwelling Voice of Grace.

46. You might ask: Why is that? For if He manifested the holy life within them, would the soul not then become holy? Answer: No; we have an example in the Devil, in whom the holy life was once manifest, but his will-essence was a thistle. Thus, a "child of the thistle" uses Grace only for pride, like Lucifer; for **GOD** knows the science <term>science <gloss>original: "Scienß," meaning the active quality, inner knowledge, or dynamic potential of a being</gloss></term> of the Groundless <term>Groundless <gloss>original: "Ungrund," Böhme’s term for the absolute, unmanifested divine infinity that precedes all creation</gloss></term>, and how it has formed or revealed itself into a "Ground"—whether it be a root out of the darkness, as from the dark fire-life, or a root out of the luminous fire-life.

47. Then you might say: So Cain is a root out of the dark fire, and therefore he cannot attain Grace? Answer: No, for he was from Adam’s soul; but the dark fire from the Wrath <note>original: "Zorne" or "Grimme," the stern, foundational quality of God's nature in Böhme's system</note>, or the property of the dark world, had forced itself into the true soul. This did not happen from the outside, but swung itself upward from the Center <note>the "Centrum," or the internal source where the soul's properties are generated</note>, specifically during the Fall of Adam. Cain came from this root; therefore, he had to be a servant of **GOD’s** Justice, so that Justice might kill the free will in Abel within Grace.

48.
