# XV. Concerning Chapter 9

...said: You shall die, if you eat of Good and Evil) <note>A reference to God's warning to Adam in Genesis 2:17</note>; this one entered into death, and into that death God spoke His voice, so that the first will, in Grace, might become living again; and from this came Abel.

49. The other will (reborn in sin), which had not existed in the beginning but had arisen in the Fall, separated itself into the life of nature; that was Cain. Therefore, this will was a thistle-child, whom **GOD** had not created, but who had instead proceeded out of the center <term>center <gloss>original: "Centro," referring to the innermost point of origin or the "ground" of the soul's being</term> of the soul.

50. According to the essence of the soul, they both, Abel and Cain, came from one essence; but according to the will, they separated. Not that Abel was born pure and without sin, for sin clung to him in the will of death—though it is not truly a death <note>Böhme suggests that for the righteous, physical death is not a finality but a transformation</note>; rather, the voice of righteousness in Grace killed it, so that it might make him alive in itself. But in the flesh, the will of sin was manifest; therefore, the righteousness of **GOD** killed him through Cain, for according to the flesh he was also subject to the law of sin. But in the soul's will, the voice of Grace (within him) had killed [the sin] and made it alive in itself; therefore he was also a prototype of Christ, standing within the image of Christ.

51. Therefore, this is the true ground of Cain’s hardening <term>hardening <gloss>original: "Verstockung," often translated in theological contexts as obduracy or the state of being "hardened" against God</term>: that **GOD** did not harden him out of His Divine will—for He cannot, because He alone is good—but rather the newly arisen will from the center of the soul hardened itself in its own desire. For as the desire entered into its own likeness within the wrath of nature, it found its likeness in the design of nature (as in the separation of darkness and light). This likeness took hold of him and possessed him—understand, the newly arisen false will, which was a murderer and a servant of **GOD’s** wrath. But the true created, formed soul from Adam’s essence, in which the embodied voice of **GOD** lay, was not yet judged or predestined to damnation, as reason so erroneously believes (which judgment belongs to no man, but to the righteousness of **GOD**).

# Chapter 9. Divine Election

->K 2<-
<meta>catchword: 58. So</meta>

52. And it is not at all as some think, as if Cain were born from the Devil's will or from the serpent's seed. Rather, he was born from Adam's soul and body; but Adam’s adopted natural will governed him. He was an image of the fallen, unregenerate Adam, in whom the Promise and the spoken Divine Voice lay within without an actual life, as a true possibility for the new birth. But this same possibility was not in Cain’s power according to the false <note>The "false" will refers to the egoistic, self-centered will that emerged after the Fall</note> will; instead, it lay in the ground of the soul and waited for Christ’s voice. Christ, in that same possibility, awakened Himself in the precious name **JESUS** and accepted the poor sinners into grace. With His voice, He called into the "locked-up" sinners and awakened that same still-standing ground of the first indwelling word <note>original: "Einsprechung" — this refers to the "breathing into" or the divine word planted in the soul during creation</note>, just as happened with the thief on the cross and many others.

53. For if it were true that **GOD**, in His preordained will, had hardened Cain, then no judgment through the justice of **GOD** could come upon Cain, nor could any curse have entered into him; for what **GOD’S** purpose creates, **GOD’S** justice does not curse, as happened to Cain.

54. For justice is the order of the initial spoken Word <note>The "spoken Word" refers to the creative act of God that established the laws of existence</note>, ensuring that all things remain in the order in which the "Speaking" introduced them into life. Nothing falls into judgment if it remains within the order in which it was created.

55. If a will arising from **GOD’S** purpose (understand: from the Divine purpose) had hardened Adam and Cain, then Justice would have no objection, for this will of hardening would then exist within the Divine order.

56. Therefore, the will toward hardening in Adam and Cain arose during the Fall, in the inequality of the divided properties <term>properties <gloss>original: "Eigenschaften," referring to the spiritual forces or qualities—such as heat, light, or bitterness—that became unbalanced within the soul after the Fall</term>, where each property grasped itself within the essence and darkened and killed the image of **GOD** in the light.

57. **GOD’S** purpose is the center <term>center <gloss>original: "Centrum," the innermost point of origin</gloss></term> of the human ground <note>The "ground" (Grund) refers to the fundamental, underlying essence of the soul</note>, which is the spoken and counter-spoken Word of **GOD**. That same captured human will was indeed hardened in that very purpose of **GOD**, as the Scripture says. But no one wants to understand the "ground"; instead, people only say, "**GOD’S** purpose does it," and no one wants to search out the basis of that purpose—to see that it lies within man himself, and not in **GOD**.

<summary>This page refutes the idea of "predestined" damnation for Cain, arguing that his hardening was a result of the fallen human will breaking the divine order, not a choice made by God. Böhme emphasizes that the potential for grace remains in the "ground" of every soul, waiting for the voice of Christ.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological argument against the idea of divine predestination to evil. Following the discussion of Cain and Abel on page 147, the author now addresses why God cannot be the source of "hardening" or sin, distinguishing between God’s essential nature of love and the "Center of Nature" where wrath resides.</meta>

<page-num>148</page-num>
<header>XV. Regarding Chapter 9.</header>

58. If **GOD** had possessed a preordained purpose for the Devil, then that same purpose would be the Devil’s own will. However, in the differentiation of the divine expression, the purpose toward malice emerged into a specific <term>Principle</term><note>In this context, a "Principle" refers to one of the fundamental realms of existence—light, dark, or the material world—within the author's cosmology.</note>. It became manifest within itself through its own established differentiation out of the <term>Great Mystery</term><note>original: "Mysterio Magno" — This term refers to the vast, unformed potentiality or the "chaos" from which God brings forth all created things, both light and dark.</note>. It is according to this that **GOD** calls Himself a "wrathful **GOD**," yet this wrath is not **GOD** in His essence. Rather, it is the <term>Center of Nature</term><note>original: "Centrum der Natur" — This represents the raw, foundational forces of existence which, when separated from God's light, manifest as fire and wrath.</note>, acting as the cause for the divine revelation of the Kingdom of Joy; for in **GOD** proper, no wrath is manifest, but only a burning love.

59. For if there were a will toward hardening <note>original: "Verstockung" — often used in a theological sense to mean the spiritual state of being closed off from grace, or "reprobation."</note> within **GOD**, then these scriptural verses would not be true: *Psalm 5:5*, "You are not a **GOD** who is pleased by wicked ways." Also, *Ezekiel 18:23* and *33:11*, "As surely as I live, I do not desire the death of the sinner." Likewise, the Ten Commandments, which forbid evil, would be contradicted.

60. If **GOD** had willed for Cain to kill Abel, then the fifth commandment would not be right; furthermore, **GOD** pronounced a heavy punishment upon Cain, saying: "Whoever sheds human blood, by humans shall their blood be shed" (*Genesis 9:6*). If He willed it to be so, then no one would need to keep His commandments. Where, then, would His justice and the judgment in truth remain? *Hosea 13:9* says in the Scriptures: "Israel, your ruin comes from yourself."

61. Thus, we should condemn no one, but only the vices and sins that appear openly in the godless. For these arise from the self-will that originated in Cain and Adam—out of the <term>Center of the Dark World</term>. **GOD** did not manifest or give birth to this will in humanity in the beginning; rather, the Devil is to blame for it.

62. We should condemn this false will in its essence and deeds, but not the poor soul, which lies hidden in this heavy prison within the indwelling <term>Voice of Grace</term><note>original: "Gnaden-Stimme" — This refers to the restorative "word" of God planted in the human heart after the Fall to allow for redemption.</note>. This Voice of Grace, according to its first embodiment in Paradise after the Fall, may well be awakened through the voice of Christ. This happens through His children in whom the Spirit of Christ dwells, just as occurred with the thief on the cross, the tax collector, and also Mary Magdalene, and many hundreds of thousands of poor imprisoned souls. For the Scripture says (*1 Timothy 1:15*): "It is a precious and worthy word, that **JESUS** Christ has come into the world to save all poor sinners." And *Revelation 3:20* states: "He stands at the door and knocks,"

<summary>The author argues that God’s essential nature is pure love, and that human sin and "hardening" arise from the self-will of the "dark world" rather than divine decree. He emphasizes that while we must condemn sin, we must hold out hope for the "poor soul" which contains a hidden seed of grace that Christ can awaken.</summary>

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

namely, at the door of the poor imprisoned soul; and *Matthew 11:28*: "Come to me, all who are weary and burdened, and I will give you rest."

63. He stands in the internal foundation of grace <term>spoken into</term><note>original: "eingesprochenem." In this theology, the "Word" of God is literally breathed or spoken into the core of human existence as a seed of potential salvation.</note> Adam, in the center of the soul, and calls to it as long as the soul carries the body upon the earth, to see if the poor soul might wish to turn toward Him; then He says: (if it happens that the soul turns toward Him) "Knock, and it will be opened to you"; knock at the first voice of grace that has been incorporated into you, and it will be moved. Likewise: "Ask, and you shall receive." Likewise: "My Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it." *Luke 11:9-13*.

64. Thus, it does not now depend on <term>self-ability</term><note>original: "Selber-Können." This refers to the human ego’s attempt to achieve salvation through its own power or works, which the author argues is impossible.</note> and taking; but rather on asking and knocking, for the promise of grace has been "spoken into" the act of asking in Christ Jesus, so that it wishes to give itself over to the one who asks. For it stands written: "Christ has come to seek and to make blessed that which is lost." *Matthew 15:24* and *18:11*.

65. Question: Who are the lost? Answer: Cain, Ishmael, Esau, and all hardened people imprisoned in sin; these Christ has come to seek and to make blessed, and He wills that they should not be lost. But the self-born, false murderer in Cain He does not want, nor the scoffer in Ishmael, just as He also does not want the hunter in Esau; rather, He wants the true foundation of the first-born souls in which the voice of grace lies.

66. Because He does not want the scoffer Ishmael, He cast him out of the house with his mother—understand this "scoffer in Ishmael" as the self-conceived and evil will that arose in Adam, together with Hagar, who represents the <term>fragmented nature</term><note>original: "schiedliche Natur." This refers to nature when it is divided against itself or separated from the divine unity, leading to conflict and "wrath."</note>; understand by this the divided property of nature.

67. First, Hagar fled from Sarah and would not let herself be disciplined, for she wanted to rule with the scoffer in Abraham’s goods. But when she came into the wilderness, the Angel of **GOD** spoke to her: "Where have you come from, Sarah’s maid?" And she said: "I have fled from my mistress." And He told her to turn back and humble herself before her mistress; and He spoke further to her: "I will so multiply your seed that it shall not be numbered for its great multitude; you are pregnant and shall bear a son, whose name you shall call Ishmael."

This refers to the state of perfect balance between the internal qualities of a being, which was lost at the Fall.</note>—and in his own desire (that is, in his own self-generated will), he became pregnant with the "Mocker" <note>Ishmael is referred to as "the Mocker" based on Genesis 21:9.</note>. But the properties of nature were not for that reason severed from God, as can be seen here with Hagar; for the Angel spoke to her: He would multiply her seed so greatly that it could not be numbered for the multitude, but she must turn back to the Free Woman and humble herself under her hand. > "I have heard your misery, you poor imprisoned soul, in this wilderness. Turn back; indeed, you are pregnant with the Mocker from the Kingdom of Nature of your <term>constellation</term><note>original: "Constellarion" (likely a typo for Constellation). This refers to the astrological and natural influences that shape a person's earthly character.</note> and you shall give birth to him. But I will bless you, and from the Kingdom of Nature you shall give birth to twelve princes who shall come into my blessing." This signifies the twelve Apostles of Christ who come in His blessing, whose seed cannot be numbered for the great multitude; and how the poor sinner, if he only turns back at this call of the Angel, enters into that same twelve-fold Apostolic grace. However, he must go back to the Free Woman with the soul’s will; but the Mocker is born in his constellation with a self-will, which will shall not inherit the Kingdom of God.

What Abraham gave to Hagar and Ishmael now signifies the true gift in Paradise.

70. When Adam was cast out, God gave him the gift beforehand—the <term>spoken Word of Grace</term><note>original: "eingesprochene Gnaden-Wort." In Böhme’s theology, this is the "inner promise" or seed of Christ placed into humanity immediately after the Fall.</note>—and within that same gift stood the blessing. No new creature can emerge within man, for even though the soul’s fragmented life-forms produce a "Mocker," the inward ground remains the Word of God.

71. Therefore, nature shall not perish; rather, only the false, self-originated will born of <term>inequality</term><note>original: "Ungleichheit." This refers to the disharmony or imbalance of the internal properties (the "Qualitäten") after the Fall.</note> must be cast out and die. The state of perfect harmony or divine balance.</note> or God’s Kingdom) and had become blind to God; but now I have seen the one who looked upon me in my misery with his gaze of grace." That is, he looked into me *afterward*, when I was already blind to the sight of God. This "afterward" refers to the time when the Kingdom of Nature had already become a "Mocker" with its new self-will. Because of this, she called that well a "Well of the Living One who has looked upon me," which well is located between Kadesh and Bered.

# XV. Concerning Chapter 9. 152

...[the well] of the Living One, as in the center of the soul, in its inward ground. For the outward man may indeed be a mocker; but God willed to bring forth twelve princes from his inward ground (into which Grace had been incorporated), whose seed would be innumerable. However, outwardly, Nature would stand in the dominion of twelve princes of the corrupted nature, as twelve princes then came forth from him externally. Thus, the Spirit of God in Moses points to the inward ground, and we see this clearly before our eyes.

73. For when Ishmael was born, the outward ground followed the corrupted kingdom of nature; he was a mocker, and God commanded him to be cast out. But when he was cast out, and Hagar had moved the boy away from her so that she would not have to see him die in the wilderness, the boy Ishmael lay there and wept. Then God heard the voice of the boy, and the Angel of God called to Hagar from heaven and said: "What ails you, Hagar? Do not fear, for God has heard the voice of the boy where he lies. Arise, take the boy and lead him by the hand, for I will make him into a great nation." And God opened her eyes so that she saw a well of water; then she went and filled the flask with water and gave the boy a drink, and God was with the boy (Gen. 21:17-20). And they dwelt in the wilderness of Beersheba by the Well of the Living and Seeing One <note>original: "Brunnen des Lebendigen und Sehenden" — a reference to the well Beer-lahai-roi mentioned in Genesis.</note>.

74. This symbolic figure is as clear as day and manifest against those erroneous opinions that judge and condemn Ishmael; it could not be clearer, if only they would look past their erroneous opinions. For the mocker Ishmael in the outward kingdom of nature was indeed evil and cast out from the status of childhood; but when he lay there and wept (which signifies <term>repentance</term><note>original: "Busse." In Böhme's thought, this is not just being sorry, but the active turning of the soul's will back toward the divine light.</note>), then God opened the eyes of Hagar (representing the kingdom of inner nature, according to the soul) within the incorporated <term>Well of Grace</term><note>original: "Gnaden-Brunnen."</note>, so that she saw the wellspring of Christ and gave the boy—the poor soul—a drink from the well at Beersheba, that is, within the divided properties of life.

75. This drinking signifies both Baptism and Circumcision, wherein Christ wished to give the divided <term>configurations of life</term><note>original: "Lebens-Gestalten." These are the fundamental spiritual forces or "qualities" that Böhme believes constitute all reality.</note> a drink in their thirst from His well. But Ismae-

# Chapter 9. Election by Grace.

...of the mocker according to the outward nature, should be cut off by circumcision, which occurs through repentance and the casting away of the mocking will: then Christ baptizes with the Holy Spirit from the Well of the Living and Seeing One; then the soul dwells by that same well, and God is with it, just as He was with Ishmael.

76. For it is not the mocking will that is the seed which God blessed, but rather the inner ground within the gift of grace. For God said to Abraham: "In Isaac shall your seed be blessed," meaning that in Christ, Ishmael shall have the blessing. For the corrupted nature-will shall not be the heir in God’s Kingdom, but shall always be cast out. However, nature in its ground and original state <note>original: "Urstande" — a term used by Böhme to describe the primal, uncorrupted source of a thing's being.</note> is God’s Word, as the spoken Word in its differentiation <term>Schiedlichkeit: Böhme's concept of how the unified Divine Word expresses itself through variety and distinct forms.</term>, within which the fountainhead of life is from JEHOVAH; as the source of love sprung forth in the name of JESUS, that shall inherit it.

77. This inward nature also points to Japheth (Gen. 9:27), of whom the spirit of Noah said that he should dwell in Shem’s tents—namely, in Isaac’s, which is to say, in Christ’s well. Shem’s tent signifies the new birth from Christ, into which Japheth and Ishmael were to come; for the text says: "And God was with the boy Ishmael," but not with the mocker; rather, He was in the inward ground, which was to be revealed in Christ. Since God was with him, and he along with his mother lived by the Well of the Living One—as in Christ within His gift of grace—who then will condemn him, as the erroneous world does? Quite rightly is the outward Ishmael (as the will of mockery) condemned, but not Abraham’s inherited right nature from the blessing; rather, it is Abraham’s earthly will, from the serpent’s seed <note>The "serpent's seed" refers to the corrupted nature inherited after the Fall of Man.</note>, that is rejected.

78. For Ishmael is an image of the Kingdom of Nature, according to the poor corrupted Adam, who must die and decay within us, but must rise again according to the first-created image in Christ, leaving the mocker Ishmael in the earth. And Isaac is an image of the new man in the humanity of Christ, where Adam’s nature and Christ are within one another; there the false will is dead in Christ; although Adam’s nature is present there, it lives in the Spirit of Christ. <note>original: "Gal. 2:20" — "I have been crucified with Christ; it is no longer I who live, but Christ lives in me."</note>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of Chapter 9 of Genesis, moving from the figure of Ishmael to the figure of Jacob and Esau. Böhme is explaining how the biblical patriarchs represent different aspects of the human soul and the process of divine redemption.</meta>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

79. Therefore **JESUS** took on Adam’s nature, but not Adam’s self-born false will; rather, He took on the poor, divided form of life within nature, within **God’s** righteousness and purpose, so that the first Adam might stand firm in his righteousness through **Christ**.

80. Thus, **Ishmael** arose from the image of **God’s** righteousness, which He created in **Adam**: and **Isaac** arose in the image of Grace, which surrendered itself into **God’s** righteousness in **Christ**, filling it with love and stilling the wrath. For **Christ** was meant to transform the mocker within **Ishmael**—who had become manifest in **God’s** righteousness—with the love-tincture <note>original: "Liebe-Tinctur" — In Böhme's spiritual alchemy, a 'tincture' is a penetrating power that can transform the inner quality of a substance or soul.</note> of His blood, so that he could return to sonship in **Christ**. From this sonship, righteousness had cast him out (as if from **Abraham’s** goods) as one excluded from the inheritance of the nature of the formed and spoken Word of **God**.

81. The figure of **Jacob** and **Esau** is now the counterpart, showing how **Christ** would be cast out of the kingdom of nature (that is, from its inborn false will). For when He had taken our sin upon Himself in the Adam-like nature—understand here the fountainhead <note>original: "Quell" — the source or spring from which qualities arise.</note> from which sin gushed forth, namely the divided life-forms in human nature—He spoke afterward in **John 18:36**: *My kingdom is not of this world*—meaning it is not within the divided four elements, but in the Temperature <term>Temperatur: A state of perfect balance and harmony where all conflicting qualities are unified; the "pure element" of the heavenly world.</term>.

82. Because **Christ** had taken on humanity in its divided properties, the righteousness of the outward order would not tolerate Him within itself; for He had sprouted from a different righteousness—the heavenly one—and entered our poor humanity in the property of this world to help us.

83. Therefore He says: *The Son of Man has nowhere to lay His head*, **Matthew 8:20.** yet He also said that *all power in heaven and on earth had been given to Him by His Father*; **Matthew 28:18.** by this, He meant the inner ground of all being, namely Eternity, which lies hidden in this world and was revealed in **Christ**. That same revelation was not "at home" in this world, and it possessed none of this world’s essence as a personal holding or property.

84. This image—of how **Christ** was to be cast out and driven from this world—was **Jacob**, whom his

<meta>catchword: brother</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that Christ took on human nature to harmonize its "divided" qualities and that the exile of Jacob prefigures Christ's own status as a stranger to the fallen world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 9, moving from the rejection of Ishmael to the reconciliation between Jacob and Esau. Böhme uses this biblical narrative to illustrate how the harsh "righteousness of nature" (represented by Esau) is ultimately overcome and transformed by the "gift of grace" (represented by Christ).</meta>

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

Brother Esau, acting as the kingdom of external natural righteousness <note>original: "äussern Natur-Gerechtigkeit" — Böhme refers to the strict, often harsh laws of the physical and moral world that demand justice without mercy.</note>, constantly sought to kill Jacob, so that Jacob had to flee from him. This is just as Christ had to flee from the Pharisaic righteousness within the kingdom of nature, until the time came that Jacob returned from Laban with his gifts and went to meet Esau, surrendering himself to him, whether Esau chose to kill him or let him live. But Jacob was not yet the true one whom the righteousness of nature, within God’s purpose, was meant to seize and kill; rather, it was Christ.

85. Thus, we see here once again the figure of Christ and Adam: for when Jacob went to Esau and sent the gifts ahead to meet him, Esau’s wrath was shattered and turned into great mercy <term>Erbärmde: An archaic German word for deep, heart-felt compassion or mercy.</term>, so that he fell upon Jacob’s neck and wept (*Genesis 33:4*). He did him no harm but received him in love. This is the figure of Christ within our humanity.

86. Within our humanity lay the Father’s wrath, like the angry Esau awakened in righteousness and wrath, just as Esau was against Jacob. But Christ sent His gift of grace—the love within His blood, drawn from the essence of the heavenly world—into our nature and into God’s righteousness (representing the first Adam-like birth), meeting nature head-on. And when righteousness saw and felt this love within itself, God’s wrath within the righteousness of nature was turned into great mercy. Because of this, the wrath lost all its legal claim and was shattered; therefore the sun lost its light within God’s righteousness <note>A reference to the darkness during the Crucifixion.</note>, the earth trembled in this shattering, the rocks split apart, and the dead (whom God’s righteousness had swallowed up in death) arose within this mercy.

87. For Esau was concerned with the legal right of the birthright, which he had sold to Jacob, yet he did not know that God had ordained it so to prefigure the relationship between Christ and Adam. Therefore, he treated Jacob as an enemy because Jacob held Abraham’s blessing; the righteousness of the self-willed nature wanted that blessing to stay in Esau (representing Adam’s corrupted nature). However, the nature of the self-will had lost God’s inheritance; the second, new Adam, Christ, brought it back into nature. Thus, the first right (the first natural life) had to die and become alive again in Christ; and Esau, in his...

<summary>Böhme interprets the reconciliation of Jacob and Esau as a symbol of Christ's sacrifice, where His "gift of love" shatters the Father's wrath and restores humanity's lost inheritance. This process requires the death of the "natural" self-will so that a new life can begin in Christ.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological exploration of Romans Chapter 9, specifically focusing on the figures of Esau and Jacob as archetypes of the "natural man" versus the "spiritual man." Böhme explains the "rejection" of Esau not as a personal cruelty from God, but as a necessary step in the death of the self-willed, beastly nature so that the true human (the Adamic essence) can be redeemed through Christ.</meta>

### XV. Regarding Chapter 9.

87. ...as a hunter, [Esau] could not inherit God’s Kingdom in righteousness; instead, he was cast out, even while still in his mother’s womb, when the children had done neither evil nor good, so that God’s righteousness might be fulfilled in His purpose for the creation of all creatures.

88. But in Christ, He [God] accepted him [Esau] again according to the gift of grace, based on the inner ground of the true Adamic human, and not according to the "right" of his living nature, by which he was named or called "Esau." For the **E** is the inner ground where the Paradisal gift lay hidden; but the **sau** <note>original: "sau" — In German, *Sau* means a sow or beast. Böhme uses this wordplay to separate the divine spirit "E" from the "beastly" self-will of the fallen, earthly nature.</note> was the rejected beast of the kingdom of self-will according to earthly things. Of this, the Scripture says: *Romans 9:13*, "Esau have I hated," even while he was still in his mother's womb, so that God’s election might stand—not that Esau in his false, selfish nature-life should be God’s child, but rather Christ within the true Adamic nature in Esau.

89. The Adamic nature was meant to lose its "right" entirely in the **sau** <note>the beastly nature</note>, according to its own will and life. But the essence of the Adamic nature, which was the formed, spoken Word of God, was to remain in Christ and be reconciled with Christ’s gift within the wrath. This was the image shown when Jacob sent the gift to meet Esau and called him his "lord." There, the wrath in Esau was reconciled regarding the "right of nature," and it began to turn into the greatest mercy; he fell upon Jacob’s neck, kissed him, and surrendered his will into the birthright in Jacob (*Genesis 33:4*).

90. For Christ had to give Himself entirely unto death and subject the human "nature-right" to His Father as Righteousness <note>In Böhme’s thought, "Righteousness" often acts as a synonym for the Father's strict, consuming fire or justice.</note> itself. In this, "Esau" <note>referring to the selfish, fallen nature</note> died away; then God awakened the first Adam—the true human who was created in God’s original purpose—in the grace of love (which had fulfilled the requirements of righteousness before God). Then it was no longer Esau, but a member of Christ.

91. That the Scripture thus addresses predestination <term>Prädestination: The theological concept of "God's election," where God chooses certain paths or persons for salvation according to His divine will.</term> is entirely correct. For Esau is the image of God's wrath that arose in Adam, and that image is condemned so that God’s righteousness is satisfied, and the richness of His grace in Jacob (as in Christ) might be made manifest <meta>continuity: the German word "offenbar" (manifest) is completed here from the catchword "fenbar" on the previous page.</meta>

<summary>Böhme interprets the biblical rejection of Esau as a metaphor for the necessary death of the fallen, "beastly" self-will (the "sau") so that the inner spiritual human (the "E") can be reconciled to God through Christ. He argues that predestination applies to the different natures within a person rather than a simplistic choice between individuals.</summary>

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

...might be made manifest. For the life in the **natural will** <note>original: *Natur-Willen*; the self-centered, fallen will that seeks its own power apart from God</note> (which was called *Esau*) was *Adam’s* New Life, according to the awakened properties of the dark world, as it was also with *Cain* and *Ismael*; God’s Justice in His wrath had seized this life and manifested itself within it, and that was condemned. However, the **soul-ground** <note>the deep, underlying spiritual foundation of the human being</note>, as the entire center of nature—namely, the formed Word according to the soul—was not for that reason rejected by God. No, no; they had sprouted from the children of the Saints, not from the **sow** <note>original: *Sau*; a reference to the beastly, godless nature discussed on the previous page</note>, as happens now in many where the inner ground is full of devils.

92. The gift of grace of the **in-spoken voice** <note>the divine Word or "Logos" breathed into the human essence</note> lay in the inner ground, but not in the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a technical term meaning the "essential being" or "vital substance" of a thing</note> of life, as it was in *Jacob*, *Isaac*, and *Abel*. This <term>Ens</term> was *Christ*, who wished to speak Himself with His voice into this in-spoken word, into the inner ground of the poor souls (who were held captive in the wrath of God), as it is written: *I am come to seek and to save that which was lost* <note>original: "Ich bin kommen zu suchen, und selig zu machen das verloren ist." A translation of the Latin biblical quote from Matthew 18:11 / Luke 19:10.</note>—namely *Ismael*, *Esau*, and their like, who were seized and lost in God’s hate. Thus *Christ* said now that He was come to seek the poor sinner who was lost, and not the righteous. *Matthew 9:13.*

93. For *Jacob*, *Isaac*, and *Abel* were the righteous ones, because grace had revealed itself in them and had put to death the self-will of sin in the soul-life, giving itself as a new life to the true, original ground of life. Thus they were now righteous in that same new life and had peace with God’s Justice—understand this as according to the soul; but according to the outer life, they were still under the curse, which is why their outer body had to die. For it was not they themselves by nature who were righteous, but grace made them righteous; which grace gave itself into an <term>Ens</term> of life within them, in which the life burned—this new Divine fire transformed the hate of God’s wrath into love, in which they were righteous.

94. So you might say: Why not also in *Cain*, *Ismael*, and *Esau*? Answer: No, the purpose of God must stand—namely, the order of His spoken Word. He does not turn the Word back; His wrath must not be killed and broken, for it is a cause by which grace might be made manifest; furthermore, it is the cause that grace...

## XV. Concerning Chapter 9

[...] is transformed into a **kingdom of joy**; He is also the cause of grace becoming a **fiery love**. Christ, however, is the other purpose; He revealed this in Abel, Isaac, and Jacob, and presented the figure <note>original: *Figur*; a symbolic or prophetic type</note> of how it was to be.

95. For Christ was to be manifested within the justice of God’s wrath, so that it might be known what grace was. Adam stood in God’s grace and in His wrath, but in the <term>Temperature</term> <note>original: *Temperatur*; a state of perfect balance where all spiritual properties exist in harmony without one dominating another</note> neither was manifest in his life, for they stood in equal weight. Now, if grace was to be manifested, then the wrath had to be manifested beforehand, or first, so that grace would be caused to move within the wrath, and to give itself over to the wrath and extinguish it; which giving-in and extinguishing is the cause of the Divine **kingdom of joy** and **fiery love** in the life of man. From this, God’s mercy, as well as faith, love, and hope—as the trust in God—took its <term>origin</term> <note>original: *Urstand*; the primordial source or state from which something emerges</note> in man, which could not be so in the <term>Temperature</term>.

96. For a thing that stands within equal weight has no movement or desire toward anything; it is One, and is its own self. When it goes out of the <term>Temperature</term>, it becomes many; moreover, it is fragile and loses its <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: *Selbheit*; the state of independent, ego-driven existence apart from the divine unity</note>. To such a thing, help—as grace and mercy—is necessary. But if it does not happen immediately that it is helped, it enters into hope; and if hope is promised that help shall come to it, then it enters into faith. Faith causes the desire in hope, and the desire takes the promise into itself and grasps it, so that it becomes substantial <note>original: *wesentlich*; having the nature of an essence or being</note>. And in that same substance is now the grace and the mercy. For that same substance is taken in the promise and grasped into a being; which being must surrender itself to the first law <note>original: *Rechte*; right or law</note> that the thing has made in itself. And when that happens, the first "maker" finds a new life within itself, which has arisen from hope and from faith and from desire, through this "grasping-inward." And it finds that this is more spiritual than the first [life] from which the thing originated; therefore, it cannot resist it, but must let the spiritual life dwell within itself.

97. And here originates the <term>restoration</term> <note>original: *Wiederbringung*; the spiritual reintegration or "bringing back" of the soul to its original divine state</note> of the first being that had broken itself, and [shows] that the final body is better than the first; for it is entirely spiritual, [arising] out of faith, hope...

# Chapter 9. Election of Grace.

...born of [understanding] and love; the first fire ignites this with its desire, from which the fiery love arises.

98. Therefore, understand us correctly: *Adam* was the entire image of **God** in love and wrath, but he stood in the equality of properties <note>original: *Gleichheit der Eigenschaften*; a state where no single quality dominates others</note>, and neither was manifest over the other. But when he allowed himself to be seduced into lust through the *Devil's* deceit, that same image broke, and the properties of the <term>Temperature</term> <note>the state of perfect spiritual balance</note> were divided. Now he stood in need of help, so **God** spoke the Word into him; the hungry desire for help accepted this, grasped it, and placed its will therein as a hope that he would be assisted. This desire then grasped that hope into an <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a Latin term meaning an "essence" or "being," used here to describe the spiritual substance of faith</note> of the being. Now the In-spoken Word became essential and was called Faith—as a "taking-in" that received the <term>Scienß</term> <note>original: *Scienß*; from the Latin *scientia*, referring here to a dynamic "root-knowledge" or the creative spark of the eternal will</note> of the eternal will and surrendered itself to it. For this essence was nobler than the first essence arising from the purpose of the spoken word. Thus, fiery love emerged from the wrath-fire within the purpose of eternal nature. For this *Ens* of faith was unbreakable and endured within the wrath-fire; in this receiving of the wrath-fire, the fire of grimness was transformed into joy-filled love.

99. And this is now the ground of *Christ* from the In-spoken Word, who separated himself in *Adam* into a distinct figure within the *Ens* of nature. From this came *Abel*, and from the broken figure came *Cain*. Now, *Abel* also had *Cain’s* nature in the *Ens* of faith, in which the soul stood; but the broken will was transformed into a whole one, for the breakage rested in the *Ens* of faith, which was *Christ’s* figure. But it was promised to *Adam's* soul (meaning the property of the broken nature of the soul and the body) that the woman's seed should crush the head of the introduced serpent-property and help *Adam*. Therefore, that same <term>Serpent-Crusher</term> <note>original: *Schlangen-Treter*; a reference to the Messiah in Genesis 3:15</note> had to be another person than *Adam*—one in whom **God** would be manifest, who could do this, and who could awaken the In-spoken Word in *Adam*; that is, one who also possessed the power and strength of the "In-speaking" itself.

100. For although the In-speaking was living and manifest in *Adam*, it concerned his children, in whom the In-spoken ground was covered with sin, and...

## XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

...was not yet separated, as with Cain and Abel, and also because the human <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a Latin term for "essence" or "being," referring here to the fundamental spiritual substance of a person</note> within the sinner (which God's justice had seized in wrath) possessed a voice of grace that spoke into him and awakened the inner, first "In-spoken" ground of the Word of divine power.

101. For the God **JEHOVAH** spoke the name **JESUS** into *Adam*, after the Fall, into an actual life; that is, He revealed it in the heavenly essence, which had faded away. This same name **JESUS** became a life within the soul (inasmuch as **God** spoke it into the soul). Through this "In-speaking," a divine desire was awakened once more out of death in *Adam's* soul. The soul's awakened desire took hold of itself, and this same awakened desire was the beginning of **Faith**. This separated itself from the property of false desire into an image, as into an essence, and from that came *Abel*; and from the characteristic nature of the *Adamic* soul, according to earthly lust, came *Cain*.

102. Now, however, the sound of the Word that **God** spoke in also lay at the root of the soul's own characteristic nature, in the *Cainite* essence. But this essence was not capable of receiving the divine life through the "In-speaking" of the Word, for the awakened wrath of **God** had become manifest within it, according to His purpose of speaking out into nature in <term>differentiation</term> <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the state of being divided into separate, distinct, and often conflicting qualities</note>. Thus, this same soulful essence now required another, even greater "In-speaking" into the spoken Word, so that it might also become living within the soulful essence.

103. This could not happen unless it came from a divine resonance or "In-speaking," where the speaking proceeded simultaneously from divine life and from the root of the soul's life—where there would be a divine, holy soul that would speak itself into the corrupted soul (which is blind to **God**) with soulful and divine power, so that the soulful might enter the soulful, and the divine enter the divine, awakening one within the other.

104. For this was **God’s** intent: He did not wish to abandon the poor, corrupted, truly *Adamic* soul; rather, He presented it in *Cain's* image, and placed the name **JESUS** in the other lineage <note>original: *Linea*</note> opposite him. Within that lineage also was the soulful ground, so that the name **JESUS**, with the new life of the soulful ground, should speak itself into *Cain’s* soul.

### Chapter 9. The Election of Grace.

And this image was Abel, from whose lineage CHRIST came, according to our humanity; and he came to call the poor sinner, imprisoned in the hatred of God, to repentance. He possessed a human soul newly born in God, and he was able to speak into the soul and also into the "In-spoken" Word of God (which occurred in Paradise), and awaken the soul within a new Divine hunger, so that the soul took this in-spoken inherited Word into itself, from which a new life also arose for it.

105. Therefore understand us rightly, for we speak of precious things as we well recognize them in God’s grace: The image of Cain, Ishmael, Esau, and their like are all unregenerate men, and they are the true Adam after the Fall; these God calls with the In-spoken Word, which He taught us in Christ, and which He still speaks today through the newly born children into these corrupted children of Adam, calling to them: "Come all to me"—not just some, but all.

106. Then **CHRIST** is born in them, and according to this newly born ground, they are no longer in this world, but in heaven. For it is the holy heaven itself, as the true temple of God, where God-Man and God dwell within, where the Word becomes flesh (understand this as heavenly, spiritual flesh), which this holy soul-fire eats from Christ's flesh and derives its life therefrom.

is wrath and envy; and such was Adam after the Fall (without the re-speaking of grace), as were Cain, Ishmael, Esau, and all men, outside the **aim of grace** <term>aim of grace</term> <note>original: *Gnaden-Ente*; likely a variation of *Gnaden-Ende*, meaning the final purpose or destination of grace</note> of love, from which the Light originates.

116. Now the question is: Did **God’s** Justice, in His purpose, hate Esau unto eternal destruction? Answer: Yes, in his own power, it could be nothing else. The greater question is: Was it the will of the pure, true **God** that Esau, Cain, and many thousands should perish eternally? Answer: No; rather, Christ was **God’s** purpose, insofar as **God** is called a **God**.

117. In Christ, **God** wills that all people be helped: 1 Timothy 2:4. But His wrath wills to swallow all in whom it is manifest; yet the Scripture says: **God** did not send His Son into the world (as in, into humanity) that He might judge, harden, or destroy it, but that He might save it. John 3:17. To this you say: "Yes, those whom He wills." Answer: Yes, but He calls them *all* to Him; they should all come. Why do they not all come? You say: "He does not draw them to Himself within them." Answer: That is not true; He draws them all; He teaches all within them. For they know in the **light of nature** <term>light of nature</term> <note>original: *Lichte der Natur*; the innate moral and intellectual awareness granted to all humans</note>, where He meets the ungodly in his understanding and shows him what is right—which they even teach and confess themselves to be right, yet they do not do it. Question: How does it happen then that He does not draw them all? Answer: There lies the ground, dear **soiled little piece of wood** <note>original: *besudeltes Hölzlein*; a Böhmean metaphor for the corrupted human soul</note>; just smell within your own bosom—what do you smell of? If you are merely caught in the **purpose of grimness** <term>purpose of grimness</term> <note>original: *Vorsatze des Grimmes*; the manifestation of God's wrathful quality</note>, in its **constellation** <term>constellation</term> <note>original: *Constellation*; the spiritual and psychological configuration of a person's nature</note> like Esau, Ishmael, and the like, there is still hope. But if you are a thistle grown from inherited, actual sins, where **God’s** purpose in wrath has modeled itself into a figure of life—of which **God** said in the justice of His purpose: "He would punish the sins of the parents upon the children unto the third and fourth generation"—then it is dangerous. For this living purpose in the wrath of **God** has already possessed a figure in the **science** <term>science</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; from the Latin *scientia*, referring here to the formative, creative knowledge within the divine Word</note> of the **speaking Word** <note>original: *sprechende Wort*; the creative Logos</note>, and is renewed from the incorporated
<meta>catchword: ground</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that while God's justice "hates" the sinful nature of those like Esau, God's ultimate will in Christ is the salvation of all humanity. He explains that individuals remain lost not because God fails to draw them, but because their inner "constellation" or inherited sin creates a nature resistant to the Light.</summary>

# Chapter 9: The Election of Grace.
L 3

ground of grace, separated not out of **God’s** purpose, but through the source of sins. This source has completely united itself with the wrath in the Divine purpose and led into a life of darkness; there, the embodied grace lies far off, and Christ has died and rests in the grave; and before he rises, this evil spirit has descended into the abyss. These are now held by the purpose of **God** and not given to the grace of Christ, for they are **thistle-children**; their will is a living devil in an angel's form among other people.

118. The purpose of **God** knows every essence <term>essence</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a Latin philosophical term used by Böhme to describe the fundamental being or "stuff" of a person</note> while it is still a seed in man and woman, and knows what this wood will be useful for when it becomes a tree. And the "thistle" does not only come from the mother’s womb out of the first ground, but also through the external influences of time, by which most are corrupted.

119. Christ calls all of these; many of them also still have a little spark of divine drawing within them, which the Purpose gives to Christ, as His voice, so that at times they learn to hear Christ within them: and these are now called and summoned. But external influences corrupt that again and crucify Christ’s voice and calling before He is born as a man within them, and they introduce the **serpent-essence** <term>serpent-essence</term> <note>original: *Schlangen-Ens*; the corrupted, sinful nature inherited from the Fall of Adam and the influence of the devil</note> in Christ’s place. And when it then comes to the "election" in the harvest-time, when the grain is threshed and winnowed, this person is found to be only the chaff of a grain and has no divine weight or gravity in themselves. They remain behind in the center of darkness, in **God’s** justice in wrath; then it is said: *Few are chosen*; for the Father chooses for Himself only the good fruit for His food, and the rest He gives to the cattle. So it is also here: what does not grow up in the divine essence and is not born of **God**, that **God** cannot behold.

120. So you say now: Was Esau then finally reborn and saved out of **God’s** hate? Answer: We should not judge that, for **God** says: *Vengeance is mine, I will repay in my justice.* (Romans 12:19). We say with certainty that Esau was born in Adam’s sin, as a true image of Adam after the Fall, and was seized in the mother's womb in the purpose of **God’s** wrath, as all poor

He warns against judging the final salvation of figures like Esau, noting that all are born into Adam's sin.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 9.

...sinners: And Jacob stands in the image of Christ, in newly-born love, as a prototype of Christ—the Christ who came to call the poor sinner and to make him blessed (so far as the Justice of **God** in His wrath allows him to follow, because of the inherited horrors enclosed within the eternal Science <term>Science</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; in Böhme's thought, this refers to the soul’s inherent "root of knowledge" or the spiritual center where the will and imagination are formed</note>, as well as the actual horrors that come from holding onto them).

121. But because Esau came from and was born to holy parents, and stood there only in the differentiation <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the state of being separated or distinguished within the natural order</note> as an image of corrupted nature; and since **God** had also separated the image of Christ from that same seed of his parents and placed it against him as his brother Jacob; and since this Jacob ultimately brought him (Esau) into the greatest mercy through his gift and humility; this signifies the gift of Christ within Esau. It suggests that Christ wished to turn him around and pull him out of the wrathful, seized Purpose of **God's** Justice, so that he might weep in repentance for his evil will and do penance—just as he did when he embraced Jacob, wept on his neck, and let the spirit of murder sink away from Jacob. Therefore, we should by no means condemn him. We condemn him only according to the Scripture, which condemns him in Adam’s malice while he was not yet reborn; in which concept **God's** Justice is sufficiently satisfied, while grace is revealed through repentance.

122. We do not know whether **God** did not convert him, which the figure <note>the symbolic representation or biblical "type"</note> of Jacob coming to him from Laban clearly indicates. For in Adam he was dead, but in Christ he might become alive; for the Gate of Grace stood open toward him just as much as toward his parents, who were in the lineage of Christ. However, that they also possessed Adam's poison and death in the flesh, and the source of sins from Adam, is proven by Esau, Ishmael, and Cain.

123. But we should not believe Reason <note>original: *Vernunft*; often used by Böhme to describe human logic that lacks spiritual illumination</note> here, which says: **God** hardened Esau and judged him to eternal damnation. It cannot be proven in Holy Scripture that **God** hardened Esau, or that it was the Divine Will; rather, the Purpose in **God’s** Justice did it—not through an intervention of a settled Divine Will, but out of the corrupted nature from Adam's property, within Esau’s own being. It was not a foreign accident or intervention, as Reason judges, which...

### XV. Regarding Chapter 10.

...I have hated. <note>original: *gehasset*; a reference to Romans 9:13, "Jacob I loved, but Esau I hated."</note> Explanation: Here, human reason lies blind, and it is exactly as explained at length previously: for that was God's purpose, which He granted to Adam after the Fall. The first purpose is the natural first Adam; he was the "greater" one, as the first image of God in the purpose of the divine *science* <note>original: *Scienz*; in Böhme’s terminology, this refers to a divine "knowing" or the latent potentiality within the divine Wisdom</note> from the speaking Word of the multiplicity <note>original: *Schiedlichkeit*; the quality of being distinct or separable into various powers and forces</note> of powers; but in him, grace was not yet manifest, much less the great love and humility found in Jesus.

7. Therefore, God came with the second purpose, which lay hidden in grace, and placed it into the first image, and revealed grace through the first image. He killed the first life within grace and raised the life of grace in the first purpose, elevating it above the purpose of the greater image, which was the first natural man.

8. Therefore, the text in Moses says to Rebecca: *The elder shall serve the younger,* Genesis 25:23, so that the purpose in the revelation of grace might stand; for Esau, in the greater first image of Adam, "I have hated," because he wished to be his own master, to live in both evil and good, and would not recognize grace. But Jacob, in my true divine purpose—whom I have born from eternity out of my divine will of grace—him "I have loved" and appointed as lord over nature. Therefore Christ said: *All power has been given to him,* Matthew 28:18, for He was the "smaller" one, coming out of God's humility and love. These God set over the kingdom of His wrath, so that the kingdom of His wrath should serve God in the smaller one—that is, in God’s grace—and there become manifest.

9. And for this reason, the inheritance was also outwardly withdrawn from Ishmael, to indicate that God had given the inheritance to the human being who would be born out of grace. In this "hating," reason now goes astray and does not understand the foundation, as mentioned above.

10. Further: Romans 9:14–18. *What shall we say then: is God unjust? God forbid! For He says to Moses: I will be gracious to whom I am gracious, and I will have mercy on whom I have mercy.* So it does not depend on anyone's willing or running, but on God's mercy. For the Scripture says to Pharaoh: *For this very purpose I have raised you up, that I...*

<summary>Böhme explains the election of Jacob over Esau as a metaphor for the triumph of divine grace over the natural, fallen human state. He argues that the "greater" natural man must serve the "smaller" spiritual man born of God's humility.</summary>

There is no other will in God toward mercy except the single one He has revealed in Christ.

# Chapter 10. The Election of Grace.

19. Understand this correctly: it was said to Pharaoh: *For this very purpose I have raised you up and hardened you, that I might make my name known in all lands.* Rom. 9:17. Pharaoh was not born out of grace, or from the "willing of grace," but rather from the "willing of wrath" <note>In the author's theology, God possesses two "willings": one of love/grace and one of wrath/fire. Individuals are often seen as manifestations of one of these two foundational principles.</note>. And since God wished to make His name known—how He is a Lord, and how His grace rules over wrath—He awakened the wrath in the hardened Pharaoh. He seized him within the purpose of His wrath and held him so that he could not see the works of God; for he was blind toward God until God let the forms of His fury be seen in the <term>Turba Magna</term> <note>The "Great Turba" is a technical term used by Jacob Böhme to describe a state of spiritual upheaval, chaos, or the "awakened wrath" of God that breaks forth when a person or nation reaches its limit of iniquity.</note>.

20. But that the iniquity of the Egyptians had reached its full measure at this time is indicated by Scripture when it says: *that Israel would have to serve the Egyptians for 400 years, and then God would judge that same nation, for their iniquity leading to hardening was not yet full.* Gen. 15:13. But with Pharaoh, it was full, and the hardening was present in him. Therefore, God’s purpose in wrath used him as an instrument. For the Egyptians had awakened the plagues; thus they also had to serve the glorious revelation of Divine Grace over God’s children, so that God might show His wrath upon the ungodly and His grace upon His children.

21. For the time of Pharaoh was a time of a specific goal, as all things lie within "goal, time, measure, and weight." Wisdom of Solomon 11:21 <note>original: "Sap. 11: 22."; the author references the deuterocanonical Wisdom of Solomon, which notes that God ordered all things by measure, number, and weight.</note>.

22. The supposed "external purpose" <note>The idea that God's predestination is an arbitrary choice made from the outside, rather than a process occurring within the soul's nature.</note> is powerfully cast down in this single text of St. Paul. Human reason imagines that God chooses for Himself some special people of a specific name—just as the various sects rage in their disputes, wishing to be blessed and "called children" based on their sectarian name alone, above all other peoples.

23. Furthermore, as St. Paul says: Rom. 9:24, 26. *Whom He has called, namely us, not only from the Jews but also from the Gentiles; as He also says through Hosea: "I will call them my people, who were not my people; and her my beloved, who was not beloved; and it shall come to pass in the place where it was said to them, you are not my people, there they shall be called children of the living God."* **Explanation:** Here we see the first "calling" in Paradise, through the spoken...

<summary>This page explains that Pharaoh's hardening was the result of his being born from the "willing of wrath" once the iniquity of Egypt was full. It argues against sectarian views of predestination, asserting that God's "calling" extends to both Jews and Gentiles based on internal spiritual reality rather than outward names.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 10.

...the powerful Word of Grace, which presses from the One toward all people.

**24.** For the Gentiles were not of Abraham’s seed, with whom God made a covenant; however, the first covenant of the Word of Grace—which was spoken into humanity in Paradise—remained within them as a foundation. For this reason, Saint Paul says that God called and elected not only the Jews in their covenant, but also the Gentiles in the covenant of Christ. He called those His "beloved" who did not know Him and who, externally in their ignorance, were not His people. But the "Purpose of Grace," which had been incorporated into humanity in Paradise after the Fall through the <term>In-speaking</term> <note>The "In-speaking" (German: *Einsprechen*) is a central concept in Böhme’s thought, referring to the moment God spoke the promise of the "crusher of the serpent" into the human soul after the Fall, planting a seed of grace in all of humanity.</note>, lay within them. Because of that Purpose, God called them His beloved. This incorporated Word was awakened in them through the Spirit of Christ—as that same Voice of Grace had taken on a human soul—so that their soul, which lay locked in darkness, heard the incorporated Voice of Grace within the voice of Christ. This was like the awakening of a new In-speaking, and love was kindled in the soul.

God does not merely look at human knowledge—choosing a people for His children based on His decree simply because they know how to speak His name better than other nations. Rather, God looks to His Purpose established in Paradise. He had this Purpose from eternity in the <term>unimaged figure</term> <note>The "unimaged figure" (German: *unbildliche Figur*) refers to the eternal idea or archetype of humanity as it exists in Divine Wisdom, prior to the physical creation of individual "creatures."</note> of man, as the first ground of humanity. There, man was seen in the name of Jesus within Divine Wisdom, not as a physical creature, but in a <term>magical imagination</term> <note>In this context, "magical" refers to the spiritual power of the will or desire to bring an internal image into being. It is the formative power of the soul.</note>. This "imagination" or inner formation also existed in the inner ground of the Gentiles, as a formation shared by all; except for the "children of wrath," where that same formation took shape within the wrath of God. This formation of wrath does not apply to entire nations, but rather to the <term>thistle-children</term> <note>A reference to the "tares" or weeds among the wheat; those who have turned away from the light to grow in the "thistles" of sin.</note> who are seized by their inherited and actual sins within the purpose of wrath.

**25.** As it was said to Elijah, when he said to God: "Israel has entirely departed from You, and I alone am left, and they are seeking my life." God answered: "I have left for myself seven thousand who have not bowed their knees before Baal." These are the ones who, although they lived externally among the Gentiles and dwelt among the false Jews, nevertheless kept their hearts in the

### Chapter 10: The Election of Grace

...directed toward the true God, and were zealous in blindness and lack of understanding, like Saul, until grace awakened in Saul so that he became seeing.

**26.** For Saul believed he was doing a service to the true God by exterminating those who wished to transform the Divine Law into another appearance which he did not recognize; he was zealous for the Law of God from the bottom of his heart, intending to please God thereby. He did this not because of a "Purpose of God’s Wrath"—as if the Wrath had seized him and placed him into the life of darkness, or as if God had viewed him as one entirely hardened in death through a special purpose or a "particular election." No, he was also one among the **7000** <note>original: "7000" — A reference to 1 Kings 19:18, where God tells Elijah He has reserved 7,000 faithful people who have not bowed to Baal. Böhme uses this to represent the hidden "remnant" of the faithful within any people.</note> in whom the Covenant of Grace, from the true seed of Abraham and the promise in Paradise, lay within. But the way to that same grace was not yet manifest to him; he was zealous in the Law of Righteousness and demanded that which he himself could not do. However, the hidden grace within him could do it; this grace revealed itself in his zeal and used him as an instrument of testimony for grace.

**27.** Therefore, it is a form of blindness and ignorance when a people says: "We have Christ's teaching; God allows Christ to be preached among us and not among that other nation; therefore God, according to His Purpose, has chosen us as children of grace. And even though we are no better in our lives than they are, He has nevertheless chosen us in His Purpose and atoned for our actual and inherited sins in Christ, so that we need only comfort ourselves with this and accept it as a **gift of grace**." For [they claim] our works count for nothing before God; rather, it is the choice of His Purpose that matters, whereby He makes the ungodly righteous in His Purpose, and by the purpose of His Will, He draws the ungodly out of Hell and makes them blessed.

**28.** Listen, you **blind Babylon** <term>Babylon</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, "Babylon" represents the corrupted, institutionalized church which prioritizes doctrine and external power over the inner, living spirit of Christ.</note>, covered under Christ’s purple mantle like a harlot under a crown, who is full of the lust of whoredom and yet calls herself a virgin: What is this "Election" and "Grace" in which you comfort yourself, while covering your whoredom and the vices of all wickedness with that same mantle of grace? Where does it stand in the Scripture that a harlot becomes a virgin through **letters of nobility** <note>original: "Herren-Briefe" — literally "letters from lords," referring to legal decrees or official pardons.</note> and gifts of grace? What Emperor can make a defiled woman into a

### XV. Concerning Chapter 10

...made him a virgin <note>original: "Jungfrau machen" — In Böhme's mysticism, the "Virgin" represents the pure, divine wisdom and the restored state of the soul.</note> simply because of His favor and goodwill? Can that really be so? Where then remains the virgin in the heart and in chastity? God demands the very abyss of the heart and says in Matthew 5:18: *Not a single tittle of His Law of righteousness shall pass away until it is all fulfilled.* With what do you intend to fulfill that righteousness if you are without the Divine Being within you?

**29.** You might say: "Christ has fulfilled it for me once and for all and satisfied the Law." Answer: That is true; but what does that have to do with you, who are outside of Christ and walk in your own way? If you are not in Christ within actual grace, then you have no part in Him, for He said: *He who is not with me is against me, and he who does not gather with me scatters.* <note>Luke 11:23</note>.

**30.** It is not a matter of an **imputed grace** <note>original: "zugerechnete Gnade" — A theological concept where righteousness is credited to a believer's "account" externally; Böhme argues this is insufficient without internal change.</note> from the outside, but an inborn, childlike grace from the flesh and blood of Christ, which takes the merit of Christ into itself. It is not the human being born of man and woman, out of corrupted nature, who attains the grace of **adoption as a child** <note>original: "Kindschaft" — The state of being a child of God through spiritual rebirth.</note>, such that he might comfort himself and say: "Christ has done it; He declares me free from sins; I only need to believe that it has happened." No, the devil knows that too, as does the damned soul who comforts himself with this imputed righteousness and grace—but what does that help him if he is still damned? For *not everyone who says, "Lord, Lord," shall enter the kingdom of heaven, but those who do the will of my Father in heaven,* says Christ in Matthew 7:21.

**31.** But what is that same will they must do so that they may come to the adoption of children? There Christ says: *Those who turn back and become as children* (Matthew 18:3), and *are born of water and Spirit from God* (John 3:5)—these are the ones. For Christ is the will of God, and those who wish to do that will must be born of Christ, from His flesh and blood (from the Word that became human, which extinguished death and sin in humanity and walked in love). They must take the merit of Christ into their soul and, according to the inner, embodied **ground of grace**, become the living Christ, like a true branch on His grapevine.

**32.** Not by seeking comfort in an assumed, foreign...

# Chapter 10. Election of Grace.

...merely an appearance; but **essentially** <note>original: "essentialiter" — meaning in substance or reality, not just in theory.</note>, as independent, substantial children of Christ, where the **spoken-in Covenant of Grace** <note>original: "eingesprochene Gnaden-Bund" — Böhme's concept that God's word of grace is physically infused into the human essence.</note> is fulfilled with actual being. Here the soul eats and lives from Christ's flesh and blood, and this does not happen from the outside, but within itself, as Christ speaks forever to the fiery soul in God’s righteousness: *Take, and eat my flesh, and drink my blood, so you remain in me, and I in you.* <note>John 6:56</note>.

33. The **fiery science** <note>original: "feurische Scienz" — A technical term in Böhme’s thought referring to the soul’s active, formative power or indwelling knowledge that seeks to manifest itself.</note> of the soul, according to the inner eternal ground of God's true righteousness in His decree toward the creature of the soul, must introduce itself into Christ’s flesh and blood, into actual substance. This is not done through a foreign appearance, but through that which God revealed in Adam after the Fall and fulfilled in Christ with humanity—where God became man, and man became God. This continues now in His members who sprout from the same root, in whom Christ becomes alive in the embodied Covenant of Grace and takes the soul and humanity into Himself.

34. So it does not depend now solely on **outer knowledge**—that I merely know I have a gracious God in Christ who has wiped away sin in humanity—but rather it depends on this: 

(1) That it also happens in me, so that Christ, who has risen from the dead, also rises in me and rules over sin in me as well. 

(2) That He also kills sin in me, which is the nature in its own evil will, so that this same will might also be crucified and killed in Christ within me.

(3) And that a new will might arise out of nature in Christ's spirit, life, and will within me; a will that desires God, lives for Him, and obeys Him. This is the will that fulfills the Law—that is, it gives itself over to the Law in obedience and fulfills the Law with the **divine will of love**, so that the Law in its righteousness becomes subject to the desire of love and also finds joy in that love.

35. Only then does the wrath of God sink away from the soul, and the soul is delivered from pain in the spirit of love and lives in God. To this end, an **earnest repentance** <note>original: "ernste Busse" — A total internal turning away from self-will toward God.</note> is required, in which the poor soul opens its maw, as the **fire-mouth** <note>original: "Feuer-Mund" — The soul's core, which Böhme describes as a fire that must be quenched by divine love.</note>, into God’s decree of wrath and takes hold of the embodied grace through the promise of Christ: that He will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it. <note>Luke 11:13</note>. This offered grace must, as a living, speaking Word, be [received] into the inner ground of the first voice of grace spoken into Adam...

He argues that mere intellectual belief is insufficient unless Christ's death and resurrection are physically and spiritually reenacted within the individual's own will.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 16

...to be encompassed by the soul, as by the center of nature, and through the **divine science** <note>original: "Scientz" — in Böhme’s writing, this refers to a formative, active power or "knowing-will" that creates reality, rather than just academic knowledge.</note> of the **Abyss** <note>original: "Ungrund" — a central Böhme term referring to the "groundless" or "bottomless" primordial source of God's being, existing before any manifestation or desire.</note>, so that it becomes a resolution for repentance and for the turning-away of the **abominable will** <note>original: "Greuel-Willens" — the ego-driven, corrupt will of fallen humanity.</note>. Within this resolution, the Spirit of Christ—in the primary ground of the beloved grace (which, according to the Scriptures, presses from one man onto all)—gives birth to a new life. In this new life, the will to sin dies and passes away, and a true branch grows out of Christ’s tree, so that thereafter sin reigns only in the mortal flesh. This same new branch, however, has broken through the wrath of God in Christ—through the decree of wrath and through eternal death—into the life of grace, just as Christ says: *Whoever believes in me shall never die, but has passed through from death to life.* <note>John 5:24</note>

36. Thus, faith is not an external thing, where someone might say: "The **Election of Grace** <note>original: "Gnaden-Wahl" — the theological concept of predestination or being chosen by God.</note> is with us, for Christ is taught and acknowledged; He has chosen us before other nations so that we may hear His voice; although we are evil, He has nonetheless forgiven us our sins in His decree and killed them through Christ's merit; we need only accept this and be comforted by it; it is imputed to us from the outside and given as a gift of grace."

37. No, no, that will not do. Christ Himself is the imputed grace, and the gift, and the merit. Whoever has Him within, and who is himself that Christ in his own inner ground, he is a Christian; he is crucified and died with Christ, and lives in His resurrection. To such a person, grace is imputed in Christ’s spirit and life, for he no longer needs to let himself be hung on the cross; rather, he **puts on** Christ in all His merit; he puts on the crucified and resurrected Christ within himself, and now takes His yoke upon himself. But this is not called merely "knowing" and "comforting," for Christ does not dwell in the body of wickedness.

38. If Christ is to rise in you, the will of death and the devil must die in you; for Christ has broken death, destroyed hell, and become a Lord over death and hell. Wherever He enters into a person, there death and hell must be broken and give way in the inner ground, that is, in the soul. He destroys the devil’s kingdom in the soul, and gives birth to it as a child of God, and to His
<meta>catchword: Temple</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that salvation is an internal transformation rather than a mere external legal decree, refuting a passive view of grace. He explains that for Christ to be truly resurrected within a person, the individual's "abominable will" must die to allow for a literal indwelling of the divine spirit.</summary>

# Chapter 10. The Election of Grace <note>original: "Gnaden-Wahl." A central topic in the Reformation concerning predestination and how God chooses those who are saved.</note>

...Temple, and gives to it His will, and kills the will of the corrupted nature; that is, He **transmutes** <note>original: "transmutiret" — Böhme often uses alchemical terminology to describe the soul’s transformation from a "base" state of sin into "divine gold."</note> it into the true image of God. For it is written in 1 Corinthians 1:30: *Christ is made unto us righteousness through His blood.* Now, if a person desires to have this righteousness, they must drink His blood so that it may justify them; for **justification** <note>original: "Rechtfertigung" — the process by which a sinner is made right before God.</note> occurs in the blood of Christ within the person—in the soul itself—not through an external, **imputed, foreign appearance** <note>original: "äusserlichen, zugerechneten, fremden Schein" — Böhme is critiquing the common Lutheran doctrine of "imputed righteousness," where God legally "credits" righteousness to a sinner without necessarily changing their inner nature. Böhme insists on an actual, internal change.</note>.

39. That is the "imputed foreign appearance" which is given to us in the blood of Christ through grace while we are dead in sins; thus God gives us this **gift of grace** within us for a new life. This new life kills sin and death, and places us before God as children of grace; for Christ, with His blood of love, fulfills God’s righteousness within the **wrath** <note>original: "Zorne" — in Böhme’s system, God’s wrath is a foundational, fiery quality of nature that must be "tempered" or "extinguished" by the "water" of divine love.</note> in us, and transforms that same wrath into divine joy.

40. Therefore, if a person does not find themselves within the Divine Will, or at least within a heartfelt desire and longing to perform repentance, to obey God, and to **put on Christ** <note>A reference to Galatians 3:27.</note>: let them not say that they are a true Christian. The **mouth-chatter** <note>original: "Mund-Geschwäße" — hollow talk or empty profession of faith.</note>—where one confesses Christ as God’s Son with the tongue and comforts oneself with His grace, but keeps the serpent with its poisonous will toward **pride, greed, envy, and malice** <note>original: "Hoffart, Geiß, Neid und Bosheit" — the four fundamental "dark" desires that Böhme believes characterize the fallen human soul.</note> in the heart (only wishing to continue doing evil)—all of that helps nothing. Such a person only crucifies Christ and mocks His merit; for with the tongue they confess Him, but with the serpent’s venom in the heart, they pelt Him with filth and stones. They do nothing more than the devils did, who acknowledged Christ as the power of God when He drove them out of the possessed.

41. For it is not those who merely confess Christ with the mouth who are therefore children, but those who do the **will of His Father** who is in heaven—that is, in Christ Himself. For Christ is the Father’s "good will," which no one can perform unless they are in Christ and do it in Christ’s spirit and life.

42. For not all who descend from Abraham are God's children, but the children of the promised seed, **reborn** <note>original: "neugeboren" — the core of Böhme’s theology: the "New Birth" or "Regeneration."</note> from that same seed. They are children who are reborn from the blood of Christ [the first ground in the blood of
