43. For verbal confession without the inner, essential ground of Christ is the true **Antichrist**, who confesses Christ with words but denies Him with power, and has set himself in Christ’s place; he says one thing, and wills and does another. Therefore the prophet Hosea says that the Lord calls that "His love" which was not "His love" <note>Referencing Hosea 2:23: "I will say to them which were not my people, Thou art my people." Böhme interprets this as God's acceptance of those who live righteously without formal religious knowledge.</note>—namely, those who do not know Christ in name and essence, and know nothing of His revelation in humanity, but yet go with their souls into their inward ground, where grace was embodied in Paradise through the **divine breathing-in** <note>original: "Einsprechen" — the act of God speaking His Word into the human soul.</note>, and they take hold of grace in God’s mercy. These are those who neither hear nor have the Gospel, yet believe in the One God and commit themselves with all their powers to Him, and would gladly know and love God if they only knew what they should do; they also strive with their whole heart in righteousness and truth. These, because they do not hear or know Christ in His revealed voice, are outwardly not "God’s love"; but according to the inward ground, they are rooted in the love of grace (as in the Paradisiacal ground) in the **ingrafted Word** <note>original: "eingeleibte Wort" — the idea that God's creative Word is physically and spiritually woven into the human essence.</note>. These, says God, He would lead to His Supper, for they were His love; and precisely because they testify in power that the **work of the Law**, and the love of God’s grace, is written in their hearts <note>original: "Gesetzes Werck... in ihr Hertz geschrieben" — a reference to Romans 2:14-15 regarding the "natural law" known to those outside the faith.</note>, they are a law unto themselves. This Law Christ once fulfilled through His blood in His grace, which pressed from One upon all—upon all who are born out of the ingrafted grace in the spirit of the will.

44. For although the text in **John 3:18** says: *He who does not believe in the name of the only-begotten Son of God is judged already*; yet one cannot say that these aforementioned do not believe in Him. Truly, the outer human...

# Chapter 10. The Election of Grace.

...believe in and confess Him, for they do not know that God’s Son has become human; but the very same inner, **embodied ground** <note>original: "eingeleibter Grund" — the spiritual foundation or "seed" of Christ's presence planted within the human soul.</note> of the **spoken Word of Grace** <note>original: "eingesprochenen Worts der Gnade" — Böhme’s term for God's promise of redemption "spoken" into the human heart after the Fall.</note>, into which they have incorporated themselves with their soul, believes in them until the Day of the Revelation of Jesus Christ, when He shall manifest His Kingdom.

45. For even the Fathers of the Jews did not know Christ in the flesh, but rather in a prototype, as embodied grace which manifested itself through the figure in the Covenant and in their Law. They did not put on Christ in the flesh until His revelation in the flesh; but in the first embodied Covenant and Word, in its power, they did put Him on. However, when Christ fulfilled that same Covenant with humanity, and satisfied the law of wrath concerning sin with His own blood, and killed the sin within them (which had held humanity back), then all those who had believed in Him in His Covenant put on Christ in the flesh. That is to say, those who had put on the Covenant in power, as in the spirit, were fulfilled in the Covenant with **heavenly substance** <note>original: "himmlischen Wesen" — the spiritual "body" or essence of the divine world.</note>. This includes even those whose outer bodies had long since decayed, but whose souls lived in the Covenant of power. All of these put on Christ in His resurrection within them, and many of them rose with Him from death in His body after His resurrection, and allowed themselves to be seen in Jerusalem as a testimony that they had arisen in Christ and had put on Christ in the flesh, who has fulfilled their faith within humanity.

46. Therefore it is told to you, you **blind Christendom** with your **mouth-chatter** <note>original: "Mund-Geschwäße" — Böhme's frequent critique of "formulaic" or "nominal" Christians who speak of faith but do not live it.</note>, that without Christ in the flesh you are as far, and much farther, from Christ than the pious heathens, Turks <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was a common term for Muslims.</note>, and other nations who do not know Christ but who walk upon the inward, first ground.

47. For outside of Christ, man has no God, because the God **JEHOVAH** <note>Böhme associates the name "Jehovah" with God the Father in His aspect of holy fire and power.</note> has given humanity to Christ as the name and power of **JESUS** (which reveals itself out of **JEHOVAH**). Thus, if a stranger approaches the God **JEHOVAH** and yields himself to Him, the God **JEHOVAH** gives that person to Christ. For Christ also said: *Father (that is JEHOVAH), I have lost none of those whom you have given me* <note>A reference to John 17:12.</note>; that is, the God **JEHOVAH** will [be] in the soul...

48. Your presumed election (of a special adoption of children apart from the new birth) is your snare and fall; the wrath of **God** makes your false path, which you walk, into a snare that entangles you, and leads you in your outward finery into the pit of death and hell. This is why your children are almost entirely murderers, greedy, fornicators, thieves, envious, malicious, perjurers, faithless, stubborn, resistors of the truth, and proud—standing in the mind of the devil after power, honors, and authority, to oppress and trample the miserable in their hearts. Outwardly they shine with hypocrisy, and cover this villainy with the grace of Christ. Your election and purpose, **God**! must serve as a cover for their villainy, though you have chosen nothing for yourself except Christ in His members who are born from Him; for Christ alone is the **Election of Grace** <note>original: "Gnaden-Wahl"</note> itself. But your own justice in your zeal (not **God**) finds them in your wrath, which is why things go so poorly. *Sighing, Wish, and Prophecy of the Author.*

49. O deep grace of **God**, awaken yourself once more in us poor, confused, blind children, and tear down the throne of the **Anti-Christ** and the devil, which he has built up in hypocrisy, and let us at last see your face. O **God**, the time of your visitation is indeed here, but who knows your arm, in the face of the great vanity of the **Anti-Christ** <note>original: "Wieder-Christs" — literally "Against-Christ."</note> in his built-up kingdom? Destroy him, **Lord**, and tear down his power, so that your child **Jesus** may be revealed to all youths and peoples, and we may be delivered from the power, pride, and greed of the **Anti-Christ**. Hallelujah! From the sunrise and the midnight, the **Lord** hisses with His power and

->The 11th Chapter.<-

# Further Comparison and Explanation of the Verses concerning Election.

->Summaries.<-

Explanation of the verse in Romans 10:6, 9. §. 1. The Word must not only be in our mouth, but also in our heart. 2. It must be innate <note>original: "ingeboren" — literally "in-born," suggesting that the divine Word must take root and grow as a new nature within the person.</note>; an external election is of no use. 3. Further, an explanation of Romans 10:13. 4. He who has the Word in his heart is a Christian. 5. Grace comes from Christ, but works must testify that grace is alive in the soul. 6. He who does not bring forth works of love is dead; 7. for election applies to those who bring forth the fruits of grace. 8. Further, an explanation of Romans 11:7–10. 9. 10. Those who hear the Word but do not take it into their hearts are hardened by God’s wrath; pride, worry, greed, selfhood <term>selfhood: "Selbheit" — Böhme's term for the ego-driven nature that centers on the "I" rather than God.</term>, and self-love are the bolts <note>original: "Riegel" — the spiritual barriers that lock the heart against the entrance of divine light.</note>. ibid. Election, however, applies only to those born from the seed of faith; 11. for only the circumcision of the heart counts before God. Those who are merely circumcised outwardly are like weeds among the wheat. 12. Likewise, the false person who makes a great display <note>original: "Gepräng" — pomp or outward showy piety.</note>. 13. The 1st part of the false Israelites are the false shepherds, who have established themselves in the "Letter" <note>The literal, academic study of scripture without spiritual life.</note> and yet are only wolves. 14. The 2nd part are the princes and officials who call injustice "right." 15. These two parts are the Whore and the Beast <note>Referring to the figures of the Great Whore and the Beast in the Book of Revelation, symbolizing corrupt church and state power.</note>, who do not attain election. 16. For God does not take thistle-seed to produce wheat from it. 17. He desires only to do good to those who love Him and keep His commandments. 18. The Kingdom of Nature is contained within the seed. If a soul in the Kingdom of Nature is seized by wrath and grows into a thistle, it is its own fault; 19. for it treads grace underfoot; it knows God’s will but does not do it. 20. This comes from God’s justice according to nature, from inherited sins and other abominations. 21. Such "thistle-children" may walk among Christians, but they remain weeds; 22. and just as the sun’s heat withers the thistle, so Christ sits within the godless as a judge. 23. Satan sits in the thistle at the ground of the soul as a prince of this world and tears the Word from the heart. 24. Therefore Christ says: "You are from below, from your father the devil." 25. Just as the teachers of today are also such wolves, who persecute and brand one another as heretics; 26. they run in the grimness of God’s wrath and stir up war. 27. They are like the Pharisees; they have no love of God in their hearts and do not follow Christ in the new birth <term>new birth: "Wiedergeburt" — the spiritual transformation where the soul is reborn through Christ's spirit.</term> 28, 29. Everything that is outside of Christ is nothing but hypocrisy. 30.

Furthermore, Romans 11:15–16; Explanation. 31. As the tree is, so is the fruit, likewise. The forgiveness of sins pertains only to the inward ground <note>original: "inwendigen Grund" — the deepest, most essential part of the human spirit.</note>. 32. God allows the wicked to be preached to and calls them to repentance: for in Him there is no compulsion. 33. If the wicked person converts, God receives him into grace; yet he remains clinging only to the letter <note>original: "am Buchstaben hangen" — focusing on the outward literal meaning of scripture rather than its living spirit.</note> and will not listen to Christ within him, though true humility is necessary. 34. But if the soul stands purely before God and waits in resignation <term>resignation: "Gelassenheit" — a key mystical concept describing a state of yielding one's self-will to God's will.</term>, then Christ comes to her aid, 35. and the Holy Spirit intercedes for her with unspeakable groanings <note>original: "unaussprechlichen Seuffzen" — a reference to Romans 8:26.</note>, as it pleases God. 36. All others are thieves and murderers. 37. Christ alone is the door; He enters the human heart through the Word, 38. and if the poor sinner converts, he can be helped. 39. For although man cannot convert himself, the soul still has the power to swing itself into the ground <note>original: "in den Grund zu schwingen" — Böhme uses the motion of "swinging" or "leaping" to describe the soul’s energetic movement toward its divine source.</note>. 40. Should someone say that the soul cannot do this, he is blind. 41. For the soul is indeed from the eternal abyss <term>abyss: "Abgrund" — literally "without ground," the primal, unconditioned depth of God before creation.</term>, 42. and although she cannot swing herself into the light by her own power, she can nevertheless do so into the cause of the light. 43. etc.

Saint Paul says in Romans 10, verses 6 to 9: "The righteousness that comes from faith speaks thus: Say not in your heart, 'Who will ascend into heaven?' (that is nothing else than to bring Christ down) or 'Who will descend into the deep?' (that is nothing else than to bring Christ up from the dead). But what does it say? 'The Word is near you, namely in your mouth and in your heart.' This is the Word of faith that we preach." Explanation: Who would preach to us of a foreign, adopted grace <note>original: "fremden angenommenen Gnade" — Böhme critiques the idea that grace is merely a legal status "adopted" by God without an internal change in the person.</note>, since it is the Word of faith alone that hovers in power in our mouth and heart?

2. How shall the wicked be converted through the foreign appearance of an adopted sonship, unless he takes the Word, which he carries in his mouth and with which he confesses Christ, into his heart, so that the soul may grasp it in its innermost ground? Where is the adopted sonship, except where the Word takes root and dwells in the heart of the soul? Where does God accept people dead in sins—in whom His wrath alone lives—and compel them through a separate election into the purpose of His grace? He lets the Word hover in the mouth of the wicked, and also in his ears; but if his heart does not grasp it in the soul, He lets the light in the Word go out in the ears and hearts of the wicked, and this because the wicked [remains] in the purpose of His

# Chapter 11. The Election of Grace.

[...seized by] wrath, and the soul has awakened and ignited the life of darkness, with its inherited and introduced vanity, such that it is a life of thistles and serpents, to which the Word of God’s love does not attach itself.

3. So if the Word, which hovers in our mouths and hearts, makes us children of faith, then no foreign adoption <note>original: "Annehmung" — referring to the theological concept of being "accepted" or "adopted" by God.</note> through a special outward appearance of being chosen can count; rather, it is the In-born Word, speaking out again from that same inner-birth <term>inner-birth: "Ingeburt" — a central concept in Böhme’s mysticism, referring to the spiritual rebirth of Christ within the human spirit.</term>, where Christ speaks from His own ground with and through the soul—that is the sonship of adoption. For if you confess with your mouth **JESUS**, that He is the **LORD**, and believe in your heart that God has raised Him from the dead, you will be saved. Romans 10:9. But not through a separate delusion; rather, the Spirit of Christ must confess in you that Jesus Christ has risen from the dead within you. Your lip-confession <note>original: "Maul-Bekennen" — literally "mouth-confessing," used here as a dismissive term for superficial or hollow religious profession.</note> without the resurrection of Christ in you helps you nothing, for Christ spoke: *Without me you can do nothing.* Likewise: No one can call God a Lord without Christ being in him; for he does not grasp the word **LORD** in its power without Christ; therefore, his calling Him "Lord" is without life. For there is no difference between Jews and Greeks; there is one **LORD** over all, rich toward all who call upon Him. Romans 10:12.

4. Furthermore: Romans 10:13. *For whoever shall call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.* Explanation: Here Saint Paul makes no distinction among peoples; rather, whoever desires God in his heart, to him He gives the salvation which He offers in Christ.

5. Where then is the chosen people who boast that God has chosen them above other peoples because they can speak of Christ’s humanity? Since He has His kingdom among Jews and Greeks, and since he alone is a Jew and a Christian who is so in the heart of the soul? Where then is external, imputed grace without the sonship of the soul? When has God ever chosen a devil and made him a child of God? Surely never.

6. So, note this: Grace does not come from the merit of works, but from the fountain of life, Christ alone; however, works testify that grace in Christ is in the [soul...]

# XV. Concerning Chapter 11.

[...so that the] soul might be alive; for if the work does not follow, then Christ has not yet risen from the dead in you: for whoever is born of God performs divine works; but whoever is of sin serves sin with his works.

7. No one should boast of being a Christian unless he desires to perform divine works in the love of Christ; otherwise, it is but a foreign appearance <note>original: "fremder Schein" — an outward show or facade that lacks the inner reality of faith.</note> without the life of Christ.

8. The election to sonship <note>The theological concept of being chosen as a child of God.</note> concerns only those who are alive in grace and perform good works in grace; but the others, who console themselves with sonship through an "acceptance of grace" while working only abominations in their hearts, are hardened by the purpose of God's wrath. However, concerning those who are not born of grace, yet wish to attain it through their own works and merit (which shine outwardly but are dead within, and shine only for the sake of appearance), St. Paul says:

9. Romans 11:7-10. What then? What Israel seeks, he does not obtain, but the election <note>original: "die Wahl" — the chosen/elect.</note> obtains it; the others are hardened, as it is written: Isaiah 6:10. God has given them a spirit of bitterness <note>original: "erbitterten Geist" — often translated in Bibles as a "spirit of stupor."</note>; eyes, so that they do not see, and ears, so that they do not hear, until this very day. And David says: Let their table become a snare, and a trap, and a stumbling block, and a retribution to them; blind their eyes so they do not see, and bend their back always. Psalm 69:23-24.

10. **Explanation.** Which of Israel does the Spirit mean here, in Isaiah 6:10 and St. Paul: those who are not among the chosen, whom God wills to harden in His wrath? **Answer:** He means those who, when they hear the Word, take it into their ears and grasp it as a lesson for the intellect <note>original: "Vernunft" — human reason or logic, which Böhme often contrasts with deep spiritual understanding.</note>, but do not take it into the soul so that it takes root in the abyss <term>abyss: "Abgrund" — in Böhme’s mysticism, the "groundless" depth of the soul where God’s grace or wrath is encountered.</term>; it does not reach the first indwelling grace because pride and self-interest <term>self-interest: "Eigenheit" — the state of being focused on the "self" or ego rather than God.</term> stand in the way, as does the "care of the belly" <note>A traditional idiom for worldly anxieties and physical desires.</note>; the spirit is a bolt before it, and the pride of selfhood <term>selfhood: "Selbheit" — the egoistic nature that seeks to be its own master.</term>, one's own fleshly love, has seated itself in God’s place.

11. These show off outwardly with grace and grasp it with the work of their hands, seeking to earn grace through works, just as the false Jews did, who only...

<summary>This page argues that true election and "sonship" with God are proven by inner spiritual life and divine works, rather than outward religious performance or intellectual learning. Böhme explains biblical "hardening" as the result of human pride and "selfhood" blocking the soul's access to grace.</summary>

### Chapter 11. The Election of Grace.

[...those who] clung to works and did not have faith in the depths of their souls: Of these, St. Paul says that what Israel seeks through works, he does not obtain, but the election obtains it. Romans 11:7. For the election applied only to those Jews who were Jews in the abyss of their souls, and were born from the seed of faith—those who were born from the promised seed as from the <term>spoken-in Word</term> <note>original: "eingesprochenen Worte" — a central concept in Jacob Boehme’s theology referring to the divine Word or "breath" of God infused into the soul at creation and again through grace.</note>, within the covenant of Abraham and Adam, and who had been circumcised by the Word in their hearts.

12. For it was not the circumcision of the foreskin of the flesh that mattered before God, but that of the heart: that of the flesh, however, was the seal and sign of the inward ground, showing how grace would cut away sin from the soul. But for those who only dealt with outward works, it was not so; for they were among Israel like weeds among the wheat, which spreads itself out over the wheat and flutters about grandly, wanting to be seen as a great growth. But it bears no good fruit and is otherwise useful for nothing but to be burned at harvest time, for it only pricks everything around it and takes up space.

13. In the same way, the false person also seats himself in the temple of God and calls himself a Christian, performing many <term>show-works</term> <note>original: "Scheinwercke" — religious actions done for the sake of appearances rather than out of true devotion.</note> through which he wishes to have the reputation of being the best Christian. He learns art, studies, and knows how to say much about God; he teaches others, but does so for the sake of profit and honor, just as the Pharisees did, who feigned great holiness and wore large borders on their priests' robes and offered long prayers as a facade of outward piety.

14. But Christ said: 
> They devour widows' houses and travel over land and sea to make a single convert <note>original: "Juden-Genossen" — a proselyte or convert to Judaism.</note>, and when he is made, they make him a child of hell, twice as much as they are themselves. Matthew 23:15.

These, then, are the ones who put on such a great facade and say they sit in Christ's stead, and that their words are God's word. These spread themselves out and puff themselves up, and in their hearts they strive only after honor, greed, and pride. Whatever they say, people are expected to hold it as if it were God's voice from heaven—even if that voice, coming from a false mind, has [inserted] itself into the written word—

Using metaphors of weeds and the wheat and the example of the Pharisees, the author warns against those who use the word of God for personal gain and status.</summary>

# XV. Regarding Chapter 11.

[...and are] instituted, and under the letter of the Word—just as weeds flutter among the wheat—yet it is still claimed to be God’s Word. If anyone speaks against it and points out this "false child," their own **pride** <term>pride: original "Hoffart," implying a haughty, arrogant spirit that places itself above God</term> cries out: "He is a **fanatic** <term>fanatic: original "Schwärmer," a term used in this period for those who claimed direct spiritual inspiration outside of official church structures</term> and despises the office; guard yourselves against him, he misleads you! Only come here to me, for here is the right office, which is instituted by God." And even though they are not instituted by God, but through human favor, and do not serve God but rather their own bellies, their pride, and self-love; yet in their own minds, they believe themselves to be the "beautiful child of grace." They imagine they have so much grace to spare that they might sell it dearly for money to others, based on the power of their supposed great holiness; but whoever buys this, buys a thistle in place of good seed.

15. The other part of the false Israelites of Abraham’s natural seed are those who, by the power of nature, are set over Israel as princes and rulers in all offices—whatever name they may have, from the greatest to the least—with the purpose that they should be protectors of justice. These all put forward a great appearance of truth, and lift themselves so high in their offices through their own **conceit** <note>original: "Dünckel"</note>, that they think they are self-empowered gods. They believe that whatever they want to do must be right; that their office holds such power that everything they do must be called "good." Yet they do not seek the justice of God in His purpose for the order of nature, much less the justice in love, which He has revealed through the **grace of Christ**. Instead, they put their own invented justice—serving their own honors and fleshly, lustful pride—in the place of Divine justice and truth. They only let the law of God hover in their mouths; but the heart has taken on the "right" of a thistle, which flutters over the good herbs and pricks everything around it, spreading itself far and wide, yet bearing no good seed itself.

16. These two parts (excepting the children of God who are still among them) are now the **Whore** and the **Beast** in the **Revelation of John** <note>referring to the apocalyptic figures in the Book of Revelation, often interpreted by Boehme as symbols of corrupt church and state power</note>, through which the devil is a prince of this world among men, whom the angel plunges into the abyss of the **sulfur-pool** <note>original: "Schwefel-Pful," or the Lake of Fire</note>. These are not true Israelites born from the seed of the promise, and [reach...]

### Chapter 11. The Election of Grace.

[...they] do not attain sonship; rather, it is the Election—which alone seeks and accepts the children of faith within the righteousness of grace—that attains it. The Whore together with the Beast, however, are hardened in their lusts of malice, **pride** <term>pride: original "Hoffart," implying a haughty, arrogant spirit that places itself above God</term>, greed, envy, wrath, and unrighteousness; they are the Anti-Christ, acting as the **Title-Christian** <term>Title-Christian: one who holds the name of Christian in name only</term> and **Mouth-Christian** <term>Mouth-Christian: one who speaks of faith but does not live it</term>, a devil in the form of an angel, just as Lucifer was in heaven, who was cast out as a false seed; so too are these.

17. For the Election of the House-Father of all beings seeks only good seed; it does not choose thistle-seed for itself to make a grain of wheat out of it, as Reason <note>In this context, "Reason" refers to human logic that lacks spiritual illumination</note> imagines—thinking that God takes the entire false seed and makes a child of God out of it, so that He might show His wealth of grace through a special purpose. No, that does not happen. Let the godless one—that is, one who has sprung from a true seed but has introduced the inclination toward abominations into himself through his inherited **constellation** <term>constellation: the astrological or spiritual configuration of one's nature at birth</term>—repent and enter into his inner ground, and be born out of grace; then it may happen.

18. For God said to Moses: *I will show mercy to those who love me and keep my commandments, even unto the thousandth generation.* Exodus 20: 6. This showing of mercy is nothing other than a planting of the **Covenant of Grace** <note>original: "Gnaden-Bund"</note> into their seed, just as it was promised to Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, and David: that He would so greatly bless and multiply their seed, according to the promised embodied grace, that it could not be numbered.

19. But the kingdom of nature, within God’s purpose of righteousness, also stood within this seed according to the quality of the soul, which was meant to work alongside it. However, in many, the soul's will turned away from the kingdom of the purpose of grace. Now, that those souls were seized in the kingdom of nature within wrath, and grew into thistles, was not God’s fault, but rather the fault of the **science** <term>science: here used in the sense of "scientia" or "knowledge," specifically the soul's self-awareness or formative power</term> of the soul’s ground, coming from the eternal ground toward nature—as the free will of the **Abyss** <term>Abyss: original "Ungrund," the groundless or bottomless source of all being in Böhme's philosophy</term> moves toward the natural ground of the soul.

20. Therein lies the first ground of the **thistle-children** <note>A metaphor for those who reject divine grace and persist in their own ego</note>, who trample the embodied grace of the spoken Word with the feet of their false lust, and do not wish to be children of grace. Of this, Christ Himself (as this gate of grace) says in Micah 7:1: He was like a grape-gatherer who...

# XV. Concerning Chapter 2.

[...desired to] set; Likewise, He often wanted to gather the children of Israel as a mother hen gathers her chicks under her wings, but they were not willing. (Matthew 23:37).

21: Here Reason <note>Böhme uses "Reason" (Vernunft) to represent human logic that attempts to understand God without spiritual illumination</note> speaks: "They were not able to; indeed, they cannot." Answer: Why? Reason: "They are thistle-children." Answer: Why? Reason: "It is because of God’s purpose." Answer: It is out of the purpose of Divine Justice, according to the order of the creation of nature, as well as from the distinctiveness of the Speaking within the Word <note>original: "Sprechen im Wort," referring to the divine act of creation where God’s breath becomes the substance of the world</note>, when <term>Science</term> <term>Science: original "Scienz," referring here to the active, self-aware will or the "knowledge" inherent in a soul's specific nature</term> as the selfhood of the <term>Abyss</term> <term>Abyss: original "Ungrund," the groundless, irrational depth of the Divine before it manifests as light or dark</term> takes hold of its own first ground; that is what it is. For in that place, God’s wrath takes hold in the center of nature, in the seed of men, incorporating their inherited sins as well as future actual abominations; there God's anger often establishes a root in the parents' sin and embeds itself in the <term>Science</term> of the <term>Abyss</term>. From this, a thistle-root later arises in the seed, where God punishes the sin of the parents in their seed until the third and fourth generation, according to the Scripture.

22. These thistle-children also come from Israel, but not from grace; that is, the grace that was embodied in them in Paradise grows within them toward judgment, just as the sun’s burning <term>essence</term> <term>essence: original "Ens," a term for the fundamental substance or "being-ness" of a thing</term> indeed gives itself into the thistle, but not according to the <term>Tincture of Love</term> <term>Tincture of Love: the spiritual "dye" or quality of divine love that transforms a soul from within</term>, but rather according to the thistle’s own nature. For the thistle cannot receive it in any other way than in the likeness of its own essence, just as a toad sucks only poison from a good essence.

23. And just as the sun’s heat finally withers the thistle and ends its life; so too does Christ sit with His embodied grace upon His judgment seat within the godless person. He allows him to misuse the holy name of God in his mouth for a time as an oath for his falsehood, and to boast of being a true Christian under Christ’s merit in his supposed office (through which he thinks he serves God and awakens grace). He allows him to play the hypocrite and to shine as he will; He even lets him prophesy in Christ’s name, like Caiaphas, who advised that it was better for one man to die for the people than for the whole nation to perish. He also lets him grow fat and prominent in his Pharisaical office; He even gives him the calling grace in His testaments, just as the sun with its good power...

24. Christ calls them ravenous wolves, John 10:12, who devour the simple flock with their blasphemies, and kill them with the poison of their mockery of Christ, and swing themselves upward like thistles among the wheat, and place themselves in human honor, and confuse the world, and cause the thistle-children to bring about war and devastation to lands and people,

### XV. On Chapter 11.

...to which they faithfully contribute, counsel, and serve with their poisonous, embittered spirit. Therefore they are those of whom Saint Paul speaks in Romans 11:8–10, citing the Prophet David in Psalm 69:23: *Let their table become a snare, and a trap, and a cause of offense, and a retribution for them: Blind their eyes so they do not see, and bend their backs always.* <note>Böhme applies these biblical curses to the religious dogmatists of his day, suggesting their own intellectual "tables"—their doctrines—become the very traps that blind them.</note> That is, they repay one another in their own blindness, while in **Christ’s office** <note>original: "Christi Amte," referring to the ministry or position of a clergyman</note> they strive only for power and pleasure, so that they persecute, revile, and despise one another, and attribute Christ’s name within one another to the devil; in their beds they only strive for how they might meet one another with cunning, and justify <note>original: "beschönen," literally "to make beautiful," meaning to use rhetoric to make a bad cause look holy</note> their cause with the Scripture, as if they were doing this out of **Divine zeal** for the truth, to please God, and to serve their brothers.

27. These run like mad dogs, wolves, and evil insane beasts, in the fury of the kindled wrath of God, and devour the name of Christ from the **mouths of the laity**, and fill their hearts and mouths full of the blasphemy of their heart's false **invention**, <note>original: "Gedichtes," referring to man-made dogmas or fabricated stories as opposed to divine revelation</note> so that one person despises, blasphemes, <term>brands as a heretic</term> <note>original: "verketzert," the act of labeling someone a "Ketzer" or heretic to justify their exclusion</note>, and considers the other unfit for the sake of Christ’s name and their own invented opinions; yet they only devour themselves in such a way that one party exterminates the other, and they repay one another for their wickedness and falsehood, as David says here.

28. These are now those of whom Christ said, who sit in the highest seats in the schools, and love to be greeted in the marketplaces, Matthew 23:6–7; who put on a show of reason, but their hearts are full of bitter gall: and their ways are harmful, **asp’s venom** <note>original: "Otter-Gift"</note> is under their lips, and they serve me in vain, says the Prophet. None of these are among the election of the **Children of God**, but only those of whom Christ says: *Love one another, by this all will know that you are my disciples.* John 13:35. Likewise, *If you remain in my word, blessed are you if you do it.* John 13:17. Likewise, *He who does not leave houses, money, goods, wife, children, and denies himself, and follows me, is not my servant.* Luke 14:26, 33. The heart must surrender everything and hold nothing as its own, but think that he is only a servant of God and his brothers in his station, and with that which he has to administer, he should do as God [requires] of him...

### Chapter 11. The Election of Grace.

...demands and desires of him, rather than merely covering himself with the **cloak of Christ** and His merit while remaining a greedy, proud, envious, and angry person underneath.

29. All these, as many as there are, as long as they remain so, are those of whom Saint Paul and David speak here; they may well be called, but they are not among the **chosen of grace** <note>original: "Wahl der Gnaden," referring to the theological concept of election or predestination</note> unless they turn back during the time of grace, abandon everything in their hearts, and follow Christ.

30. No grace imputed from without accepts them unless they first become **children of grace**; only then does the imputed grace (which is Christ) take up residence within them. Outside of Christ, they are nothing but Pharisees and hypocrites; no matter how they might shine with **imputed grace** <note>original: "zugerechneten Gnade," a reference to the Reformation doctrine that believers are "counted" righteous because of Christ, which the author warns can be misused as a cover for unchanged behavior</note> as they wish, they are still wolves, whom Christ commands us to beware of. Even if they say: "Here is Christ’s Church," it is all for nothing; *by their fruits you shall know them*, says Christ in Matthew 7:16. If they do not follow Christ, they are thieves and murderers, says Christ. Even if they object that the office makes men into high priests who have weaknesses, and wish to cover themselves with that excuse: it counts for nothing; the heart must be in Christ and walk in Him. and although Saint Paul said that "desire clings to the flesh" and "sin dwells in the outer flesh" (Romans 7:17, 18), one can clearly see who desires to put those desires to death and follow Christ. For where greed and pride dwell within, a Pharisee has taken up lodging; excuse yourself however you will, you still have him around your neck.

31. Furthermore: Romans 11:15, 16. *For if their (the Jews') loss is the reconciliation of the world, what would that be other than receiving life from the dead; if the firstfruits are holy, the whole lump is holy, and if the root is holy, so the branches are also holy.* **Explanation.** This single text strikes down all opinions that God imputes grace to the godless [by decree], and instead places it upon the foundation of the **root**, indicating that God does not harden some by His will because He wants to prove through them what His grace is. For Saint Paul says: *What would that be other than taking life from the dead?* He places the "hardening" upon the root—namely, that an evil tree bears evil fruit, and a holy tree bears holy branches, and the **wrath of God** gives birth to children of wrath, and—

<summary>This page argues that true salvation requires an internal transformation rather than a mere "imputed" righteousness that covers an unchanged heart. It uses the metaphor of the root and branches from Romans 11 to explain that holiness must be organic and essential to the believer's nature.</summary>

### XV. On Chapter 11.

...such things [come] from the sin and vanity of men, which must nonetheless serve as a light to the Heathens, as he says in Romans 8:28: *To those who love GOD, all things must work together for the best*, who are called and born according to the purpose of grace.

32. Regarding the **forgiveness of sins**, where the Scripture says He forgives them their sin and credits grace to them for a **justification** <term>justification: the theological concept of being declared righteous before God, not through one's own deeds, but through divine grace</term>; this applies solely to those in whose inner depths Christ lives. Even though sin still clings to them in the flesh—as it did to David and many others—so that they often fall, the grace within them helps them up again and erases the sin and transgression.

33. This does not apply to the **hardened** <note>original: "Verstockten," referring to those whose hearts are stubborn or closed to spiritual influence</note>, those without repentance and a total transformation. They must not continue to sin (while lying dead in their own will toward sin) [and claim] that GOD is the cause of their damnation; [nor should they expect] to see His grace shown through a special calling or force to convert them, as if He were to make an angel out of a devil by a special decree—otherwise Lucifer and his followers would also have cause for hope. Rather, He lets His sun shine into their mouths and ears for the entire day of their lives, calling them and saying: "Do not harden your hearts with actual sin, so that the Word may echo and take root in your hearts."

34. For it is indeed possible that a poor, dead sinner may be converted if he is willing to stand still from his own mental images and listen for a moment to what the Lord says within him. But the hardened, embittered spirit does not want to hear the Lord's voice speaking within himself; instead, he only cries out: **"The Letter! The Letter!"** <note>original: "Buchstabe! Buchstabe!"; a critique of those who rely only on the literal, written text of the Bible while ignoring inner spiritual experience</note> claiming the written word alone is it. He pulls this back and forth and boasts of it, but the **living Word**, which spoke the Letter into existence, he will neither tolerate nor hear within himself. If he is to come to knowledge, he must first let the Letter be killed within him; only then does the Spirit make the Letter truly alive. That is, he must die to the Letter and hold himself so unworthy that he is not even deserving of the literal word—like the poor **tax collector** <note>A reference to the parable of the Pharisee and the Tax Collector (Luke 18), where the humble sinner is justified over the prideful religious leader</note> in the temple—and feel that he no longer has any righteousness through the literal word, like one who has lost everything and is not worthy to lift his eyes to GOD, or for the earth to carry him, or to be counted among the number of GOD's children...

<summary>This page discusses the nature of divine justification, arguing that forgiveness belongs to those who harbor an internal "living Christ" despite their human failings. It critiques those who cling to the "Letter" of scripture without internal transformation, contrasting the humble sinner with the pridefully hardened heart.</summary>

It connects the internal spiritual experience to the biblical metaphors of the sheepfold and the door in John 10.</meta>

# Chapter 11. Election of Grace

...shall be accounted; thus he has lost everything, and the letter has killed him <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 3:6, "the letter kills, but the Spirit gives life."</note>, for he thus submits himself to **GOD’s** judgment. In this, he must hope only in the pure mercy of **GOD**, without any worthiness of his own, and sink himself into it like a dead man who has no life in him—no matter what [God] may do with him—and he must despair of all his works and sink himself with hope alone into the innermost, purest grace of **GOD**.

35. This the soul must do: and if it does so, and can remain therein for a moment, then the first engrafted covenant—as the gifted grace—seizes it and gives itself into the soul. Now, as soon as this happens, the Spirit of **Christ**, as the in-speaking <term>in-speaking: the internal, spiritual voice of Christ or the "Inner Word" speaking directly within the human heart</term> living Word, arises in the soul and begins to speak **GOD’s** Word; and in that hour the **Holy Spirit** proceeds there from the Father and the Son, and represents the soul in **GOD’s** righteousness with unspeakable groaning in prayer, as it is written in **Romans 8:26.**

36. We—that is, the poor soul—do not know what we should pray, but the Spirit of **GOD** represents us with unspeakable groaning, as it pleases **GOD**. And there, the letter—which had killed the soul in the law of **GOD’s** righteousness—makes it alive again, and appoints it as a teacher of His Word, both in the power of the living Word and in the literal word <note>original: "buchstabischen Worte," meaning the written scripture or the external message</note>; for only these later enter through the door into the sheepfold of **Christ**, and the sheep hear their voice, as **Christ** says.

37. But all the others together, by whatever name they are called, who do not enter through the door of the living Word through the literal word; they climb in elsewhere and are thieves and murderers, **John 10:1-3**, as **Christ** said, and the sheep do not hear their voice.

38. For **Christ** alone is the door; understand this as the living **Christ** in His life and speaking in and out of the soul; He goes through the literal word into the hearts of men, as through **Peter’s** sermon on the day of Pentecost. Whoever sets himself up as a teacher of the literal word otherwise is not sent by **GOD**, and comes only because he wants to steal—namely, he wants to steal the honor from **Christ** and take it for himself.

39. And thus the poor [man], killed in **GOD’s** wrath...

<summary>This page explains that spiritual rebirth requires a total surrender of self-worth to God's mercy, allowing the Holy Spirit to intercede and the "living Word" to speak from within. It distinguishes true teachers, who enter the sheepfold through the "door" of Christ's internal presence, from false teachers who only use the external word to seek their own honor.</summary>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 11

...man shall become living again, even if he were already dead. For **Christ** has come to call sinners to repentance and not the righteous: and when such a poor sinner, who was locked away in **GOD’s** wrath, comes [to Him], there is joy in heaven before **GOD’s** angels, more than over ninety-nine righteous (Luke 15:7) who are already grasped [by grace] and are branches of the saints, and do not first require such a foundation <note>original: "Grund," referring to the fundamental basis of conversion</note>, but rather the foundation already lies within them. Among the [sinners], however, the foundation becomes manifest within **GOD’s** wrath, and here **GOD** proves through them how life springs forth out of death, and how **Christ** destroys the devil's kingdom and breaks hell apart.

40. Therefore, this is our true conclusion: that no intentional decree of damnation has been made over any man such that it would be impossible for him to be converted. For although man cannot convert himself, his soul has the power—by virtue of its original state <term>original state: Böhme uses "Urstand" to describe the soul's primal condition before time and creation</term> arising from the eternal science <term>science: "Scienz," a mystical, active knowledge or "seeing" within the divine mind</term> of the Groundless <term>Groundless: "Ungrund," Böhme’s term for the absolute, unmanifested divine depth that exists before all created things</term>—to swing itself into the Abyss, into the Ground wherein **GOD** begets and speaks His Word. In this Abyss, the gift of grace lies within all men, and it leans more toward the soul than the soul leans toward this deep grace. There, the soul may indeed be grasped by **GOD’s** grace, so that it falls into **Christ’s** arms; He gives the soul the ability and the power far more readily than the soul even desires it.

41. But if someone were to say that the soul cannot "swing itself into the Abyss," he speaks as one who still understands nothing of **GOD’s** mystery, nor what the soul or an angel is; he wishes to break the branch from the tree in which he himself stands.

42. The soul was spoken into a creature out of the Abyss: who then will break the right of eternity, so that the eternal will of the soul—which proceeded from the eternal, singular Will into a creature—should not be allowed to swing itself back with that same will of the creature into its Mother from whence it came?

43. Into the Light (which has been extinguished for the [fallen] will), the soul cannot swing itself by its own power; but into the *cause* of the Light—wherein there is neither evil nor good—it can swing itself, for the soul itself is that very ground. If it only [sinks] itself out of its own image-forming <note>original: "Bildlichkeit," the soul's tendency to create its own self-centered reality or "images"</note>, into itself, upon the Abyss—
<meta>catchword: sinks</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues against the doctrine of predestined damnation, asserting that the soul originates from the divine "Groundless" and retains the inherent capacity to surrender itself back into that eternal source. He describes conversion as the soul "swinging" back into the Abyss where God's grace is always present and waiting.</summary>

# Chapter 12. The Election of Grace.

In the Acts of the Apostles, chapter 13:48, it is written: *As many as were appointed to eternal life became believers.* This is a stumbling block to human reason, and it does not understand it.

2. When did this "seeing-before" <note>original: *Versehung*, usually translated as providence or predestination; Böhme emphasizes the literal "seeing" (*sehen*) of God</note> begin? You might ask: from eternity, before any creature existed? Yes, I say so too; but it did not begin *within* the creature from eternity, for the creature did not yet exist.

3. **God** saw in love and wrath what would come to be if He enclosed the eternal nature within a creaturely form. For He saw well within Himself from eternity how the balanced state <term>Temperatur: the harmonious equilibrium of divine forces before they are divided</term> would express itself in a differentiation <term>Schiedlichkeit: the condition of being distinct, divided, or manifold</term>, and how this differentiation would enclose itself in a creaturely will, so that there would be an opposition <note>original Latin: *Contrarium*</note>; and this is the very ground of Divine Revelation. However, Scripture does not say that **God** ordained the wills in this differentiation from eternity to an eternal evil willing, or to an eternal good willing, such that each must inevitably will that which He has unavoidably ordained. For the change in the willing of **Lucifer** and **Adam** proves that they were free in their willing; but in the Fall, **Adam** lost his good-willing.

4. Now, in the cited text of Acts 13:48, it refers to the time here after the Fall: those who were "seen-before" <note>original: *versehen*</note> for this purpose at that time out of the eternal willing. For the text reads: And the **Lord** added to them as many as were provided, or seen in the **Light of Grace**, whose Divine Eye was open; those were, at that time, seen and provided for out of and within the inner ground, as Acts 2:47 states more clearly: *The Lord added daily those who were being saved.* It does not say "those who were saved from eternity"; rather, the text says "those who *were being saved*." Those who were being saved out of the eternal election in **Christ Jesus** were added daily to the congregation.

5. Question: Why not all at once? Answer: They had not yet been saved. They were indeed in the "seeing-before" <note>original: *Versehung*</note> or the "seeing" of **God**, in that they *would* be saved; but the decree only arrived with the addition to the congregation, at the moment they were saved.

6. Why were only 3,000 souls converted on the Day of **Pentecost**, and yet more and more thereafter? Answer: they were not yet "seen-before" within themselves; that is, "seen-before" in this place, when Grace rises up and [breaks] through the "Ver-," <note>Böhme is performing a wordplay on the German prefix *ver-* (as in *versehen*), often associating it with the "burning away" or "consuming" aspect of God's wrath that must be passed through to reach Grace</note> as if through the wrath

<sig>N 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: breaks,</meta>

<summary>Böhme explores the concept of predestination (the Election of Grace), arguing that while God "sees" all from eternity, human beings possess a free will that was lost in Adam's fall and is restored through the daily process of salvation in Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Election of Grace, Predestination, Free Will, Acts 13:48, Adam, Lucifer, Differentiation, Divine Revelation, Pentecost</keywords>

Böhme is refuting the idea of a harsh, individual predestination from eternity, arguing instead for a general provision of grace that interacts with human will over time. The previous page ended with a discussion on how God "saw-before" the Fall and provided a remedy.</meta>

# XV. Regarding Chapter 12.
<meta>catchword: not</meta>

breaks, then creaturely providence begins from the eternal grace-seeing, or insight. For how may a thing be ordained from eternity that has not existed from eternity?

7. How could the soul—from eternity, when it was still a being <term>Ens: a Latin term for an essence or fundamental substance</term> and a play within Divine Wisdom—be ordained to become a devil? This would be horrible to think or speak of, and yet it would allow for no other understanding if one insisted upon an "eternal ordinance." In such a case, all teaching would be in vain. Why should grace be preached to those who cannot err or fall, and who stand in an irrefutable predestination <note>original: *Prædestination*</note>?

8. This "seeing-before" from eternity is understood in Christ: that those who became believers were seen from eternity in the Wisdom. It means that when God would one day move and lead nature into differentiation <term>Schiedlichkeit: the condition of being distinct, divided, or manifold</term> for the purpose of creaturely revelation, the name JESUS (as the highest love of God) would give itself into the knowledge-potency <term>Scienz: from the Latin *scientia*, here referring to the inward seed of awareness or the "science" of the soul's will</term> of the fiery will within that differentiation. It would lead itself into the kingdom of joy within that fiery potency and transform the wrath into a fire of love in the soul of man (which had to originate <note>original: *urständen*; to arise from a primordial ground</note> from the fiery potency). There, grace in the name of JESUS intended to marry itself to the soul’s ground as a banner <note>original: *Panier*; a military standard or rallying flag</note>, just as happened in Paradise after the Fall. That same banner was placed in the seed of the woman, within which lay the providence from which all people come; but the differentiation in the fiery potency lasts as long as souls are being born.

9. There is no fixed ordinance from eternity over each individual soul that was to be born, but only a general providence of grace: the ordinance begins with the "time of the tree" <note>Böhme often uses the image of a tree to represent the growth of human generations and the unfolding of time</note>. Furthermore, the "seeing" is still within the seed: before it becomes a creature, God knows the ground of what will become of it, but the judgment belongs to the harvest-time, just as Christ speaks in all his parables.

### Concerning Lydia, the Dealer in Purple.

10. Where it is written in Acts 16:14: *The Lord opened her heart, so that she attended to what Paul said, and became a believer in the name of JESUS*; this is the same with Lydia as with all foreign nations who [did] not [know] the name of JESUS—

<summary>Böhme argues against the idea that God predestines individual souls to damnation from eternity, suggesting instead that God provides a general "banner" of grace in Christ that responds to the soul's development in time. He uses the biblical figure of Lydia to illustrate how God opens the heart to faith within the flow of human history rather than through an ancient, fixed decree.</summary>

### Chapter 12. The Election of Grace.

...do not know, and yet they reach for the inward ground beyond all imagery <note>The author uses "imagery" (*Bildlichkeit*) to refer to external religious forms or mental concepts that are not the direct, living experience of God.</note>, desiring to recognize the One God and to yield themselves to Him; these are seized by the embodied grace of the spoken Word. Without the knowledge of reason <note>original: *Vernunft*; often used by Böhme to describe human intellect that is blind to spiritual truths</note>, they are chosen and born as children of grace. This is also how we should think of Lydia <note>A woman from Thyatira, described in Acts 16, whose heart was "opened" by God</note>: although she might have initially taken Paul for a foreign teacher. But when she heard him preaching the law of righteousness, and how the law of sins (which holds man captive) had been fulfilled in such grace, her inner ground moved in its hunger for justification within the embodied grace. Christ became living in her, so that she perceived Christ's voice in the words of Paul; for Christ was teaching within her, and Christ became the one hearing within her.

11. But for the other heathens, it was not so, for they stood only in imagery; their hearts were not directed toward the One God to recognize Him, for they had their heathen idols whom they served, and only wanted to hear something "new" from Paul. Nevertheless, the Word entered their ears and pressed into those who were of a "good ground," who afterward were converted when they heard more of Christ preached; as indeed many thousands were converted afterward in that same place once the Word seized them more fully. Thus, many were also converted afterward among those who heard Peter on the Day of Pentecost and yet mocked him that same day; but as the Word echoed more within them, the hour of their inward hearing arrived. Just as with Longinus <note>The legendary name given to the Roman soldier who pierced Christ's side with a spear (John 19:34)</note>, who pierced Christ in the side—the hour of his conversion only came when he heard many say that Christ was God’s Son; and he became a martyr for Christ’s sake, as the histories report.

12. And one should not say here that Lydia, above others, was ordained to this from eternity so that she alone should hear Paul (Acts 16). She was at that time in a state of Divine preparation and wished to understand the true ground of God; her heart longed for it, and therefore God opened her heart. The others, however, were not yet prepared at that time; rather, when the Holy Spirit began to knock at their hearts, they only caught it in their ears, until they should open up to Him and

He emphasizes that even those who initially mock or resist the Gospel may later be converted when their "hour of inward hearing" arrives.</summary>

### XV. On Chapter 12.

...they meditated upon this and searched the Scriptures to see if things were as Paul said: Acts 17:11. As is also said of the Ephesians, that when they heard the Word more, they already had a hungry door of the heart open, where Christ had room with His Word.

13. This is how it has happened with all the heathens, and also with the Jews who initially mocked Christ as He hung on the Cross; but when they saw what happened there, many of them struck their hearts, turned back, and said: "Truly, this was a righteous man and the Son of God." Luke 23:47.

14. This happened to those Jews whose inner ground <note>The "ground" (*Grund*) is a central concept for Böhme, referring to the deepest, most essential part of the human soul where it meets the Divine.</note> stood open at that time; to them God opened the embodied grace <note>original: *eingeleibte Gnade*; this refers to grace that has taken form or "body" within the human spirit through the Word.</note> in the Spirit of Christ. For one finds much in histories, that many a person for a long time mocked Christ in their fashioned heathen imagery <note>The author uses *Bildlichkeit* (imagery) to describe the mental idols, worldly concepts, or external religious forms that block spiritual sight.</note>, and yet finally, when they went into the serious ground of their own self and wanted to actually perceive what kind of "fables" (as they called them) were being said of Christ, they were converted.

15. For as soon as the heart stands still from its imagery and swings itself into its own ground, the voice of Christ in the Word penetrates and knocks at the essence of the soul.

16. The imagination <note>original: *Einbildlichkeit*; the process of being captivated by or "forming oneself into" earthly things.</note> of the earthly being prevents the heart from standing still for God and from coming into its inward ground, where God teaches and hears. For God Himself is present in all places through everything, as it is written: "Do I not fill all things?" Jeremiah 23:24. Why then does the soul need to swing itself elsewhere to hear God, other than into its own abyss? <note>original: *Abgrund*; for Böhme, the "abyss" is the bottomless, eternal origin where God dwells, found within the soul's own depth.</note> There God is and dwells from eternity to eternity; He only needs to become manifest in the creature. For this purpose, He stands in the Spirit of Christ in that same inner ground and knocks at the soul: if the soul now turns toward Him, Christ Himself opens the door of grace and moves in with her, and eats the Supper with her, and she with Him. Revelation 3:20.

->Explanation of the saying in Matthew 13:11 and Luke 8:10.<-

17. In these places it is written: "To you it is given to understand the Kingdom of God, but to others in parables, so that..."

<summary>Böhme explains that conversion occurs when the soul silences its worldly "imagery" and turns inward to its own "ground" where God is eternally present. He emphasizes that God is omnipresent and knocks at the door of the soul, requiring only a "stillness" from earthly distractions to be heard.</summary>

### Chapter 12. The Election of Grace. 203

...they hear it and do not understand. Similarly, He explained the parables to the disciples, and not to the others.

18. Here, human reason <note>In Böhme’s thought, *Vernunft* (reason) refers to the limited, earthly intellect that cannot grasp divine mysteries without spiritual enlightenment.</note> lies as if dead, for it sees nothing without the Divine Light. It assumes nothing else than that Christ did not wish to grant it to the others—that they were not worthy of it—despite the fact that the people followed Him and heard Him teach with hungry desire. But there is a different "a.b.c." <note>A phrase meaning the basic principles or true spiritual logic.</note> and understanding here. Christ said to His disciples: "My Father will send you another Comforter, the Spirit of Truth, who proceeds from the Father; when He comes, He will remind you of everything I have said to you: for He will take from what is Mine and proclaim it to you." John 16:13–14.

19. It was not intended that the Father’s voice in Christ—residing in God's righteousness <note>*Gerechtigkeit* (righteousness) often refers to the strict divine justice or the "Law" in Böhme’s terminology.</note>—should enter the hearts and ears of the laity and listeners in that way (except for a few through whom the Father wished to work miracles). Instead, the voice that was to enter them was the one the Holy Spirit would bring forth from Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection: the voice of the open Door of Grace.

20. For before Christ’s suffering, the voice of the Holy Spirit in Christ was still within God’s righteousness, as if in the Law. But in Christ’s death, the Law of God’s righteousness was fulfilled. Thus, afterward, the Holy Spirit went out through that fulfillment—through Christ’s wounds, blood, and death—in the greatest mercy, in the Spirit of Christ. This was the voice the poor sinners were to hear, those who followed Him with desire. To the disciples, however, the Father’s voice in God’s righteousness was given, so that they should hear it from Christ; for they were first to be clothed with that same fiery righteousness <note>A reference to the "First Principle" or the consuming fire of God’s power and justice, which Böhme associates with the Father.</note> in which the Father’s omnipotence stood as the foundational ground of the soul. Afterward, on the Day of Pentecost, the Holy Spirit from Grace-Love—from Christ’s fulfillment of righteousness—was given into the fiery righteousness of the Father.

21. When that happened, the "tongues" (representing the Father’s righteousness) were divided within them, and the Spirit of Christ went out through that division of God’s righteousness with the flame of love. This happened so that they might be grounded in the spirit of the Law and the Gospel of Grace; for they were to perform miracles, and the power of miracles comes from the Father’s omnipotence and property...

<summary>Böhme explains why Christ spoke in parables, distinguishing between the "Father's Voice" of strict righteousness/Law and the "Holy Spirit's Voice" of Grace that was fully released only after the Crucifixion. He describes the transition from the old legalistic understanding to the spiritual empowerment of the disciples at Pentecost.</summary>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 12.

and not through the quality of love and humility, which should only suffer and yield itself into **God’s** law and justice <note>In Böhme’s theology, "justice" (*Gerechtigkeit*) often refers to the strict, fiery "Law" or the "Wrathful" side of God that must be tempered by Love.</note> in anger, and fulfill that anger with love and suffering, and also walk in the love of mercy, as we clearly see in the person of **Christ**.

22. When **Christ** wished to perform miracles, He first prayed to His Father, as if into the fiery omnipotence, into justice: but when He had fulfilled the Father’s justice with His love and humility in His blood—the love-**tincture** <term>tincture: in Böhme’s mystical chemistry, a "tincture" is a spiritual essence or elixir that can transform a lower property (like wrath) into a higher one (like love).</term> of the name **JESUS**—then the Father’s justice in its wrath became subject to the love of **Christ**. From this subjection, after **Christ’s** ascension, other people (besides the disciples) were to hear the **Holy Spirit** speak and understand the parables of **Christ**, as it then happened, that they afterward fully understood all mysteries. But the others were to be born through the spirit of humility from **Christ’s** love, from the process of **Christ’s** suffering and death, and were to follow Him in His process under the **banner of the cross** in patience, and [surrender themselves] out of **God’s** justice with their humil-

# Chapter 12. Election by Grace.

25. For through the blood of **Christians**, **God’s** Justice in its wrath was brought into the great Love-Mercy, so that within **God’s** Justice, such wonders and deeds occurred through the humility of **Christ** among the **Christians**. This has now been lacking for a long time, ever since people have wanted to place the Spirit of **Christ** in man upon soft cushions and into fat bellies, into power, pomp, and glory. Yet, He appeared and was made manifest only because He willed to suffer and to fulfill **God’s** wrath in His Justice by surrendering Himself to His suffering.

26. Therefore, look at yourself, you so-called **Christendom**: does your righteousness now stand in the patience of **Christ’s** suffering? Do you seek anything more in your **Christian** name than for **Christ** to be made manifest in you through His love, His suffering, and His death, so that you desire only to become like His image (with which He fulfilled **God’s** Justice)?

27. Just look at yourself! Do you not seek merely vain excuses, and use the suffering of **Christ** to cover up your heathen, idolatrous image? What are you doing, you supposed **Christendom**? Where have you put the suffering and the patience of **Christ** in His obedience? You wicked one! Behold, a messenger is coming from **God’s** Justice, and he demands that from your attached **Christian** name with fire and sword, to destroy you as faithless, and to manifest His true children of obedience in His love. You shall soon experience this, if we speak as we ought. Amen!

# XV. Concerning Chapter 12.

...before **GOD**, it was not manifest until the justification <note>original: *Rechtfertigung*. In Böhme's theology, this is the process by which the soul is brought back into right relationship with God through Christ's sacrifice.</note> had occurred in the blood of **Christ**. Thus, those whom the **Father** had chosen as the instruments for the trial and suffering of **Christ** <note>original: *Proceß Christi*. This term describes the sequence of Christ's earthly life and passion as a necessary divine procedure.</note> were not to know beforehand what they were doing. But after they had done it, **GOD** opened their understanding for conversion; for this reason, **Christ** petitioned the **Father’s** Justice—which wished to swallow up these murderers and blood-judges in wrath—that **GOD’s** Justice might forgive them through the blood of **Christ**.

29. No one truly knew the **World-Savior**, not even the **Apostles** themselves, until the revelation that came from His death. One should not say that **GOD** particularly hardened these men so that they could not recognize **Christ**; rather, no one truly knew what His office <note>The "office" refers to Christ's specific role or duty in the plan of salvation.</note> was until after He had fulfilled that for which He had come.

30. These men who judged and killed **Christ** sat in the office of the Law of **GOD’s** Justice. The Law, acting as **GOD’s** Justice, killed **Christ**. They, however, believed they were doing **GOD** a service, and they were zealous for **GOD’s** Justice within the Law. This Law had also chosen them as instruments for the fulfillment of the Law in **Christ**, acting as the officials of the Law.

31. This was also the case with **Saul**, who was zealous for the Law of **GOD’s** Justice with true Divine zeal, just as the Law required—until the fulfillment of the Law seized him in the very heat of his undertaking. It indicated to him that this zeal for the Law had been fulfilled with blood. From then on, he was no longer to be zealous in the Law of the **Father’s** Justice, in the fire, but rather in the Law of fulfillment, in the love of **Christ**.

32. For those who crucified **Christ** are not the greatest sinners, because they were meant to do it by virtue of the office they held in the Law. Rather, the greatest sinners are those who, after the fulfillment of the Law, mock **Christ** and kill Him in His members <note>His "members" refers to the community of believers or the "body of Christ."</note>, remaining dead in their own sins. This is even after grace has been offered to them through the fulfillment of the Law in the power of the Spirit, accompanied by wonders and deeds. They stop up their ears and only blaspheme against it. These people blaspheme the **Holy Spirit** within the merit of **Christ**, despite His glorious revelation and the grace offered to them.

### Chapter 12. Election by Grace.

33. Therefore, we should look at the Scripture correctly and not speak of a special hardening <note>original: *Verstockung*. This refers to the theological concept of God "hardening" a person's heart so they cannot repent, famously applied to Pharaoh in the Book of Exodus.</note>, for Christ said: *They know not what they do*; no one knew who Christ was until His death, when they first recognized Him.

34. If, hereafter, someone should wish to say—following the words of Christ—"I do this or that, and I know not what I do; **GOD** has thus hardened me, I must do it. Likewise: I must steal, lie, and also practice usury, greed, and anger, and thereby pursue pride"; let such a one look closely at what he is, and whether he be not a child of the Devil, who has hardened him with such a delusion. If **GOD** had so hardened him that he *must* do it, then the Law of His justice would be removed from him, as would the teaching of the Gospel; for he would then be doing what he ought and must do, and could inevitably not be otherwise. All of this runs contrary to the Law of the Father’s justice, and contrary to the Law of the Son in His Gospel; such a man has no proof with which to excuse himself when **GOD’s** truth casts him into hell as a liar—a hell whose child he already is within the seized wrath of **GOD**, being born from the father of lies, as Christ said of Satan in John 8:44.

35. Furthermore, **Reason** <note>original: *Vernunft*. In Böhme's writings, "Reason" is often personified as the limited human intellect that tries to understand divine mysteries through logic alone, often leading to error.</note> raises an objection: Christ prayed for Peter that his faith might not fail, Luke 22:32. Why did He not also pray for the others, that their faith might not fail? Therefore, Reason says, there must indeed be a "predestined purpose" <note>original: *Vorsatz*. Refers to the divine decree or intention regarding who is saved.</note>.

36. **Explanation:** As was mentioned above, Peter and the other Apostles received the ground of faith from Christ’s voice before the fulfillment of the Law. Their faith still rested in the Law of the Father, as in the Spirit of **GOD’s** justice; therefore, Christ said to them: He would send them another **Comforter** <note>original: *Tröster*. This refers to the Holy Spirit (the Paraclete).</note>, namely the Spirit of Truth, who would take faith from Christ’s fulfillment and death, from His resurrection and restoration <note>original: *Wiederbringung*. A central concept in Böhme’s theology referring to the return of all things to their original divine state.</note>; He would remain with them and lead them into all truth, and would take from what is His and declare it within them.

37. The first faith was given to them from the Father, when He gave them to Christ as His disciples; within that faith still lay **GOD’s** justice in wrath. Satan desired to sift and penetrate this faith <note>See Luke 22:31.</note>, to see if it were that which should and would take his kingdom from him within man, and [the] hell...

<summary>Böhme argues against using "divine hardening" as an excuse for sin, asserting that such a mindset stems from the devil rather than God's decree. He explains the transition of the Apostles' faith from the Father's Law of justice to the Spirit of Truth made possible by Christ's resurrection.</summary>

### XV. From Chapter 12.

...destroy. That faith which, in the wrath of **GOD**, upon the true test of trial, could not yet endure in the fire; for this, the name **JESUS** interceded for them, so that this foundation—in which they were afterward to perform wonders in faith, love, and humility—might not cease within them. Otherwise, the miracles would not have occurred with such fiery power over life and death, but rather as an expression of **GOD’s** justice, which love overcame through the blood of Christ.

38. To the others, however, this faith was not yet given, for they were not Apostles, but had to wait for the promise; then the **faith of grace** <term>faith of grace: *Gnaden-Glaube*, a faith received as a gift through divine favor rather than earned or inherent</term> was given to them. And in this same faith of grace, Christ also intercedes for them, just as He did for Peter, so that their faith does not fail, as it is written: *He sits at the right hand of **GOD** and represents us, and pleads without ceasing with the justice of **GOD**, with unutterable sighs for us, within our very selves* <note>original: *mit unaussprechlichen Seufzen für uns*. A reference to Romans 8:26, suggesting that Christ’s spirit prays within the believer.</note>. If only we would finally learn to see and understand the Scripture and move away from useless chatter toward the foundation of truth.

39. Therefore, no one should say that Christ does not intercede for all people as He did for Peter, so that their faith might not fail; for He is the actual intercession, acting as the prayer within our very selves. Why then do we dally so long with such objections? We have been asked to explain these, and we mean it faithfully; for when Christ said: *Father, forgive them, they know not what they do*, He was praying for all those who did not yet know Him, but who would yet come to know Him.

40. But as for the objection: "Did He allow Judas to despair?"—look at what the Scripture says concerning Judas. Christ says in John 17:12: *I have lost none of those whom you have given me, except the lost child; that the Scripture might be fulfilled, which says, he who eats my bread treads upon me with his feet*, Psalm 41:10 <note>original: *tritt mich mit Füssen*. Modern translations usually render this as "lifted up his heel against me."</note>. Do you not see that Christ called him a "lost child" <note>original: *verloren Kind*. Usually translated in English Bibles as the "son of perdition."</note>, one who was already a thistle beforehand, whom the wrath in **GOD’s** justice had birthed into his own life?

41. Thus this Judas had to be called an Apostle as a figure and as the betrayer of Christ, to signify what kind of people would in the future be found among Christ’s teachers: namely, that they would eat the bread of the cup of Christ under the appearance of great holiness, and yet would only betray Christ in His members <note>original: *Gliedern*. In Böhme's thought, "members" refers to fellow believers who together make up the "body of Christ."</note> and help sentence Him to death; as such...

### Cap. 12. Election of Grace.

Thus these Judas-brothers must be a tool of the Justice of **GOD** in wrath <note>In this context, "wrath" refers to the stern, judging quality of God's nature, which Böhme balances against His love.</note> for that purpose, and they must be counted among the Apostles, so that people believe them to be Apostles.

45. No one can say with any foundation that **GOD** hardened Judas out of a special will and purpose so that he could not have been converted; rather, the Justice of **GOD** in wrath had seized him and formed and born him into a thistle <term>thistle: *Distel*, Böhme uses this botanical metaphor to describe someone whose internal nature is prickly and spiritually unproductive</term> before he was an Apostle—even in the seed, before the soul was born—as if from inherited sin, where **GOD** punishes even unto the third and fourth generation.

46. Thus wrath placed its own figure with Judas alongside Christ, in his office, so that one should recognize...
