# XV. Regarding Chapter 12.

it should be, it was **GOD’S** Will that His wrath should be extinguished in man, and yet the specific will of that wrath remained, dwelling within itself in **GOD’S** Justice as a <term>Center</term> <note>original: *Centrum*. In Böhme’s philosophy, the "center" is the point of origin or the "wheel" of nature from which all qualities manifest.</note> for the revelation of **GOD**, as was previously explained regarding the Center.

47. But should someone say: What can a child in the mother's womb do about being a thistle? To him it is said: it is the fault of the root (of which the thistle itself is a part), just as Christ said: <margin>Matthew 7:18.</margin> A corrupt tree cannot bring forth good fruit. The wrath of **GOD** also desires to be manifest in creatures, yet this is not from **GOD’S** purpose, but from the purpose of the fury <note>original: *Grimm*. This refers to the harsh, stern, or "fierce" quality of eternal nature that exists before it is enlightened by love.</note> of eternal nature itself; which, however, is not **GOD**, but fury, and serves as a cause of the fire from which the light is made manifest. If you see nothing here, then may **GOD** counsel you.

48. But if one were to say: Judas was sorry for his crime; that is indeed true. But the Devil is also sorry that he cannot be a good angel, but must be a devil; and because he cannot be so, he despairs of the grace of **GOD**, and that is his eternal hell.

49. So it was also with Judas; he was sorry that he was cast out from **GOD’S** grace, but he did not *desire* grace, for the wellspring for the desire of grace was not in him; he was not born of faith, as if from the promised seed. And although he indeed came from the same nature in which faith lay, and also had the "ingrafted Word" in the abyss of his soul, his soul already possessed a shape of darkness, which was entirely dead to grace and quite unfit for life. For even if a thistle were planted in honey, only a "fat" thistle would grow out of it. Grace does not belong to these, for Christ said to his disciples: Take and drink, this is my blood, which is shed for you and for many. The <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*. A term borrowed from alchemy meaning the spiritual essence or life-force that has the power to transform and refine.</note> was in the blood. The sun does not give its holy tincture to the thistle, which has a false life in opposition to the tincture; the sun does give it <term>Ens</term> <note>original: *Ens*. A Latin term for "being" or "essence"; the fundamental substance of a thing’s existence.</note> and substance, but the thistle is not capable of receiving the "jewel" <note>A metaphor for the precious gift of the Holy Spirit or divine light.</note>; it only receives a quality from the sun according to its own nature, as it serves its needs; thus it is also to be understood in this case. <margin>St. Paul says:</margin> For this reason, because you do not discern the Body of the Lord <note>original: "Darum, daß ihr nicht unterscheidet den Leib des HErrn." A reference to 1 Corinthians 11:29 regarding taking communion unworthily.</note>,

# Chapter 12. The Election of Grace.

the godless man receives it unto judgment, just as the thistle receives the sun. **1 Corinthians 11:29**.

50. **Likewise**, a further objection is raised by human reason regarding the blind man in **John 9:2**, where the disciples of **Christ** asked: Who has sinned, this man or his parents? To whom **Christ** gave the answer: Neither his parents nor this man have sinned; but rather, so that the works of **GOD** might be made manifest.

51. Explanation: **GOD** has enclosed the kingdom of this world in time, goal, measure, and weight. <note>original: *Sap. 11: 22*. This refers to the Wisdom of Solomon 11:20: "but thou hast ordered all things in measure and number and weight."</note> And the works of **GOD** stand in a <term>working figure</term> <note>original: *würckende Figur*. For Böhme, a "figure" is a physical manifestation or symbolic representation that expresses a hidden spiritual reality.</note>: when the figure is meant to become manifest, that thing in which and through which it shall be revealed is also present.

52. Since **Christ** was to be made manifest in this believing man born blind, before His suffering and the fulfillment of the Law of Nature; so the Law, with the eyes of nature, had to kill him from the beginning, so that **Christ** might open the eyes of faith for him—which eyes of faith thereafter also open the eyes of nature through grace. And this was a figure of how we had become blind toward **GOD** in **Adam**, and how we would become seeing again in **Christ**. For this blind man's blindness did not come from any particular inherited sin, for he was a <term>seed of faith</term> <note>original: *Glaubens-Samen*. This refers to an internal spiritual spark or potential that is predisposed to receive God's light.</note>, in whom **Christ**, through His assumption of humanity, had become active, and in which he also believed in Him; but this inner seeing of faith from **Christ** was not yet valid; he was first to become seeing through a human voice.

53. For when **JESUS** became man, then the human element was born into **GOD’S** vision; but the Law of **GOD** still held this vision captive in poor sinners, until our eyes saw through His death and from the fulfillment of the Law. Therefore, since this man had become seeing in the seed of faith while in his mother's womb—through **Christ’s** entry and revelation in humanity—nature killed his sight, so that he would not have to see with faith through the light of nature: for **GOD’S** justice in the law of nature had not yet been fully satisfied.

54. Thus, this man had to be born blind, so that the Divine eye in faith might make him seeing through the <term>speaking-in</term> <note>original: *Einsprechen*. This describes the act of God breathing or speaking His holy power into the soul, a central concept in Böhme’s theology of conversion.</note> of the holy name of **JESUS**, so that the glory of **GOD** would be made manifest. And one should not say that this—

Böhme explains that spiritual sight (the eyes of faith) must often supersede natural sight to fulfill divine justice.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Chapter 12 regarding the "Election of Grace." # XV. Concerning Chapter 12.

[...seeing], this blind man was not born blind through a special predetermination, but rather he was one from the root of the seed of faith; which faith the name **JESUS** (as **God’s** light in love) was meant to make seeing. He was one in the **clockwork of Christ** <note>original: *Uhr-wercke Christi*. Böhme uses this mechanical metaphor to describe the interconnected, predestined movements of spiritual history as a functioning machine designed by God.</note>, given by **God** the **Father** to **Christ** for his **process** <term>process</term> <note>original: *Proceß*. In Böhme’s theology, this refers to the mystical and physical "course" of Christ’s life—suffering, death, and resurrection—which every believer must also undergo internally.</note>, just as the Pharisees in the clockwork of the law of **God’s** justice also came to join the **process of Christ**.

55. **Likewise**, the saying in **Matthew 24:24** is also brought up by human reason, by which it wishes to maintain that **God** wants people to be deceived and damned, where **Christ** says: *False Christs and false prophets shall arise, so that in error, if it were possible, even the elect would be deceived.*

56. Explanation: This text says they *shall* arise; it does not say, however, that they are sent by **God**, much less out of **Christ**, to whom all power was given in heaven and on earth.

57. Thus, these false prophets were to arise out of the purpose of **God’s** wrath, as if out of the zeal of justice, and sift the hearts of the false **mouth-Christians** <note>original: *Maul-Christen*. A term for hypocrites who profess faith only with their mouths but lack internal spiritual transformation.</note> who call themselves Christians; these were to be sifted out of the **process of Christ** through this embittered spirit of **God’s** wrath, so that they believed the spirits of lies, because they call themselves Christians and yet **Christ** is not in them, but rather they are children of wrath. Thus they were to present their images of abomination and false interpretation, so that the children of the false name of **Christ**, covered with **Christ’s** purple mantle <note>A reference to the robe placed on Jesus during his mocking trial; here it symbolizes a superficial or hypocritical covering of holiness.</note>, might cling to them, and the true Christians might separate themselves from them, so that it might be known who **Christ** is: and also through the false prophets, the **process of Christ**—with betrayal, killing, and suffering—might be made manifest, and **Christ** would be killed forevermore by the Pharisees and heathens for the sake of their false divine service.

58. For **God’s** justice demands the Church of **Christ** in blood, and forevermore presents a cause with false prophets and Christians, which false prophets—along with the heathens, as tyrants—ceaselessly kill **Christ** in his members and sacrifice them to the justice of **God**, whereby **God’s** wrath is killed in the true Christians.

59. If one wishes to know these same false prophets now,

<summary>Böhme explains that God does not "will" the deception of the damned, but rather allows false prophets to arise as a sifting mechanism to distinguish true believers from hypocrites. He views the suffering of the Church as a continuation of the "Process of Christ," where the death of the faithful serves to extinguish divine wrath.</summary>

<keywords>Election of Grace, Clockwork of Christ, Process of Christ, False Prophets, Mouth-Christians, Divine Wrath, Divine Justice, Sifting of Souls, Martyrdom</keywords>

### Chapter 12. The Election of Grace.

->O 3<-

[...seeing] who they are, one should only look at those who have pieced together their opinions from the letter <note>original: *Buchstaben*. Böhme follows the Pauline distinction between the "dead letter" of literal scripture and the "living spirit" of internal experience.</note>, and have perhaps set up grand postils <note>A postil is a collection of sermons or biblical commentaries.</note> full of abusive writings and cudgels of **God’s** wrath, where one sect strikes the other in the eye and decries it as false. Yet these same shouters live exactly like one another; they write only for their own honor, wishing to be seen as highly learned people upon whom all the world should look, as if they were Christ. In truth, they are only the titular and **mouth-Christians** <note>original: *Maul-Christus*. A "mouth-Christian" is someone who professes faith with their lips but does not possess the spirit of Christ in their heart.</note>, without grace. They live entirely outside of Christ’s **process** <term>process</term> <note>original: *Proceß*. The necessary internal journey of a believer that mirrors Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection.</note> in the vain lusts of the flesh, and daily devise more intrigues for new orders and divine services, through which they might gain a glistening appearance. Thus they are honored all the more and endowed with riches to fill the belly of their god **Mauzim** <note>original: *Mauzim*. A reference to the "god of fortresses" in Daniel 11:38, often used by Reformation-era writers to symbolize the papacy or the "god of the belly"—material greed disguised as religion.</note> of the stomach.

60. These do not have Christ’s Spirit in them, nor are they apostles of Christ, but are all together only false prophets who interpret from the letter without true knowledge. For what they say, they themselves do not know or believe; they are the very ravening wolves of whom Christ spoke, who have no knowledge of Christ in them, even as they prophesy.

61. But of those who are in Christ, He said: it is not possible that they may be deceived. These are the ones in whom Christ has become man; according to their inner ground, they are in Christ, in heaven, and in **God**. They hear Christ speaking within them, for they hear only **God’s** Word and not the false prophets. If one were to weed out <note>original: *ausgäten*. Literally "to weed a garden."</note> these same false prophets now from all the sects, the apostolic host—those who call themselves apostles—would become very small.

62. Therefore, one should by no means say that **God** permits such false prophets to arise because He did not want to grant salvation to those people (who might otherwise have been saved). Human reason errs when it imagines that **God** has ordained one group for salvation and the other for damnation, and that because **God** wills it so, He sends them powerful delusions only so that they should fall, that He may demonstrate His wrath upon them.

63. You dear brothers, who are dismayed by such a delusion, we advise you: do not teach this delusion, be...

<summary>Böhme critiques hypocritical theologians who prioritize literal doctrine and worldly status over the internal spirit of Christ. He argues that false prophets arise from human greed rather than a divine decree to damn specific individuals.</summary>
<keywords>Election of Grace, false prophets, mouth-Christians, Mauzim, predestination, letter vs. spirit, clerical greed, inner word</keywords>

# XV. Regarding Chapter 12.

63. ...assured long ago in Christ’s Spirit within you from the very foundation, otherwise you will be caught in God’s righteousness along with the number of false prophets. If you do not have the door of Christ open in your souls, so that you may go out and in through Christ’s Spirit and find true and certain pasture for the sheep, so that you may graze them in Christ’s grass, then just leave it be.

64. Your **school-learning** <note>original: *Schulen-Kunst*. This refers to formal academic theology or "scholasticism," which Böhme often contrasts with direct spiritual experience.</note>, where you strike and overcome one another with words of reason and thereafter write and teach such victories of reason as Christ’s truth—that counts for nothing before God. For Christ called these people thieves and murderers, who climbed in through another door (such as through **logical conclusions** <term>logical conclusions</term> <note>original: *Vernunft-Schlüsse*. Arguments based on human logic and intellect rather than divine revelation.</note>) without Christ’s knowledge and will, outside of His Spirit and knowledge. If you are not armed with Christ’s Spirit, then do not go to war against such a powerful enemy as the devil, nor against God’s righteousness in His wrath. You will achieve nothing there with your logical conclusions without the blood of Christ in you; instead, you will only be captured by God’s strict righteousness within your logical conclusions and be chosen as false prophets in the wrath of God.

65. For no one is a prophet unless he is born from God’s righteousness within the great **clockwork** <note>original: *Uhrwerck*. Böhme uses the metaphor of a clock or "work of wheels" to describe the complex, interconnected, and predetermined movement of the cosmos and divine history.</note> of Divine order, in the spoken Word, at the goal of that same time, where the Holy Spirit of God speaks through that same goal in Divine order. He must be a "goal" <note>A "goal" (*Ziel*) in this context refers to a specific point of manifestation or a destination in the unfolding of God's plan.</note> in the clockwork within the **Great Mystery** <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*. In Böhme's thought, the *Mysterium Magnum* is the vast, hidden potential of God from which the visible world is expressed through the Word.</note>, through which the Spirit of God points toward another goal of revelation. Just as the prophets were such, and still are today, who stand at the goal of the great clockwork in the **providence of grace** <note>original: *Gnaden-Vorsehung*. Often translated as "predestination of grace."</note> in Christ Jesus, where God provided for us (meaning, "foresaw" us) in Christ Jesus before the foundation of the world. He must stand within God’s righteousness with his prophetic spirit, at the very point where God has placed the name of His love into righteousness, so that he might be born from the foundation of the law of God’s righteousness and purpose, and then also from the foundation of the ordained grace, so that he may teach the Law as God’s righteousness and also the Gospel as God’s love and the fulfillment of the Law.

66. This one is a right prophet, and no other, for

<meta>catchword: he</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that true spiritual authority comes from being part of the divine "clockwork" and the Great Mystery rather than from academic logic. He warns that those who rely on "school-learning" and human reason are false prophets who risk being caught in the judgment of God's righteousness.</summary>

->### Chapter 12. The Election of Grace.<-

...he is the goal of a kingdom in the **Great Mystery** <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*. A central concept in Böhme’s thought, referring to the divine chaos or "groundless" source from which the entire universe and all spiritual realms emerge.</note>, through which and out of which the order of the kingdoms on earth arises; he is the mouth of that same kingdom. But because he must teach how God's righteousness in **wrath** <note>original: *Zorne*. In Böhme's theology, the "wrath" of God is a foundational, fiery quality of divine nature that must be tempered or "transmuted" by love and grace.</note> shall be put to death by grace, and that grace must entirely surrender itself to the killing of righteousness before the wrath; so too, in the **process of Christ** <note>The "process of Christ" refers to the spiritual lived experience of following Christ's path through suffering to resurrection.</note>, he is sacrificed along with that same righteousness of God by the false prophets and Pharisees. For this must and shall be, so that his goal may also be led through the blood of Christ—through the wrath—and so that the goal of righteousness may be placed into grace; therefore the prophets must become martyrs of Christ.

67. Note this well, all you who wish to teach and believe you are called to it: look closely at your calling within yourselves, whether you are called by God in his **clockwork** <note>original: *Uhrwerck*. Böhme uses the image of a clock to describe the intricate, interconnected, and predetermined movement of God’s providence in history.</note> in Christ? Whether Christ has called you with his voice within you? If not, then you are nothing other than false prophets who run without being sent, and do not enter into the sheepfold through the door of Christ.

68. The fact that you rely on a **human calling** <note>original: *Menschen Ruff*. This refers to being appointed by a church hierarchy or university rather than by a direct spiritual experience or divine command.</note> may indeed count before men, and God also allows himself to be pleased by what humans do when it happens within his order—especially if you surrender yourselves from a human calling into God's calling, and also think on how you might become capable of the Divine calling within your human calling. Where that is not the case, and you remain only in a human calling in your own will, then you sit upon the **seat of pestilence** <note>original: *Stuhl der Pestilenz*. A reference to Psalm 1:1, often translated as the "seat of the scornful."</note>, and are Pharisees and false prophets. And even if there were many hundreds of thousands of you, the office does not make you prophets and shepherds of Christ unless you enter through Christ’s living door. And although this will likely not taste good to the Pharisee, the time is nevertheless born and the goal is at hand, that it shall be made manifest, and no human cunning helps against it anymore; woe to the people who despise this, for they shall be consumed in the zeal of God's righteousness.

69. Likewise, Reason also brings up the prophet Jonah as its proof that God forces people to evil and good as his purpose, just as He forced Jonah so that he had to go to Nineveh. Jonah 1.

70. Explanation: Hear, Reason, do not be mistaken, God—

->## XV. Concerning Chapter 12.<-

...Christ’s Spirit does not let itself be judged by human reason. Jonah was a prophet, born from the goal of the covenant, and stood as a **figure of Christ** <note>In Böhme’s theology, a "figure" (Typus) is a historical person or event that serves as a living prophecy or symbolic blueprint for Christ’s later life and work.</note>, just as Christ was to be cast into the **wrath** of God within the throat of the great whale of Divine Justice (in order to fulfill that justice); how He was to enter the sea of death, and how the wrath of God (which He overcame within that same whale of death) was to let Him go forth from itself, alive and free, just as Jonah came out from the belly of the whale.

71. He was a figure of Christ, born from the goal of the great **clockwork** <note>original: *Uhrwerck*. This refers to the mechanical precision of divine providence and the unfolding of time.</note>, out of the **Great Mystery** <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*. This refers to the foundational "ground" of all existence, containing the potential for both light and dark, or grace and wrath.</note>, out of both of God's purposes—namely, out of His grace and out of His justice. He was presented as a figure, like a play or drama of the Spirit of God, where the Spirit looked upon and pointed toward Christ through this figure: how the humanity of Christ—as our own assumed humanity—would be terrified before Nineveh, that is, before the danger of life. For Christ said, when the time had come for Him to enter Nineveh, meaning into God’s wrath:
> Father, if it be possible, let this cup pass from me. <note>Luke 22:42</note>
Likewise, He often hid Himself from the Pharisees, as from the Ninevites, just as Jonah did before Nineveh.

72. Furthermore, this figure indicates that when we poor **Jonites** <note>Followers of Jonah’s path; those called to speak God's truth who feel the impulse to flee from the burden of their calling.</note> are meant to announce God’s punishment and judgments to the people, and must venture our lives among them for the sake of the truth, we often seek excuses. We betake ourselves to the "sea of the world," seeking comfortable days, and flee from God's command, remaining silent out of fear of the Ninevites. It is then that the whale of God’s wrath comes and swallows the prophets into its mouth.

73. But the fact that Jonah was driven there by force indicates that God the Father's purpose in Christ should and must stand firm. Even though Adam turned away from God’s obedience into the worldly imagery of this life (whereby man was delivered over to the great whale, death), God’s purpose still stood: that Adam should rise again in Christ from the belly of death.

74. That is the figure of Jonah, dear brothers, and not your own personal purpose or a forced compulsion toward evil or good. It is the figure of Christ; therefore, cease from such conclusions <note>Böhme is likely arguing against strict predestination or "double predestination" here, suggesting the story is about the cosmic process of salvation rather than individual damnation.</note>, and do not blaspheme the Holy Spirit in His wonders within the figure of

<meta>catchword: Chri-</meta>

<summary>Böhme interprets the biblical story of Jonah as a symbolic "figure" of Christ’s death and resurrection, arguing that even when man (represented by Adam or Jonah) falters, God's redemptive purpose remains certain.</summary>

## Chapter 13.
# A Summary Conclusion of All These Questions.
### Summaries.

Explanation of the saying in John 17: 6. §. 1. 2. What the Father's "giving" is, and to whom He reveals Himself? ibid. God's love and wrath have no limit and are immeasurable. 3. Christ speaks Himself into all people, but not all hear Him; 4. so the Devil also speaks into them. 5. To save repentance for the very end is dangerous, 6. whereby wrath devours many people. 7. How true repentance happens? 8. It is difficult if the foundation is false. ibid. The outward splendor of funerals is the Devil's mockery. 9. God’s speaking in the human word forgives sin. 13. The "New Birth" must be present. 14. 15. Reason will take offense at the <term>Author's</term> <note>original: *Autoris*. Referring to Böhme himself.</note> manner of speaking, but it is not written for her <note>In Böhme’s writing, "Reason" (Vernunft) is often personified as a female figure who relies on logic rather than spiritual illumination.</note>. 16. We should seek, ask, and knock; not turn back into the serpent and crucify Christ. 17. God draws all people: also all know that there is a God. 18. The Christians who hinder the truth with their opinions shall be heavily judged. 19. Exhortation to harmony and love. 20. 21. Whoever has Christ has everything, 22. and love is the only ground of religion. 23. and following.

Finally, Reason also introduces the saying of Christ, John 17: 6. where He says: **"Father, I have revealed your name to the people whom you gave me from the world."** With this, she <note>Reason</note> wants to prove that Christ reveals His name to no one unless the Father first gives that person to Him out of His divine purpose, whether the person wills it or not.

2. Explanation: O you miserably blinded Reason, how are you so blind! Do you know what the Father's "giving" is? It is the center in the soul, acting as the Father’s will in the <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: *Scienz*. Derived from the Latin *scientia*, Böhme uses this to mean a sort of "inner science" or "formative knowledge"—the inherent capacity of the soul to direct its own desire and will.</note> of eternal righteousness, where the <term>Scienz</term> is burdened either with a desire for abominations or with the divine love of grace; thither she gives the speaking word in God’s righteousness; either into a root...

<header>XV. Regarding Chapter 13</header>

...into a thistle, or into a root of the faith-seed. To the root in the faith-seed Christ is revealed: for it is Christ's root, from which a Christian is born in Christ. To those or these Christian people Christ has always revealed himself from the beginning of the world, and given them God's name, for He himself is God's name.

3. This text is not to be understood as if God, from the beginning of the world, had made a decree, and placed that decree into a certain order and compulsion, regarding how many He would give Him, and which ones; and that this could not be overstepped, as captive reason <note>Reason (Vernunft) that is "captive" refers to human intellect that relies only on logic and dogma, unable to grasp spiritual mysteries.</note> understands it. No, no, the Tree of Christ is immeasurable; God's grace, and also his righteousness in the fire, are both immeasurable. For had God set a limit in love and wrath, it would exist within a measurability and have a beginning, and so one would also have to think that it would come to an end. No, no, the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil stands in the eternal ground where there is no time nor limit. God's grace in Christ is immeasurable and from eternity, as is also the realm of nature in the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio Magno*. In Böhme's thought, the "Great Mystery" is the chaotic, unmanifested source of all potentiality.</note>, out of which the fiery <term>Science</term> <note>original: *Scienz*. Not "science" in the modern sense, but an active, driving "spark of knowledge" or "will-force" that activates the spirit.</note> has revealed itself from the will of the <term>Abyss</term> <note>original: *Ungrunde*. The "Ungrund" is the "groundless" or "bottomless" void—the absolute nothingness that precedes the manifestation of God’s will.</note>. Just as Christ revealed God's name to people (as the root of the faith-seed) from the beginning of the world, so too does He until the end of the world: for thus He also said to His disciples when they asked Him about the end of the world: "As the lightning comes from the east and shines to the west, so will be the coming of the Son of Man." Matthew 24:27. Just as the sun gives itself to all things throughout the day, and shines upon them, and penetrates into all things, be they good or evil; so also does the Divine Sun, Christ, as the true light of the world.

4. Christ withdraws himself from no one with his light of grace; He calls them all and shines into them with his voice, excluding none at all: but they do not all hear and see him, for they are not of God. The <term>Science</term> <note>active knowing or inner drive</note> of the Father's bottomless will, in the soulful creature, has introduced itself into a foreign imagery, becoming a thistle of the snakes; this will sees and hears nothing when God's righteousness speaks within it: "Do right, or I will kill you," for

<meta>catchword: this</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that God's grace and Christ's presence are immeasurable and universal, comparing Christ to a sun that shines on all people regardless of their state. He rejects the idea of a fixed, limited number of the elect, blaming "captive reason" for such restrictive doctrines.</summary>

# Chapter 13. The Election of Grace.

<meta>catchword: For</meta>

"this and that is sin; do it not, or you will be cast out by GOD."

5. When the soul hears this within itself, the Devil comes in his serpent-form and speaks into the <term>Science</term> <note>original: *Scienz*. This refers to the soul’s active, inner "spark" of knowledge or will-force that drives its desires and perceptions.</note>: "Tarry yet in the flesh in this or that pleasure, such as greed, pride, envy, anger, adultery, gluttony, and mockery; there is still time enough for you to perform repentance at your end. First, gather a great treasure for yourself so that you no longer need the world; only then enter into a pious life, for then you can live in solitude without the world’s mockery and have no need of it."

6. Thus, one day and one year is added to another until the hour of death; then, one wishes to be a child of grace and to be blessed, even though one has been stuck in the "serpent" the entire time. Then the priest is expected to come with God's body <note>A reference to the Eucharist or Holy Communion administered as Last Rites.</note> and bring along the new <term>Angelic-Birth</term>, though many a priest does not possess it himself and is merely a guest in that same state of being.

7. These people, because they are stuck in the "serpent," are not given to Christ <note>original: *Christo*</note>, but rather to the wrath of GOD. The wrath of GOD does not let them go unless the <term>Science</term> of the soul turns itself within toward Grace. And when that happens, it is a "giving" [from the Father], for the Divine Sun immediately shines into the still-standing <term>Science</term> and ignites it; and this ignition is now the Name of GOD which Christ gives to the soul. From this, the soul begins to draw from Christ and to work a repentance of forgiveness, once it begins to stand still from the <term>Imagination</term> <note>original: *Einbildung*. In this context, it refers to the soul "modeling" or "forming" itself after worldly or false images rather than the divine image.</note> of falsehood.

8. For it is said: "To do [the sin] no more is the greatest repentance." This happens when the ground of the soul begins to be still from its <term>Imagination</term> and enters into its <term>Abyss</term> <note>original: *Abgrund/Ungrund*. The "Groundless" or the eternal source of the soul where it is free from created things.</note>, which it has the power to do—unless it is already a thistle, then it runs and grows until the end of time. However, there is no judgment upon it from the outside, but only its own judgment while it is in the life of this world, until the time of harvest. But it is difficult if both the inner and outer ground of the external <term>Constellation</term> <note>original: *Constellation*. The astrological and spiritual alignment of a person's nature at birth.</note> is false; these commonly run to the end in this way. Then comes only a <term>Judas-Repentance</term> <note>A reference to the despairing, "too late" regret of Judas Iscariot, which leads to destruction rather than transformation.</note>, and the "tickling" or comfort of Christ's suffering helps them little if the <term>Essence</term> <note>original: *Ens*. The actual spiritual substance or "being."</note> of faith is not present.

9. The pomp of the magnificent burials of the dead beast <note>Referring to the physical body or the animal nature of the unredeemed human.</note> is only the Devil's mockery, with which he mocks them.

Böhme warns that late-life rituals are ineffective if the soul's actual essence has not fundamentally changed from the "serpent" nature to the "angelic" nature.</summary>

It moves from the concept of the Father "giving" souls to the Son toward a deep explanation of how grace and forgiveness function as an internal chemical-like change rather than a mere legal decree.</meta>

# XV. Concerning Chapter 13.

9. For <term>imputed grace</term> <note>original: *zugerechnete Gnade*. This refers to the theological concept of "imputation," where Christ’s righteousness is credited to the believer.</note> is not valid from the outside—as if we were released by external words of grace, like a lord or prince grants life to a murderer out of mercy. No, no; the imputed grace of Christ must become manifest in us, in the inward ground of the soul, and become our life.

10. One should not save repentance for the end of life, for an old tree takes root poorly. If Christ is not in the soul, there is no grace or forgiveness of sins; for Christ himself is the forgiveness of sins. He <term>transmutes</term> <note>original: *transmutiret*. A term borrowed from alchemy, suggesting the literal transformation of one "base" substance—here, the soul's wrath—into a "noble" one, the Divine Fire.</note> the horrors introduced into God's wrath within the soul with his blood, and changes them into the Divine Fire. It is as he said to the Pharisees regarding the paralyzed man <note>original: *gichtbrüchtigen Menſchen* - literally "gout-stricken" or "palsied."</note>, when he spoke: "Your sins are forgiven." This occurred when the man caught Christ’s voice in his soul; then the living Word within him forgave his sins—that is, it overcame the sins and stepped upon the head of the will of the "serpent’s" introduced horrors with the fire of love.

11. Thus, no one can forgive sin but Christ within man. Where Christ lives in man, there is <term>absolution</term> <note>original: *Abſolution*. The formal release from guilt or sin.</note>. For when Christ said: "Receive the Holy Spirit; whosever sins you remit, they are remitted; and whosever sins you retain, they are retained," this refers to the true Apostles and their right successors who have taken the Holy Spirit from Christ, and who themselves live and are in Christ and have Christ's voice within them. These have the power to <term>speak into</term> <note>original: *einzusprechen*. Böhme uses this to describe a spiritual transmission where the Word is literally "spoken into" the being of another.</note> the hungry soul the living Word of Christ that dwells within them—and no one else has this power. Let them call themselves what they like or put on a grand appearance; they must be Christ’s Apostles if they wish to administer his office; otherwise, they are only Pharisees and wolves.

12. Also, the soul must open its "hungry mouth" toward this "speaking-in," otherwise the Word does not enter it. For the Word did not enter into everyone when Christ himself preached and taught, but only into the hungry and thirsty souls, of whom Christ said: "Blessed are those who hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled"—understand this as being filled with the fullness of his Word.

13. For the forgiveness of sins does not rest with men...

<meta>catchword: giving,</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that forgiveness is not a legal formality but a spiritual transmutation where Christ's presence within the soul transforms divine wrath into love. He asserts that true spiritual authority to forgive sins belongs only to those who truly possess the Holy Spirit and can "speak" the living Word into receptive souls.</summary>

<keywords>imputed grace, transmutation, forgiveness of sins, inner ground, absolution, hungry soul, apostolic succession, Divine Fire, internal repentance</keywords>

### Chapter 13. Election of Grace

...but in the power of Christ’s word which dwells within man; it is not man’s speaking that forgives sin, but God’s speaking within the human word. This does not enter into the false thistle <note>A metaphor for the unrepentant or "thorny" nature of the fallen man.</note>, but into the soul, where the <term>seed of faith</term> <note>original: *Glaubens-Same*. In the author's theology, this is a divine spark or potentiality within the soul that can grow into a new spiritual life.</note> lies within the sound of the movement, and where the soul stands still from the formation of the <term>serpent-desire</term> <note>original: *Schlangen-Begierde*. This refers to the egoistic, worldly cravings and "poison" inherited from the Fall of Man.</note>.

14. Therefore, do not rely on men; they cannot forgive your sins or give you grace, unless you yourselves hunger and thirst after righteousness. To delay repentance until the end of life is a <term>Judas-repentance</term> <note>original: *Judas-Busse*. A false or despairing repentance that comes too late or lacks true transformation, named after the despair of Judas Iscariot.</note>; it is not enough merely to be comforted by words, but one must be born anew.

15. So, dear brothers, I have wished to answer briefly from the fundamental ground regarding these points mentioned; and this is my opinion: that the sayings of the Scripture are all true, but <term>Self-Reason</term> <note>original: *Eigene Vernunft*. Human intellect that attempts to understand divine mysteries through logic alone, without spiritual enlightenment.</note> errs and does not understand them outside of Christ. The Apostle says: *We have not received a spirit of slavery, that we should fear again, but a spirit of adoption* <note>original: *kindlichen Geist* - literally "child-like spirit."</note>, *whereby we cry: Abba, dear Father.* Romans 8:15. We have not received the mind of the world or of the flesh in the promised grace, but the child-like mind of Christ, who has made us free from the law of sin. Therefore, every person should be of the same mind as Jesus Christ, the only man in grace, says the Apostle in Philippians 2:5. And whoever does not have this mind perceives nothing of what belongs to the Spirit of God; it is foolishness to him, and he does not comprehend it. 1 Corinthians 2:14.

16. Now, if in this very sharp exposition we might seem "mute" or silent to some, or a stumbling block or offense—because they might say we use strange, unusual speech in our fundamental ground—then we say with truth before God’s eyes that we have no other way to give it than as it has been given to us in the mind of Christ. Whoever is of Christ will well understand it; as for the other mockers and "clever-talkers" who have Reason as their master, we have written nothing for them.

17. However, we admonish our dear brothers in Christ to look through and read such a <term>treatise</term> <note>original: *Tractat*.</note> with patience, for its name is: *The longer, the dearer* <note>original: *Je länger ie lieber*. A proverbial expression meaning something becomes more precious the more time one spends with it; also a common name for the honeysuckle plant.</note>: the more it is sought, the more is found. Because Christ himself tells us to seek, knock, and ask, and has given us the promise that—

<summary>The author argues that forgiveness is a divine act within the soul rather than a human decree, emphasizing that spiritual understanding requires the "Mind of Christ" rather than mere human logic. He encourages patient study of his difficult "treatise," promising that its value grows with repeated reading.</summary>

<keywords>Election of Grace, Seed of Faith, Self-Reason, New Birth, Judas-Repentance, Mind of Christ, Jacob Böhme, spiritual transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the transformation of the soul, moving from the definition of a "Judas-repentance" to the active responsibility of the believer to respond to the Holy Spirit's drawing. It warns against the pride of "Babel"—the confusion caused by prioritizing personal opinion over divine truth.</meta>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 13.

17. We are to receive and find; thus we should not desire to stand still in sins and wait until the **grace of God** overcomes and forces us; nor should we think that **God’s** Spirit would make good out of evil, except for the poor sinner who is not yet entirely a <term>thistle</term> <note>original: *Distel*. A recurring metaphor in this text for a soul so hardened by ego and malice that it can no longer receive the "dew" of divine grace.</note>. He indeed sometimes overtakes such a one in his sins and draws him away from them: if he allows himself to be drawn, it is good; but if he will not at all, but steps again into the <term>serpent</term> <note>original: *Schlange*. Refers to the "serpent-desire" or the fallen, selfish nature of man.</note>, and crucifies ***Christ***, he blasphemes the **Holy Spirit**, of whom the **Scripture** says that he has no forgiveness eternally. ***Hebrews 6: 6, 7; chapter 10: 26.***

18. There is no human being who may say that he has not been drawn at some time, especially in his thoughts; even the ungodly are so. ***Christ*** shines upon all nations, upon one as upon the other; to the one in his revealed name; to the other people, however, in a name of the **One God**: He draws them all. And because of His drawing and the knowledge which is written in their hearts—so that they know there is a **God** whom they should honor—and they do not do so, they will be judged.

19. How much more, however, will we be judged, we who call ourselves ***Christians*** and have the true knowledge, but suppress the **truth** and transform it into lies for the sake of a preconceived opinion that we once imagined and made known to the world? And even if we are afterwards led to the light, we allow ourselves more honor than **God**, and wish to hide, besmirch, and cover the light with <term>alien interpretations</term> <note>original: *fremder Deuteley*. This refers to human intellectualizing or "spin" that obscures the simple spiritual truth to protect one's reputation or dogma.</note>, so that human delusion sits in ***Christ’s*** place as an idol. For it happens many times in this way, and <term>Babel</term> <note>In this context, Babel symbolizes the "city of confusion," representing sectarianism and the corrupt, prideful state of institutional religion.</note> stands entirely within this: that many a man does not cease to defend his once-declared opinion, even if he must <note>original: *bey den Haaren herzu ziehen*. Literally "to pull by the hair"—a German idiom meaning to use a far-fetched or forced argument.</note> drag the entire ***Scripture*** into it by force.

20. Dear Lords and brothers, let us give the honor to ***Christ***, and meet one another in a friendly manner with modest words and instruction; let each one present his gifts to the other in a brotherly will: for there are many kinds of **knowledge** and interpretations; as long as they proceed from the **Mind of Christ**, they all stand upon **One Ground**.

21. We should not [despise/condemn] one another because of the unequal gifts...

It critiques the pride of sectarian "Babel," where scholars twist Scripture to defend their own reputations, and calls for brotherly unity in the "Mind of Christ."</summary>

### Chapter 13. Divine Election.
<note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*. This refers to the theological concept of "Election by Grace" or Predestination, a central debate during the Reformation regarding how God chooses who is saved.</note>

...follow, but rather rejoice in love with one another, that **God’s** wisdom is so inexhaustible; and think upon the **future**, how well it shall be for us when all this knowledge will become manifest from **One**, and in one soul, so that we all recognize **God’s** gifts and have our joy in one another, and each will rejoice in the other’s gift, just as the beautiful flowers in their various colors and virtues rejoice next to one another in **One Mother** <note>In the author's mystical philosophy, the "Mother" often refers to the "Eternal Nature" or the divine matrix from which all things grow.</note> on the earth: such also is our resurrection and return.

22. Why then do we want to quarrel here about a knowledge of the gift? In ***Christ*** lie all the treasures of wisdom: if we have Him, we have everything; but if we lose Him, we have lost everything, and also ourselves.

23. The sole ground of our religion is that we love ***Christ*** within us, and love one another as ***Christ*** has loved us, in that He gave His life for us into death: which love does not become manifest in us unless ***Christ*** is born as man and made manifest in us; He gives us His love, so that we love ourselves in Him as He loves us; for He gives our soul His **flesh and blood** forever to eat and to drink: and whichever soul does not eat and drink this, has no **Divine Life** within it. *John 6: 54.*

24. Therefore I admonish the beloved reader, if anything in this <term>treatise</term> <note>original: *Tractat*.</note> should be too complex <note>original: *scharfsinnig*. Literally "sharp-minded" or "acute," referring to something that requires deep intellectual or spiritual effort to grasp.</note> for him; that he would give **God** the glory, pray, and read this rightly: everything that the sun shines upon, and the heavens encompass, as well as hell and all the depths, lies within man; he is an inexhaustible **fountain-spring**; in time, he may completely and very well comprehend and grasp this high ground which **God** has given to us (as a simple man).

25. We would have him warned against mocking alone, as his soul and eternity are dear to him; for he will not touch us, but the fierce wrath of **God** within himself. But me, who have been the cause for this work, he may indeed touch, for I stand without his touching in ***Christ’s*** bonds. But I would have him exhorted in love, to show himself as a brother in ***Christ***; and

<summary>The author describes a vision of spiritual harmony where individual gifts are celebrated like flowers in a meadow, centered on the inner birth of Christ rather than intellectual dispute. He concludes with a warning to the reader to approach these deep mysteries with prayer rather than mockery.</summary>

### XV. Concerning Chapter 13.

17. We ought to receive and find; thus we should not willingly remain in sins and wait for **God’s** grace to overtake and compel us. Nor should we think that **God’s** Spirit intends to make good out of evil, except for the poor sinner who is not yet entirely a thistle <note>In this mystical context, a "thistle" represents a person whose heart has become completely hardened and prickly with sin, making them unable to receive the "dew" of grace.</note>; he is indeed sometimes overtaken by Him in his sins and drawn away from them. If he then allows himself to be drawn, it is well; but if he is entirely unwilling and instead steps back into the serpent <note>To "step back into the serpent" refers to returning to the state of the Fall or the deceptive nature of the ego.</note> and crucifies **Christ**, he blasphemes the **Holy Spirit**, of whom the Scripture says that he has no forgiveness eternally. *Hebrews 6:6-7; chapter 10:26.*

18. There is no human being who can say that he has not been drawn at some time, especially in his thoughts—even the godless man is thus. **Christ** shines upon all nations, one like the other: to one in His revealed Name <note>This refers to the Christian world where the name of Jesus is known.</note>; to another nation, however, in the name of the **Only God**. He draws them all: and because of His drawing and the knowledge which is written in their hearts—so that they know there is a **God** whom they ought to honor—if they do not do so, they shall be judged.

19. How much more, then, shall we be judged, we who call ourselves Christians and possess the true knowledge, yet suppress the truth and turn it into lies for the sake of a preconceived opinion that we have once imagined and made known to the world? And even if we are afterwards led toward the light, we grant ourselves more honor than **God**, and wish to hide, smear, and cover the light with foreign interpretations <note>original: "Deuteley." This carries a pejorative sense of "fanciful or hair-splitting interpretation."</note>, so that the delusion of men might sit as an idol in **Christ’s** place. For it often happens thus, and **Babylon** <note>original: "Babel." For the author, this signifies the "City of Confusion," representing the warring, dogmatic institutional churches of his time.</note> stands entirely within this: that many a man does not cease to defend his once-declared opinion, even if he must pull the entire Scripture in by the hair <note>A German idiom meaning to force or strain a text to fit a meaning it does not naturally have.</note>.

20. Dear lords and brothers, let us give the honor to **Christ**, and meet one another kindly with temperate words and instruction; let each one present his gifts to the others in brotherly goodwill. For there are many kinds of knowledge and interpretations; as long as they proceed from the mind of **Christ**, they all stand upon **One Ground**.

21. We should not [persecute] <note>The catchword "folgen" at the bottom of the page suggests the start of the next sentence, likely "folgen oder verfolgen," meaning to pursue or persecute.</note> one another because of unequal gifts...

# Chapter 13. Divine Election <note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*</note>.

follow, but rather rejoice in love with one another that **God’s** wisdom is so inexhaustible; and think upon the future, how well it shall be for us when all this knowledge shall become manifest from One, and in one soul, so that we recognize all of **God’s** gifts and have our joy in one another, and each will rejoice in the other's gift, just as the beautiful flowers in their various colors and virtues on the earth rejoice next to one another in one Mother <note>In Böhme’s thought, the "Mother" often refers to the *Mysterium Magnum* or the divine nature from which all variety grows.</note>: such also is our resurrection and return.

### XV. Of the Chapter.

where he is able, through Divine gifts, to provide an even clearer explanation; when I then see such an explanation, I will rejoice in his gift and thank the Most High, who so richly bestows all kinds of gifts among us. Amen.

->THE END.<-

Note <note>original: *Nota*</note>: The author considers this book on the **Election of Grace** <note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*; the theological concept of how God chooses those who will receive salvation</note> to be his most profound work, written from the deepest foundations of his awakened **Divine gift**. The errors regarding **Predestination** <note>The doctrine that all events, especially the salvation or damnation of souls, have been willed by God.</note> prevalent at that time provided the occasion for it. In particular, however, it was born out of the request of Mr. Balthasar Tilken and the author’s **Theosophical friends** <term>literally "wisdom of God"; a circle of spiritual seekers interested in mystical and direct knowledge of the Divine</term>. This article had already been addressed in the *Second Apology* against the aforementioned B. Tilken (see No. XX), where a note at the end of page 164 also mentions that said *Apology* was called the *Treatise on the Election of Grace* until the subsequent completion of this more detailed work. Related to this are the **Epistles** <note>original: *Sendbriefe*; the author’s published correspondence</note> 16, 23, 39, 40, 41, and 42.
